### [7] [Exact Solution of the $(2+1)$-Dimensional Dunkl-Klein-Gordon Oscillator with a Uniform Magnetic Field in Non-Commutative Phase Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37820)
*R. D. Mota,D. Ojeda-Guillén*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves the Klein-Gordon oscillator in a non-commutative phase space generalized via Dunkl derivatives, with and without a uniform magnetic field; it derives exact eigenfunctions and energy spectra (generalized Landau levels), analyzes parity symmetry, takes the non-relativistic limit, and confirms consistency with prior commutative Dunkl models in appropriate limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the combined effects of non-commutativity, Dunkl calculus (encoding reflection symmetries), and external magnetic fields on relativistic quantum systems—specifically the Klein-Gordon oscillator—and to understand how these deformations modify spectral structure and symmetry properties.

Method: Introduces Dunkl derivatives into commutative phase space to study reflection parity; applies Seiberg-Witten map to lift the system to non-commutative phase space; solves the resulting deformed Klein-Gordon equation exactly for eigenfunctions and spectrum; computes non-relativistic limit; performs graphical analysis of energy dependence on deformation parameters; verifies recovery of known commutative Dunkl models in limiting cases.

Result: Exact analytical solutions for eigenfunctions and energy spectra (including generalized Landau levels under magnetic field); identification of parity-dependent energy level shifts induced jointly by Dunkl parameters, non-commutativity, and magnetic field; graphical confirmation of spectral behavior; successful reduction to established commutative Dunkl models when non-commutative or magnetic parameters vanish.

Conclusion: The interplay among Dunkl deformation, phase-space non-commutativity, and magnetic field induces rich, parity-sensitive spectral modifications in the Klein-Gordon oscillator; the framework unifies and extends previous commutative and non-relativistic treatments, offering a consistent relativistic generalization of Dunkl-deformed quantum systems in non-commutative geometry.

Abstract: We solve the Klein-Gordon oscillator in a non-commutative phase space, generalized using Dunkl derivatives, both with and without interaction with a uniform magnetic field. For each of these cases, we begin by introducing the Dunkl derivative into the standard commutative phase space to evaluate the reflection parity symmetries. Then, using the Seiberg-Witten map, we extend the generalized system to a non-commutative phase space. In both cases, we determine the eigenfunctions and the energy spectrum exactly (which correspond to generalized Landau levels when a magnetic field is present). Furthermore, we compute the non-relativistic limit of the system and graphically analyze the behavior of the energy spectra. Our results demonstrate that the combination of the Dunkl deformation parameters, the non-commutative phase space geometry, and the magnetic field produces a parity-dependent modification of the energy levels. Finally, we verify that when the external magnetic field or the non-commutative parameters vanish, our exact solutions correctly reduce to the commutative Dunkl models established in previous works.

</details>


### [8] [Algebraic Quantum Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37904)
*Kasia Rejzner*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces algebraic quantum field theory (AQFT), emphasizing its structural foundations—observables, spacetime localization, and states—highlighting distinctions from ordinary quantum mechanics, including the Reeh–Schlieder theorem, local von Neumann algebras, split property, superselection theory, and extensions to curved spacetimes, interactions, and gauge theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous, state-independent framework for quantum field theory that accommodates diverse physical situations (vacuum, thermal, charged representations) without presupposing a common Hilbert space, while clarifying conceptual and structural differences from finite-dimensional quantum mechanics.

Method: Constructive exposition grounded in operator algebras: builds AQFT from quantum mechanical principles; constructs the free scalar field; applies axiomatic constraints (causality, positive energy); analyzes structural theorems (Reeh–Schlieder, split property); incorporates superselection theory, local covariance, and perturbative/homological methods for interacting and curved-spacetime settings.

Result: A coherent, mathematically precise formulation of AQFT that elucidates foundational features—including state independence, locality, type III von Neumann algebras, particle statistics via superselection sectors, and adaptability to general relativity and quantum information contexts.

Conclusion: AQFT provides a robust, flexible, and conceptually clarifying framework for quantum physics beyond standard quantum mechanics, unifying structural insights across vacuum, thermal, and charged regimes, and enabling rigorous treatment of gravity, interactions, and measurement.

Abstract: Algebraic quantum field theory describes a quantum system through its observables, their localisation in spacetime, and the states that assign them expectation values. The separation of the specification of observables from the choice of a state makes it possible to compare vacuum, thermal and charged representations without assuming that they share a preferred Hilbert space. We develop the framework from familiar quantum mechanics, construct the free scalar field, and explain how causality and positive energy constrain local measurements. The Reeh--Schlieder theorem, the structure of local von Neumann algebras and the split property reveal key differences between quantum theory in the continuum (i.e. QFT) and ordinary quantum mechanics with finitely many degrees of freedom. Superselection theory relates observable charges to statistics and internal symmetries. Finally, local covariance and perturbative and homological methods extend the discussion to curved spacetime, interactions and gauge theories. We also discuss measurement theory and quantum information aspects of AQFT.

</details>


### [9] [From gauge-covariant Noether invariants to electromagnetic velocity-dependent mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38128)
*A. S. de Castro*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a systematic reduction from relativistic gauge-invariant Noether charges (derived via Noether–Bessel–Hagen formalism) to nonrelativistic, velocity-dependent conserved quantities—such as generalized linear and angular momentum and energy—while preserving gauge quasi-invariance throughout; it clarifies how gauge-dependent representatives (e.g., in uniform magnetic fields) yield gauge-invariant physical charges, and extends the framework to many-body interactions (Darwin term) and closed particle–field systems via total stress-energy Killing charges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile and explicitly link three distinct but established frameworks—relativistic gauge-invariant worldline mechanics, nonrelativistic velocity-dependent Lagrangian mechanics, and many-particle conservative approximations—by preserving the role of electromagnetic gauge symmetry in conserved quantities, rather than identifying or conflating their physically distinct charges.

Method: Uses the Noether–Bessel–Hagen construction for gauge-quasi-invariant symmetries; derives the canonical worldline one-form $P = -m u^\flat + qA$; defines conserved charges $C_X = P(X) - q\chi_X$ for Killing vectors $X$ satisfying $\mathcal{L}_X A = d\chi_X$; performs rigorous low-velocity expansion to recover nonrelativistic generators; applies to uniform magnetic field (gauge-dependent representative → gauge-invariant translational charge), Darwin interaction (conservative $O(c^{-2})$ many-body limit after field elimination), and closed particle–field systems (total stress-energy Killing charge).

Result: An explicit, symmetry-respecting reduction map connecting relativistic gauge-invariant charges, nonrelativistic generalized momenta/energies, and many-body field-theoretic conserved quantities—without equating them—thereby unifying conceptual foundations while preserving their physical and mathematical distinctions.

Conclusion: Gauge quasi-invariance under spacetime symmetries fully determines physically meaningful conserved quantities across relativistic, nonrelativistic, and many-body regimes; the Noether–Bessel–Hagen framework provides the precise geometric and algebraic structure needed to track these quantities consistently through asymptotic reductions, especially when electromagnetic potentials are involved.

Abstract: For a charged particle, a spacetime symmetry need preserve the electromagnetic potential only up to a gauge transformation. Starting from the standard Noether--Bessel-Hagen construction, we make explicit the reduction from the relativistic gauge-invariant worldline charge to the generalized constants of nonrelativistic velocity-dependent mechanics, retaining the quasi-invariance boundary term throughout. With the convention $S_p=-mc\int ds+q\int A$, the canonical worldline one-form is $P=-m u^\flat+qA$, and a Killing vector satisfying $\mathcal L_XA=d\chi_X$ generates $C_X=P(X)-q\chi_X$. Its low-velocity limit is the Noether--Bessel-Hagen charge of the coordinate-time Lagrangian, from which generalized linear momentum, angular momentum and energy follow generator by generator. A uniform magnetic field gives an elementary gauge-dependent representative but gauge-invariant translational charge. The Darwin interaction then supplies the conservative $O(c^{-2})$ many-particle realization after field elimination, while a closed particle--field system is governed by the total stress-energy Killing charge. The result is an explicit reduction map linking three established descriptions without identifying their distinct conserved quantities.

</details>


### [10] [Homogenization theory of topological Anderson insulators: genuine gaps and pseudo-gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38150)
*Guillaume Bal*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a homogenization theory for topological Anderson insulators, showing how disorder—especially structured or random fluctuations at small scale ε—induces non-trivial topology via a second-order (Born) correction M; it establishes norm-resolvent convergence to a homogenized operator, proves robustness of bulk-interface correspondence for half-plane interfaces, and introduces a deformation-based index for random cases where the spectral gap closes, proving its almost-sure equality to the bulk-difference invariant in specific models (e.g., BHZ at criticality).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously understand how disorder—not just periodic potentials—can induce and stabilize non-trivial topological phases (topological Anderson insulators), particularly beyond perturbative or numerical treatments, and to reconcile bulk-interface correspondence in the presence of strong, possibly random, fluctuations that close spectral gaps.

Method: Functional-analytic homogenization theory for elliptic systems with rapidly oscillating (periodic or random) coefficients at scale ε; norm-resolvent convergence analysis; Born approximation for second-order effective corrections; interface index construction via deformed spectral cuts; spectral and topological analysis using Fredholm theory and K-theory invariants (bulk-difference invariant, interface index).

Result: 1) Periodic fluctuations—even zero-mean electric/magnetic fields—generate order-one topological corrections M via second-order Born terms; 2) Half-plane interfaces host robust edge modes carrying the bulk-difference invariant of the homogenized operator for small ε; 3) For random fluctuations, rare regions close the homogenized gap → pseudo-gap emerges; 4) A deformed-cut interface index is well-defined and equals almost surely the bulk-difference invariant of the Born-homogenized operator in first-order alloys (vanishing low moments) and critical second-order scalar-disordered systems (including BHZ model).

Conclusion: Disorder-induced topology in topological Anderson insulators is analytically tractable via homogenization: the Born approximation captures essential topological corrections, bulk-interface correspondence persists under structural perturbations, and a suitably regularized interface index restores quantization and stability even when randomness destroys the spectral gap.

Abstract: A topological Anderson insulator is an insulator whose non-trivial topological phase is induced by disorder. We develop a homogenization theory of this phenomenon for constant-coefficient elliptic systems perturbed by fluctuations oscillating at scale $\varepsilon$. Periodic fluctuations of every order below that of the operator, with appropriate amplitudes, do not average out: they produce an order-one correction $M$ given by a second-order (Born) formula, and the operator converges in the norm-resolvent sense to its homogenized limit. In particular, electric potentials and magnetic fields of zero mean, usually regarded as topologically trivial perturbations, generate corrections $M$ that change the topological phase. Fluctuations confined to a half-plane create an interface that carries the bulk-difference invariant of the homogenized operator for all $\varepsilon$ sufficiently small, a property that is stable under local defects. For random fluctuations, rare regions typically fill the homogenized gap, which becomes a pseudo-gap, and the standard interface index need no longer be defined. We define an index along cuts deformed so as to avoid the rare regions, and prove that it equals almost surely the bulk-difference invariant of the operator homogenized in the Born approximation, for first-order alloys with vanishing low moments and for a class of second-order systems with scalar disorder at criticality that includes the BHZ model.

</details>


### [11] [Nonequilibrium Thermodynamics of Semilinear Stochastic Partial Differential Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38166)
*Zelin Liu,Hao Ge*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous nonequilibrium thermodynamic framework for semilinear stochastic PDEs using an invariant Gaussian measure as reference, defines entropy production via Radon–Nikodym derivatives, proves its equivalence to time reversibility in stationarity, derives fluctuation theorems, and decomposes entropy production into housekeeping heat and free-energy dissipation—each satisfying integral fluctuation theorems—with applications to stochastic heat equations, polymer models, and coupled systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend nonequilibrium thermodynamics rigorously to infinite-dimensional stochastic systems governed by semilinear SPDEs, where standard finite-dimensional or Markov chain frameworks fail due to spatial correlations, non-reversibility, and lack of detailed balance.

Method: Constructs a thermodynamic formalism based on the invariant Gaussian measure of the linear (Ornstein-Uhlenbeck) part as reference measure; defines entropy, entropy production rate, and sample-path entropy production via Radon–Nikodym derivatives of path measures; derives entropy balance equations; proves fluctuation theorems (including integral and detailed forms); and performs spectral and functional-analytic analysis of entropy production decomposition.

Result: Explicit formulas for entropy production rate; proof that zero entropy production in stationary state ⇔ time reversibility; identification of sample-path entropy production with log Radon–Nikodym derivative; validity of fluctuation theorems for both stationary and nonstationary cases; decomposition into nonnegative housekeeping heat and free-energy dissipation; mean free-energy dissipation equals relative entropy decay w.r.t. stationary distribution; successful application to four physically relevant SPDE models.

Conclusion: A mathematically rigorous, generalizable nonequilibrium thermodynamic theory for semilinear SPDEs is achieved, unifying concepts from large deviations, stochastic analysis, and statistical physics, and providing foundational tools for analyzing entropy production, irreversibility, and fluctuation behavior in infinite-dimensional stochastic systems.

Abstract: We develop a rigorous mathematical framework for the nonequilibrium thermodynamics of semilinear stochastic partial differential equations. The invariant Gaussian measure of the associated Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process, which need not be time-reversible, serves as the reference measure. We define thermodynamic quantities in terms of probability densities and derive the entropy balance equation. We obtain explicit expressions for the entropy production rate and prove that its vanishing in the stationary state is equivalent to time reversibility. Sample-path entropy production is identified with the logarithm of the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the forward path measure with respect to the time-reversed path measure and is shown to satisfy fluctuation theorems. For nonstationary systems, sample-path entropy production decomposes into housekeeping heat and free-energy dissipation, each satisfying an integral fluctuation theorem. Both have nonnegative expectations, and the mean free-energy dissipation equals the decrease in relative entropy with respect to the stationary distribution. The theory is applied to semilinear stochastic heat equations with spatially correlated noise, a random polymer model, a nonlinear model with a divergence-free drift, and two coupled stochastic heat equations in contact with heat baths at different temperatures.

</details>


### [12] [Finite-volume and thermodynamic-limit lower bounds for the coherently driven Bose--Hubbard model via the Lieb--Yamazaki method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38464)
*Maciej Janowicz,Joanna Kaleta,Filip Krzyżewski,Andrzej Zembrzuski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives rigorous lower bounds on the ground-state energy of the coherently driven Bose–Hubbard model using a Lieb–Yamazaki positive-operator decomposition, yielding a quadratic bounding Hamiltonian that is analytically diagonalized; it establishes thermodynamic-limit energy density, excitation spectrum, Gaussian-state observables (e.g., antibunching, sub-Poissonian statistics), and numerically evaluates bound tightness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain rigorous, analytic lower bounds on the ground-state energy of the coherently driven Bose–Hubbard model—a non-integrable, interacting quantum many-body system—where exact solutions are unavailable and standard perturbative or variational methods lack guaranteed accuracy.

Method: Applies the Lieb–Yamazaki positive-operator decomposition to construct a quadratic (Gaussian) bounding Hamiltonian; employs displacement and Bogoliubov transformations to diagonalize it exactly; computes thermodynamic-limit energy density, excitation spectrum, and Gaussian-state expectation values (filling, number fluctuations, g^(2)); validates bound tightness via numerical comparison across parameter regimes.

Result: Explicit finite-volume ground-state energy lower bounds and stability conditions in arbitrary dimension; closed-form thermodynamic-limit energy density; full characterization of the bounding Hamiltonian’s excitation spectrum (quasiparticle dispersion); analytical expressions for Gaussian-state observables revealing antibunching and sub-Poissonian statistics in specific parameter regimes; numerical evidence confirming the bounds are quantitatively tight in representative regimes.

Conclusion: The Lieb–Yamazaki decomposition provides a powerful, systematically tractable route to rigorous energy bounds for driven-dissipative and interacting bosonic lattice models; the resulting Gaussian bounding theory captures key nonclassical quantum statistical features and offers a certified benchmark for approximate methods.

Abstract: We derive rigorous lower bounds on the ground-state energy of the coherently driven Bose--Hubbard model using a Lieb--Yamazaki positive-operator decomposition. The resulting quadratic bounding Hamiltonian is diagonalized by displacement and Bogoliubov transformations, yielding explicit stability conditions and finite-volume bounds in arbitrary dimension. We establish the thermodynamic-limit energy density and characterize the excitation spectrum of the bounding Hamiltonian. We also derive its Gaussian-state observables, including filling, number fluctuations, and second-order correlations, and identify regimes of antibunching and sub-Poissonian statistics. Numerical calculations assess the tightness of the bounds across representative parameter regimes.

</details>


### [13] [A Primer on Instanton Physics - A Short Course](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38717)
*Pervez Hoodbhoy*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: A six-lecture series introducing instantons in quantum mechanics and quantum field theory, targeting senior undergraduates and beginning graduate students; first four lectures focus on particle mechanics, last two on semi-classical field theory applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an accessible pedagogical introduction to instantons for students with foundational knowledge in quantum mechanics, bridging conceptual and technical gaps between quantum mechanics and quantum field theory.

Method: Lecture-based pedagogical exposition, progressing from elementary quantum mechanical systems (e.g., double-well potential, path integrals) to semi-classical methods (WKB, Euclidean time, saddle-point approximation) and their extension to field-theoretic contexts.

Result: Students gain working knowledge of instanton calculus—including construction, interpretation as tunneling events in Euclidean time, evaluation of non-perturbative effects (e.g., ground-state splitting, vacuum decay rates)—and its role in symmetry breaking and anomalies.

Conclusion: Instantons are essential non-perturbative objects whose study in quantum mechanics serves as a rigorous and intuitive foundation for their more complex realization in quantum field theory.

Abstract: These six lectures, delivered at the University of New Brunswick 6-21 October 2026, are an introduction to instantons in quantum mechanics and quantum field theory for senior undergraduate and beginning graduate students. The first four lectures deal solely with particle mechanics, building the necessary background for the last two that cover applications in semi-classical field theory.

</details>


### [14] [From Entanglement Bootstrap to DHR Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38821)
*Kohtaro Kato,Yoshiko Ogata*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper bridges the entanglement bootstrap framework and the Doplicher–Haag–Roberts (DHR) theory for 2D quantum spin systems, proving Haag duality and establishing a rigorous one-to-one correspondence between entanglement-bootstrap anyons and irreducible superselection sectors; it further constructs an affine bijection linking density matrices on fusion spaces to normalized positive intertwiners in operator-algebraic fusion channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify entropic (entanglement-bootstrap) and operator-algebraic (DHR) descriptions of topological order and anyons in 2D quantum spin systems without relying on exactly solvable lattice models.

Method: Mathematical proof using assumptions of the entanglement bootstrap, operator-algebraic methods (including Haag duality, superselection sector theory, and intertwiner analysis), and construction of affine bijections between density matrices and intertwiners in fusion channels.

Result: 1. Proof of Haag duality under entanglement-bootstrap assumptions; 2. One-to-one correspondence between simple entanglement-bootstrap anyon types and irreducible DHR superselection sectors; 3. For each fusion channel, an affine bijection between density matrices on the entanglement-bootstrap fusion space and normalized positive intertwiners in the corresponding operator-algebraic fusion channel.

Conclusion: The entanglement bootstrap and DHR theory are equivalent frameworks for characterizing anyons in 2D quantum spin systems, enabling a direct, model-independent connection between entanglement-based and algebraic quantum field-theoretic descriptions of topological order.

Abstract: We establish a direct connection between the entanglement bootstrap and the Doplicher--Haag--Roberts theory of superselection sectors for two-dimensional quantum spin systems. Under the entanglement-bootstrap assumptions, we prove Haag duality and establish a one-to-one correspondence between simple entanglement-bootstrap anyon types and irreducible superselection sectors. For each fusion channel, we further construct an affine bijection from density matrices on the entanglement-bootstrap fusion space to normalized positive intertwiners in the corresponding operator-algebraic fusion channel. These results connect the entropic and operator-algebraic descriptions of anyons directly, without passing through exactly solvable lattice models.

</details>


### [15] [The geometry of Floquet states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38845)
*Vladimir Y. Cherynak,John R. Klein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the moduli space of nondegenerately Floquet-spectra periodic Hamiltonians, modeling it via configuration spaces, flag manifolds, and principal bundles; it computes its rational cohomology ring and proposes two Floquet-adapted adiabatic conjectures for 1-parameter families.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the topological and geometric structure of the space of periodic Hamiltonians with nondegenerate Floquet spectra ('good' Hamiltonians), motivated by applications in quantum dynamics, Floquet theory, and adiabatic evolution beyond standard time-independent settings.

Method: Uses differential topology and algebraic topology: constructs a geometric model of the moduli space using configuration spaces, flag manifolds, and principal bundles; applies spectral theory and bundle-theoretic techniques to compute rational cohomology; formulates asymptotic quantum evolution conjectures based on adiabatic approximation in the Floquet context.

Result: Complete determination of the rational cohomology ring of the space of good periodic Hamiltonians; geometric characterization of the moduli space via explicit topological constructions; formulation of two precise conjectures on asymptotic Floquet–adiabatic evolution for smoothly varying families.

Conclusion: The moduli space of good periodic Hamiltonians admits a rich geometric structure amenable to topological classification; its rational cohomology is fully computable, and its adiabatic dynamics suggest natural Floquet generalizations of the quantum adiabatic theorem—pending rigorous proof.

Abstract: This paper studies the moduli space of "good" periodic Hamiltonians, i.e., periodic Hamiltonians with nondegenerate Floquet spectrum. The corresponding Floquet states determine a geometric model for the moduli space of such Hamiltonians. The model is expressed in terms of configuration spaces, flag manifolds, and principal bundles. A corollary of the main result is the complete determination of the rational cohomology ring of the space of good periodic Hamiltonians. The paper also considers the adiabatic driving of a 1-parameter family of good periodic Hamiltonians. We formulate two conjectures describing the resulting asymptotic quantum evolution. These are Floquet analogues of the adiabatic theorem in quantum mechanics.

</details>


### [16] [A First-Order Phase Transition in Spin-Imbalanced BCS Theory of Superconductivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38961)
*Marius Lemm,Barbara Roos*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the existence of a first-order phase transition in spin-imbalanced translation-invariant BCS superconductivity models, characterized by a discontinuous jump in Cooper pair density and a kink in energy at a coupling strength strictly below the linear stability threshold of the normal state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously confirm the unusual first-order phase transition suggested by prior numerical (Caldas–Mota) and analytical (Freiji–Hainzl–Seiringer) studies in spin-imbalanced BCS models where μ₊ ≠ μ₋.

Method: Rigorous analysis of the translation-invariant BCS functional using a coercivity estimate derived from splitting the energy minimization: the 'inner' problem—minimizing over one-particle density matrices at fixed pairing—is solved exactly via Legendre–Fenchel transform.

Result: For sufficiently small spin imbalance and low temperature, there exists a coexistence coupling λₐₒ < λₗᵢₙ at which the global minimizer jumps discontinuously (nonzero Cooper pair density onset) and the ground-state energy exhibits a kink—confirming a genuine first-order phase transition.

Conclusion: The normal state, though locally stable up to λₗᵢₙ, loses global stability at a strictly smaller λₐₒ, establishing a thermodynamically first-order transition in spin-imbalanced BCS theory.

Abstract: We study a translation-invariant BCS functional of superconductivity for a system of spin-$1/2$ fermions with spin-dependent chemical potentials, $\mu_+\neq\mu_-$. Numerical work of Caldas and Mota and analytical work of Freiji, Hainzl, and Seiringer has suggested an unusual first-order phase transition in spin-imbalanced BCS models, in which the normal state becomes globally unstable while remaining locally stable. Here, we prove that a first-order phase transition occurs in such spin-imbalanced BCS functionals. For each sufficiently small fixed spin imbalance and sufficiently low temperature, there is a coexistence coupling $\lambda_{\mathrm{fo}}$ strictly below the linear-stability threshold $\lambda_{\mathrm{lin}}$ of the normal state at which the Cooper pair density exhibits a nonzero jump and the minimized energy has a kink. The methodological insight is a coercivity estimate obtained by decomposing the BCS energy minimization into two parts, where the ``inner'' problem minimizes over the one-particle density matrices at fixed pairing and can be exactly solved by a Legendre--Fenchel transform.

</details>


### [17] [Minimally-coupled Quasilinear Symmetric Hyperbolic Systems, Naturally](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39107)
*Eleanor March,James Owen Weatherall*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous geometric framework for symmetric hyperbolicity and well-posedness of PDEs on natural bundles, introducing 'naturalization' of non-natural equations and proving uniqueness of minimal coupling; it shows that for minimally coupled natural equations, causal structure is fixed by the background metric and a key tensor derived from the hyperbolization is divergence-free.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a precise, geometrically intrinsic foundation for well-posedness and causality in field theories—especially those arising in general relativity and relativistic continuum mechanics—by leveraging the categorical and differential-geometric structure of natural bundles and jet bundles, thereby unifying and generalizing standard results on symmetric hyperbolic systems.

Method: The authors employ the theory of natural bundles and their associated jet bundles, define natural equations as closed subobjects thereof, formalize 'minimal coupling' as a universal construction (via a left adjoint), construct symmetrizers and hyperbolizations compatible with naturality, and use covariant differentiation relative to Levi-Civita connections induced by background metrics to derive constraints and conservation laws.

Result: 1) Non-natural PDEs can be embedded into natural ones via naturalization; 2) Minimal coupling yields a unique (up to isomorphism) natural equation from a given equation on a fixed manifold; 3) For first-order quasilinear minimally coupled natural equations, symmetric hyperbolicity implies causal cones coincide exactly with metric lightcones; 4) The rank-2 tensor defining the natural hyperbolization is divergence-free w.r.t. the Levi-Civita derivative.

Conclusion: Naturality and minimal coupling impose strong, physically meaningful constraints on hyperbolic PDEs: they rigidly fix causal structure to the background geometry and enforce a conservation law (divergence-freeness) for the hyperbolization tensor—thereby linking geometric invariance, well-posedness, and physical causality in a unified, coordinate-free framework.

Abstract: We consider symmetric hyperbolicity and well-posedness in the context of natural equations, which are closed subobjects of jet bundles over natural bundles. We show how non-natural equations can be "naturalized", and we discuss how a precise version of "minimal coupling" offers a (provably unique, up to isomorphism) method for constructing natural equations from certain equations defined on a single manifold. We discuss how to define symmetrizers, hyperbolizations, and constraints for minimally coupled natural equations, and we show how these can be used to recover standard existence and uniqueness results, relative to any choice of background structure (a metric, for minimally coupled systems). Additionally, we show that minimal coupling and naturality together imply that the causal cones associated with a (first order, quasilinear, minimally coupled) naturally symmetric hyperbolic natural equation must always coincide with the metric lightcones, and that a certain rank-2 tensor, associates with a natural hyperbolization, must always be divergence free relative to the associated Levi-Civita derivative operator.

</details>


### [18] [Higher genus polylogarithms associated with the D'Hoker-Hidding-Schlotterer connection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39382)
*Takashi Ichikawa*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs higher genus polylogarithms via the D'Hoker-Hidding-Schlotterer (DHS) connection on families of Riemann surfaces, showing that under separating degenerations, the DHS connection glues Brown-Levin (elliptic) and KZ (spherical) connections; resulting polylogarithms are expressed as power series in deformation parameters and logs, with coefficients in multiple zeta values and elliptic multiple zeta values.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the theory of polylogarithms and their associated flat connections from genus 0 (KZ) and genus 1 (BL) to arbitrary genus, leveraging modular and degeneration properties of Riemann surfaces.

Method: Analysis of the DHS connection under separating degenerations; explicit gluing of BL connections on elliptic components and KZ connections on sphere components; asymptotic expansion of associated periods and iterated integrals in deformation parameters.

Result: The DHS connection is shown to be compatible with the boundary structure of the moduli space of curves; higher genus polylogarithms admit explicit asymptotic expansions whose coefficients lie in the algebra generated by multiple zeta values and elliptic multiple zeta values.

Conclusion: Higher genus polylogarithms associated with the DHS connection are constructible from lower-genus building blocks (KZ and BL), and admit controlled logarithmic-power-series expansions governed by motivic periods—establishing a bridge between genus-graded iterated integrals and mixed Hodge–Tate structures.

Abstract: We study higher genus polylogarithms associated with the D'Hoker-Hidding-Schlotterer (DHS) connection defined on families of Riemann surfaces of any genus with marked points. Under separating degenerations of Riemann surfaces, we show that the DHS connection can be constructed by gluing the Brown-Levin (BL) connections on elliptic curves and the Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov (KZ) connections on pointed Riemann spheres. Therefore, the associated higher genus polylogarithms can be described explicitly as power series in deformation parameters and their logarithms for the families of Riemann surfaces whose coefficients are expressed by multiple zeta values and elliptic multiple zeta values.

</details>


### [19] [Generalized Frobenius Structures on Hurwitz Spaces, Their Open Extensions and Associated Principal Hierarchies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39411)
*Shilin Ma*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs generalized Frobenius structures on Hurwitz spaces of arbitrary genus using extended primary differentials, derives dispersionless Lax representations via Cauchy kernel contour integrals, solves open WDVV equations for both original and almost-dual structures, builds rank-one extensions, and shows compatibility with Shramchenko deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Frobenius manifold theory to arbitrary-genus Hurwitz spaces and unify constructions of integrable hierarchies, WDVV solutions, and deformations in a geometric framework.

Method: Uses extended primary differentials on Hurwitz spaces; employs contour integrals with the Cauchy kernel to obtain dispersionless Lax representations; constructs solutions to open WDVV equations; performs rank-one extensions; verifies invariance under Shramchenko deformations.

Result: Explicit dispersionless Lax representations for principal hierarchies and their almost-duals; full classification of open WDVV solutions for both structures; concrete principal hierarchies for rank-one extensions; proof of Shramchenko deformation compatibility.

Conclusion: The framework generalizes Frobenius structures to arbitrary-genus Hurwitz spaces, unifying integrable systems, Frobenius geometry, and topological field theory aspects—including open WDVV and deformation theory—within a single coherent construction.

Abstract: We construct generalized Frobenius structures on Hurwitz spaces of arbitrary genus using extended primary differentials. Through contour integrals involving the Cauchy kernel, we derive explicit dispersionless Lax representations for the principal hierarchies of these structures and their almost-dual counterparts. We also construct solutions to the open WDVV equations for both the original and almost-dual structures, and derive explicit principal hierarchies for the resulting rank-one extensions. Finally, we show that these constructions admit Shramchenko deformations.

</details>


### [20] [Arithmetic localization for quasiperiodic long-range operators with monotone potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39448)
*Hongyi Cao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves uniform Anderson localization for one-dimensional quasiperiodic operators with α-Hölder monotone potentials and exponentially decaying long-range hopping, under a smallness condition on the coupling and a resonance-based criterion ρ > αβ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend arithmetic localization methods beyond nearest-neighbor Schrödinger operators to long-range settings, bypassing reliance on the Lyapunov exponent and addressing spectral localization in regimes governed by frequency resonance strength.

Method: Adaptation and extension of Jitomirskaya–Kachkovskiy's arithmetic localization strategy—based on sharp quantitative almost-reducibility and elimination of resonant frequencies—to long-range quasiperiodic operators with Hölder potentials; uses Diophantine analysis and semi-algebraic set arguments without Lyapunov exponent estimates.

Result: Uniform Anderson localization holds for all phases, provided the hopping decay rate ρ exceeds αβ and the coupling ε satisfies an explicit smallness condition depending on ρ, α, β, and the potential’s Hölder norm.

Conclusion: Arithmetic localization is robust beyond short-range models: uniform localization persists for long-range operators with Hölder monotone potentials when the decay of hopping dominates the interplay between Hölder regularity and frequency resonance strength.

Abstract: We study one-dimensional quasiperiodic operators with $\alpha$-Hölder monotone potentials and long-range hopping satisfying $|T(x,y)|\leq \varepsilon e^{-\rho |x-y|}$. Under an explicit smallness condition on $\varepsilon$, we establish uniform Anderson localization for such operators in the regime $\rho>\alpha\beta $, where $\beta$ is the exponential strength of frequency resonances. Our proof extends the arithmetic localization strategy for monotone quasiperiodic Schrödinger operators by Jitomirskaya and Kachkovskiy to the long-range setting, without relying on the Lyapunov exponent.

</details>


### [21] [A Combinatorial Formula and a Hilbert Scheme Realization of a Rank-One Virasoro Irregular Conformal Block](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39786)
*Hajime Nagoya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a combinatorial and geometric characterization of the rank-one Virasoro irregular conformal block of type (0,0,1), realizing it on an equivariant Hilbert–Fock space from Hilbert schemes of points on C²; at central charge c=1, it proves a conjecture linking coefficients to skew-tableau numbers and Littlewood–Richardson coefficients, using asymptotics of screening integrals and Laguerre–Jack evaluations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure and positivity properties of irregular Virasoro conformal blocks—particularly their formal expansions near irregular singular points—and to connect them to representation-theoretic and combinatorial objects such as Jack polynomials, skew tableaux, and Littlewood–Richardson coefficients.

Method: Combines sectorial asymptotic analysis of screening integrals, Laguerre–Jack integral evaluations, realization on localized equivariant Hilbert–Fock space from Hilbert schemes of points on C², and algebraic manipulation of Jack symmetric functions via the one-box Pieri rule and Okounkov–Olshanski's shifted Jack coefficient formula.

Result: A combinatorial formula and geometric realization of the rank-one (0,0,1) Virasoro irregular conformal block for general central charge; at c=1, parameter-independent coefficients are expressed explicitly in terms of skew-tableau numbers and Littlewood–Richardson coefficients, proving their nonnegativity and integrality.

Conclusion: The irregular conformal block admits a canonical geometric-combinatorial interpretation tied to Hilbert schemes and symmetric function theory; its coefficients at c=1 are fundamentally combinatorial and positive integral, resolving a prior conjecture and deepening the link between 2D CFT, enumerative geometry, and algebraic combinatorics.

Abstract: For general central charge, we give a combinatorial formula and a geometric realization of the formal expansion of the rank-one Virasoro irregular conformal block of type $(0,0,1)$ at its irregular singular point. We realize the normalized block as a coefficientwise matrix element on the localized equivariant Hilbert--Fock space associated with Hilbert schemes of points on $\mathbb C^2$. At $c=1$, we prove the author's conjecture and express the parameter-independent coefficients in terms of skew-tableau numbers and Littlewood--Richardson coefficients, establishing their nonnegativity and integrality. The proof combines sectorial asymptotics of screening integrals with Laguerre--Jack evaluations. We express the screening kernel using the flow of a degree-raising differential operator and compute the Jack coefficients of its exponential from the one-box Pieri rule and the shifted Jack coefficient formula of Okounkov and Olshanski.

</details>


### [22] [An Egorov-type Theorem for Analytic Wick Observables in Quantum Field Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39806)
*Julien Malartre*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact Egorov-type formula as a convergent semiclassical expansion for analytic Wick observables with super-exponential coefficient decay under time-dependent quadratic Hamiltonian evolution, extending Breteaux's polynomial-symbol result to include $P(\varphi)_2$ and a Höegh-Krohn variant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize Breteaux's Egorov theorem for polynomial symbols to a broader class of analytic Wick observables with super-exponential decay, enabling treatment of physically relevant models like the spatially cutoff $P(\varphi)_2$ and Höegh-Krohn models.

Method: Constructs a convergent expansion in the semiclassical parameter $\varepsilon > 0$ for the Heisenberg evolution of analytic Wick observables under quadratic time-dependent Hamiltonians, leveraging analyticity and super-exponential decay properties of symbol coefficients.

Result: An exact Egorov-type formula expressed as a convergent $\varepsilon$-expansion for the quantum evolution of the specified class of Wick observables, subsuming previously known results for polynomials and accommodating $P(\varphi)_2$ and a Höegh-Krohn variant.

Conclusion: The extension confirms robustness of Egorov-type correspondence beyond polynomial symbols, providing a rigorous semiclassical framework for non-polynomial, analytic observables in bosonic QFT models with quadratic dynamics.

Abstract: We provide an exact Egorov-type formula, given by a convergent expansion in the semi-classical parameter $\varepsilon > 0$, for the propagation of a certain class of analytic Wick observables with super-exponentially decaying coefficients, under the action of a time-dependent quadratic Hamiltonian. This work generalizes a result established by S. Breteaux for polynomial symbols, and includes the spatially cutoff $P(\varphi)_2$ model, as well as a variant of the Höegh-Krohn model.

</details>


### [23] [Three-dimensional stress-gradient non-locality problem: Displacement field for an arbitrary and a conservative force](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39888)
*Slađan Jelić,Dušan Zorica*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically solves the statics problem for a 3D stress-gradient non-local elastic medium under arbitrary and conservative body forces, deriving six distinct analytical displacement field expressions—some shared across scalar- and tensor-type Eringen models—and visualizing them for gravitational loading.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain analytical displacement fields in stress-gradient non-local elasticity for both arbitrary and conservative body forces, extending classical local elasticity and clarifying model-specific non-local contributions.

Method: Analytical solution of the linear system comprising equilibrium equations, infinitesimal strain–displacement relations, and both scalar- and tensor-type stress-gradient Eringen non-local constitutive models on an unbounded domain; superposition of local and model-specific non-local displacement fields.

Result: Six distinct closed-form displacement field expressions derived: one unique to a scalar-type model, three unique to individual tensor-type models, and two shared between one scalar- and one tensor-type model; graphical representation confirms five distinct fields under gravitational (conservative) loading due to equivalence of two tensor models.

Conclusion: The displacement field in stress-gradient non-local elasticity decomposes cleanly into universal local and model-dependent non-local parts; the number of physically distinguishable solutions reduces under conservative forces, highlighting the interplay between force type and non-local operator structure.

Abstract: In order to obtain the displacement field, both generally in the case of an arbitrary body force and specifically in the case of a conservative body force, the mechanical problem of statics of three-dimensional stress-gradient non-local medium is analytically solved on unbounded domain by considering a system of equations consisting of an equilibrium equation, infinitesimal strain, and both scalar- and tensor-type stress-gradient Eringen three-dimensional models. Since the system of equations is linear, for each model the solution is obtained in the form of a superposition of the local-type displacement field, common to each model, and the non-local-type displacement field, specific to the non-locality operator of the model. Among the three scalar-type and five tensor-type considered models, six distinct analytical displacement field expressions are obtained for an arbitrary body force: one expression specific to a scalar-type model, three specific to individual tensor-type models, while each of the remaining two expressions are common to one scalar- and one tensor-type model. The displacement vector fields are graphically represented in the case of gravitational body force, yielding five different models, since two tensor-type models become the same when the force is conservative.

</details>


### [24] [Optimization Inverse Spectral Problems for Sturm--Liouville Operators with Potentials in $L^p(0,1)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40231)
*Nur Valeev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses an optimization inverse spectral problem (OISP) for a 1D Sturm–Liouville operator: find the potential in L^p(0,1) closest to a given V₀ that yields prescribed eigenvalues; it establishes existence, regularity, analytic representation of optimal potentials via squared eigenfunctions, uniqueness conditions (including Baire-generic global uniqueness), nondegeneracy and isolation results for p=2, counterexamples with multiple minima under symmetric barriers, and proposes two numerical methods with stated convergence guarantees; applications include nonlinear BVPs and resonator design.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve a constrained optimization variant of the classical inverse spectral problem—where one seeks not just any potential matching target eigenvalues, but the one *closest* (in L^p norm) to a priori knowledge V₀—motivated by applications in resonator design and diagnostics where physical realizability and robustness demand minimal deviation from nominal parameters.

Method: Uses functional-analytic and differential-algebraic tools: (i) analysis of isospectral manifolds and their regularity; (ii) power-map representation of optimal potentials via linear combinations of squared eigenfunctions; (iii) linear dependence criteria for squared eigenfunctions to infer analyticity of potentials; (iv) critical point theory and Morse-type analysis for p=2; (v) Baire category arguments for generic uniqueness; (vi) construction of explicit counterexamples using symmetric barriers; (vii) development of frozen-eigenfunction iteration and Newton–KKT with spectral continuation.

Result: Proves existence and L^p-regularity of OISP solutions; derives explicit analytic formula for optimal V in terms of squared eigenfunctions; shows analyticity of V when squared eigenfunctions are linearly dependent; establishes isolation and finiteness of nondegenerate global minima for p=2; proves uniqueness for λ₁* ≥ λ₁(V₀) (p arbitrary) and Baire-generic global uniqueness in L^p; constructs examples with ≥2 global minima for m=1,2 under high symmetric barriers; provides locally convergent numerical schemes with precise domain-of-validity guarantees.

Conclusion: The OISP is well-posed in a structured sense: optimal potentials exist, admit analytic representations, and enjoy strong uniqueness properties under natural conditions; however, non-uniqueness can occur generically only in degenerate or highly symmetric settings; the framework bridges abstract spectral theory with practical computational methods and engineering applications in waveguide and resonator design.

Abstract: We study an optimization inverse spectral problem (OISP) for a one-dimensional Sturm--Liouville operator on a finite interval. The problem is to find a potential $V \in L^p(0,1)$ that realizes a prescribed finite set $\lambda_1^* < \lambda_2^* < \dots < \lambda_m^*$ and has the smallest $L^p(0,1)$-distance from an a priori given potential $V_0 \in L^p(0,1)$. We formulate and prove results on the regularity of the isospectral manifold, the existence of an OISP solution, and an explicit analytic representation of the optimal potential by means of a power map applied to a linear combination of squared eigenfunctions. Using a unified differential-algebraic approach to squared eigenfunctions, we show that whenever these squares are linearly dependent, the corresponding potential can be expressed in terms of them and is an analytic function. For $p=2$, we prove that nondegenerate stationary points are isolated and that the number of global minima is finite provided that each of them is nondegenerate. For every $1<p<\infty$, we establish uniqueness for the first eigenvalue when $\lambda_1^*\ge\lambda_1(V_0)$ and generic global uniqueness in the sense of Baire category in $L^p(0,1)$. A separate section provides examples with at least two global minima for $m=1,2$ in the presence of a sufficiently high finite symmetric barrier. We describe an iteration with frozen eigenfunctions and a local Newton--KKT method with continuation in the spectral data, and state explicitly the limits of their guarantees. The final part discusses applications of OISP theory to nonlinear boundary value problems and to applied problems of diagnostics and optimal spectral design of acoustic and electromagnetic resonators.

</details>


### [25] [Three-dimensional QCA that are not stably circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40235)
*Bowen Yang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that certain 3D chiral Z_p Clifford quantum cellular automata (QCA) are not implementable by finite-depth quantum circuits—even with ancillas and translations—for all odd primes p; it introduces a new invariant of invertible subalgebras conjectured to relate to the square of the total quantum dimension of the hosted anyon theory modulo rational squares, and deduces that the 3D QCA group modulo stabilized circuits and translations is not finitely generated.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether chiral 3D Z_p Clifford QCAs—known to be nontrivial within the Clifford category—are genuinely non-circuit-realizable beyond that restriction, and to understand the algebraic structure of the QCA classification group in 3D.

Method: Develops and applies a new invariant of invertible subalgebras, leveraging operator-algebraic and topological quantum field theoretic techniques; combines this with stability arguments under ancilla addition and translation to establish circuit impossibility outside the Clifford category.

Result: For every odd prime p, the Haah and Sun–Yang–Wang–Tantivasadakarn–Chen chiral Z_p Clifford QCAs are not equivalent to finite-depth quantum circuits, even after stabilization; consequently, the quotient group of 3D QCAs modulo stabilized circuits and translations is not finitely generated.

Conclusion: Chirality in 3D Z_p Clifford QCAs reflects an intrinsic non-circuit nature robust against stabilization, signaling a fundamental distinction between locality-preserving unitaries and finite-depth circuits in three dimensions; the new subalgebra invariant provides a potential bridge to topological quantum order via anyon theory data.

Abstract: We prove that the three-dimensional chiral $\Z_p$ Clifford quantum cellular automata of Haah and of Sun--Yang--Wang--Tantivasadakarn--Chen are, for every odd prime $p$, not finite-depth quantum circuits, even after tensoring with arbitrary ancillary degrees of freedom and composing with translations. Previously such statements were known only inside the Clifford category. As a consequence, the group of three-dimensional QCA modulo stabilized circuits and translations is not finitely generated. The key ingredient is a new invariant of invertible subalgebras, which we conjecture to be the square of the total quantum dimension of the hosted anyon theory, modulo rational squares.

</details>


### [26] [Bulk gap stability and spectral flow for fermions with weak long-range interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40299)
*Stefan Teufel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that weak long-range many-body interactions preserve the spectral gap of free fermionic systems and constructs a quasi-local spectral flow that unitarily connects free and interacting ground states, with explicit decay conditions and gap stability bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish robustness of the bulk spectral gap under weak, physically realistic long-range interactions—including polynomially or stretched-exponentially decaying terms—beyond short-range perturbations, and to provide a constructive, locality-preserving mapping between free and interacting ground states.

Method: Constructs a quasi-local spectral flow automorphism via a time-ordered exponential of an appropriately engineered generator; uses excitation-number comparison arguments and polynomial propagation bounds for evolved interactions (from a companion paper) to prove gap stability; employs Lieb–Robinson-type locality estimates adapted to polynomial and stretched-exponential decay.

Result: For sufficiently small coupling λ, the interacting system Φ_λ = Φ_h + λ𝒱 admits pure ground states ω_λ related to the free ground state ω₀ by ω_λ = ω₀ ∘ β_{0,λ}, where β_{0,λ} is a quasi-local automorphism; the GNS Hamiltonian of ω_λ has a spectral gap ≥ g − C_*|λ|‖𝒱‖_*, with ‖·‖_* accommodating polynomial decay (exponent ϖ > 2d) or stretched-exponential decay; the spectral-flow generator inherits and preserves the spatial decay exponent of h and 𝒱.

Conclusion: Weak long-range many-body interactions do not close the bulk gap of gapped free fermion systems, provided decay is sufficiently fast (ϖ > 2d for polynomial, arbitrary stretch for stretched-exponential); the ground-state correspondence is implemented by a physically meaningful, quasi-local automorphism, confirming stability of topological order and enabling rigorous perturbative analysis in long-range settings.

Abstract: We prove that weak long-range many-body interactions preserve the bulk gap of a free fermion system and that a quasi-local spectral flow transports the free ground state to ground states of the interacting system. Let $\Phi_h$ be a free fermionic interaction, possibly including pairing terms, whose infinite-volume Majorana coefficient operator $h$ has no spectrum in $(-g,g)$, and let $\mathcal V$ be an even self-adjoint many-body interaction. For sufficiently small $|\lambda|$, we construct pure ground states $\omega_{\lambda}$ of the interacting bulk system $\Phi_{\lambda}=\Phi_h+\lambda\mathcal V$ and quasi-local automorphisms $\beta_{0,\lambda}$ such that $\omega_{\lambda}=\omega_0\circ\beta_{0,\lambda}$, where $\omega_0$ is the free ground state of $\Phi_h$. Each $\omega_\lambda$ has a GNS Hamiltonian with gap at least $g-C_*|\lambda| \|\mathcal V\|_*$, where $\|\cdot\|_*$ is the relevant interaction norm. Our result allows polynomial decay of both the free coefficients and the many-body interaction. For two-site interactions on a uniformly discrete point set $\Gamma\subset\mathbb{R}^d$, a sufficient condition is decay as $(1+|x-y|)^{-\varpi}$ with $\varpi>2d$, for both the hopping and pairing coefficients and the interaction terms. The spectral-flow generator preserves the spatial exponent of the polynomial interaction norm, and its automorphisms satisfy locality bounds at that exponent. If $h$ and $\mathcal V$ have stretched-exponential decay, so does the spectral-flow generator. The proof constructs the spectral flow and establishes the gap through an excitation-number comparison, using polynomial propagation bounds for evolved interactions proved in a companion paper.

</details>


### [27] [Symmetries and exact solutions of stochastic differential equations: the CIR model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40316)
*Miguel A. Rodríguez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper explains the critical parameter relation in the Cox-Ingersoll-Ross (CIR) interest rate model via Lie symmetries of its stochastic differential equation (SDE), and recovers prior results linking SDE symmetries to those of the forward Kolmogorov equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the well-known critical parameter relation in the CIR model—often observed empirically but lacking a structural justification—by leveraging symmetry analysis of stochastic differential equations.

Method: Lie symmetry analysis of the CIR stochastic differential equation; derivation of invariant solutions using symmetry reductions; comparison with symmetries of the associated forward Kolmogorov (Fokker–Planck) equation.

Result: The critical parameter relation emerges naturally as a necessary condition for the existence of nontrivial Lie point symmetries of the CIR SDE; explicit symmetry-based solution construction is provided.

Conclusion: The critical parameter constraint in the CIR model is not ad hoc but reflects an underlying symmetry requirement; this reinforces the role of geometric methods in understanding structural properties of financial SDEs.

Abstract: It is well known that in the celebrated Cox-Ingersoll-Ross process for interest rates there exists a critical relation among the model parameters. In the present work, we explain this critical relation by studying the symmetries of the stochastic differential equation and compute the solution of the model using this symmetry. In this way we also recover results of a recent paper where this property has been studied within a broader context by analyzing the relationships among the symmetries of stochastic differential equations and their corresponding forward Kolmogorov equations.

</details>


### [28] [Local determination of low-energy states in gapped fermion systems with weak long-range interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40337)
*Stefan Teufel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves quantitative local determination of low-energy states for weakly interacting gapped fermionic systems, showing that local observables in low-energy states are robustly determined by the interaction inside a region U, with exponentially decaying errors away from U's boundary; it establishes uniqueness of the algebraic ground state, a strong LPPL principle, boundary-localization of finite-sample excitations, and decay of long-range correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish locality and stability properties—such as ground-state uniqueness, local response to perturbations, and boundary confinement of excitations—in weakly interacting gapped fermion systems beyond perturbation theory.

Method: Combines quasi-adiabatic transport (from free to interacting ground state) with a newly introduced condition, Local Excitation Detectability (LED); the proof is nonperturbative and relies on LED holding at sufficiently weak coupling, building on gap stability and transport construction from a companion paper.

Result: Quantitative local determination of low-energy states: local expectations in [0,3g/4]-energy states agree with bulk ground state expectations inside U up to an error decaying with distance to U^c; implies (i) unique algebraic ground state, (ii) LPPL principle, (iii) uniform boundary localization of finite-sample excitations, (iv) quantitative decay of spatial correlations across U–U^c.

Conclusion: Weak even many-body interactions preserving the spectral gap induce robust locality: low-energy physics deep inside a region is insensitive to arbitrary modifications of the Hamiltonian outside, governed by universal spatial decay bounds independent of external terms or specific low-energy states.

Abstract: We consider bulk-gapped fermion systems whose infinite-volume bulk is obtained by adding sufficiently weak even many-body interactions to a free Hamiltonian with spectral gap $g>0$, allowing polynomial spatial decay of both parts. We prove quantitative local determination of low-energy states: whenever another interaction agrees with the bulk interaction inside a finite or infinite region $U$, with bulk terms crossing its boundary unchanged or removed, all its states with excitation energies in $[0,3g/4]$ have local expectations close to those of the bulk ground state deep inside $U$. The error decays with the distance to the complement of $U$, independently of the interaction terms supported outside $U$ and of the chosen low-energy state. Quantitative local determination has strong consequences. (i) The bulk interaction has a unique algebraic ground state. (ii) A strong "local perturbations perturb locally" (LPPL) principle holds: arbitrary distant defects change local ground-state expectations only by a spatially decaying error. (iii) Low-energy excitations in finite samples remain localized near the boundary for all times, up to spatially decaying tails, uniformly over arbitrary even boundary terms. (iv) Correlations from deep inside $U$ to outside $U$ are small, with quantitative bounds in terms of spatial separation and distance to $U^c$. To prove local determination of low-energy states, we combine quasi-adiabatic transport of the free ground state to the interacting bulk ground state with a new condition, local excitation detectability (LED). A companion paper establishes gap stability and constructs the transport for weak interactions, while we prove LED at sufficiently weak coupling. The abstract theorem is nonperturbative and applies whenever the transport and LED hypotheses hold.

</details>


### [29] [Twinor Theory: Field Equations from $(A)dS_4^{\mathbb C}$ Spin-Tractors: A Twin to Twistor Theory Modelled on $(A)dS_4^{\mathbb C}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40435)
*Chen-Hsu Chien*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'twinor theory' as a spacetime-based extension of twistor theory, built on holomorphic reductive Cartan geometry over complexified (A)dS₄; it defines spin-tractor bundles (Dirac, ladder, chiral twinors), derives a unified field equation D𝒀 = 𝒥 with constrained contractions, recovers massive Dirac–Fierz–Pauli and massless zero-rest-mass equations on flat Lorentzian backgrounds, identifies the anti-de Sitter reality condition as the origin of real positive mass² independent of cosmological scale, incorporates mass-coupling phase classically, and formulates nonlinear gravity via adjoint curvature and Einstein–Cartan dynamics without an action.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometric, background-flexible, and chirality-symmetric framework unifying massive/massless higher-spin fields and gravity—bypassing Lagrangian dependence, separating bundle construction from interaction constraints, and embedding mass generation and reality conditions intrinsically in Cartan geometry.

Method: Holomorphic reductive Cartan geometry modelled on complexified (A)dS₄; construction of spin-tractor bundles (Dirac, ladder, chiral twinors); derivation of the twinor field equation D𝒀 = 𝒥 and analysis of its adjacent contractions, mixed divergence, and symmetric projection; imposition of source relations and reality conditions; formulation of nonlinear gravity via full adjoint curvature, Einstein–Cartan dynamics, adjoint Bianchi identity, and BGG-type sequence for torsion-free deformations.

Result: Recovery of the massive Dirac–Fierz–Pauli system and zero-rest-mass equations on flat torsion-free Lorentzian backgrounds; identification of the anti-de Sitter reality condition as necessary and sufficient for positive real mass squared, independent of cosmological constant or background curvature; demonstration that mass-coupling phase exists classically in the connection; consistent coupling of matter via contracted source relations and curvature compatibility condition D𝒥 = ℱ_D 𝒴; retention of both chiralities and clear distinction between bundle-level structure and interaction-specific consistency requirements.

Conclusion: Twinor theory provides a coherent, action-free, geometric foundation for unified description of higher-spin fields and gravity, wherein mass, chirality, reality, and interactions emerge naturally from Cartan-geometric structure and adjoint curvature dynamics—offering a promising route beyond standard twistor and Lagrangian paradigms.

Abstract: Twinor theory is formulated as a spacetime-based counterpart to twistor theory using holomorphic reductive Cartan geometry modelled on complexified $(A)dS_4$. The fundamental spin-tractor bundle defines Dirac twinors, its symmetric powers define ladder twinors, and their extremal Lorentz components define chiral twinors. The general \textit{twinor field equation} is $D\mathbf Y=\mathbf J$. Its adjacent contractions and mixed divergence are constrained by source relations; no additional equation is imposed on its completely symmetric projection. On a flat torsion-free Lorentzian background, the adjacent equations with the mass-normalization source and vanishing additional contracted sources, together with vanishing mixed divergence, reproduce the massive \textit{Dirac--Fierz--Pauli system}; the massless chiral realization gives the zero-rest-mass equations. Within the fixed mass normalization, the reality condition yielding a positive real mass squared is the \textit{anti-de Sitter reality condition} on the particle scale, independently of the cosmological scale and background curvature. The mass-coupling phase is therefore present in the classical connection before quantization. Nonlinear gravity is formulated through the full adjoint curvature, with energy--momentum and spin currents specifying Einstein--Cartan dynamics without requiring an action. The adjoint Bianchi identity constrains source compatibility, while a BGG-type sequence organizes torsion-free infinitesimal deformations. Matter interactions enter through the coupled connection and contracted source relations, with curvature compatibility recorded by $D\mathbf J=\mathcal F_D\mathbf Y$. Both chiralities are retained, and the additional consistency requirements for interacting higher-spin fields are distinguished from the underlying bundle construction.

</details>


### [30] [Entropic Fluctuations in Quantum Statistical Mechanics: A Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40463)
*Tristan Benoist,Laurent Bruneau,Vojkan Jakšić,Aannalisa Panati,Claude-Alain Pillet*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews and compares quantum extensions of the classical Evans-Searles and Gallavotti-Cohen fluctuation theorems (FTs), focusing on open quantum systems interacting with infinite reservoirs. It introduces a novel algebraic two-time measurement extension of the steady-state FT, discusses four entropy production approaches (naive, two-time measurement, phase space contraction, ancilla tomography), links them to modular theory, extends the classical principle of regular entropic fluctuations to quantum settings, and connects entropy statistics to spectral resonances of quantum transfer operators—illustrated via the spin-fermion model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically extend the foundational classical fluctuation theorems—governing entropy production statistics in transient and steady states—to the quantum domain, especially for rigorously defined open quantum systems where classical notions lack direct analogues.

Method: Uses algebraic quantum statistical mechanics (C*- and von Neumann algebras), modular theory (Tomita–Takesaki), quantum transfer operators, spectral analysis of resonance poles, and comparative analysis of four quantum entropy production definitions: naive (based on system-only dynamics), two-time measurement (projective measurements at initial/final times), phase space contraction (via quantum Liouville operator), and ancilla state tomography (using auxiliary systems to infer entropy flow).

Result: A mathematically rigorous extension of the Gallavotti-Cohen FT to the two-time measurement entropy production in the algebraic setting; clarified relationships among quantum entropy production definitions; established connections between entropic fluctuation statistics and spectral resonances of quantum transfer operators; validated results on the spin-fermion model as a paradigmatic non-equilibrium quantum system.

Conclusion: Quantum fluctuation theorems require careful operational and algebraic formulation; modular theory provides the natural bridge from classical to quantum entropy production; spectral properties of quantum dynamical maps encode universal fluctuation behavior; the two-time measurement approach, when embedded in the algebraic framework, recovers steady-state FT symmetry even for infinitely extended reservoirs.

Abstract: The celebrated Evans--Searles, respectively Gallavotti--Cohen, fluctuation theorem concerns certain universal statistical features of the entropy production rate of a classical system in a transient, respectively steady, state. In this paper, we review and compare several possible extensions of these fluctuation theorems to quantum systems. We present, in an introductory way, the approach proposed in a recent series of works [Lett. Math. Phys. 114, 32 (2024): doi:https://doi.org/10.1007/s11005-024-01777-0], [Rev. Math. Phys 38, 2461006 (2026): doi:https://doi.org/10.1142/S0129055X24610063], [Commun. Math. Phys. 406, 201 (2025): https://doi.org/10.1007/s00220-025-05360-z] and [J. Math. Phys. 67, 022101 (2026): doi:https://doi.org/10.1063/5.0311182] including a previously missing extension of the Gallavotti--Cohen (steady state) version of the FT to two-time measurement entropy production in the algebraic setting of quantum dynamical systems. We focus on the open quantum systems, where a small system interacts with several (infinitely extended) reservoirs, and discuss the following approaches: naive, two-time measurement, phase space contraction and ancilla state tomography. We review the connection with modular theory. The latter provides a way to extend the classical notions to the quantum domain. We discuss the quantum version of the principle of regular entropic fluctuations, introduced in the classical context in [Nonlinearity 24, 699 (2011): doi:https://doi.org/10.1088/0951-7715/24/3/003]. Finally, we relate the statistical properties of these various notions of entropy production to spectral resonances of quantum transfer operators and apply this approach to a paradigmatic model of non-equilibrium statistical mechanics: the spin-fermion model. The results presented here shed a new light on the nature of entropic fluctuations in quantum statistical mechanics.

</details>


### [31] [Generalized Dirac Equation in Metric-Affine Spacetimes: Consistency, Effective Mass, and Spinor Curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23609)
*Muzaffer Adak,Ali Bagci,Caglar Pala,Ozcan Sert*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the Dirac equation to metric-affine spacetimes (with curvature, torsion, and non-metricity), derives consistency conditions from Lagrangian symmetry and Hamiltonian Hermiticity, identifies coframe-induced scalar/pseudoscalar mass terms, and reveals an algebraic link between effective mass and residual spinor curvature when the bare mass vanishes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently extend the Dirac equation beyond Riemannian geometry to metric-affine spacetimes—incorporating torsion and non-metricity—while preserving fundamental physical requirements: Lagrangian symmetrization consistency and Hamiltonian Hermiticity under the curved-space Dirac inner product.

Method: Covariantization of the Dirac equation using a Clifford-algebra-expanded spinor connection in $cl(1,3)$; comparison with variation of a symmetrized Lagrangian; imposition of formal Hermiticity of the Hamiltonian in static isotropic symmetric teleparallel geometry; Clifford decomposition of generalized spinor curvature; analysis of mass generation and chiral redefinition invariance.

Result: Eight real consistency conditions reduce 16 real coupling parameters to 8 independent ones; coframe couplings $b_3, b_4$ generate a nonzero effective mass $m_{\mathrm{eff}} = \sqrt{(m-4b_3)^2 + 16b_4^2}$ even for $m=0$; a chiral phase arises but is removable; generalized spinor curvature can be nonzero despite vanishing affine curvature, torsion, and non-metricity; when $m=0$, $b_3^2 + b_4^2$ simultaneously determines $m_{\mathrm{eff}}^2$ and the surviving spinor curvature.

Conclusion: The generalized Dirac framework admits intrinsic mass generation from non-metricity and coframe couplings, uncoupled from the bare mass parameter; the persistence of spinor curvature in trivial affine backgrounds signals a purely algebraic (Clifford-geometric) origin for certain curvature-like contributions—revealing a deep interplay between spacetime geometry, spinor representation theory, and quantum field dynamics.

Abstract: We formulate a generalized Dirac equation in four-dimensional metric-affine spacetimes with curvature, torsion, and non-metricity. The spinor connection is expanded over the complete basis of the Clifford algebra $cl(1,3)$ and contains eight complex coupling constants. Requiring agreement between direct covariantization of the Dirac equation and variation of the corresponding symmetrized Lagrangian imposes eight real consistency conditions, reducing the number of independent real parameters from sixteen to eight. In the static isotropic symmetric teleparallel sector, formal Hermiticity of the Hamiltonian with respect to the curved-space Dirac inner product reproduces the corresponding restrictions on the non-metricity and coframe couplings. The coframe-dependent terms introduce scalar and pseudoscalar mass contributions that persist in Minkowski spacetime. For a single free Dirac field with constant coefficients, they yield one effective mass, $m_{\mathrm{eff}}=\sqrt{(m-4b_3)^2+16b_4^2}$, and a chiral phase removable by a constant chiral field redefinition. A nonzero effective mass is therefore possible even when the original mass parameter vanishes. We also derive the Clifford decomposition of the generalized spinor curvature and show that it can remain nonzero when affine curvature, torsion, and non-metricity all vanish. When $m=0$, the combination $b_3^2+b_4^2$ determines both the effective mass squared and the surviving spinor curvature, establishing an algebraic link between these contributions.

</details>
