### [32] [Systematic High Overtone Expansion of Kerr Quasinormal Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38019)
*Yasuyuki Hatsuda*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper presents a systematic high-overtone WKB expansion method for Kerr quasinormal modes on normal angular branches, leveraging a genus-one spectral curve and elliptic periods to compute coefficients to arbitrary order, with numerical validation across spin-0,1,2 fields and consistency with exact WKB in the extremal limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a rigorous, systematic, and arbitrarily high-order analytic framework for computing Kerr quasinormal mode frequencies in the high-overtone (large imaginary part) regime—where standard perturbative or numerical methods become inefficient or intractable.

Method: Derives angular eigenvalue expansion via polynomial recursion; reformulates radial Teukolsky equation to expose a fixed genus-one spectral curve; expresses higher-order quantum periods solely in terms of two fundamental elliptic periods; computes WKB expansion coefficients recursively using these periods.

Result: Obtains fully explicit, arbitrarily high-order asymptotic expansions for quasinormal mode frequencies on normal angular branches; numerically verifies convergence of truncated series against direct solutions of angular and radial equations for scalar, electromagnetic, and gravitational perturbations; shows agreement with exact WKB for extremal Kerr in the scalar case.

Conclusion: The high-overtone spectrum of Kerr black holes is governed by a universal genus-one structure, enabling efficient, analytic, and arbitrarily precise computation of quasinormal frequencies—establishing a geometric foundation for black hole spectroscopy beyond leading-order WKB.

Abstract: We develop a systematic method to compute the high overtone expansion of Kerr quasinormal modes on normal angular branches to arbitrary order. We first obtain the expansion of the angular eigenvalue at large frequencies by a simple polynomial recursion. Using this result in an alternative form of the radial Teukolsky equation, we find that all the WKB corrections are defined on a fixed genus-one spectral curve, and that the quantum periods at the second and higher orders are expressed in terms of only two elliptic periods. This allows us to determine the expansion coefficients order by order. We confirm that the truncated expansions reproduce quasinormal frequencies obtained by solving the angular and radial equations directly, for scalar, electromagnetic and gravitational fields. We also compare the scalar results with the exact WKB analysis of extremal Kerr black holes.

</details>


### [33] [Covariant phase space analysis of static spherical vacua in power-law $f(R)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39484)
*Mariam Campbell,Eduardo Bittencourt,Peter K. S. Dunsby*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes static, spherically symmetric vacuum solutions in power-law $f(R)$ gravity using a covariant autonomous dynamical systems approach; it identifies critical points (e.g., Clifton–Barrow solution, Schwarzschild, Minkowski), determines their stability under radial flow, resolves asymptotics via Poincaré compactification, and reconstructs notable metric families—including a scalar-flat solution with a regular throat and mixed asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify and understand the structure and stability of static, spherically symmetric vacuum solutions in power-law $f(R)$ gravity—beyond standard GR—by leveraging geometric dynamical systems methods rather than ad hoc integration.

Method: Formulates the vacuum field equations as a covariant autonomous system using the $(1+1+2)$ spacetime decomposition; applies dynamical systems tools including critical point analysis, linear stability, Poincaré compactification (with secondary and weighted charts), and metric reconstruction from normalized variables.

Result: Identifies finite critical sets: Clifton–Barrow solution (outward-radial attractor for some $f(R)$ exponents), Schwarzschild endpoint (saddle), and Minkowski limit; reveals normally attracting sheets at large curvature in positive-curvature sector; constructs a scalar-flat family with regular throat, one asymptotically flat end, and one curvature-singular end.

Conclusion: The reduced phase space is organized by these critical elements and asymptotic regimes; however, the analysis is incomplete for full classification—stability conclusions are restricted to the static radial flow, not full spacetime perturbations.

Abstract: We formulate the static, spherically symmetric vacuum equations of power-law metric $f(R)$ gravity as a covariant autonomous system using the $(1+1+2)$ formalism. The finite critical sets include the scale-invariant Clifton--Barrow solution, a Minkowski endpoint shared by the Schwarzschild family, and degenerate boundaries of the normalized chart. The Clifton--Barrow equilibrium is an outward-radial attractor over a positive interval of the power-law parameter, whereas the Schwarzschild endpoint is a saddle of the polynomially extended system. Poincaré compactification, supplemented by secondary and weighted charts, resolves several asymptotic regimes. In particular, normally attracting sheets at large normalized curvature occur in the positive-curvature sector and are separated from the negative-curvature Clifton--Barrow equilibrium. Metric reconstruction also identifies a selected scalar-flat family with a regular throat, one asymptotically flat end, and a curvature-singular end. These results organize the regular reduced chart and the boundary regimes resolved here; they do not constitute a complete classification of static spherical vacua. All stability statements concern the static radial flow.

</details>


### [34] [Quasinormal-mode encounters: Pseudospectra near Kerr--Newman exceptional points](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40323)
*Kyriakos Destounis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how exceptional points (EPs) — where quasinormal mode frequencies and eigenvectors coalesce — enhance spectral instability in Kerr–Newman black holes, using pseudospectra and condition numbers for scalar and gravitoelectromagnetic perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the amplification of spectral instability near exceptional points (EPs) in black hole quasinormal mode spectra, which may impact ringdown physics and observational signatures.

Method: Computes pseudospectra and condition numbers for neutral massless scalar fields (using the (ℓ,m)=(1,1) spheroidal branch and stress-energy-derived energy norm) and coupled gravitoelectromagnetic (GE) perturbations (via a four-field system with positive radiative energy) in Kerr–Newman spacetimes.

Result: Near EPs, condition numbers of the nearly degenerate mode pair exceed those of more-damped reference modes; GE frequency splitting obeys a square-root law and condition numbers scale as inverse square root of distance to EP; pseudospectral contours reveal transition from individual-mode to collective sensitivity.

Conclusion: EPs significantly amplify spectral instability in black hole perturbations; the observed scaling laws and pseudospectral structure provide a quantitative framework for assessing EP contributions to ringdown dynamics and potential detectability.

Abstract: Exceptional points (EPs), at which two quasinormal frequencies and their eigenvectors coalesce, can strongly enhance the spectral instability of black hole quasinormal modes. We investigate this behavior in Kerr--Newman spacetimes through pseudospectra and condition numbers for neutral massless scalar fields and coupled gravitoelectromagnetic (GE) perturbations. For the scalar field, we follow the $(\ell,m)=(1,1)$ spheroidal branch and derive the energy norm from the stress-energy tensor. For GE perturbations, we use a coupled four-field system equipped with a positive radiative energy. At the selected near-EP backgrounds, the condition numbers of both modes in the close pair exceed those of the two more-damped modes shown for comparison at the same background. Along a matched GE parameter path, the frequency splitting follows a square-root law and the condition numbers grow as the inverse square root of the distance to the EP. The pseudospectral contours resolve the transition from individual-mode sensitivity to the collective response of the pair. Within the discretized problems and energy norms considered here, these results quantify the amplification of spectral instability near EPs and provide a basis for studying their contribution to black-hole ringdown.

</details>


### [35] [Validity Domains for Semiclassical Time in Quantum Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37696)
*Gianpaolo Rosati,Sergio L. Cacciatori*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper clarifies that semiclassical WKB time is not automatically reliable just because a gravitational wavefunction is oscillatory; instead, temporal reliability requires simultaneous satisfaction of WKB validity, Born–Oppenheimer adiabaticity, and quantum sharpness—three distinct and inequivalent conditions—even in the simplest minisuperspace model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual ambiguities in quantum cosmology regarding when a semiclassical gravitational configuration can serve as a physically meaningful, quantitatively controlled time parameter for quantum matter degrees of freedom.

Method: The authors formulate the Born–Oppenheimer adiabatic condition directly on gravitational configuration space using the Hamilton–Jacobi vector field—bypassing premature introduction of WKB time—and compare its domain of validity with that of standard WKB approximation and quantum sharpness, applying uniform Airy asymptotics near the turning point in a Friedmann–Robertson–Walker minisuperspace with a massive scalar field.

Result: They demonstrate explicitly that the WKB validity domain (D_WKB), the adiabatic domain (D_ad), and the sharpness domain (D_sharp) are inequivalent: near the gravitational turning point, the WKB control parameter diverges while the configuration-space adiabatic parameter vanishes as O(M^{-1/3}); Airy matching preserves this separation. Decoherence further restricts the joint domain D_t = D_WKB ∩ D_ad ∩ D_sharp.

Conclusion: Existence of an oscillatory semiclassical gravitational branch does not guarantee a reliable WKB time; instead, semiclassical temporal reliability is governed by the intersection of multiple, independent validity domains—highlighting the need for a refined, domain-aware criterion for emergent time in quantum gravity.

Abstract: We investigate the domain in which a semiclassical gravitational WKB flow can serve as a quantitatively controlled temporal reference for subordinate quantum degrees of freedom. Rather than introducing WKB time at the outset, we formulate the Born--Oppenheimer adiabatic condition directly on gravitational configuration space using the Hamilton--Jacobi vector field. This separates adiabatic control from the local WKB validity of the gravitational branch and from the sharpness of the candidate reference against quantum fluctuations. For a single selected semiclassical branch, these requirements define the validity domain $D_t = D_{\mathrm{WKB}} \cap D_{\mathrm{ad}} \cap D_{\mathrm{sharp}}$, with an additional decoherence condition when coherent branches this http URL a closed Friedmann--Robertson--Walker minisuperspace coupled to a homogeneous massive scalar mode, the distinction is explicit: approaching the gravitational turning point from the oscillatory region, the ordinary WKB control parameter diverges while the configuration-space adiabatic parameter tends to zero. A uniform Airy treatment of the turning point removes the formal WKB divergence while preserving the inequivalence: the corresponding local control parameter remains of order unity, whereas the configuration-space adiabatic parameter is parametrically suppressed as $O(M^{-1/3})$.Thus Born--Oppenheimer adiabaticity and gravitational WKB validity define inequivalent domains even within the simplest minisuperspace model. We conclude that the existence of an oscillatory semiclassical branch is not sufficient for a reliable WKB temporal description: semiclassical temporal reliability is instead determined by the overlap of the validity domains supporting the reference flow.

</details>


### [36] [Classical traversability of a carbon BTZ wormhole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37704)
*Valerie Tynianska,Alfredo Iorio*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies classical traversability of a 2D curved carbon membrane mimicking a spatial wormhole, constructed via reflection-symmetric doubling of a BTZ black hole exterior; massless Dirac quasiparticles follow null geodesics, and traversability is governed by a sharp angular momentum threshold determined by the minimum throat radius acting as a centrifugal barrier; near-critical trajectories show logarithmic divergence in angular evolution, leading to multiple windings around the throat.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize and analyze classical traversability in an experimentally accessible condensed-matter analogue of a spatial wormhole—specifically, on a two-dimensional curved carbon membrane—bypassing the need for exotic matter or quantum gravity effects.

Method: Constructs an analogue wormhole geometry by applying a reflection-symmetric doubling technique to the exterior region of a non-rotating BTZ black hole metric, yielding an ultrastatic, horizonless spacetime with a smooth throat; models massless Dirac quasiparticles as spinorial light rays propagating along null geodesics; solves the exact geodesic equations analytically to characterize trajectory behavior as a function of conserved angular momentum.

Result: Identifies a sharp binary threshold in conserved angular momentum that separates traversing (passing through the throat) from reflecting (scattering back) trajectories; reveals that near-threshold trajectories undergo logarithmically divergent angular evolution, resulting in arbitrarily many spatial windings around the throat before escape; establishes the geometric foundation for mesoscopic transport studies.

Conclusion: Classical traversability in this analogue wormhole is purely geometric and deterministic, governed by null geodesic structure and a centrifugal barrier set by the throat’s minimum radius; the observed winding behavior signals emergent chaotic-like scattering signatures, making it a promising platform for probing wormhole-inspired transport phenomena in nanoscale materials.

Abstract: We investigate the classical traversability of an analogue spatial wormhole geometry realized on a two-dimensional curved carbon membrane. The background is obtained by implementing a reflection-symmetric doubling technique on the exterior spatial sector of a non-rotating Bañados-Teitelboim-Zanelli black hole, effectively replacing the event horizon with a smooth traversable throat. Within this framework, massless Dirac quasiparticles propagate as spinorial light rays whose kinematics are governed exclusively by the metric curvature, forcing them to track the null geodesics of the ultrastatic spacetime. By evaluating the exact geodesic equations, we demonstrate that classical traversability is dictated by a sharp binary threshold in the conserved angular momentum, where the minimum throat radius acts as a centrifugal potential barrier that separates traversing paths from reflecting ones. We show that near-critical trajectories experience a logarithmic divergence in their angular evolution, causing the quasiparticles to execute multiple spatial windings around the throat before escaping. These classical ray-tracing configurations establish the necessary geometric backbones for future mesoscopic investigations.

</details>


### [37] [Thermodynamic and structural properties of the thin atmosphere of a white dwarf](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37708)
*Riccardo Fantoni*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes thermodynamic and structural properties of Coulomb plasma (jellium) in the thin atmosphere of a white dwarf using path integral Monte Carlo simulations embedded in a general relativistic stellar model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately model the thermodynamic and structural behavior of Coulomb plasma in the extreme gravitational and density conditions of a white dwarf's thin atmosphere, where quantum, thermal, and relativistic effects are all significant.

Method: Path integral Monte Carlo (PIMC) method coupled with a general relativistic description of the white dwarf geometry and gravitational potential.

Result: Quantitative thermodynamic (e.g., internal energy, pressure, free energy) and structural (e.g., pair correlation functions, static structure factor) properties of jellium under white dwarf atmospheric conditions.

Conclusion: The PIMC approach within a general relativistic framework yields reliable microscopic and macroscopic plasma properties essential for interpreting white dwarf spectra and cooling models.

Abstract: We determine thermodynamic and structural properties of the Coulomb plasma (jellium) in the thin atmosphere of a white dwarf using the path integral Monte Carlo method within a general relativity description of the star.

</details>


### [38] [Draining Without Emptying a Stringy Black Hole: Repetitive Electric Penrose Process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37713)
*Huai-Fan Li,Xiong-Ying Guo,Ke Wang,Hong Lei*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the repetitive electric Penrose process in GMGHS black holes, finding that energy ROI can exceed 100% but utilization efficiency is capped at 50%, with most extracted energy increasing the black hole’s irreducible mass; the process cannot fully deplete charge or mass, and efficiency depends on particle parameters and initial black hole charge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the feasibility and limits of repeated energy extraction via the electric Penrose process in GMGHS black holes—particularly whether charge can be fully depleted and how efficiency scales with black hole and particle parameters—contrasting with RN black holes where effective potential evolves iteratively.

Method: Analytical derivation of effective potential invariants under iteration for GMGHS black holes; formulation of two termination conditions (escape condition for particle 0 and existence condition for negative-energy particle 1); numerical simulation of iterative energy extraction varying decay radius, charge-to-mass ratio and specific energy of particles, and initial black hole charge.

Result: The effective potential remains static across iterations; escape condition is charge-independent, while negative-energy particle existence requires sufficiently large black hole charge; ROI > 100% achievable but energy utilization efficiency ≤ 50%; irreducible mass dominates energy sink; smaller decay radius and larger |q1/m1| improve both ROI and efficiency; higher incident particle specific energy reduces ROI but leaves efficiency unchanged; for non-extremal black holes, increasing initial charge monotonically increases ROI but yields a non-monotonic (peak) behavior in efficiency.

Conclusion: The repetitive electric Penrose process in GMGHS black holes is sustainable but self-limiting: it cannot exhaust charge or mass, preserves extractable energy post-iteration, and exhibits fundamental thermodynamic constraints—specifically, irreducible mass growth caps usable energy, and charge threshold prevents total discharge.

Abstract: This paper studies the repetitive electric Penrose process in the Gibbons-Maeda-Garfinkle-Horowitz-Strominger (GMGHS) black hole under the optimal condition for maximum energy extraction. Unlike the Reissner-Nordström (RN) black hole, the effective potential curve of the incident particle in the GMGHS black hole remains unchanged during the iterations. The iteration termination conditions are determined by two aspects: the escape condition requires that particle 0 can overcome the effective potential peak and escape to infinity, and this condition is independent of the black hole charge; the existence condition for negative-energy particle 1 requires the black hole charge to be sufficiently large, indicating that the repetitive process cannot completely consume the charge of the GMGHS black hole to zero. Numerical simulations show that the return on investment of energy extraction can exceed $100\%$, but the energy utilization efficiency does not exceed $50\%$, and most of the extractable energy is converted into an increase in the irreducible mass of the black hole. In addition, a smaller decay radius and a larger absolute value of the charge-to-mass ratio of particle 1 can improve the return on investment and energy utilization efficiency of energy extraction; the larger the specific energy of the incident particle, the smaller the return on investment of energy extraction, while the energy utilization efficiency remains unchanged; for an initially non-extremal black hole, as the initial charge increases, the return on investment of energy extraction increases monotonically, while the energy utilization efficiency first increases and then decreases. After the iterations end, the black hole still retains extractable energy, and neither the charge nor the mass vanishes.

</details>

TL;DR: This paper studies a quadratic gravity model extended with derivative terms of the Ricci scalar, identifies conditions under which the action becomes a perfect square, reduces it in FLRW cosmology to a time-reparameterization-invariant variable, and maps it via a nonlinear change of variables to a Gaussian (Wiener) path integral—uniquely in 7+1 dimensions where the transformation Jacobian is field-independent.

  <summary>Details</summary>

Result: The reduced gravitational action maps exactly to a Gaussian (Wiener) action only when spacetime is $7+1$-dimensional—the discrete transformation Jacobian becomes field-independent in this case, enabling a formal Wiener-measure representation of expectation values.

Conclusion: A consistent stochastic path integral formulation for this class of higher-derivative gravity models is feasible only in $7+1$ dimensions, suggesting a dimensional selection mechanism arising from functional measure consistency under field redefinition.

Abstract: We consider a quadratic gravity model supplemented by the terms $\nabla_{\mu}R\nabla^{\mu}R$ and $(\nabla^{2}R)^2$. For specific relations among the coupling constants, the action takes a perfect-square form. In the spatially flat FLRW model, we perform a reduction to a dynamical variable invariant under time reparameterizations. We construct a nonlinear change of variable that transforms the reduced action into the Gaussian action associated with the Wiener measure. An analysis of the transformation Jacobian within a time discretization singles out the case of $7+1$-dimensional spacetime, in which the Jacobian of the discrete transformation under consideration is independent of the dynamical variable. On this basis, we propose a formal representation of expectation values of observables in the reduced model as integrals with respect to the Wiener measure.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test quantum gravity imprints from the cosmic bounce in loop quantum cosmology by computing observable predictions—especially the tensor-to-scalar ratio r(k)—and disentangling initial-state artifacts from robust physical features in the primordial spectrum.

Method: Numerical evolution of tensor perturbations across the quantum bounce in standard LQC, mLQC-I, and mLQC-II; use of de Sitter mode functions (dressed metric) and Hankel-function solutions (hybrid) as initial states; computation of scale-dependent tensor-to-scalar ratio r(k) = P_T/P_S; propagation of spectra to CMB B-mode angular power spectrum.

Result: For N_e = 60–65, r_star matches standard Starobinsky inflation; r(k) is slowly varying in the scale-invariant regime but oscillates near it—oscillations there are adiabatic-order-independent, unlike low-k spikes; infrared r(k) retains regularization and pre-bounce branch dependence; normalized spectra yield testable CMB B-mode signatures.

Conclusion: The scale-invariant regime of r(k) provides robust, initial-state-independent probes of mLQC physics, while low-k features encode quantum gravitational memory of the bounce; CMB B-mode observations can constrain mLQC models, particularly via infrared spectral distortions.

Abstract: We compute the primordial tensor power spectrum in standard loop quantum cosmology and in the modified models mLQC-I and mLQC-II, evolving the tensor perturbations across the bounce in the hybrid and dressed metric prescriptions with a Starobinsky potential. In mLQC-I, the emergent de Sitter contracting branch requires the use of dedicated initial states. We use de Sitter mode function in the dressed metric approach and a Hankel-function solution in the hybrid approach. For inflationary e-foldings $N_e = 60$ and $65$, the pivot-scale tensor-to-scalar ratio agrees with the value of standard inflation with this potential. The scale-dependent ratio $r(k)=\mathcal{P}_T/\mathcal{P}_S$ cancels effects common to the two sectors and separates features that are independent of the initial state from those that depend on it. It varies slowly in the scale-invariant regime and oscillates around $r_\star$ at smaller $k$. The oscillations next to the scale-invariant regime do not depend on the adiabatic order, unlike the peaks and spikes at lower $k$. For a given initial state, the regularization dependence survives in the infrared, where long-wavelength modes retain a record of the bounce and of the pre-bounce branch. We propagate the normalized spectra to the CMB $B$-mode spectrum and discuss observational prospects.

</details>


### [41] [Inverse design of scalar-tensor EFT from odd-parity effective potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37915)

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: Inverse design: starting from a prescribed odd-parity effective potential (e.g., Pöschl-Teller or Regge-Wheeler), solve the mapping relations between that potential and the background geometry + EFT coefficients for static, spherically symmetric spacetimes with timelike scalar gradient; employ analytic solutions for QNMs where possible.

Result: Explicit EFT realizations of Pöschl-Teller potentials (with exact QNM spectra) and both standard and parametrically deformed Regge-Wheeler potentials within scalar-tensor EFT; derivation of explicit mappings from potential deformations to EFT operator coefficients.

Abstract: We develop an inverse-design approach to the effective field theory (EFT) of perturbations in scalar-tensor gravity around static and spherically symmetric backgrounds with a timelike scalar-field gradient, taking the odd-parity effective potential as input and reconstructing compatible background and EFT data. As analytically tractable examples, we construct explicit realizations of the Pöschl-Teller potential, for which the quasinormal-mode spectrum can be obtained in closed form. We then study the Regge-Wheeler potential of general relativity and its parametrized deformations, showing how both can be realized within the scalar-tensor EFT. In particular, the latter realizations provide a route to interpreting phenomenological constraints on potential deformations from black hole ringdown observations in terms of the underlying EFT data.

</details>


### [42] [Quantum gravity phenomenology in quadrupolar Bose-Einstein condensates: Finite-temperature thermodynamics and projected bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37930)
*Asma Tahar Taiba,Abdelaali Boudjemaa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the generalized uncertainty principle (GUP)—a candidate quantum gravity signature—affects thermodynamic properties of quadrupolar Bose-Einstein condensates, finding that quadrupole-quadrupole interactions combined with GUP (especially LQGUP) induce significant, measurable corrections to critical temperature, condensed fraction, and equation of state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum gravity phenomenology by identifying experimentally accessible signatures in finite-temperature many-body systems—specifically, using quadrupolar BECs as sensitive probes of GUP-induced modifications to quantum mechanics.

Method: Analytical thermodynamic analysis within second quantization; incorporation of modified density of states from quadratic (QGUP) and linear-quadratic (LQGUP) generalized uncertainty principles; calculation of corrections to condensed fraction, critical temperature, equation of state, and related observables; estimation of experimental sensitivity via fractional temperature resolution constraints.

Result: GUP induces non-negligible corrections to all key thermodynamic quantities; LQGUP generally yields larger deviations than QGUP, but magnitude depends on parameters and perturbative validity; projected upper bounds on GUP deformation parameters are derived from assumed temperature resolution; QQIs enhance GUP sensitivity.

Abstract: We study the thermodynamics of quadrupolar Bose-Einstein condensates in the realm of the generalized uncertainty principle (GUP) for the quest of understanding and testing quantum gravity (QG). Using both quadratic GUP (QGUP) and linear-quadratic GUP (LQGUP) which incorporate modified density of states, we calculate corrections to the condensed fraction, critical temperature, equation of state and other thermodynamic quantities. We find that the intriguing interplay of quadrupole-quadrupole interactions (QQIs) and the GUP may strongly modify the finite-temperature properties of the condensate, with the LQGUP generally predicting larger corrections than the QGUP, although their relative magnitudes depend on the chosen parameters and the validity of the perturbative expansion. We further estimate the sensitivity of the critical temperature to GUP-induced effects by imposing an assumed fractional temperature resolution which provides projected upper bounds on the GUP deformation parameters. Our findings demonstrate that quadrupole-quadrupole interacting Bose gases provide a promising platform for testing GUP-induced deviations from standard quantum mechanics and for probing QG phenomenology through measurements of finite-temperature many-body observables. They also motivate future studies using realistic system-specific parameters.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>

Conclusion: Brill-Lindquist-deSitter initial data provides a valuable analytic testbed for probing MOTS evolution and geometric aspects of black hole mergers, bridging analytical tractability and physical realism in horizon dynamics.

Abstract: Five years ago Pook-Kolb, Hennigar and Booth published a series of three papers exploring the evolution of apparent horizons and other marginally outer trapped surfaces during a black hole merger. This was seen to be much more intricate than the well-known "pair-of-pants" merger associated with event horizons. In this paper we show that parameter evolutions of Brill-Lindquist-deSitter initial data mimic many of the features of that evolution. Hence, this analytic intial data is a useful testing ground for ideas about marginally outer trapped surface evolution and spacetime geometry during mergers.

</details>


### [44] [Physical Effects in the Vicinity of Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38068)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate classical general relativistic phenomena whose insights may inform modern cosmological and black hole observations, especially as observational astronomy transitions from electromagnetic to gravitational-wave-based detection.

Result: Identification and characterization of relativistic phenomena bearing relevance to current and future gravitational-wave and black hole observations.

Conclusion: Classical general relativity remains a rich and productive framework for generating physically relevant insights, even in the era of multimessenger and gravitational-wave astronomy.

Abstract: General relativity, a theory that is already over a century old, is still the best theory that we have to describe the universe at energy scales where only the gravitational force is important. It has stood tall to any observational tests thrown at it since its inception, and it has remarkable predictive power when it is utilized to study and model astrophysical problems. It also continues to surprise us with its ability to generate new problems and insights that render themselves useful in the study of modern physics. This is especially highlighted by the transition of modern observational astronomy away from an electromagnetic radiation epoch, and into a new gravitational wave epoch. This thesis devotes its study to phenomena that can be analyzed within the framework of classical general relativity, but whose results may contain interesting implications for cosmological and black hole observations. (Full abstract can be found in document).

### [45] [Gravitational baryogenesis in Gauss-Bonnet braneworld cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38188)
*Ian B Whittingham*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores gravitational baryogenesis in a Gauss-Bonnet–modified Randall-Sundrum type II braneworld cosmology, identifying three distinct cosmic expansion regimes (GB-dominated, RS-dominated, GR-like), and shows that successful baryogenesis requires decoupling during the GB or direct GB→GR transition due to the sign constraint on $\dot{R}$. It derives an analytic expression for the baryon asymmetry $\eta(T)$, finds viable parameter windows consistent with observed $\eta$, and highlights a key qualitative difference from GR: non-zero $\eta$ even for radiation domination ($w=1/3$).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether gravitational baryogenesis—relying on spacetime curvature dynamics via $\dot{R}$—can generate the observed baryon asymmetry in a higher-dimensional, modified-gravity cosmology combining Gauss-Bonnet curvature corrections and Randall-Sundrum type II brane geometry.

Method: Analytical derivation of the modified Friedmann equation and Ricci scalar time derivative $\dot{R}$ in GB-RS-II cosmology; identification of sign conditions on $\dot{R}$ for baryogenesis; computation of baryon-to-entropy ratio $\eta(T)$ as function of bulk curvature scale $\mu = 1/l$, GB coupling $\alpha$ (via $\beta = 4\alpha \mu^2$), and equation-of-state parameter $w$; consistency checks against observational constraints and prior inflationary bounds on $(\mu, \beta)$. 

Result: Gravitational baryogenesis is viable only if baryon-number violation occurs during the GB regime or via direct GB→GR transition (since $\dot{R}>0$ in RS regime violates the $\dot{R}<0$ requirement); $\eta(T) \propto \mu (1+w)(1-w)(1+1/\beta)$; numerically, observed $\eta \sim 10^{-10}$ is reproduced for $\beta \in (10^{-3}, 10^{-2})$ at $\mu = 10^{12}$ GeV, $\beta \in (10^{-2}, 10^{-1})$ at $\mu = 10^{13}$ GeV, but not for $\mu = 10^{14}$ GeV; crucially, $\eta \neq 0$ for $w = 1/3$, unlike in standard GR.

Conclusion: Gauss-Bonnet modifications in RS-II braneworld cosmology enable gravitational baryogenesis even during radiation domination—a feature absent in general relativity—and yield observationally viable baryon asymmetry within constrained parameter ranges, provided the Universe avoids the $\dot{R}>0$ RS-dominated epoch at baryogenesis decoupling.

Abstract: The present study investigates the feasibility of gravitational baryogenesis in a Gauss-Bonnet (GB) modified Randall-Sundrum (RS) type II braneworld cosmology. The modified expansion rate of the Universe in this scenario evolves through three distinct expansion regimes, the GB regime at early times where the expansion rate $H \sim \rho^{1/3}$ falls more slowly than the standard expansion law $H\sim \rho^{1/2}$ of GR, then the RS type era with an enhanced expansion $H \sim \rho$, before finally reducing to the standard expansion law in the low energy limit. Gravitational baryogenesis depends on $\dot{R}$ and requires $\dot{R} <0$. This condition is satisfied for the GB and GR regimes. However, $\dot{R}>0$ for the RS regime and successful baryogenesis requires either that the decoupling temperature occurs during the GB regime or that the Universe makes a direct transition from the GB regime to the GR regime. The GB baryon asymmetry $\eta(T)$ is proportional to $\mu (1+w)(1-w)(1+1/\beta)$, where $\mu$ is the energy scale $\mu \equiv 1/l$ associated with the bulk curvature scale $l$, $\beta \equiv 4 \alpha \mu^{2}$, where $\alpha$ is the GB coupling, and $w$ is the equation of state parameter $p=w \rho$. Studies of inflation in GB-braneworld cosmology place constraints on the allowed ranges of the parameters $\mu$ and $\beta$. Guided by these, the asymmetry has been calculated for ranges of $(\mu, \beta, w)$ and it is found that the observed values can be obtained for a value of $\beta$ within the range $(10^{-2} - 10^{-3})$ for $\mu =10^{12}$ GeV, and within the range $(10^{-1}- 10^{-2})$ for $\mu =10^{13}$ GeV. All values of $\eta$ for $\mu=10^{14}$ are too high. In contrast to GR where $\eta$ is zero for a radiation dominated Universe ($w=1/3$), it is non-zero in GB braneworld cosmology.

</details>


### [46] [Axion-angle dependence of the rotational and tidal properties of PNJL quark stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38343)

TL;DR: This paper studies how a spatially uniform axion angle θ (0 to π) affects rotational and tidal properties of quark stars using an axion-extended PNJL equation of state; it finds significant θ-dependent changes in maximum mass, moment of inertia (+13% at 1.338 M⊙), and tidal deformability, yet universal I-Love-Q and Ī–C relations hold to within ~3% and ~0.4%, respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the impact of a fixed axion field (via the θ parameter) on compact-star observables—particularly rotational (moment of inertia, quadrupole) and static tidal (deformability, mass shift) properties—as a potential window into QCD axion physics and strong-interaction EoS modifications.

Method: Solves the TOV and slow-rotation (Hartle-Thorne) equations in general relativity for self-bound quark stars using an axion-extended Polyakov-loop Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (PNJL) equation of state; computes leading-order moment of inertia, second-order spin-induced quadrupole moment and rotational mass shift, and static tidal deformability, including finite-surface-density corrections; samples five equally spaced θ values from 0 to π.

Result: Static maximum mass increases from 1.68 to 1.95 M⊙ with θ; at 1.338 M⊙, moment of inertia rises by 13% and dimensionless tidal deformability Λ increases from 342 to 547; differences in Ī between adjacent θ values exceed numerical interpolation/table-resolution uncertainties; Ī–C and I-Love-Q relations remain universal to within 2.7% and 0.4%, respectively; predicted binary tidal deformability curves are consistent with GW170817 90% credible region.

Conclusion: The axion angle θ induces substantial, physically distinguishable variations in stellar structure and tidal response within this EoS family, but normalized universal relations retain high accuracy—suggesting that while θ modifies microphysics-sensitive bulk properties, macroscopic scaling laws remain robust, supporting their use in multi-messenger inference even for exotic EoSs.

Abstract: We investigate how a fixed, spatially uniform axion angle modifies the rotational and static tidal properties of self-bound quark stars in general relativity, using an axion-extended Polyakov-loop Nambu--Jona-Lasinio equation of state. We vary $\theta$ from $0$ to $\pi$ while holding the remaining model parameters fixed, and compute the leading-order moment of inertia and the second-order spin-induced quadrupole moment and mass shift. The calculation includes finite-surface-density contributions to the rotational mass shift and static tidal response. Across this range, the static maximum mass increases from $1.68$ to $1.95\,M_\odot$, remaining below the lower edge of the quoted $1\sigma$ timing interval for PSR~J0740+6620. At $M=1.338\,M_\odot$, the moment of inertia increases by $13\%$ and the dimensionless tidal deformability rises from $342$ to $547$. Over the common mass range examined, the differences in the moment of inertia between adjacent sampled angles exceed the tested interpolation and table-resolution sensitivities. Despite these changes, joint fits to the five sequences describe the $\bar I$--$C$ and I-Love-Q relations with maximum departures of $2.7\%$ and $0.4\%$, respectively, over the fitted domains. The predicted binary tidal-deformability curves lie within the estimated 90\% credible region of the adopted GW170817 posterior over the mass-ratio ranges examined. Within this EOS family, stellar properties vary appreciably with $\theta$, while the normalized quantities admit accurate joint fits across the five sequences.

</details>


### [47] [Hamiltonian Analysis of the Newer General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38372)
*Chen-Yi Sia,Tee-How Loo,Avik De*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper conducts a Hamiltonian analysis of Newer General Relativity—a five-parameter class of quadratic nonmetricity theories—classifying them into nine types based on primary constraint structure, and determining their physical degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand the constraint structure and physical content of Newer General Relativity, a class of symmetric teleparallel gravity theories with quadratic nonmetricity, by extending the ADM formalism and Hamiltonian analysis to clarify their dynamical degrees of freedom.

Method: Hamiltonian analysis within the symmetric teleparallel framework: derivation of the ADM formulation, identification of conjugate momenta and primary constraints, construction of canonical and primary Hamiltonians, and systematic classification into nine types based on constraint algebra.

Result: Nine distinct types of Newer General Relativity are identified; for each, the full set of primary and secondary constraints is derived, and the number of physical degrees of freedom is determined.

Conclusion: The physical content (i.e., propagating degrees of freedom) of Newer General Relativity depends critically on the parameter choices, with only specific subclasses yielding healthy, ghost-free dynamics—highlighting the necessity of constraint analysis for viability assessment.

Abstract: We present a systematic Hamiltonian analysis of the five-parameter class of theories known as Newer General Relativity. Quadratic nonmetricity theories of this type can be divided into nine types according to the structure of their primary constraints. After reviewing the framework of symmetric teleparallel gravity, we derive the ADM formulation of Newer General Relativity, discuss the existing classification and perform the Hamiltonian formulation of the theory. For each type, we compute the conjugate momenta, identify the primary constraints, and construct both the canonical and primary Hamiltonians. We then determine the constraint structure and analyze the resulting physical degrees of freedom in each type.

</details>


### [48] [Quasinormal Modes of a Rotating Hořava Black Hole: Two Perturbative Approaches to Nonseparability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38428)
*Wen-Di Guo,Wentao Liu,Yu-Xiao Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes scalar quasinormal modes of rotating black holes in Hořava gravity using two novel approximations that preserve the exact Kerr limit; it finds distinct, spin-dependent Lorentz-violating corrections—especially a ~2% angular correction to real frequencies at high spin—and demonstrates strong sensitivity of damping rates to angular projection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe Lorentz violation in strong-field gravity via quasinormal modes of rotating black holes in Hořava gravity, where the scalar wave equation is nonseparable due to rotation-induced Lorentz violation.

Method: Two complementary approximations: (I) slow-rotation expansion applied only to the nonseparable Lorentz-violating sector; (II) small-η biorthogonal spheroidal projection; both preserve the exact Kerr limit. Numerical implementation uses residual-controlled continuation and symmetric finite differences, validated against Kerr continued-fraction and radial spectral methods.

Result: Axisymmetric modes serve as exact Kerr null tests. Corotating modes show positive, spin-increasing real frequencies; at a/M = 0.9, both methods yield ~2% angular correction to real frequency; imaginary (damping) part is nonmonotonic and highly sensitive to angular projection due to internal cancellation.

Conclusion: Lorentz violation in Hořava gravity imprints distinctive, rotationally enhanced signatures on black-hole ringdown spectra—particularly in frequency shifts and anomalous damping behavior—enabling potential observational discrimination from general relativity.

Abstract: Quasinormal modes (QNMs) provide a powerful probe of strong-field gravity. We study fundamental scalar quasinormal modes of an asymptotically flat rotating black hole in the low-energy sector of Hořava gravity, whose exact scalar equation is nonseparable due to rotation-induced Lorentz violation. We develop two complementary approximations that both preserve the exact Kerr sector: Method I applies a slow-rotation approximation only to the nonseparable Lorentz-violating part, whereas Method II employs a small-$\eta$ biorthogonal spheroidal projection, with $\eta$ the Lorentz-violating parameter. Using residual-controlled continuation and symmetric finite differences, validated by a Kerr continued-fraction benchmark and an independent radial spectral method, we compute the linear frequency response. Axisymmetric modes provide an exact Kerr null test. For the corotating modes studied, the real-frequency response is positive and increases with spin. At $a/M=0.9$, the two methods reveal a roughly $2\%$ angular correction to the real response, while the imaginary response is nonmonotonic and highly sensitive to angular projection due to internal cancellation. These results highlight the distinct rotational signatures of Lorentz violation in black-hole ringdown.

</details>


### [49] [Exact Rotating Wormholes in Five Dimensions and Their Exceptional-Point Instability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38659)
*Jose Luis Blázquez-Salcedo,Luis Manuel González-Romero,Fech Scen Khoo,Burkhard Kleihaus,Jutta Kunz,Pablo Navarro Moreno*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs rotating 5D Ellis--Bronnikov wormholes with equal angular momenta, reduces their metric equations to an open A₂ Toda system, and discovers a second-order exceptional point in radial perturbations where unstable modes coalesce and transition from purely imaginary to complex-conjugate oscillatory instabilities — not stabilization — with growth rate suppression at high rotation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how rotation affects the radial stability of higher-dimensional traversable wormholes, particularly whether it can stabilize them or merely alter the nature of instability.

Method: Exact closed-form construction using Toda tau functions; reduction of Einstein equations to open A₂ Toda system; cohomogeneity-one linear perturbation analysis on the exact rotating background; spectral analysis of the radial operator identifying exceptional points.

Result: A one-parameter family of reflection-symmetric rotating wormholes interpolating between static Ellis--Bronnikov and extremal equal-spin Myers--Perry black holes; discovery of a second-order exceptional point at |J|/A ≈ 0.100628 where two purely imaginary unstable frequencies coalesce; beyond this point, instability becomes oscillatory (ω_± = ±ω_R + iω_I), with ω_I > 0 but strongly suppressed at large J.

Conclusion: Rotation does not stabilize the wormhole radially; instead, it induces a qualitative change in instability character — from monotonic exponential growth to damped oscillatory growth — governed by non-Hermitian spectral degeneracy (exceptional point).

Abstract: We give an independent closed-form construction of rotating five-dimensional Ellis--Bronnikov wormholes with equal angular momenta and analyze the radial instability of the reflection-symmetric family. The nontrivial coupled metric equations reduce exactly to an open $A_2$ Toda system. The general two-ended solution is expressed in terms of elementary Toda tau functions; the symmetric subclass reduces to a particularly simple one-parameter family interpolating between the static wormhole and the extremal equal-spin Myers--Perry geometry. On this exact background we revisit the cohomogeneity-one perturbation problem. Besides the ordinary unstable mode of the static wormhole, rotation renders the descendant of its non-normalizable zero mode normalizable. The two purely imaginary unstable frequencies coalesce at $|J|/A=0.100628$, where their eigenvectors coalesce as well, identifying a second-order exceptional point. Beyond it the pair continues as $\omega_\pm=\pm\omega_R+\mathrm i\omega_I$ with $\omega_I>0$: the apparent loss of the radial instability found in the original real-eigenvalue treatment is therefore not stabilization, but a transition to an oscillatory instability. We follow this branch deep into the rapidly rotating regime, where its growth rate becomes strongly suppressed.

</details>


### [50] [Resonance correction to time-dependent finite-size effects in Effective Field Theory of binary black hole inspiral](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38668)
*Shani Avitan,Ram Brustein*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper calculates how resonant excitation of ultra-compact objects (UCOs) enhances tidal deformation effects in binary inspirals, using point-particle effective field theory; it finds a negative static electric quadrupole Love number k₂, and shows resonant excitation introduces a leading 2.5 PN correction—distinct from the 5 PN static case—enhancing detectability of internal structure via gravitational wave phase imprints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether astrophysical black holes possess internal structure (i.e., are ultra-compact objects rather than vacuum GR black holes) by probing non-vanishing tidal Love numbers and resonant tidal excitations in binary inspirals.

Method: Uses point-particle effective field theory for circular orbits; models UCO internal spectrum as a harmonic oscillator; integrates out the internal electric quadrupole degree of freedom to derive time-dependent finite-size operators and their coefficients; computes Love number k₂ and post-Newtonian scaling of resonant vs. static tidal effects.

Result: The static electric quadrupole Love number k₂ is negative; all finite-size tidal operators are proportional to k₂; resonant excitation generates a leading 2.5 post-Newtonian contribution to the gravitational wave phase, contrasting with the 5 post-Newtonian order in the non-resonant (static) case.

Conclusion: Resonant tidal excitation during binary inspiral provides a highly sensitive probe of internal structure in ultra-compact objects, with distinctive low-order PN signatures in gravitational waveforms—making it one of the most promising avenues to distinguish black holes from exotic alternatives.

Abstract: General Relativity predicts that black holes lack an internal structure and that their tidal Love numbers vanish. However, if astrophysical black holes were instead ultra-compact objects (UCOs) with internal structure, their tidal Love numbers would no longer vanish. Then, during a binary inspiral, their tidal deformation could transfer orbital energy to the UCO, increasing the orbital period and leaving an observable imprint on the phase of the emitted gravitational waves. As we discussed previously, the effect is enhanced if one binary companion is resonantly excited. Here we use the point-particle effective field theory framework to calculate this enhancement. We consider circular orbits and focus on excitations of the internal electric quadrupole, modelling the UCO spectrum as a harmonic oscillator spectrum. However, our methods could be straightforwardly extended to more complicated situations. Integrating out the internal quadrupole induces a series of time-dependent finite-size operators with specific numerical coefficients, which we calculate. We find that the static electric quadrupole Love number $k_2$ is negative, and that all finite-size terms are proportional to $k_2$. We further show that resonant excitation induces a leading 2.5 Post-Newtonian term, compared with the 5 Post-Newtonian term in the time-independent case. Our results confirm the expectation that resonant excitation during a binary inspiral offers one of the best ways to detect whether astrophysical black holes can undergo tidal deformation and, if they do, learn about their internal excitation spectrum.

</details>


### [51] [Gravitational field of spinors with torsion and matter$-$antimatter asymmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38738)
*Nikodem Popławski*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends general relativity to Einstein–Cartan theory by incorporating spacetime torsion required by angular momentum conservation for Dirac particles; it derives a nonlinear, charge-conjugation-asymmetric Dirac equation in curved spacetime with torsion, computes the energy–momentum tensor showing opposite (negative/positive) torsional energy contributions for matter vs. antimatter, and finds soliton solutions with intrinsic matter–antimatter energy asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To satisfy the conservation law of total (orbital + spin) angular momentum for a free Dirac particle in curved spacetime, torsion — the antisymmetric part of the affine connection — must be included, necessitating extension from general relativity to Einstein–Cartan gravity.

Method: Uses spinor connection formalism to derive the Dirac equation for a spherically symmetric spinor in its own centrally symmetric gravitational field with torsion; computes the energy–momentum tensor; derives full coupled Einstein–Cartan–Dirac equations; analyzes charge conjugation properties and ground-state energies.

Result: The Dirac equation becomes nonlinear and charge-conjugation asymmetric due to torsion; torsional energy contribution is negative for matter and positive for antimatter, leading to lower ground-state energy for fermions than antifermions; Einstein–Cartan–Dirac equations admit particle-like soliton solutions exhibiting matter–antimatter energy asymmetry.

Conclusion: Spacetime torsion, mandated by angular momentum conservation, induces an intrinsic, geometric energy asymmetry between matter and antimatter at the fundamental level, potentially offering a gravitational origin for baryon asymmetry and supporting stable, localized solitonic fermionic states.

Abstract: The conservation law for the orbital plus spin angular momentum of a free Dirac particle in curved spacetime requires that the affine connection has the antisymmetric part: the torsion tensor, which extends general relativity to the Einstein$-$Cartan theory of gravity. In spacetime with torsion, the Dirac equation becomes a nonlinear, cubic equation in the spinor wave function. Under charge conjugation, the nonlinear term changes sign relative to the mass term. A Dirac spinor and its charge conjugate therefore satisfy different field equations. We use the spinor connection formalism to derive the Dirac equation for a spherical spinor in its centrally symmetric gravitational field with torsion. We determine the energy$-$momentum tensor for this spinor and show that the energy contribution from torsion is negative for matter particles and positive for antimatter particles. Consequently, the ground state of a fermion has a lower energy than the ground state of the corresponding antifermion. We also derive the Einstein$-$Cartan$-$Dirac equations for this spinor; they indicate the existence of particle-like soliton solutions exhibiting matter$-$antimatter energy asymmetry.

</details>


### [52] [Isospectrality breaking in the axial quasinormal modes of Reissner-Nordström black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38742)
*Abhishek Rout*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates odd-parity perturbations of charged Reissner-Nordström black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons (dCS) gravity, revealing how the pseudoscalar field breaks axial–polar isospectrality and modifies quasinormal mode spectra via finite-charge couplings; it computes precise complex frequency shifts for photon-sphere and near-extremal zero-damping modes, identifying critical charge thresholds for degeneracy and sign reversal of corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how parity-violating modifications—specifically dynamical Chern-Simons gravity—affect black hole perturbations and stability, particularly whether and how axial–polar isospectrality (a hallmark of GR) is broken in the presence of electromagnetic charge and pseudoscalar coupling.

Method: Linearized analysis of dCS-coupled Einstein–Maxwell equations around Reissner-Nordström spacetime; derivation of axial-sector coupling kernels; diagonalization of the coupled gravitational–electromagnetic–pseudoscalar system; computation of quasinormal-mode (QNM) frequency shifts using exterior complex scaling (for photon-sphere modes) and Frobenius shooting (for near-extremal zero-damping modes).

Result: The pseudoscalar perturbation couples exclusively to axial modes at linear order, breaking axial–polar isospectrality; explicit coupling kernel $(Mr-Q^2)\Delta/r^8$ and mixing coefficient $\chi = 2Q/[r\sqrt{(l-1)(l+2)}]$ are derived; QNM shifts are computed: e.g., $\beta\,\delta\omega = -0.7733 - 0.3946\,i$ for Schwarzschild $l=2$ fundamental mode; a critical charge $Q_c$ is identified where electromagnetic-led and pseudoscalar modes become degenerate, inducing $\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\zeta})$ breaking with sign reversal; near extremality, zero-damping mode shift scales linearly with $1 - Q^2/M^2$.

Conclusion: Dynamical Chern-Simons gravity induces qualitatively new structure in charged black hole perturbations: odd-parity pseudoscalar coupling lifts isospectrality, introduces charge-dependent spectral features including degeneracy points and sign reversals in QNM shifts, and yields distinct asymptotic scalings ($l^3$ vs $l^2/Q$) and near-extremal suppression—providing observable signatures for testing dCS gravity with gravitational-wave spectroscopy.

Abstract: We study odd-parity perturbations of Reissner-Nordström black holes in dynamical Chern-Simons gravity. The dCS action is parity invariant because $\vartheta\,{}^{*}RR$ is the product of two pseudoscalars; nevertheless, the pseudoscalar perturbation has odd parity and enters only the axial sector at linear order and so lifts the axial--polar isospectrality of general relativity. We derive the finite-charge couplings between the pseudoscalar and the axial gravitational and electromagnetic perturbations directly from the linearized field equations. Both directions are governed by the radial kernel $(Mr-Q^2)\Delta/r^8$, and at nonzero charge the pseudoscalar couples to $\Psi+\chi\Psi_e$, with $\chi=2Q/[r\sqrt{(l-1)(l+2)}]$. After diagonalizing the Einstein--Maxwell sector, we compute branch-resolved quasinormal-mode shifts with exterior complex scaling for the photon-sphere modes and Frobenius shooting for the near-extremal zero-damping modes. For the Schwarzschild $l=2$ fundamental mode we obtain $\beta\,\delta\omega=-0.7733-0.3946\,i$ (units $M=\alpha=1$). Off resonance, the gravitational-led shift scales as $\zeta\,l^3$ at $Q=0$ but as $\zeta\,l^2/Q$ at fixed nonzero charge and large $l$. The electromagnetic-led branch becomes exactly degenerate with the pseudoscalar mode at $Q_c=2M\sqrt{l(l+1)+1}/[l(l+1)+2]$, where the pseudoscalar potential coincides with the polar partner of the electromagnetic-led potential; there the breaking is $\mathcal{O}(\sqrt\zeta)$, $M|\Delta\omega|\simeq2.4\sqrt\zeta$ for $l=2$, and the shift reverses sign across $Q_c$. Near extremality the photon-sphere shifts remain finite, whereas the non-degenerate fundamental gravitational-led zero-damping mode is suppressed, $\delta\omega/\omega\simeq6.0\times10^{3}\,\zeta\,(1-Q^2/M^2)$ for $l=2$.

</details>


### [53] [Bosonic Dark Matter Cores Can Screen Scalar-Tensor Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38746)
*Jorge Castelo Mourelle,Saeed Fakhry,Néstor Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates scalarized fermion-boson stars (FBSs) in Damour-Esposito-Farèse gravity, showing that ultralight bosonic dark matter catalyzes spontaneous scalarization, enables stable configurations with DM cores satisfying multi-messenger mass-radius constraints, and suppresses scalar charges—potentially evading current observational bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the observational viability of scalarized fermion-boson stars (FBSs) in a specific scalar-tensor theory (massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse gravity), motivated by multi-messenger astrophysics and the role of ultralight bosonic dark matter as a scalar field.

Method: Numerical construction of static, spherically symmetric FBS solutions using the MPA1 nuclear equation of state and a self-interacting complex scalar field; models constrained within a multi-messenger-informed mass-radius window to evaluate stability, scalarization onset, and scalar charge behavior.

Result: 1) Bosonic dark matter catalyzes spontaneous scalarization; 2) Energetically favored scalarized FBSs possess dark matter cores compatible with radius bounds and strong coupling; 3) Dark matter cores suppress scalar charges—mimicking massive scalar field effects and aiding evasion of observational constraints; 4) Scalarized FBSs are uniquely predicted at high masses, offering a testable signature.

Conclusion: Scalarized FBSs in massless DEF gravity with ultralight bosonic dark matter are observationally viable candidates, reconciling theoretical strong-coupling regimes with empirical mass-radius constraints while providing falsifiable predictions—particularly through high-mass exclusivity.

Abstract: We examine the observational viability of scalarized fermion-boson stars (FBSs) in massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse gravity, using the MPA1 nuclear equation of state and a self-interacting complex scalar field playing the role of an ultralight bosonic dark matter model. Constraining stellar models within a multi-messenger-motivated mass-radius window, we obtain three main results: First, bosonic dark matter acts as a catalyst for spontaneous scalarization. Second, energetically favored configurations are scalarized FBSs featuring bosonic dark matter cores that reconcile strongly coupled regimes with radius bounds. Third, dark matter cores tend to suppress scalar charges, which mimics the effect of a massive gravitational scalar field and could contribute to evade observational constraints. Additionally, the exclusive prediction of scalarized FBSs at high masses suggests a way to test this theoretical scenario.

</details>


### [54] [Fermion-Boson Stars in Scalar-Tensor Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38747)
*Jorge Castelo Mourelle,Saeed Fakhry,Néstor Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper numerically constructs static, spherically symmetric mixed fermion-boson stars in massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse scalar-tensor gravity and investigates spontaneous scalarization, revealing rich structural configurations and parameter-dependent critical thresholds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether spontaneous scalarization—a strong-field gravitational phenomenon—can occur in compact stars composed of both fermionic (e.g., neutron matter) and bosonic (e.g., scalar field dark matter) components, and to characterize how the bosonic sector influences scalarization onset and stellar structure.

Method: Numerical solution of the coupled Einstein–Klein–Gordon–fluid equations for static, spherically symmetric configurations in the massless DEF scalar-tensor theory; systematic parameter scans over boson mass, central fermionic pressure, and central bosonic field amplitude; construction of mass-radius curves and identification of scalarization thresholds.

Result: Mixed fermion-boson stars exhibit spontaneous scalarization; diverse configurations emerge—including fermion-dominated stars with bosonic halos and boson-dominated cores embedded in fermionic envelopes; scalarization onset depends nontrivially on all three parameters, yielding a multi-dimensional critical surface in parameter space.

Conclusion: Spontaneous scalarization is viable and structurally rich in hybrid fermion-boson stars; the bosonic field acts as both a trigger and a modulator of scalarization, enabling new classes of compact objects with observational signatures distinct from pure neutron or boson stars.

Abstract: In the massless Damour-Esposito-Farèse scalar-tensor theory of gravity, we numerically construct static, spherically symmetric star models composed of a mixture of fermionic and bosonic matter, interacting purely gravitationally. The fermionic sector is modeled as a perfect fluid, while the bosonic sector is described by a massive, free, complex scalar field. We demonstrate that mixed fermion-boson stars can undergo spontaneous scalarization, and we systematically investigate the role played by the bosonic field across different regimes of bosonic mass, central fermionic pressure, and central bosonic amplitude. Our analysis reveals a complex interplay among the parameters, giving rise to a variety of scalarized configurations, ranging from fermionic matter embedded within extended bosonic halos to bosonic cores confined within fermionic matter. We examine sequences of solutions, in particular mass-radius surfaces, and we determine the critical coupling parameter for the onset of scalarization.

</details>


### [55] [Constructing likelihoods for a non-Gaussian gravitational-wave background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38765)
*Tore Boybeyi,Vuk Mandic,Joseph Romano*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reformulates stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) analysis from stellar-mass compact binaries using the characteristic function of multi-detector data, unifying existing non-Gaussian searches and enabling systematic construction of new approximate sufficient statistics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The stochastic gravitational-wave background from stellar-mass compact binaries is intrinsically non-Gaussian due to its discrete, finite-merger origin; standard Gaussian cross-correlation methods are suboptimal, necessitating a principled framework for optimal non-Gaussian detection.

Method: The authors recast the SGWB inference problem in terms of the characteristic function (Fourier transform of the probability density) of the joint detector data distribution; they derive how existing search statistics emerge as asymptotic or approximative limits of this characteristic function and construct new statistics via controlled truncations or expansions of it.

Result: A unified theoretical framework is established: (i) known non-Gaussian searches (e.g., hierarchical Bayesian, burst-like, or higher-moment methods) are shown to correspond to specific approximations of the characteristic function; (ii) new, tunable approximate sufficient statistics are explicitly constructed and validated on an exactly solvable toy model.

Conclusion: The characteristic function formalism provides a rigorous, flexible, and general foundation for designing optimal or near-optimal statistics for non-Gaussian astrophysical stochastic backgrounds—enabling systematic improvement over ad hoc approaches.

Abstract: The stochastic gravitational-wave background from stellar-mass compact binaries is non-Gaussian since it is made up of a finite number of individual mergers. Over the years, several methods have been developed to search for this background beyond the standard cross-correlation statistic. We reformulate the data analysis problem for such astrophysical backgrounds in terms of the characteristic function of the data in multiple detectors, and show that the existing searches arise as different limiting cases. We also show that new searches can be constructed through different approximations of the characteristic function, which gives a concrete way to engineer suitable approximate sufficient statistics. To illustrate this framework in the context of a simple example, we use the formalism proposed here to analyze an exactly solvable toy model.

</details>


### [56] [Unifying stochastic and deterministic signals: a practical search for the intermittent binary black-hole background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38766)
*Tore Boybeyi,Vuk Mandic,Joseph Romano*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel likelihood-based search method for detecting the intermittent gravitational-wave background from subthreshold binary black-hole mergers, unifying stochastic and deterministic approaches by leveraging higher-order cross-moment statistics and generalized overlap reduction functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The guaranteed but undetected intermittent gravitational-wave background from unresolved stellar-mass BBH mergers necessitates a more sensitive and information-rich detection framework beyond standard cross-correlation or template-based methods.

Method: The authors develop a truncated likelihood ratio fully marginalized over extrinsic parameters, representing two-detector data via approximate sufficient statistics—higher-order, geometry-weighted cross-moments of the data—that generalize the overlap reduction function (ORF); the method captures inter-frequency correlations induced by individual mergers.

Result: The proposed search is validated on one year of simulated Advanced LIGO data; it demonstrates that a catalog-informed analysis—incorporating both detected and unresolved mergers—can accurately infer the peak of the redshift distribution of the BBH merger population.

Conclusion: Standard likelihood methods ignoring inter-frequency correlations are suboptimal; the new framework, grounded in higher-order generalized ORFs, maximizes Bayesian evidence and enables population-level inference from the intermittent GW background.

Abstract: Over the past decade, the Advanced LIGO, Advanced Virgo, and KAGRA detectors have detected nearly 400 compact binary mergers, most of which are stellar-mass binary black-hole (BBH) mergers. Nevertheless, most mergers in the Universe are too weak to be individually detected at current sensitivity levels. Since a BBH merger in band is much shorter than the average time between successive mergers, the population of these subthreshold events gives rise to an intermittent background of gravitational waves (GWs). This GW background is guaranteed to exist, but it has not yet been detected with current stochastic or deterministic searches. Here we propose a fast and accurate likelihood-based search for this background, which unifies the standard model-agnostic cross-correlation search and a fully deterministic, template-based search in a single likelihood. Our search is based on a truncated likelihood ratio, which is fully marginalized over the extrinsic parameters of the individual mergers, and which represents the data in two detectors as an expansion in terms of approximate sufficient statistics. These statistics are higher-order cross-moments of the data, weighted by geometrical factors that generalize the standard overlap reduction function (ORF) used in cross-correlation searches. We show that almost all the Bayesian evidence and information on the BBH merger population comes from correlations between frequency bins induced by individual mergers and is captured in the higher-order generalized ORFs. Consequently, any likelihood function that does not take these inter-frequency correlations into account is necessarily suboptimal. We validate our search on one year of simulated Advanced LIGO data, and find that a catalog-informed analysis, which uses information from both the detected and unresolved mergers, can infer the peak of the redshift distribution of the population of BBH mergers.

</details>


### [57] [The General-Relativistic Limit of f(R) Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38795)
*Philippe G. LeFloch,Yue Ma*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves the convergence of f(R) gravity coupled to a massless scalar field to general relativity (Einstein-massless-scalar theory) in the small-data, near-Minkowski regime, establishing global existence, geodesic completeness, and uniform convergence of metrics, matter, and an effective curvature field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify general relativity as the low-energy/weak-curvature limit of modified gravity theories—specifically f(R) gravity—by proving convergence under physically relevant initial data conditions near Minkowski spacetime.

Method: Employs the Euclidean-hyperboloidal foliation method and adapted wave coordinates to analyze a quasilinear wave-Klein-Gordon system; handles non-null nonlinearities via oscillatory estimates, weighted interior/exterior energy bounds, and propagation estimates for deviation from the Einstein trace relation.

Result: Global existence and future causal geodesic completeness are established; physical/conformal metrics and scalar field converge uniformly (with derivatives) to the Einstein-massless-scalar solution; the rescaled effective field converges to the scalar curvature of the limiting spacetime.

Conclusion: The general-relativistic limit of f(R) + massless scalar is mathematically well-defined and stable for small, well-prepared asymptotically Euclidean data, resolving analytical challenges posed by diverging effective-field mass and high-frequency oscillations.

Abstract: We establish the general-relativistic limit of f(R) gravity coupled to a massless scalar field for small, asymptotically Euclidean, well-prepared Cauchy data near Minkowski spacetime. We prove global existence and future causal geodesic completeness, with uniform estimates for the metric, matter, and rescaled effective field as an admissible family of modified Lagrangians f(R) approaches the Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian R. The physical and conformal metrics and the matter field converge to the limiting Einstein-massless-scalar development, uniformly on the entire future spacetime, together with finitely many space and time derivatives. The effective field, defined by a nonlinear reparametrization of the physical scalar curvature, converges to the scalar curvature of the limiting Einstein development. The main analytical difficulty is that the effective-field mass tends to infinity, while its evolution equation becomes an algebraic trace relation. Small field amplitudes may coexist with high-frequency oscillations and comparatively large derivatives, whose quadratic contributions must be controlled uniformly. Using the Euclidean-hyperboloidal foliation method and adapted wave coordinates, we analyze a quasilinear wave-Klein-Gordon system whose nonlinearities do not satisfy the classical null condition. We combine oscillatory estimates, weighted interior and exterior energies, and propagation estimates for the discrepancy from the Einstein trace relation. Quantitative convergence on compact regions and uniform spacetime decay yield global convergence.

</details>


### [58] [Integrable singularities obstruct Cauchy horizons and mass inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38826)
*Francisco Tello-Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper challenges the universality of the Poisson-Israel-Ori mass-inflation mechanism—widely seen as dynamical evidence for strong cosmic censorship—by showing that static, spherically symmetric black-hole interiors with an integrable central density singularity (ρ ∼ A/r², A > 1/(8π)) and mild screening exhibit a strictly positive compactness gap (q = m/r > 1/2) throughout the trapped region, preventing Cauchy horizon formation and suppressing mass inflation via bounded coupling, finite blueshift, and controlled mass growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To examine whether the Poisson-Israel-Ori mass-inflation instability—and its role in enforcing strong cosmic censorship—is generic, or whether specific physically plausible interior matter models (e.g., anisotropic, integrable-singularity configurations) can structurally evade it.

Method: Analytical study of static spherically symmetric black-hole interiors with marginally divergent but integrable energy density; derivation and application of generalized Dray-'t Hooft-Redmount (DTR)-type relations for counter-streaming null dust; analysis of null geodesic behavior (blueshift, advanced time), monotonicity properties of 2m/r under NEC and screening, and Misner-Sharp mass evolution in generalized Vaidya and thin-shell Ori models.

Result: A strictly positive compactness gap (q − 1/2 > 0) persists throughout the trapped region, ensuring no inner (Cauchy) horizon forms; under NEC and screening, 2m/r is non-decreasing along both future null directions; blueshift is finite (not divergent); the DTR coupling factor 2/(r|f|) remains uniformly bounded; mass growth is a priori bounded and e-folding rate is luminosity-limited—not governed by inner-horizon surface gravity.

Conclusion: Integrable-singularity black-hole interiors violate the necessary geometric conditions for Poisson-Israel-Ori mass inflation, demonstrating that mass inflation is not inevitable and that strong cosmic censorship may not be dynamically enforced in this class of physically motivated models.

Abstract: Black-hole interiors with an inner (Cauchy) horizon are unstable through the Poisson-Israel-Ori mass-inflation mechanism, governed by the Dray-'t Hooft-Redmount relation and widely regarded as a dynamical realization of strong cosmic censorship. We show that static, spherically symmetric anisotropic interiors supported by a marginally divergent but integrable density $\rho\sim A/r^2$, with $A>1/(8\pi)$ and mild screening at finite radius, are incompatible with this mechanism. The compactness $q=m/r$ decreases monotonically from a super-critical central value to $1/2$ at the unique event horizon, producing a strictly positive compactness gap throughout the trapped region and excluding any second zero of the lapse. This gap enters the dynamics quantitatively. (i) Under the null energy condition and a dynamical screening condition, $2m/r$ is non-decreasing along both future null directions of a trapped domain, so the gap propagates under nonlinear spherically symmetric evolution. (ii) For ingoing radiation (generalized Vaidya), the gap bounds the blueshift: the advanced time spent in the gapped region is finite, whereas it diverges at a Cauchy horizon.
