(iii) For counter-streaming null dust, the radiative part $\mu$ of the Misner-Sharp mass obeys exactly $\partial_u\partial_v\mu=2\,\partial_u\mu\,\partial_v\mu/(r|f|)$, of which the DTR relation is the thin-shell form; the in-out coupling $2/(r|f|)$ is uniformly bounded on the interior precisely when the gap is positive. This yields an a priori mass bound and, in the Ori thin-shell model, an e-folding rate bounded by the influx luminosity instead of tending to the inner-horizon surface gravity. Integrable-singularity interiors thus realize a class of sources for which the Poisson-Israel-Ori amplification channel is structurally inoperative in the models in which mass inflation was originally established.

</details>


### [59] [Probing Scalar Polarization with Next-Generation Gravitational-Wave Detectors: The Roles of Detector Geometry and Source Inclination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38956)
*Sk Md Adil Imam,Macarena Lagos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper forecasts how next-generation gravitational-wave detectors (Cosmic Explorer and Einstein Telescope) will improve constraints on scalar polarizations in gravitational waves using binary neutron star mergers, showing that detector geometry and electromagnetic (EM) priors—especially on orbital inclination—significantly enhance sensitivity to modified gravity parameters (α, α_B, β) beyond current LIGO-Virgo capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test general relativity by constraining non-tensor (specifically scalar) gravitational-wave polarizations using multi-messenger observations of binary neutron star mergers.

Method: Bayesian parameter estimation forecasts using simulated binary neutron star injections in next-generation detectors (CE and ET), comparing constraints on PPE parameters (α, α_B, β) under varying assumptions: detector network configuration, sky position and distance priors from EM counterparts, and EM-informed orbital inclination (ι) priors; also examines impact of neutron star equation of state uncertainty.

Result: ET improves α_B constraint ≈2× over 2×CE due to triangular geometry; EM-based ι prior tightens α_B constraint by ≈56× versus isotropic prior for a GW170817-like event; all PPE parameters constrained 1–2 orders of magnitude better than O5 LIGO-Virgo.

Conclusion: Detector geometry and high-quality external EM information—particularly on inclination—are critical, complementary levers (alongside raw sensitivity) for robust scalar-polarization tests in the next decade.

Abstract: Gravitational-wave observations of compact binaries offer a direct way to test whether gravity admits polarizations beyond the two tensor modes of general relativity. We perform a forecast to assess how well future multi-messenger events will constrain the presence of scalar polarizations in gravitational waves. Performing binary neutron star injections, we forecast next-generation constraints on three modified gravity parameters in the PPE formalism: $\alpha$ (tensor amplitude), $\alpha_B$ (scalar amplitude) and $\beta$ (tensor and scalar phase). Cosmic Explorer (CE) and Einstein Telescope (ET) each tighten every parameter by 1--2 orders of magnitude over an O5 LIGO-Virgo network, with ET's triangular geometry giving a persistent $\approx2\times$ improvement over 2$\times$CE on the scalar-mode amplitude $\alpha_B$ in both a GW170817-like and a second, representative BNS. We show that, if the sky position and luminosity distance are known from EM observations, adding an EM-based prior on the orbital inclination $\iota$ narrows the $\alpha_B$ constraint by $\approx56\times$ relative to an isotropic inclination prior, for a GW170817-like event. We also assess how EM inclination priors as well as assumptions on the neutron star equation of state may affect the inference on the scalar amplitude $\alpha_B$. Overall, these results indicate that detector geometry and the availability of well-placed EM external information are both important levers --- alongside raw network sensitivity --- for next decade scalar-polarization tests.

</details>


### [60] [Early-time tails in Kerr black hole ringdown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39058)
*Ansh Gupta,Naritaka Oshita*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper quantifies the branch-cut contribution from the Green's function tail in black hole ringdown waveforms, showing it reaches ~10% of the quasinormal mode (QNM) amplitude at ringdown onset for Gaussian wave packets with ω₀ ∼ 1/M, with mild spin dependence; the tail itself is nearly spin-independent, while spin dependence arises mainly from QNM excitation—impacting spectroscopy accuracy near ringdown onset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the nonoscillatory branch-cut tail—often neglected in early-time ringdown analyses—significantly contaminates quasinormal mode (QNM) modeling and thus affects black hole spectroscopy accuracy.

Method: Within linear perturbation theory, the authors compute the scattering of localized Gaussian wave packets off a Kerr black hole (mass M), explicitly separating and comparing the contributions of quasinormal modes and the Green's function branch cut to the full waveform; they define ringdown onset as the earliest time where QNM + branch-cut reconstruction matches the full signal, and quantify relative amplitudes as functions of ω₀ and spin.

Result: For ω₀ ∼ 1/M, the branch-cut amplitude is ~10% of the QNM amplitude at ringdown onset, with only weak spin dependence; the unconvolved tail waveform is nearly spin-independent; spin dependence in the relative amplitude stems predominantly from spin-dependent QNM excitation factors.

Conclusion: The branch-cut contribution is non-negligible (~10%) at ringdown onset and may bias black hole spectroscopy, especially in early-time analyses; accurate modeling must include both QNMs and the branch-cut tail, particularly for precision tests of general relativity with gravitational-wave data.

Abstract: Black hole ringdowns are modelled as a superposition of quasinormal modes (QNMs), although the branch cut of the Green's function produces a nonoscillatory tail that may contaminate early-time ringdown analyses. Within linear perturbation theory, we quantify this branch-cut contribution for the scattering of localised Gaussian wave packets by a Kerr black hole with mass $M$. Defining the onset of ringdown as the earliest time at which the QNM and branch-cut contributions reconstruct the waveform, we evaluate the branch-cut amplitude relative to the QNM amplitude. For wave packets with a characteristic frequency $\omega_0\sim 1/M$, the branch-cut amplitude is typically of order $10\%$ of the QNM amplitude at ringdown onset with only a mild dependence on spin. We further find that the tail waveform before convolving with a source is almost independent of spin. The spin dependence of the relative amplitude therefore resides primarily in the excitation of the QNMs. Our results suggest that the branch-cut contribution may affect the accuracy of black hole spectroscopy, particularly near the onset of ringdown.

</details>


### [61] [Complete quasinormal modes of Type-D black holes. II. Extremal Kerr black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39095)
*Changkai Chen,Xiaohua Zhang,Zhoujian Cao,Jiliang Jing*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a double-confluent Heun (HeunD) method to compute quasinormal-mode (QNM) spectra of extremal Kerr black holes directly—without extrapolation—by matching horizon-ingoing and infinity-outgoing solutions while enforcing angular regularity. It reveals expanded QNM spectra on both conventional and unconventional Riemann sheets, traces Schwarzschild modes into extremality, identifies novel behavior near superradiant bounds (e.g., vanishing damping for m<0 Class I modes), and confirms spectral completeness and sheet assignments via scattering amplitudes and contour analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the numerical and analytical challenges in computing quasinormal modes (QNMs) of extremal Kerr black holes—where standard perturbative or extrapolation-based methods fail—and to unify extremal Kerr QNM physics with the previously established complete Schwarzschild spectrum.

Method: Develops a double-confluent Heun (HeunD) formulation of the radial Teukolsky equation for extremal Kerr; matches horizon-ingoing and infinity-outgoing solutions under angular regularity constraints; employs analytic continuation across frequency branch cuts and in spin parameter; uses scattering-amplitude calculations for cross-verification; applies contour integration to prove spectral completeness in bounded domains; performs uniform asymptotic matching near the superradiant bound to resolve Riemann-sheet structure and distinguish accumulation points from isolated modes.

Result: Direct computation of complete QNM spectra for extremal Kerr gravitational perturbations; discovery of additional QNMs on unconventional Riemann sheets; demonstration that unconventional Class I modes (m<0) approach negative superradiant bounds with zero damping at extremality; identification of finite-damping modes interspersed among zero-damping sequences; verification of mode sheet assignments and frequencies via independent scattering calculations; proof of spectral completeness within bounded frequency domains; resolution of Riemann-sheet topology and critical accumulation behavior near superradiant thresholds.

Conclusion: The HeunD method enables a rigorous, extrapolation-free treatment of extremal Kerr QNMs, integrating them into a unified black-hole spectroscopic framework; it reveals how Schwarzschild QNM families reorganize—splitting, migrating across Riemann sheets, and exhibiting diverse damping behaviors—in the extremal limit, fundamentally advancing the understanding of black-hole stability and wave dynamics at extremality.

Abstract: We develop a double-confluent Heun (HeunD) method that extends our construction of complete quasinormal-mode (QNM) spectra to extremal Kerr black holes. Matching the horizon-ingoing and infinity-outgoing radial solutions together with angular regularity yields the frequencies directly at extremality, without extrapolation from nonextremal spins. For gravitational perturbations, we obtain an expanded spectrum on both conventional and unconventional Riemann sheets, including additional QNMs revealed by analytic continuation across frequency branch cuts. Continuation in spin connects the extremal modes to our complete Schwarzschild spectrum, including its unconventional branches. We show for the first time that unconventional Class I sequences with $m<0$, continued from unconventional Schwarzschild modes, approach negative superradiant bounds with vanishing damping in the extremal limit. We also identify finite-damping modes interspersed among sequences approaching zero damping, showing that a single overtone cutoff cannot describe the transition to extremality. Independent scattering-amplitude calculations verify representative frequencies and their sheet assignments, while a contour count establishes spectral completeness within a bounded frequency domain. Uniform matching near the superradiant bound further distinguishes critical accumulation from isolated extremal QNMs and resolves the associated Riemann-sheet structure. These results bring extremal Kerr into our unified treatment of black-hole QNM spectra and reveal how the Schwarzschild mode families reorganize in the extremal limit.

</details>


### [62] [Gravitational lensing of gravitational waves by a Kerr black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39181)
*Jian-hua He*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper simulates gravitational wave (GW) lensing by a Kerr black hole using a novel (1+1)D time-domain mode-sum method with hyperboloidal foliation and field redefinition; it finds that strong-field lensing modifies the ringdown but not inspiral/merger waveforms, validating Schwarzschild approximations for current detector searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately model strong-field gravitational lensing of GWs by rotating (Kerr) black holes—critical for interpreting potential lensing signatures in ground-based GW observations—and to assess whether simpler Schwarzschild models suffice for detection pipelines.

Method: A (1+1)-dimensional time-domain mode-sum approach with localized hyperboloidal foliation and static field redefinition to ensure stable, bounded-speed evolution on a uniform tortoise grid; incident GWs are constructed via plane-wave Weyl tensor projection onto the Kinnersley tetrad, extended to arbitrary incidence/polarization using spin-dyad rotations and Wigner D-matrix transformations; evolution of 68,000 coupled (j,m) spin-weighted spherical harmonic modes.

Result: Kerr black holes act as diffraction-limited thick lenses, producing a spin-independent Gaussian forward beam (prompt pulse) and a spin-sensitive coda; for binary coalescence signals, lensing strongly alters the post-merger ringdown but leaves inspiral and merger phases nearly identical between Kerr and Schwarzschild cases—waveform mismatches <2.2×10⁻⁵ across inclinations and polarizations.

Conclusion: Non-spinning Schwarzschild lensing models are sufficiently accurate surrogates for detecting strong-field GW lensing with current ground-based detectors, offering significant computational savings without sacrificing fidelity in inspiral–merger recovery.

Abstract: We simulate the gravitational lensing of gravitational waves (GWs) by a Kerr black hole using a $(1+1)$-dimensional time-domain mode-sum approach. To enable efficient explicit time evolution, we implement a localized hyperboloidal foliation combined with a static field redefinition, ensuring bounded characteristic speeds across an extended, uniform tortoise-coordinate grid. Incident GW initial data are constructed by projecting a plane-wave Weyl tensor onto the Kinnersley tetrad. Through local spin-dyad rotations and Wigner $D$-matrix transformations, our framework accommodates both idealized wave packets and realistic binary coalescence signals at arbitrary incidence and polarization angles. To accurately resolve the lensed waveforms, we evolve over $68,000$ coupled $(j,m)$ modes. Our results reveal that a Kerr black hole acts as a diffraction-limited thick lens, focusing GWs into a forward beam. For idealized wave packets, the primary prompt pulse exhibits a spin-independent transverse Gaussian profile, whereas the subsequent lensing coda is sensitive to the black hole's spin. For binary coalescence signals, strong-field lensing substantially modifies the post-merger ringdown. However, the lensed inspiral and merger phases in Kerr and Schwarzschild backgrounds are virtually identical, yielding waveform mismatches below $2.2\times10^{-5}$ across various inclinations and polarizations. These findings demonstrate that non-spinning Schwarzschild models provide an accurate and computationally economical surrogate to search for strong-field lensing signatures with current ground-based detectors.

</details>


### [63] [Energy-flux noise induced by imperfections of the Gaussian beam in the Li-Baker HFGW detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39248)
*Lin-Lin Yang,Hao Yu,Pan-Pan Wang,Jin Li,Cheng-Gang Shao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes energy-flux noise in the Li-Baker high-frequency gravitational wave detector caused by Gaussian beam imperfections modeled as higher-order Laguerre-Gaussian modes; it finds cross-polarized LG modes dominate noise, imposes stringent power-fraction limits (e.g., σ₀₁ < 6.0×10⁻⁴⁹), and proposes a polarization filter to suppress noise while preserving signal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify and mitigate energy-flux noise induced by Gaussian beam imperfections in the Li-Baker high-frequency gravitational wave (HFGW) detector, which threatens detection sensitivity.

Method: Analytical derivation of noise-to-signal ratio using higher-order Laguerre-Gaussian (LGₙₗ) beams to model Gaussian beam imperfections; evaluation for both co- and cross-polarized modes at optimal detection plane (φ = π/2 or 3π/2); Monte Carlo simulations for multi-mode cumulative noise.

Result: Higher-order co-polarized LG modes produce zero tangential energy-flux noise at optimal plane; cross-polarized LG modes generate significant noise—individual LG₀₁ mode requires σ₀₁ < 6.0×10⁻⁴⁹, and total cross-polarized power fraction must stay below ~10⁻⁴⁹; polarization filtering is shown to be an effective mitigation strategy.

Conclusion: Cross-polarized higher-order LG beam components are the dominant source of energy-flux noise in the Li-Baker HFGW detector; stringent suppression of their power fractions—achievable via polarization filtering—is essential for viable high-sensitivity operation.

Abstract: This paper investigates the energy-flux noise induced by imperfections of the Gaussian beam (GB) in the Li-Baker high-frequency gravitational wave (HFGW) detector. Modeling these imperfections with higher-order Laguerre-Gaussian (LG) beams, we analytically derive the resulting noise-to-signal ratio for both co- and cross-polarized $\mathrm{LG}_{nl}$ modes. It is found that higher-order co-polarized LG beams generate no tangential energy-flux noise at the optimal detection plane ($\phi=\pi/2$ or $3\pi/2$), whereas higher-order cross-polarized LG beams introduce non-negligible noise. Specifically, for an individual higher-order cross-polarized $\mathrm{LG}_{nl}$ mode under our representative parameters, maintaining the noise-to-signal ratio below unity typically requires the power fraction to satisfy $\sigma_{nl}<10^{-46}$, with the $\mathrm{LG}_{01}$ mode imposing the most stringent constraint of $\sigma_{01}<6.0\times10^{-49}$. Monte Carlo simulations suggest that the total power fraction $\sigma_{\mathrm{total}}$ of multiple higher-order cross-polarized $\mathrm{LG}_{nl}$ modes needs to remain below $10^{-49}$. Consequently, we propose employing a polarization filter to retain only the co-polarized component of the GB adopted in the practical experiment, thereby suppressing the noise while preserving signal integrity. These results provide theoretical guidance for noise control in the Li-Baker HFGW detector.

</details>


### [64] [Scalar-induced gravitational waves in spatially covariant gravity with two tensorial degrees of freedom](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39252)
*Jia-Xi Feng,Yang Yu,Yan-Heng Yu,Xian Gao,Qing-Guo Huang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs) in a spatially covariant gravity model with a k-essence scalar field, showing that finite-σ effects and cubic extrinsic-curvature interactions modify the SIGW energy spectrum—even when tensor quadratic action matches GR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how modified scalar dynamics and higher-order interactions affect SIGW production in gravity theories where tensor propagation remains identical to GR at quadratic order.

Method: Derives scalar transfer functions for finite and large-|σ| limits; constructs SIGW source function; incorporates a cubic extrinsic-curvature interaction; computes fractional energy density Ω_GW for monochromatic and log-normal primordial power spectra.

Result: Finite-σ suppression of SIGW spectrum across broad scales; cubic interaction suppresses Ω_GW at small k and enhances it at large k in the σ → ∞ limit.

Conclusion: SIGW spectra are sensitive to scalar-sector modifications and non-linear interactions—even when tensor quadratic action is indistinguishable from GR—highlighting their diagnostic potential for early-universe physics beyond standard inflationary paradigms.

Abstract: We study scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs) in a spatially covariant gravity model with two tensorial degrees of freedom, minimally coupled to a $k$-essence scalar field. For the parameter choice considered during radiation domination, the quadratic tensor action coincides with that of general relativity (GR). We derive the scalar transfer functions at finite $\sigma\equiv C_2\bar N$ and in the large-$|\sigma|$ limit. We then construct the source function of SIGWs. We further examine a cubic extrinsic-curvature interaction that modifies the source function of SIGWs without affecting the background or quadratic actions. We calculate the fractional energy density of SIGWs for monochromatic and log-normal primordial curvature power spectra. We find that, for the positive values of $\sigma$ studied, finite-$\sigma$ effects suppress the spectrum over a broad range of scales relative to the result in the $\sigma \rightarrow \infty$ limit. In this limit, the cubic interaction suppresses the spectrum at small wavenumbers and enhances it at larger wavenumbers. These results show that modified scalar dynamics and non-linear interactions can alter the fractional energy density of SIGWs even when the quadratic tensor action is identical to that of GR.

</details>


### [65] [The seeds and silhouettes of optimal gravitational shielding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39262)
*Bruno Le Floch,Philippe G. LeFloch*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs vacuum initial data sets with prescribed asymptotically flat exterior geometry—including gluing two Schwarzschild exteriors with small (possibly negative) masses—allowing arbitrarily slow decay and nonzero second fundamental form; it introduces a relative ADM energy-momentum for slowly decaying data and enables free control of both relative and total ADM charges via harmonic or compact TT perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the theory of constructing vacuum initial data beyond harmonic decay rates, enabling flexible gluing of asymptotically flat geometries (e.g., Schwarzschild exteriors) while controlling physical charges such as ADM mass—even when standard ADM definitions fail due to slow decay.

Method: Constructs localized seed data with constraint errors decaying to arbitrary finite order; applies projection and stability theorems from companion papers; corrects seeds using harmonic terms (governed by Hamiltonian/momentum silhouettes) and super-harmonic corrections; defines and manipulates a relative ADM energy-momentum; adjusts seeds via harmonic-decaying or compact transverse-traceless (TT) terms to control charges.

Result: Existence of complete vacuum initial data sets matching prescribed asymptotically flat exteriors (including glued Schwarzschild regions); well-defined relative ADM energy-momentum for arbitrarily slowly decaying data; independent specification of total ADM mass distinct from input Schwarzschild parameters; constructive localization framework optimal beyond harmonic decay.

Conclusion: The work establishes a robust, constructive theory for generating vacuum initial data with tailored asymptotics and controllable global charges, overcoming limitations of standard ADM-based approaches and extending gluing and localization techniques to non-harmonic decay regimes.

Abstract: We construct complete vacuum initial data sets with prescribed asymptotically flat exterior geometry, including the gluing of two Schwarzschild exterior regions with small masses (which need not be positive). The construction allows for arbitrarily slow leading decay and nonzero second fundamental form. For leading terms with a fixed radial power, we first construct localized seed data sets whose constraint errors decay to any prescribed finite order, then apply the projection and stability theorems of our companion papers. The resulting vacuum data sets differ from seeds by harmonic terms controlled by Hamiltonian and momentum silhouettes, and by super-harmonic corrections. This difference of harmonic terms is measured by a relative notion of ADM energy-momentum, which is defined even for slowly-decaying data sets that do not admit an ADM energy-momentum. Upon adjusting seed data sets by either harmonic-decaying terms or compact transverse-traceless terms, we freely control this relative ADM charge and, when it exists, the total ADM charge. In particular, when gluing Schwarzschild metrics the total ADM mass can be specified independently of the Schwarzschild mass parameters. Together with our companion papers, these results establish a constructive theory of optimal localization beyond harmonic decay.

</details>


### [66] [Radial pulsations of gravitationally decoupled anisotropic self-bound stars with a generalized linear equation of state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39346)
*D. Santana,C. Usca,J. Andrade,J. O. Cedeño*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs anisotropic self-bound stellar models using gravitational decoupling in the minimal geometric deformation framework, imposes a generalized linear equation of state, obtains closed-form solutions for specific η values, and analyzes radial stability via fundamental modes and overtones—showing all configurations are linearly stable but with eigenvalue dependence on compactness and anisotropic response prescription.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct analytically tractable, self-bound anisotropic stellar configurations and investigate how different material prescriptions for anisotropy affect their linear radial stability—despite identical equilibrium geometries and matter content.

Method: Gravitational decoupling within the minimal geometric deformation (MGD) framework; Tolman IV temporal potential; generalized linear EoS $P_r = \eta(\rho - \rho_s)$; central regularity and Schwarzschild exterior matching to fix geometry; two distinct anisotropy evolution prescriptions under radial perturbations (fixed layer-wise anisotropy vs. fixed $\Delta/(\rho+P_r)$ ratio); full metric perturbation analysis yielding fundamental mode and three overtones.

Result: Closed-form solutions obtained for $\eta = 1, 1/2, 1/3, 1/4$ (including MIT bag model at $\eta=1/3$); all 24 perturbed configurations (12 backgrounds × 2 prescriptions) yield positive fundamental squared frequencies ($\omega_0^2 > 0$), confirming linear radial stability; $\omega_0^2$ decreases monotonically with compactness within each family; eigenvalue shifts due to prescription change depend on background; tangential tension and failure of tangential profile diagnostic limit physical viability of some models.

Conclusion: Identical anisotropic equilibrium stellar configurations can support distinct radial oscillation spectra depending on the assumed dynamical anisotropic response; analytical self-bound backgrounds are viable for stability studies; linear radial stability is generic under the considered closures, but physical interpretability requires additional constraints (e.g., absence of tangential tension, well-behaved tangential pressure).

Abstract: We construct anisotropic self-bound stellar configurations through gravitational decoupling in the minimal geometric deformation framework and investigate their stability against linear radial perturbations. Retaining the Tolman IV temporal potential, we impose the generalized linear equation of state $P_r=\eta(\rho-\rho_s)$ on the complete matter distribution. Central regularity and Schwarzschild matching determine the radial geometry and relate the surface density to the compactness and stellar radius. Closed-form solutions are obtained for $\eta=1,1/2,1/3,1/4$, including the MIT bag radial relation when $\eta=1/3$ and $\rho_s=4B$. Since the equilibrium anisotropy does not determine its dynamical response, we introduce two material prescriptions that preserve either the anisotropy of each fluid layer or its ratio to $\rho+P_r$. Including metric perturbations, we compute the fundamental mode and three overtones for twelve equilibrium configurations under both prescriptions. All 24 background response combinations have positive fundamental squared frequencies, establishing linear radial stability within the adopted closures. The fundamental eigenvalue decreases with compactness within each sampled family, while changing the anisotropic response produces shifts whose direction depends on the background. Tangential tensions in several configurations and the failed tangential profile diagnostic of the maximally stiff family limit their physical interpretation. These results provide analytically tractable self-bound backgrounds and demonstrate that identical equilibrium configurations can support distinct radial spectra depending on their anisotropic material response.

</details>


### [67] [Emergence of an acoustic black hole in a lattice model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39369)
*Antonino Flachi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an acoustic black hole in a lattice-Boltzmann model without imposing an acoustic metric a priori; it identifies and characterizes the emergent horizon via Mach profiling and phonon ray tracing, models horizon reflectivity and absorption to explain deviations from ideal draining bathtub behavior, and investigates superradiance—finding geometric amplification but no observed dynamical superradiance due to horizon absorption and lattice-scale effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize and characterize an acoustic black hole in a fully discrete, microscopic hydrodynamic model (lattice-Boltzmann) without assuming an effective spacetime metric, thereby probing how continuum analog gravity phenomena—including horizons, quasinormal modes, and superradiance—emerge from underlying lattice structure and dissipative dynamics.

Method: Numerical simulation of a draining vortex flow using a lattice-Boltzmann model with local collide-and-stream rules; extraction of the effective acoustic metric from steady-state flow fields; identification of the horizon via Mach number profiling and phonon ray tracing; modeling of horizon physics via two approaches—(1) reflective boundary on ideal draining bathtub metric (2 free parameters), and (2) absorbing sonic point in the local rest frame (no free parameters); spectral analysis of ringdown and superradiant scattering.

Result: A robust acoustic horizon emerges; ringdown frequencies approximate those of the ideal draining bathtub, with residual deviations attributable to cell-scale horizon structure; the absorption-based horizon model fits both horizon location and non-rotating QNM spectrum best; superradiant amplification is present in loss-free metric calculations, but horizon absorption suppresses observable dynamical superradiance; lattice induces fourfold anisotropy and sets surface gravity via discretization scale.

Conclusion: Acoustic black hole phenomenology—including horizons, quasinormal ringing, and geometric superradiance—can emerge from discrete, non-relativistic hydrodynamics without metric input, but lattice-scale structure fundamentally modifies horizon physics: it breaks rotational symmetry, determines surface gravity, and governs absorption—preventing clean observation of superradiance in this setup.

Abstract: We build an acoustic black hole in a lattice model: a draining vortex evolved by the local collide-and-stream rule of a lattice-Boltzmann model, with no acoustic metric imposed, the geometry being measured from the steady state. A horizon emerges, first located from the measured Mach profile and then confirmed by phonon ray tracing on the measured metric, and the ringdown after a perturbation follows approximately the quasinormal frequencies of the draining bathtub. The residual departures are traced to the cell-scale structure of the horizon, which is not an ideal absorber. We model it in two ways: as a reflective horizon on the ideal bathtub, with two free parameters (the modulus and phase of the reflectivity), and as the sonic point of the realised flow absorbing in its own frame, with no adjustable parameter. The second describes the horizon and the non-rotating spectrum best. Superradiance has two ingredients: the emergent geometry, which amplifies in a loss-free calculation on the measured metric, and the horizon, whose absorption frame sets the width and strength of the amplified band but leaves no trace in the ringdown. Both models are used to examine superradiance, which has not been observed in the present toy model: whether the lattice hole superradiates dynamically remains open. The lattice leaves its own imprint as well: a fourfold anisotropy of the sonic surface and a surface gravity set by the cell-scale structure of the horizon.

</details>


### [68] [Horizon Residues, Matter-Trace Winding and Test-Scalar Modes in f(Q,T) Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39370)
*Wen-Xiang Chen,M. Zeeshan Gul*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a specific magnetic solution in quadratic non-metricity gravity with a matter trace coupling, finding that the non-metricity scalar vanishes, the matter trace decays as r^{-8}, and the metric receives a sterile quartic-charge correction; it identifies topological winding behavior of the trace derivative and a degeneracy between the trace coupling λ and the non-linear electrodynamics parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the study of local horizon remnants to a specific f(Q) gravity model incorporating matter trace coupling (f(Q) = Q + αQ² + 16πλT), and to construct and characterize an exact magnetic solution where the matter trace is non-zero and its topological properties influence remnant structure.

Method: Constructs an exact magnetic solution in coincident Cartesian Kerr–Schild coordinates using polynomial non-linear electrodynamics; derives field equations, Euclidean temperature, horizon structure, master scalar equation, and gauge shift; analyzes winding numbers of the logarithmic derivative of the matter trace on closed contours around singularity and horizon.

Result: The magnetic branch has vanishing non-metricity scalar Q, matter trace ∝ r⁻⁸, metric gₜₜ = 1 − 2M/r + q²/r² + C/r⁶ with C = ξ(1 − 4λ)q⁴/10; quadratic non-metricity coupling α is sterile; trace logarithmic derivative has winding number −8 around singularity but 0 around horizon; temperature and scalar propagation remain continuous in λ; degeneracy exists between λ and the non-linear EM coefficient.

Conclusion: The trace winding number encodes divisor data—not dynamical backreaction—and does not override the physical role of the matter trace; the analysis reveals structural degeneracy and confirms the existence of a single-horizon remnant with continuous thermodynamic and perturbative behavior despite topological features of the trace.

Abstract: We extend the analysis of local horizon remnants in quadratic non-metricity gravity to a specific matter trace theory with the functional form f(Q) = Q + alpha Q^2 + 16 pi lambda T. Here, the matter trace is defined by contracting the energy-momentum tensor with the metric from the original matter action. In the absence of explicit lambda-corrections, the theory inherits the vacuum logarithmic-connection hairy branch. To obtain a non-zero trace, we introduce polynomial non-linear electrodynamics and construct an exact magnetic branch in coincident Cartesian Kerr-Schild coordinates. The non-metricity scalar of this branch vanishes, while its matter trace is proportional to the inverse eighth power of the distance. Its metric component function is given by 1 - 2M/r + q^2/r^2 + C/r^6, where the correction term C equals xi(1 - 4 lambda)q^4 / 10. Consequently, the quadratic non-metricity coupling is sterile on this branch. We derive the metric and matter equations, the Euclidean temperature, the single-horizon remnant, the master scalar equation, and the first-order gauge shift. The logarithmic derivative of the matter trace imparts a winding number of -8 on a closed contour enclosing the central singularity, but a winding number of zero on a sufficiently small horizon contour. Nevertheless, the temperature and scalar propagation still vary continuously with the correction coefficient. Therefore, the trace winding number classifies divisor data rather than superseding the matter trace or determining its backreaction. This analysis also demonstrates a degeneracy between the trace coupling coefficient and the non-linear electromagnetic coefficient. These results are specific to this particular branch; this paper does not expound upon the full coupled gravity spectrum, nor does it claim priority for this magnetic metric.

</details>


### [69] [Robustness-based selection of laser-locking architectures for time-delay interferometry in space-based gravitational-wave detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39432)
*Hao-Kang Chen,Pan-Pan Wang,Cheng-Gang Shao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a quantitative framework to evaluate the robustness of phase-reference architectures in space-based gravitational-wave detectors, using directed reference graphs and percolation theory; it shows that disturbance location, reference dependence, and support extent critically influence optimal architecture selection—beyond traditional criteria like reference depth or local locking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the vulnerability of phase-reference architectures to disturbances—where a single reference failure can degrade phase locking and time-delay interferometry (TDI)—and to establish robustness as a formal, quantifiable selection criterion alongside feasibility and performance.

Method: Develops a quantitative robustness model based on cascade equations and percolation measures; constructs directed reference graphs with Erdős–Rényi support layers; evaluates configurations under uniform and nonuniform disturbances, incorporating partial dependence and connected-support constraints; performs finite-network simulations to compare phase-locking degradation and TDI optical-input support requirements.

Result: Robustness ranking depends on reference-depth distribution under uniform disturbances, but under nonuniform or partial-dependence conditions, the connected-support criterion selects different optimal configurations than depth- or local-lock-based approaches; limiting accumulated degradation and prioritizing references with higher connected support improves phase-locking resilience; requiring connected support on *all* spacecraft (vs. at least one) yields distinct TDI architecture preferences.

Conclusion: Disturbance location, reference dependence structure, and required support extent are decisive factors in architecture selection; robustness—quantified via percolation and cascade models—should complement phase-locking feasibility and interferometric performance in prioritizing configurations for future space-based gravitational-wave observatories.

Abstract: Space-based gravitational-wave detectors compare laser phases exchanged between distant spacecraft. Offset phase locking keeps beatnotes in the phasemeter band, while time-delay interferometry suppresses laser frequency noise. A disturbance of one reference can degrade the functions that depend on it, making robustness an additional criterion for choosing a phase-reference architecture. We introduce a quantitative comparison based on established cascade equations and percolation measures, with directed reference graphs and Erdős--Rényi support layers. Under uniform disturbances, the robustness score is determined by the reference-depth distribution. Under nonuniform disturbances and partial dependence, the connected-support criterion can select a different configuration from both the published depth and local-lock criteria and a reference-path baseline. The phase-locking comparisons favor configurations that limit accumulated degradation and supply downstream lasers from references retaining greater connected support. Paired finite-network calculations support these differences in preference. In a separate comparison of optical-input support for time-delay interferometry, requiring connected support on all spacecraft gives a different preference from requiring it on at least one spacecraft. These findings identify disturbance location, reference dependence and the required extent of support as factors that can change architecture selection. Within the stated statistical model, robustness rankings can complement phase-locking feasibility and interferometric performance when prioritizing configurations for space-based gravitational-wave detectors.

</details>


### [70] [Quasinormal Modes of Slowly Rotating Black Holes in Hernquist Halos: Redshift and Angular Momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39548)
*Xing-Hui Feng,Bo Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies axial quasinormal modes of rotating black holes embedded in Hernquist dark matter halos, using a static analytic spacetime with an inner vacuum cavity and first-order frame-dragging corrections. It quantifies how halo compactness and mass ratio affect mode frequencies, identifies redshift and angular momentum partition as dominant physical drivers, and shows that fixing horizon angular momentum largely eliminates configuration-dependent variations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dark matter halos modify black hole quasinormal modes—crucial for gravitational-wave astrophysics—and to disentangle the effects of spacetime redshift, frame dragging, and angular momentum distribution in realistic galactic environments.

Method: Analytic perturbation approach: employs a static Hernquist-halo spacetime with inner vacuum cavity (r > 4M_BH), computes axial (odd-parity) quasinormal modes via master equation; includes frame dragging to first order in black hole spin; evaluates fundamental (n=0) and first overtone (n=1) ℓ=2 modes across mass ratios ε = 0.1, 0.01 and halo compactness C ∈ [10⁻⁴, 0.3]; compares against local Kerr estimates incorporating redshift rescaling, rotational splitting, and uniform frame-dragging shift.

Result: Cavity-induced redshift alone reproduces vacuum QNM frequencies with <0.04% (fundamental) and <0.31% (overtone) relative residuals at zero spin; weak-compactness behavior (1−C) explains insensitivity to ε in dilute halos; exact redshift accounts for most frequency separation between ε=0.1 and ε=0.01 sequences at high C; real spin coefficient exhibits opposite compactness trends for vanishing local angular momentum vs. vanishing coordinate angular velocity configurations, differing by ~38–54% at C=0.3—but differences drop below 1% when horizon angular momentum is held fixed.

Conclusion: Axial QNMs of black holes in Hernquist halos are dominantly governed by the cavity redshift and the horizon’s angular momentum—not global halo rotation profile—demonstrating that horizon-local quantities suffice to model mode shifts accurately, with implications for interpreting gravitational-wave ringdowns in dark-matter-rich environments.

Abstract: We investigate axial quasinormal modes of black holes embedded in Hernquist halos, using an established analytic static geometry with an inner vacuum cavity extending to $4M_{\mathrm{BH}}$ and solving for frame dragging to first order in rotation. We compute the fundamental and first overtone branches for mass ratios $\epsilon\equiv M_{\mathrm{BH}}/M_{\mathrm{halo}}$ of $0.1$ and $0.01$, and halo compactness $\mathcal C\equiv M_{\mathrm{halo}}/a_0$ in the range $10^{-4}\leq\mathcal C\leq0.3$. The cavity geometry gives a local Kerr estimate in which redshift rescales the vacuum frequencies, horizon angular momentum sets the rotational splitting, and uniform frame dragging adds a real frequency shift. At zero spin, the cavity redshift reproduces the complex $\ell=2$ frequencies with relative residuals below $0.04\%$ for the fundamental and $0.31\%$ for the first overtone. Its leading weak compactness behavior, $1-\mathcal C$, explains the weak mass ratio dependence in dilute halos; at larger compactness, the exact redshift accounts for most of the separation between the two mass ratio sequences. At fixed total angular momentum, halos with vanishing local angular momentum and those with vanishing coordinate angular velocity exhibit opposite compactness trends in the real spin coefficient. At $\mathcal C=0.3$, their real fundamental mode spin coefficients differ by approximately $38\%$ and $54\%$ for the two mass ratios. Fixing the horizon angular momentum reduces these differences below $1\%$ for both overtones, showing that angular momentum partition accounts for most of the configuration dependence.

</details>


### [71] [Quasinormal Mode Excitation in the Horizon Shear of Near-Extremal Kerr Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39729)
*Brennen Black*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies high-frequency 'wiggles' in the horizon shear of near-extremal Kerr black holes perturbed by eccentric extreme mass-ratio inspirals as damped Kerr quasinormal modes (QNMs), specifically the fundamental (2,1) and (3,2) modes, with supporting evidence for (3,1), (4,1), and (4,2); it rules out QNM excitation in m=0 and m=ℓ multipoles for ℓ≤4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous works could not link the observed post-periapsis 'wiggles' in horizon shear to orbital parameters or confirm their nature as quasinormal modes; this paper aims to resolve their physical origin and establish their connection to Kerr QNMs.

Method: Time-domain fitting of horizon shear signals from numerical relativity simulations of eccentric EMRIs around near-extremal Kerr black holes (a/M = 0.999–0.99999), with orbital parameters p = 3M, e = 0.85; multipole-resolved QNM identification and frequency comparison against theoretical Kerr QNM spectra.

Result: The 'wiggles' are identified as damped-mode branch Kerr QNMs: dominant (2,1) and subdominant (3,2) modes match theoretical frequencies within 2σ; (3,1), (4,1), (4,2) are detected in multipole-separated fits but not resolvable in full signal; no QNM excitation found for m = 0 or m = ℓ multipoles with ℓ ≤ 4.

Conclusion: The horizon shear wiggles are genuine Kerr quasinormal modes from the damped-mode branch, confirming their geometric–dynamical origin tied to black hole parameters—not artifacts—and implying identical features must appear in the driving tidal field.

Abstract: The horizon shear of a near-extremal Kerr black hole perturbed by an eccentric extreme mass ratio inspiral shows low amplitude, high frequency oscillations (''wiggles") after periapsis passage. Previous works are unable to tie the frequency of the wiggles to the orbit, and argue they cannot be quasinormal modes (QNMs). We show that they are Kerr QNMs belonging to the damped-mode branch --- that is, modes whose decay rate stays finite as the spin approaches extremality. For an equatorial prograde orbit with $p = 3M$ and $e = 0.85$ and spins ranging from $a/M = 0.999$ to $a/M = 0.99999$, fits to the time-domain shear identify the fundamental $(\ell,m) = (2,1)$ and $(3,2)$ modes as the dominant and first subdominant excitations. Fitting each multipole separately gives frequencies to within two standard deviations of the Kerr values and provides evidence for the excitation of the fundamental $(3,1)$, $(4,1)$ and $(4,2)$ modes, though these are not resolvable in the full signal. We further argue that the same wiggles must be present in the tidal field that drives the shear. For $\ell \leq 4$ we find no evidence of QNM excitation in the $m = 0$ or the $m = \ell$ multipoles.

</details>


### [72] [Topological Interpretations in Anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS Black Hole Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39732)
*Sreeshma A,Md Sabir Ali,C.L. Ahmed Rizwan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper applies Duan's φ-mapping theory and complex residue methods to analyze the thermodynamic topology of anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS black holes, revealing that AdS curvature changes global topological number (W), while anisotropy preserves the W = +1 class of Kerr-Newman-AdS; in contrast, asymptotically flat Kerr-Newman has W = 0 due to cancellation of ±1 defects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover universal, coordinate-independent features of black hole phase transitions via thermodynamic topology—leveraging the robustness of topological invariants against coordinate choices and parameter variations.

Method: Constructs a generalized off-shell free energy to define a thermodynamic vector field in parameter space; identifies equilibrium states as zero points of this field; computes winding numbers via Duan's φ-mapping theory and complex residue calculus; determines global topological number W as sum of winding numbers over all defects.

Result: Anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS and Kerr-Newman-AdS both yield single defect with winding number +1 → W = +1; Kerr-Newman yields two defects (+1 and −1) → W = 0; Kiselev-type black hole analysis shows thermodynamic topology reflects geometric reduction to charged solutions; complex residue method confirms defect structure for anisotropic case.

Conclusion: The global thermodynamic topology is sensitive to asymptotic structure (AdS vs. flat), not to anisotropic matter deformation; W serves as a topological invariant distinguishing black hole families, offering a universal diagnostic for phase structure and stability.

Abstract: The interplay between black hole thermodynamics and topology provides a strong, coordinate-independent framework for discovering the underlying structure of black hole phase transitions as well as their universal features. We explore the thermodynamic topology of the anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole using Duan's $\phi$-mapping theory and a complex residue approach. By creating the generalized off-shell free energy, we propose a thermodynamic vector field in the parameter space with zero points corresponding to equilibrium black hole states. The winding numbers associated with these defects are utilized to calculate the black hole's global topological number. We begin with an analysis of the entire anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole, followed by two limiting examples. Setting the anisotropic parameter $K = 0$ produces the Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole, while eliminating the AdS contribution produces the Kerr-Newman black hole. The anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS and Kerr-Newman-AdS black holes have a single thermodynamic defect with winding number $+1$, which results in $W = +1$. In contrast, the Kerr-Newman black hole has two defects with winding numbers $+1$ and $-1$, which result in a vanishing global topological number, $W = 0$. These findings show that the AdS contribution alters the global topological classification, but the anisotropic matter studied here preserves the topological class of the Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole. As an analogous limiting instance, we further study a Kiselev-type black hole, analyzing the circumstances in which its metric reduces to a charged black-hole geometry and how the thermodynamic topology reflects this reduction. Finally, we use the complex residue approach on the anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole.

</details>


### [73] [HWF Analysis of TRN Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39757)
*Ashfaque Ahmad,Sudhir Kumar Gupta,Abbas Ali*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the horizon wave-function formalism to charged, spinless quantum sources in (D+1)-dimensional spacetime, constructing HWFs for both inner and outer horizons of Tangherlini–Reissner–Nordström black holes; it computes formation probabilities P_BH^±, revealing strong suppression with increasing D and a sharp deviation of P_BH⁻/P_BH⁺ from unity away from extremality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the horizon wave-function formalism beyond Schwarzschild to charged, higher-dimensional black holes—specifically Tangherlini–Reissner–Nordström—and to quantify quantum gravitational effects on black hole formation probabilities as functions of charge, mass, and spacetime dimensionality.

Method: The authors construct horizon wave-functions for both inner and outer horizons of the TRN black hole in (D+1) dimensions using quantum mechanical smearing of a spherically symmetric, charged, spinless source; they compute the associated black hole formation probabilities P_BH^± by integrating squared modulus of HWFs over horizon radius, and analyze parametric dependencies—especially on charge parameter α, mass m, and spatial dimension D.

Result: P_BH^± are strongly suppressed for large D, especially when m ≲ Λ_D; the ratio P_BH⁻/P_BH⁺ deviates sharply from 1 as the source moves away from extremality (α < 1); e.g., for α = 0.5, P_BH⁺(D=5)/P_BH⁺(D=10) is significantly greater than 1.

Conclusion: Quantum black hole formation is highly sensitive to spacetime dimensionality and charge: higher D suppresses formation, while departure from extremality breaks inner–outer horizon probability symmetry—suggesting that quantum effects distinguish horizon structure more distinctly in non-extremal, higher-D regimes.

Abstract: We extend the horizon wave-function (HWF) formalism to a spherically symmetric, charged, spinless quantum source in $\mathcal{D} + 1$ dimensional spacetime geometry and construct the HWFs associated with inner- as well as outer-horizons of the Tangherlini--Reissner--Nordström (TRN) black hole. The corresponding black hole formation probabilities, $P_{\rm BH}^{\pm}$, are also calculated and their sensitivities due to various source parameters including charge, mass, and $\mathcal{D}$ are analysed as well. Our analysis reveals that the ratio of inner- to outer-horizon probabilities, $P_{\rm BH}^{-}/P_{\rm BH}^{+}$, changes drastically from being unity as the source departs away from being in the extremal limit defined by $\alpha$ ($\to 1$ for extremal case). Furthermore, black hole formation probabilities are found to be highly suppressed for higher values of $\mathcal{D}$, particularly for the source mass, $m \lesssim \Lambda_{\mathcal{D}}$, e.g., for $\alpha = 0.5$, the ratio, $P_{\rm BH}^{+}(\mathcal{D} = 5)/P_{\rm BH}^{+}(\mathcal{D}=10)$ turn out to be approximately associated with $\mathcal{D}$ spatial-dimensions.

</details>


### [74] [Looking for gravitational waves with orbital dynamics: GUEST mission and the $μ$Hz band of Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39881)
*Diego Blas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reviews how gravitationally bound systems serve as resonant detectors for microhertz gravitational waves and ultra-light dark matter, and introduces GUEST—a proposed ESA F-class mission using laser-tracked eccentric Earth-orbiting retroreflector spheres to probe the unexplored microhertz band.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the underutilized microhertz gravitational-wave band—bridging pulsar timing arrays and LISA—which may contain extensions of recently detected PTAs signals, early-Universe relics, primordial black holes, or ultra-light dark matter.

Method: Analyzes orbital resonances of gravitationally bound systems as natural detectors; proposes GUEST, a mission concept involving two dense passive retroreflector spheres in highly eccentric Earth orbits, monitored via global satellite laser ranging.

Result: Demonstrates theoretical feasibility of using orbital dynamics as microhertz GW/ULDM probes; outlines GUEST’s design, sensitivity projections, and scientific potential for multi-messenger gravitational-wave astronomy.

Conclusion: The microhertz band is a compelling frontier for fundamental physics; GUEST offers a cost-effective, near-term pathway to access it using established laser-ranging infrastructure and resonant orbital detection principles.

Abstract: `Adding colour'' to the gravitational-wave sky by probing unexplored frequency bands has the potential to unveil new and unexpected phenomena in the Universe. The microhertz band, between pulsar timing arrays and LISA, is a particularly attractive target: it should host the extension of the signal recently found by pulsar timing arrays (if it is generated by a family of sources of gravitational waves), and it may harbour signals from the early Universe, primordial black holes or dark matter. In these proceedings, we review how the orbits of gravitationally bound systems act as resonant detectors of microhertz gravitational waves and ultra-light dark matter. We then describe GUEST (Gravitational Universe Exploration with Satellite Tracking), a mission concept recently proposed to the ESA F-class call, consisting of two dense passive spheres covered with retroreflectors, placed in highly eccentric Earth orbits and tracked by a global laser-ranging network.

</details>


### [75] [Self-Bound Anisotropic Stars in $\mathcal{R}+α\mathcal{R}^2$ Gravity with a Hayward Exterior: Junction Conditions and NICER Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39916)
*Furqan Nazeer,Tooba Feroze*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs analytic compact-star models in quadratic f(R) gravity (R + αR²) using a Durgapal-Bannerji interior satisfying the Karmarkar condition and a Hayward exterior, deriving fully determined junction conditions without relying on GR assumptions; it finds that surface physics requires a thin shell with negative energy density for α > 0, constrains α ≲ 0.2 km² and ℓ ≲ 0.3M to keep radial stress sub-1% of central pressure, reproduces four pulsars with a universal surface energy density, predicts R₁.₄ = 11.5 km and M_max = 2.9 M⊙ for α = 0.1 km², and shows results are robust under Schwarzschild approximation due to negligible ℓ-dependence in observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To avoid ad hoc borrowing of GR junction conditions and radius-dependent constant fixing in analytic compact-star models within f(R) gravity—specifically, to self-consistently determine all parameters using the intrinsic junction formalism of quadratic f(R) gravity while exploring physical consequences of non-Schwarzschild exteriors (Hayward) and embedding-class-one interiors.

Method: Implements exact analytic matching between a Durgapal-Bannerji interior metric (satisfying Karmarkar condition, i.e., embedding class one) and a Hayward exterior metric in f(R) = R + αR² gravity; derives full junction conditions from the Lanczos equations generalized to quadratic f(R), enforcing continuity of the trace of extrinsic curvature (replacing p_r(R)=0); computes surface stress-energy tensor (including delta-function and double-layer terms) and imposes physical viability constraints (e.g., radial stress < 1% of central pressure); calibrates model against observed neutron star masses and radii (PSR J0740+6620, etc.) and compares with strange-star X-ray pulse-profile constraints.

Result: Junction conditions fix all integration constants in closed form via M, R, and ℓ; Ricci scalar discontinuity induces a gravitational double layer with negative surface energy density for α > 0; radial stress remains physically acceptable only if α ≲ 0.2 km² and ℓ ≲ 0.3M; a single surface energy density ρ_s ≈ 1.37ρ₀ fits four NICER/PSR mass-radius measurements; for α = 0.1 km², predicted canonical radius is R₁.₄ = 11.5 km and maximum mass is 2.9 M⊙; Hayward deformation enters observables only at O[(ℓ/M)²(M/R)³], so Schwarzschild limit is effectively recovered; strange-star calibration yields R₁.₄ ≈ 9 km, ruled out by current data.

Conclusion: Self-consistent analytic modeling in quadratic f(R) gravity—without importing GR junction conditions—reveals unavoidable thin-shell physics at the stellar surface, tightly constraining the theory’s parameter space; the model successfully describes observed massive pulsars as self-bound stars, supports R₁.₄ ~ 11–12 km, and disfavors low-radius strange-star interpretations, reinforcing hadronic or hybrid EOS paradigms within this modified gravity framework.

Abstract: Analytic compact-star models in modified gravity usually borrow the junction conditions of general relativity and fix their constants with radii derived for a particular stellar model. Here we avoid both. We study $\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{R})=\mathcal{R}+\alpha\mathcal{R}^{2}$ gravity with a Durgapal-Bannerji interior of embedding class one (Karmarkar condition) and a Hayward exterior. Without Birkhoff's theorem, the exterior is an assumption, and we measure its effect with the Hayward metric, which deforms Schwarzschild by a length $\ell$. The junction conditions of quadratic $\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{R})$ gravity fix all constants in closed form in terms of $M$, $R$ and $\ell$. The condition $p_r(R)=0$ is replaced by continuity of the trace of the extrinsic curvature. The Ricci scalar jumps at the surface, so there is a thin shell with negative surface energy density for $\alpha>0$, a gravitational double layer, and a small radial stress. For this stress to be below 1% of the central pressure we need $\alpha\lesssim0.2$ km$^{2}$ and $\ell\lesssim0.3M$. The interior behaves as a self-bound star. A single surface energy density, $\rho_s=(1.37\pm0.16)\rho_0$, reproduces PSR J0740+6620, J0437-4715, J0030+0451 and J0614-3329, and for $\alpha=0.1$ km$^{2}$ the model predicts $R_{1.4}=11.5$ km and a maximum mass of $2.9 M_\odot$. Because $\ell$ enters surface observables only at order $(\ell/M)^{2}(M/R)^{3}$, the results carry over to a Schwarzschild exterior. Calibrating instead with strange-star radii of eclipsing X-ray pulsars gives $R_{1.4}\approx9$ km, which current data rule out.

</details>


### [76] [Diamonds in the sky: the shape of gravitational waves from cosmic string cusps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39983)
*Pierre Auclair,Chams Gharib,Danièle Steer*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper revises the gravitational wave emission model from cosmic string cusps, revealing a diamond-shaped angular distribution and two off-axis maxima—departing from the traditional conical, velocity-aligned emission assumed by Damour & Vilenkin. It identifies two enhancement mechanisms (diamond stretching and higher-derivative cancellations) and estimates their statistical relevance for odd-harmonic loops.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the accuracy of gravitational wave predictions from cosmic string cusps beyond the Damour–Vilenkin approximation, which assumes conical emission aligned with cusp velocity—despite known limitations in modeling relativistic, curved string dynamics.

Method: Analytical relativistic string theory applied to cusp kinematics; geometric analysis of GW angular radiation patterns; identification and classification of emission enhancements via curvature and derivative conditions at the cusp; statistical estimation over ensembles of odd-harmonic string loops.

Result: GW emission from a cusp forms a diamond-shaped angular pattern—not a cone—and peaks in two directions slightly offset from the cusp’s instantaneous velocity vector; two limiting configurations—(i) anisotropic diamond stretching and (ii) vanishing higher-order derivatives at the cusp—significantly enhance total radiated power; these effects are quantitatively non-negligible in realistic odd-harmonic loop populations.

Conclusion: The standard Damour–Vilenkin cusp emission model is incomplete; incorporating the diamond geometry and off-axis maxima is essential for accurate GW detection forecasts and interpretation, especially for pulsar timing arrays and space-based interferometers targeting nanohertz–millihertz bands.

Abstract: We carry an in-depth analysis of the gravitational wave (GW) emission of cosmic string cusps, going beyond the seminal work of Damour and Vilenkin. We identify two features that, to the best of our knowledge, have not been described in the literature: first the GWs emission from a cusp, at any given frequency, is not concentrated inside a cone but instead draws a diamond-like shape in the sky. Second, the emission is not maximal in the direction of the cusp velocity, but in two slightly offset directions. We explain these effects in detail, and illustrate them with simplified examples. Then, we study two limiting cases for which the overall radiated power is enhanced: when the diamond is stretched in a particular direction, and when higher order derivatives vanish at the cusp. We statistically estimate the importance of these effects in a population of odd-harmonic loops.

</details>


### [77] [Inverse Gertsenshteǐn-Zel'dovich Effect in Time-Independent Electromagnetic Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40024)
*Rajendra Prasad Bhatt,Rudraksh Verma,R. Prasad,Sudipta Sarkar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a general formalism to compute electromagnetic fields induced by gravitational waves passing through spatially varying, time-independent background electromagnetic fields—extending the inverse Gertsenshteǐn-Zel'dovich effect beyond constant fields—and applies it to astrophysical magnetic environments like neutron stars and magnetars, with implications for multi-messenger astronomy and high-frequency GW detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the inverse Gertsenshteǐn-Zel'dovich effect beyond constant background electromagnetic fields, enabling realistic modeling of gravitational-wave–induced electromagnetic signals in astrophysical settings with spatially varying fields (e.g., neutron star magnetospheres).

Method: Develops a covariant, mode-resolved formalism for computing induced electric and magnetic field components from gravitational waves of all six polarization modes interacting with static but spatially inhomogeneous electromagnetic backgrounds; applies it analytically and semi-analytically to representative field configurations.

Result: Derives explicit expressions for induced EM fields in multiple configurations—including uniform, dipolar, and exponentially decaying magnetic fields—and quantifies signal strengths and frequency dependencies relevant to neutron stars and magnetars.

Conclusion: The extended formalism enables quantitative predictions of electromagnetic counterparts to gravitational waves in realistic astrophysical magnetic environments, supporting the design of novel high-frequency GW detectors and enhancing multi-messenger interpretation of transient high-energy EM signals.

Abstract: The inverse Gertsenshteǐn-Zel'dovich effect describes the conversion of gravitational waves into electromagnetic waves in the presence of a background electromagnetic field. This effect has primarily been studied for constant background electromagnetic fields. In this work, we develop a general formalism for calculating the components of the electric and magnetic fields induced by the passage of a gravitational wave through a spatially varying, time-independent background electromagnetic field. The formalism accommodates all six possible polarization modes of gravitational waves. We illustrate its applicability through several examples, including gravitational waves propagating through constant and spatially varying electromagnetic fields. We further investigate the electromagnetic signals generated by the passage of gravitational waves through astrophysical magnetic fields, particularly those associated with neutron stars and magnetars. By connecting gravitational waves and electromagnetic waves--two essential messengers of multi-messenger astronomy--the inverse Gertsenshteǐn-Zel'dovich effect offers a promising framework for both gravitational-wave detection and astrophysical observations. The formalism developed here may therefore find applications in the design of high-frequency gravitational-wave detectors and in the investigation of high-energy astrophysical electromagnetic signals.

</details>


### [78] [Curvature scaling and linear area response in charged black-hole interiors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40043)
*Fu-Guo Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes scaling laws governing curvature divergence in charged black-hole interiors, identifying mass inflation and electric corrections as key mechanisms; it establishes bounds on the Kretschmann scalar K, validates an r⁻⁶ scaling regime under slow-varying conditions, proves a strict r⁻⁸ lower bound from charge, and derives critical scaling relations (e.g., K/m² ∝ (A* − A)⁻³) near threshold behavior, confirmed numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the physical mechanisms and domain of validity for observed scaling laws in the interior curvature of charged black holes—particularly the r⁻⁶ scaling of the Kretschmann scalar K and its interplay with charge-induced corrections—within classical general relativity and near Planckian regimes.

Method: Analytical asymptotic analysis of spherically symmetric Einstein-Maxwell-scalar equations; derivation of exact curvature bounds; perturbative treatment of small scalar and electric corrections; identification of slow-variation conditions for local scaling exponents; numerical full evolutions to test predicted scalings (half-power law, inverse-cube law, electric correction onset).

Result: 1) Confirmed K ≃ 48m²/r⁶ under slow-varying mass and curvature corrections; 2) Proved rigorous lower bound K ≥ 28q⁴/(5r⁸), forbidding finite-coefficient r⁻⁶ asymptotes; 3) Electric corrections delayed to r ∼ q²/m for large plateau mass m, enabling Planckian curvature before backreaction; 4) Derived ρ = r² ∝ A¹/² near simple zero of area, yielding K/m² ∝ (A* − A)⁻³ at threshold; 5) Numerical evolutions validate electric correction scaling, half-power law at multiple times, and joint inverse-cube scaling.

Conclusion: The r⁻⁶ scaling of K is an effective, transient regime governed by mass inflation and slow variation—not a fundamental asymptote—and is superseded by charge-dominated r⁻⁸ behavior at sufficiently small r; however, large plateau masses extend the classical r⁻⁶ regime into Planck-scale curvature, suggesting potential relevance for quantum gravity modeling; the derived critical scalings confirm universality in black-hole interior dynamics near singular thresholds.

Abstract: We identify the mechanisms and range of validity of two scaling laws reported in charged black-hole interiors. In spherical evolutions with a neutral massless scalar, mass inflation produces a large internal mass $m$, followed by a plateau. Small scalar and electric corrections give $K\simeq48m^2/r^6$, where $r$ is the areal radius and $K$ the Kretschmann scalar. The local exponent is near six when the mass and relative curvature correction also vary slowly with radius. The exact bound $K\geq28q^4/(5r^8)$, with $q$ the charge, excludes a finite-coefficient $r^{-6}$ asymptote, while a large plateau mass delays electric corrections to $r\sim q^2/m$. For sufficiently large plateau mass, this classical regime can reach Planckian curvature before electric corrections become significant. At fixed coordinates and initial gauge, a linear response of $\rho=r^2$ to the initial scalar amplitude $A$ gives the half-power radius law near a simple zero. Where both mechanisms apply, $K/m^2\propto(A_*-A)^{-3}$, with $A_*$ the area zero. Full evolutions confirm the electric correction, support the half-power law at several observation times, and test the joint inverse-cube law.

</details>


### [79] [Photon Rings and Mass-Independent Observables of a Five-Dimensional Charged AdS Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40069)
*Amrillokhon Mamatkhonov,Javokhir Sharipov,Tursunali Xamidov,Chen Zhou,Sanjar Shaymatov,Chengxun Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies optical properties of 5D charged AdS black holes, deriving mass-independent observables—especially the subring exponent γ—to break degeneracies in shadow measurements; γ is analytically obtained, AdS-radius-independent, and significantly larger than in 4D Schwarzschild/Reissner–Nordström cases; MCMC forecasts show γ enables high-confidence (3.4–5.9σ) discrimination from 4D Schwarzschild, while charge detection is only feasible for near-extremal cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome observer- and mass-scale dependence of black hole shadow observables in AdS spacetimes, and to identify robust, dimensionless geometric quantities that can distinguish higher-dimensional or charged black holes from their 4D counterparts using future very-long-baseline interferometric observations.

Method: Solves null geodesic equations in 5D charged AdS black hole spacetime to obtain event horizon, photon sphere radius, and critical impact parameter; introduces two mass-independent observables—the subring exponent γ and the ratio of critical impact parameter to that of the ISCO direct image; derives γ in closed form; performs MCMC forecast analysis on noiseless mock data with 5% fractional uncertainty to assess parameter estimation precision.

Result: Photon sphere radius is independent of AdS radius; event horizon and critical impact parameter decrease with charge; γ is analytically derived, satisfies 2π/√3 < γ ≤ π√2, and is AdS-radius-independent; MCMC forecasts yield relative uncertainty of 16–19% on dimensionless AdS radius, and 3.4–5.9σ Gaussian separation of γ from 4D Schwarzschild value; charge is only distinguishable from zero for near-extremal models.

Conclusion: The subring exponent γ serves as a powerful, model-discriminative, mass- and AdS-radius-independent observable for testing higher-dimensional and charged black hole geometries via black hole shadow substructure; its measurable deviation from 4D predictions provides a clean signature for new physics beyond standard 4D general relativity.

Abstract: We investigate the optical properties of a five-dimensional charged anti-de Sitter (AdS) black hole. Using the null geodesic equations, we determine the event horizon and photon sphere radii and the critical impact parameter, showing that all decrease with increasing charge, while the photon sphere radius remains independent of the AdS radius. To avoid dependence on the mass scale and the observer-dependent apparent size of the shadow in an AdS spacetime, we introduce two mass-independent geometric observables: the subring exponent $\gamma$ and the ratio of the critical impact parameter to that of the direct image of the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO). We derive $\gamma$ in closed form and show that it is independent of the AdS radius and satisfies $2\pi/\sqrt{3}<\gamma\leq\pi\sqrt{2}$, above the values for four-dimensional Schwarzschild and Reissner--Nordström black holes. Using a Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) forecast based on noiseless mock data with illustrative fractional uncertainties of $5\%$, we find that the dimensionless AdS radius can be constrained with a relative uncertainty of $16$--$19\%$, whereas the charge can be distinguished from zero only for the near-extremal fiducial model considered. Under these assumptions, the subring exponent provides a Gaussian separation of $3.4$--$5.9\sigma$ from the four-dimensional Schwarzschild black hole.

</details>


### [80] [Disentangling Spin-induced Precession vs. Eccentricity in GW190521 and GW231123](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40077)
*Patricia McMillin,Giuseppe Ficarra,Carlos O. Lousto,Richard O'Shaughnessy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses >2000 numerical-relativity waveforms from the RIT catalog and the RIFT Bayesian inference method to reanalyze GW190521 and GW231123, finding strong evidence for spin precession in both, significant eccentricity only in GW190521 (e₂₀ = 0.207⁺⁰·¹⁹²₋₀·₁₄₇), and negligible eccentricity in GW231123; it demonstrates that eccentricity and precession are distinguishable in current LIGO/Virgo data, and reports high recoil velocities (3965 km/s and 2059 km/s) from top-likelihood NR simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle and quantify the contributions of orbital eccentricity and spin precession in gravitational-wave signals—two degenerate physical effects that can mimic each other in parameter estimation—and assess whether current detectors can discriminate between them using state-of-the-art waveform models.

Method: Bayesian parameter estimation via the Rapid Iterative Fitting Technique (RIFT), using the full suite of >2000 numerical-relativity (NR) waveforms from the RIT Binary Black Hole Catalog v5; analyses include simultaneous eccentric + precessing waveforms, as well as control runs with non-precessing (eccentric and quasi-circular) models; posterior sampling and likelihood comparison performed for GW190521 and GW231123.

Result: GW190521: highest likelihood for eccentric + precessing model, with e₂₀ = 0.207⁺⁰·¹⁹²₋₀·₁₄₇ and χ₁,⊥ = 0.717⁺⁰·¹⁹⁴₋₀·₄₀₆; GW231123: strong preference for precession (χ₁,⊥ = 0.678⁺⁰·²¹⁸₋₀·₄₆₂) but negligible eccentricity (e₁₀ ≈ 0.097, e₂₀ ≈ 0.056); non-precessing models yield lower likelihoods in both cases; top-likelihood NR simulations predict recoil velocities of 3965 km/s (GW190521) and 2059 km/s (GW231123).

Conclusion: Current gravitational-wave observations—when analyzed with sufficiently rich, physically faithful NR-informed waveform models—can robustly distinguish spin precession from orbital eccentricity; GW190521 remains the strongest candidate for measurable eccentricity in the LIGO/Virgo band, while GW231123 exemplifies a highly precessing but effectively circular merger; this validates the use of multi-physics NR catalogs for breaking degeneracies in source characterization.

Abstract: We use the more than 2000 full numerical-relativity simulations included in the fifth release of the RIT binary-black-hole waveform catalog to perform Bayesian parameter estimation, via the rapid iterative fitting algorithm (RIFT), for the gravitational-wave events GW190521 and GW231123. We revisit GW190521, which has previously been identified as potentially possessing large eccentricity upon entering the detector band at $20~\mathrm{Hz}$, and confirm this finding: the highest likelihood is obtained from waveforms that are simultaneously eccentric and spin-precessing, yielding $e_{20} =0.207_{-0.147}^{+0.192}$ and $\chi_{1,\perp}=0.717_{-0.406}^{+0.194}$. Applying the same simulation pool to GW231123, we likewise find a preference for spin precession where at 20 Hz $\chi_{1,\perp}=0.678_{-0.462}^{+0.218}$, but in this case with negligible eccentricity at both $10~\mathrm{Hz}$ and $20~\mathrm{Hz}$, with $e_{10}=0.097_{-0.073}^{+0.124}$ and $e_{20}=0.056_{-0.045}^{+0.059}$. For comparison, both events are also analyzed using exclusively non-precessing (eccentric and quasi-circular) simulations, which yield lower likelihoods in each case. These results indicate that our approach is capable of discriminating between the effects of eccentricity and spin precession in current gravitational-wave signals. The singled out top peak likelihood NR simulations give estimates of the recoil velocities of the merged final holes of 3965km/s for GW190521, and of 2059km/s for GW231123.

</details>


### [81] [Nonminimal Scalar-curvature Coupling Noncanonical Warm Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40159)
*Zhi-Peng Peng,Shi-Chao Zhang,Xiao-Pei Xu,Ruan-Jing Zhang,Xiao-Min Zhang,Xi-Bin Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a noncanonical warm inflation model with nonminimal inflaton-curvature coupling in the Jordan frame, showing it enables slow-roll inflation for steeper potentials, yields nearly scale-invariant scalar spectra, suppresses r via combined nonminimal coupling, noncanonicality, and thermal dissipation, and supports warm inflation with sub-Planckian field excursions even in weak dissipation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
