Motivation: To extend conventional warm inflation by incorporating nonminimal curvature coupling and noncanonical kinetics, thereby relaxing standard slow-roll requirements (e.g., flat potentials, super-Planckian field excursions) and improving compatibility with observational constraints—particularly the tensor-to-scalar ratio.

Method: Derives modified background equations and slow-roll conditions in the Jordan frame via linear stability analysis; employs stochastic formalism to obtain the Langevin equation for inflaton fluctuations; computes primordial scalar and tensor power spectra including thermal dissipation effects.

Result: Nonminimal coupling enhances effective damping and modifies potential gradient, enabling slow-roll for steeper potentials; scalar spectrum is nearly scale-invariant; tensor-to-scalar ratio r is further suppressed by combined nonminimal coupling, noncanonical dynamics, and thermal dissipation; warm inflation is sustained even in weak-dissipation regime with sub-Planckian field excursions.

Conclusion: The noncanonical warm inflation model with nonminimal curvature coupling is a viable and flexible extension of standard warm inflation, alleviating fine-tuning issues and broadening phenomenological consistency with CMB and large-scale structure observations.

Abstract: We investigate a noncanonical warm inflationary model with a nonminimal coupling between the inflaton field and scalar curvature. Within the Jordan frame, we derive the modified background equations and establish the slow-roll conditions through a linear stability analysis. We find that the nonminimal coupling enhances the effective damping and modifies the potential gradient, facilitating slow-roll evolution even for relatively steep potentials. Using the stochastic approach, we derive the Langevin equation for inflaton fluctuations and obtain the primordial scalar and tensor power spectra. Our results show that the scalar spectrum is nearly scale-invariant, while the combined effects of nonminimal coupling, noncanonical dynamics, and thermal dissipation can further suppress the tensor-to-scalar ratio. Moreover, the model can sustain warm inflation even in the weak-dissipation regime and accommodate sub-Planckian field excursions in suitable parameter regions. These findings highlight the potential of this framework as a viable extension of conventional warm inflation.

</details>


### [82] [Rotation overcharges vectorized black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40179)
*Shengjun Guo,Guangzai Ye,Peng Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs the first rotating black hole solutions in the Einstein-Maxwell-Vector (EMV) model with nonminimal coupling, showing that vector hair can push black holes beyond the Kerr-Newman extremality bound (q > 1), with observable shadow ellipticity enhancements up to 25.6%, unattainable by standard charged or rotating black holes alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the 'charge ceiling' (extremality bound) for black holes is fundamentally imposed by vector hair or merely an artifact of spherical symmetry — since prior spherically symmetric vectorized black holes were strictly undercharged and their hair's mass was hidden due to Komar mass vanishing and degeneracy of the identity M_V = 2Ω_H J_V.

Method: Constructs the first rotating (axisymmetric, stationary) black hole solutions in the EMV model via numerical integration of the full coupled Einstein–Maxwell–vector field equations; leverages rotation to break spherical degeneracies, enabling unambiguous identification and quantification of vector hair’s mass and angular momentum; analyzes domain of existence, extremality violation, and shadow morphology using ray-tracing.

Result: Rotation lifts degeneracies: vector hair mass M_V is fixed by the invariant relation M_V = 2Ω_H J_V (nontrivially nonzero). Above a critical coupling strength, black holes exceed the Kerr–Newman extremal bound (q = Q/M > 1); maximum q² + χ² reaches 1.84. Shadow ellipticity from vector hair dominates—e.g., 8.30% at near-extremal (q,χ)=(0.9947,0.10), 19× larger than charge/rotation contributions; rises to 25.6% at overcharged (q,χ)=(1.2527,0.10). Existing ghost-instability no-go theorems do not apply due to background and dispersion violations.

Conclusion: The extremality ceiling is not absolute but depends on field content and coupling; vector hair in rotating EMV black holes enables genuine overcharging (q > 1) with distinct, potentially observable shadow signatures; stability remains open but not ruled out by current criteria.

Abstract: A charged black hole has a ceiling: extremality bounds the charge that a given mass and spin can support. Vector hair has so far only lowered it. In the Einstein--Maxwell--Vector (EMV) model, where a massless vector field couples nonminimally to the electromagnetic invariant, the spherically symmetric vectorized Reissner--Nordström black holes are strictly undercharged: vector hair is paid for in charge. Whether this ceiling is a property of the vector field or merely of spherical symmetry has remained open, because spherical symmetry hides the hair's mass as well: its Komar mass vanishes identically and the exact identity $M_V=2\Omega_HJ_V$ degenerates to $0=0$. Rotation lifts both degeneracies at once. We construct the rotating solutions of the model, the first in this setting, and find that the hair's mass is not a free parameter but a second-order invariant of the rotational response, fixed by $M_V=2\Omega_HJ_V$. Above a coupling threshold the ceiling gives way: the domain of existence crosses the extremal Kerr--Newman bound, reaching $q=Q/M>1$ with $q^2+\chi^2$ up to $1.84$, while at $\alpha=5$ the maximum charge stays below it. The shadow is where the hair becomes visible. At $(q,\chi)=(0.9947,0.10)$, near the extremal bound, the hair contributes an ellipticity of $8.30\%$, against $0.44\%$ from the charge and $0.06\%$ from rotation --- a factor of $19$ that charging a Kerr black hole cannot mimic; at the overcharged solution $(q,\chi)=(1.2527,0.10)$ the total ellipticity reaches $25.6\%$. Whether the branch is ghost-free remains open, but the existing no-go criteria do not settle it: they assume a background and a dispersion relation that do not hold here.

</details>


### [83] [Gravitational waves away from a Schwarzschild black hole: full spectrum analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40181)
*Marco Refuto,Giampiero Esposito*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the full gravitational wave spectrum near Schwarzschild null infinity using a spin-weighted scalar field formalism and Poincaré asymptotic expansions, bridging black hole perturbation theory with LIGO/VIRGO phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect rigorous asymptotic analysis in black hole perturbation theory with observational gravitational wave data from LIGO and VIRGO.

Method: Spin-weighted generalisation of a massless scalar field; Poincaré asymptotic expansion applied at Schwarzschild null infinity.

Result: Full gravitational wave spectrum consistent with LIGO/VIRGO observations; new mathematical tools linking asymptotic structure to gravitational wave phenomenology.

Conclusion: The developed formalism establishes a robust mathematical-phenomenological bridge for analysing gravitational waves in the asymptotic regime of Schwarzschild spacetime.

Abstract: Gravitational waves as one approaches Schwarzschild null infinity are the core of our investigation, treated as the spin-weighted generalisation of a massless scalar field. In particular, we compute their full spectrum, consistent with observations coming from LIGO and VIRGO experiments. This paper continues recent work by the authors, where the Poincaré asymptotic expansion was exploited in black hole perturbation theory. The mathematical formalism we developed is now intimately connected with gravitational wave phenomenology, providing new mathematical tools to investigate their features.

</details>


### [84] [Scalar--photon coupling in cosmology: Radiation evolution, recombination, and CMB signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40319)
*Y Bisabr*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how a cosmological scalar field interacting with radiation affects pre-recombination cosmology and CMB anisotropies, using an effective scalar-photon-fluid coupling with exponential potential; it computes perturbations, recombination history, and CMB/matter spectra in CLASS, finds weak observational preference for interaction (Δχ² ≈ +0.30), and concludes that definitive constraints require improved modeling of photon thermalization and collision physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observable imprints of scalar-field–radiation interactions on pre-recombination expansion history and CMB anisotropies, motivated by theoretical frameworks beyond ΛCDM where such couplings may arise.

Method: Derives coupled background and linear perturbation equations for a canonical scalar field with exponential potential interacting with a photon fluid; implements the model in CLASS; assumes Planckian photon spectrum and phenomenological treatment of photon thermodynamics/collisions; computes recombination history, CMB temperature/polarization/lensing power spectra, and matter power spectrum; uses conditional Planck 2018 likelihoods with six standard parameters fixed and A_Planck profiled.

Result: The interaction induces scale-dependent modifications to CMB anisotropies and matter power spectrum; the best-fitting interacting model (ε_i = 4×10⁻⁵, σ = 0.10) is marginally disfavored relative to ΛCDM (Δχ² ≈ +0.30); likelihood remains shallow at weak coupling, indicating no strong evidence for interaction.

Conclusion: Scalar-photon-fluid interactions produce well-defined phenomenological signatures in CMB and large-scale structure, but current data do not prefer them over ΛCDM; robust constraints await refined modeling of photon thermalization, collision dynamics, and photon-density normalization.

Abstract: Interactions between a cosmological scalar field and radiation can modify the pre-recombination expansion history and leave observable signatures in cosmic microwave background (CMB) anisotropies. We investigate an effective scalar-photon-fluid coupling, deriving the coupled background and linear perturbation equations and leading-order adiabatic initial conditions for a canonical scalar field with an exponential potential. Starting from a radiation-era scaling solution, we implement the dynamical interaction in CLASS and compute the resulting recombination history, CMB anisotropies, and linear matter power spectrum. Photon thermodynamics and collisions are treated phenomenologically, assuming preservation of a Planckian spectrum. We assess observational compatibility using conditional Planck 2018 temperature, polarization, and lensing likelihoods, holding the six standard cosmological parameters fixed while profiling the calibration parameter $A_{\rm Planck}$. The interaction produces scale-dependent modifications to CMB and matter spectra, but the conditional likelihood remains shallow at weak coupling. The best sampled interacting point, $(\epsilon_i,\sigma)=(4\times10^{-5},0.10)$, is slightly disfavored relative to the matched interaction-off $\Lambda$CDM reference ($\Delta\chi^2\simeq+0.30$). These results establish phenomenological cosmological signatures without demonstrating an observational preference for the interaction. Definitive constraints require further validation of photon thermalization, collision dynamics, and photon-density normalization.

</details>


### [85] [Curvature Blindness: Material Inference in Stars and Black Hole Candidates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40501)
*Jacob M. Krebs*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a property-level identifiability criterion in general relativity to analyze inverse problems where gravitational observations fail to uniquely determine material properties—either due to identical stress-energy sources (equal-source ambiguity) or indistinguishable observational projections despite differing spacetime histories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formalize and distinguish two types of indeterminacy in GR inverse problems: (1) distinct material states yielding identical total gravitational sources, and (2) distinct spacetime evolutions producing identical retained observational data.

Method: Develops a property-level identifiability framework; constructs and compares idealized physical models—two massless gases (demonstrating equal-source ambiguity), and causal fluid models with identical initial data but different constitutive relations (revealing divergence in curvature and scale factor evolution); applies the framework to black hole candidates by disentangling stationary exterior parameters, perturbing fluxes, and internal constituent history.

Result: Identifies concrete instances of non-identifiability: (i) entropy and temperature differences between gases with identical stress-energy tensors; (ii) linear-order curvature divergence and cubic-order scale-factor divergence between causal fluids with same initial geometry and source; (iii) classification of observables for black hole candidates into three tiers—instantaneous source equality, history-wide source equality, and observational projection—enabling tests of material property identifiability.

Conclusion: Gravitational observations alone are insufficient to uniquely identify many material properties; identifiability must be assessed case-by-case using the proposed criterion, distinguishing between degeneracies arising from source equivalence versus dynamical/observational projection.

Abstract: Gravitational observations can leave material properties undetermined in two different ways: distinct states can have the same total source, and distinct spacetime histories can have the same retained observations. We organize these inverse problems in general relativity through a property-level identifiability criterion, with applications to stars and black hole candidates. Two ideal massless gases illustrate equal-source ambiguity: their temperatures differ by a factor of $(8/29)^{1/4}$ and their entropies differ while their stress-energy tensors agree. A separate causal-fluid example starts with identical sources and geometric data but different constitutive responses; the two models' curvature histories separate at linear order in proper time and their scale factors at cubic order. For black hole candidates, the relevant distinction is between stationary exterior parameters, perturbing fluxes and constituent history. The framework provides a test for whether retained data identify a material property, separating instantaneous source equality, history-wide source equality and observational projection.

</details>


### [86] [Anisotropic overlap reduction functions for arbitrary equal-arm TDI channels: A fully analytic framework and efficient evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40548)
*Kun Zhou,Jin Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified analytic framework for computing anisotropic overlap reduction functions (ORFs) for arbitrary finite-delay TDI channels in an equilateral, static, reciprocal LISA-like constellation, enabling fast, high-precision, polarization-agnostic ORF evaluation up to ℓ=5 with verified 10⁻¹⁰⁰ accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome computational bottlenecks and numerical instability in sky-averaged ORF calculations for anisotropic gravitational-wave backgrounds, especially for high-ℓ multipoles and arbitrary TDI channels, while preserving full propagation physics and laser-noise cancellation constraints.

Method: Combines polynomial TDI laser-noise cancellation constraints, constellation symmetry group representation, directional-projection integration, and module-theoretic decomposition of admissible TDI channels; introduces a free basis separating channel algebra from angular integration, and derives two families of elementary angular kernels valid across all GW polarizations (tensor, vector, breathing, longitudinal).

Result: Derives closed-form, multiprecision-ready analytic ORF kernels up to ℓ = 5; achieves 50–5000× speedup over optimized sky quadrature at matched error; validates mixed absolute/relative error targets down to 10⁻¹⁰⁰ via independent high-precision sky integrations; provides reusable kernel libraries and generation rules for multichannel anisotropic background modeling.

Conclusion: The framework unifies and generalizes ORF computation for arbitrary finite-delay TDI channels and GW polarizations, enabling stable, ultra-high-precision, and computationally efficient anisotropic sensitivity modeling essential for next-generation space-based GW observatories.

Abstract: We establish a unified analytic framework for anisotropic overlap reduction functions (ORFs) of arbitrary finite-delay time-delay interferometry (TDI) channels in a static, reciprocal, equal-arm equilateral constellation. The construction combines the polynomial constraints for laser-noise cancellation, constellation symmetries, and directional-projection integration. A free basis of the admissible channel module separates channel generation from angular integration; two families of elementary angular kernels then generate all channel-pair intensity ORFs. The channel algebra is independent of gravitational-wave polarization, and the intensity-kernel reduction applies to tensor, vector, breathing, and longitudinal sectors. Tensor multipoles illustrate the framework here. Complete propagation effects are retained at arbitrary fixed finite angular order, with explicit examples through $\ell=5$. Prepared frequency batches, shared multiprecision expressions, and local expansions at removable delay points provide stable evaluation. In the prepared-batch benchmark, the analytic implementation is approximately 50 to 5000 times faster than optimized sky quadrature at matched error tolerances. Independent multiprecision sky integrations also validate prescribed mixed error targets down to $10^{-100}$ in the tested cases. The resulting kernels and generation rules supply reusable inputs for anisotropic multichannel background models.

</details>


### [87] [Gravitational-Wave Echoes and Greybody Cutoff from Logarithmic Throats at a Marginal-Trapping Threshold](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40631)
*Jahed Abedi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational wave echoes in near-threshold, horizonless, spherically symmetric quasi-black hole geometries with long, capped throats; it finds that echo spacing scales as Δ^(−s/2) (where Δ measures proximity to marginal trapping), distinct from logarithmic scaling in gravastars/wormholes, and confirms the scaling numerically; the prompt signal is horizon-agnostic, but echoes restore high-frequency reflectivity in horizonless cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how gravitational wave echoes arise in horizonless alternatives to black holes—specifically in 'quasi-black holes' with near-AdS₂ throats capped by regular matter—distinguishing their echo signatures from other compact objects (e.g., gravastars, wormholes) and probing implications for microstate geometry phenomenology.

Method: Analytical effective-throat model with lapse N ∝ F^(s/2), where F = 1 − 2m/r; derivation of wave propagation in the axial perturbation sector; computation of echo time delay T_echo ≈ 2Λ + δ via optical length Λ of the throat; spectral analysis of reflection/transmission across angular-momentum barrier; numerical time-domain evolutions over three decades in Δ to verify scaling.

Result: Echo spacing scales as T_echo ∝ Δ^(−s/2), controlled by redshift at throat bottom—not logarithmic as in thin-shell models; for s = 0, no distinct echoes emerge as throat merges with barrier; barrier imposes universal greybody cutoff 1 − Γ ∝ exp[−(ω − f)/T] with temperature T set by barrier curvature, independent of surface gravity; prompt waveform identical for horizon vs. capped-throat interiors; echoes reconstruct high-frequency part of full reflection spectrum only in horizonless case.

Conclusion: Capped near-AdS₂ throats in matter-supported quasi-black holes produce power-law echo delays—distinctive from logarithmic delays—and preserve prompt signals indistinguishable from black holes, making echoes a key observational discriminant; the barrier’s geometric properties govern both timing and spectral filtering, decoupling echo physics from horizon thermodynamics.

Abstract: When a static, spherically symmetric configuration is brought close to a marginal-trapping threshold, it develops a long throat. As the nondegenerate minimum of $F=1-2m/r$ approaches zero, $F\simeq\Delta+\beta(r-R_M)^2$, the proper length across the throat grows as $-\ln\Delta/\sqrt\beta$, even though the local curvature stays finite. We study how axial gravitational waves propagate in an effective model of this throat, in which the lapse is parametrized as $N\propto F^{s/2}$. For $s>0$ the throat acts as a cavity behind the angular-momentum barrier, and waves come back out as echoes spaced by $T_{\rm echo}\simeq2\Lambda+\delta\propto\Delta^{-s/2}$, where $\Lambda$ is the optical length of the throat and $\delta$ does not depend on $\Delta$. We study four-dimensional, matter-supported quasi-black holes in which a regular centre caps the near-AdS$_2$ throat. These configurations provide a simplified model for investigating capped throats in horizonless microstate geometries. The echo delay in this model is controlled by the inverse redshift near the bottom of the throat, as in capped microstate geometries. This differs from thin-shell gravastars and wormholes, where the delay grows only logarithmically with the distance to the would-be horizon. Time-domain evolutions confirm the $\Delta^{-s/2}$ scaling over three decades in $\Delta$; for $s=0$ the throat becomes part of the barrier and no distinct echo appears. The barrier also imposes a greybody cutoff on the reflected spectrum, $1-\Gamma\simeq e^{-(\omega-f)/T}$, where $T$ is fixed by the curvature of the barrier and does not depend on the surface gravity of any horizon behind it. In this model, the prompt signal is the same whether the interior contains a horizon or a reflecting throat. In the horizonless case, however, the transmitted wave returns as echoes, restoring the high-frequency part of the full reflected spectrum.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

### [88] [Gluons and Knots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10673)
*Alfredo Guevara*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a topological interpretation of the single-minus-helicity gluon scattering amplitude, linking it to 2D null Wilson loops, 3D positive transverse links, and HOMFLY-PT polynomial corner contributions via skein and Reidemeister relations; it provides a rigorously Lean-formalized derivation and tests against Catalan-number predictions in 'star' kinematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a topological structure underlying MHV-like gluon amplitudes—beyond standard perturbative QCD—and bridge scattering amplitudes with knot theory, topological field theory, and string theory via geometric and algebraic invariants.

Method: Reinterprets wall-crossing and the Weinberg soft theorem as skein relations and Reidemeister moves on subknots; constructs a state-counting formalism from corners of HOMFLY-PT polynomials; employs a Lean-formalized dependency graph (DAG) for rigorous derivation; validates via analytic and numerical tests including 'star' kinematics.

Result: Derives a new topological formula for the single-minus amplitude expressed in terms of link invariants and HOMFLY-PT corner sums; proves its consistency with Reidemeister III invariance; verifies agreement with Catalan numbers in 'star' configurations—matching known knot-theoretic results.

Conclusion: The single-minus gluon amplitude is topologically encoded in positive transverse 3D links; its kinematic piecewise constancy, wall-resonances, and factorization are governed by knot-theoretic data; this establishes a precise, conjectured link between planar N=4 SYM amplitudes, topological quantum field theory, and Chern–Simons/string dualities.

Abstract: We argue that the scattering amplitude of a single minus helicity gluon with arbitrarily many positive helicity gluons is topological. The amplitude is constant in chambers of kinematic space, its resonances are located at walls and its factorization is determined by wall-crossing. Yangian invariance dictates the amplitude to be determined by a 2d null Wilson loop that can be lifted into a positive and transverse 3d link. By reinterpreting wall-crossing and the Weinberg soft theorem as skein relations and Reidemeister moves of the subknots, we construct a state-counting description consisting of adding the `corners' of their HOMFLY-PT polynomials. Beyond the classical limit, we argue that this provides a conjectured but precise connection to topological field and string theory. The new formula is rigorously derived via a Lean-formalized dependency graph (DAG) as detailed in a companion paper, laying out the precise assumptions including Reidemeister III invariance. It is further tested exhaustively, including novel `star' kinematics where the amplitude and state count are shown to become Catalan numbers, in agreement with knot theory literature.

</details>


### [89] [Resurgence and Modularity in the First Painlevé Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38584)
*Kohei Iwaki,Noam Tamarin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an explicit connection between nonlinear Stokes phenomena for the first Painlevé equation and modular transformations of its two-parameter tau function, showing that the composition of three Stokes maps equals the modular S∘T transformation; it identifies parameter changes under 2π/5 and 8π/5 rotations as S- and T-actions respectively, computes associated tau-function multiplicative factors (normalized via Rogers dilogarithm’s five-term identity), and confirms single-valuedness after a full 2π rotation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the single-valuedness of the Painlevé I transcendent and its tau function after a full loop around infinity by linking nonlinear Stokes geometry to modular symmetry.

Method: Analyzes Stokes multipliers of the isomonodromy Lax system using exact WKB and topological recursion; tracks homology basis evolution on the elliptic spectral curve under rotational monodromy of the independent variable; identifies modular actions via Borel direction rotations (−π, 0, π) and computes tau-function prefactors using Abel’s five-term identity for the Rogers dilogarithm.

Result: Derives explicit parameter transformations: an 8π/5 rotation implements the modular T-action, a 2π/5 rotation realizes the S-action; determines precise multiplicative factors (including normalization constants) in tau-function relations under these rotations; verifies cancellation of prefactors via the Rogers dilogarithm identity, ensuring single-valuedness of both transcendent and tau function after 2π.

Conclusion: The nonlinear Stokes structure of Painlevé I is governed by SL(2,ℤ) modular symmetry, with the full-turn monodromy decomposed as S∘T; this provides a geometric, analytic, and modular unified framework for tau-function single-valuedness and transcendent monodromy.

Abstract: For the first Painlevé equation, we relate three nonlinear Stokes maps associated with a full turn around infinity to modular transformations of the two-parameter tau function through the identity $\boldsymbol S_\pi\circ\boldsymbol S_0\circ \boldsymbol S_{-\pi}=\mathsf S\circ\mathsf T$. Here the subscripts label Borel directions, while $\mathsf S$ and $\mathsf T$ denote the modular generators. Working under the analytic assumptions of the topological recursion and exact WKB constructions, we compare Stokes multipliers of the associated isomonodromy system while tracking the homology basis of its elliptic spectral curve under rotations of the independent variable. For the sectors considered, we identify the known parameter transformation induced by an $8\pi/5$ rotation with the modular $T$-action and derive a new explicit parameter relation realizing the $S$-action for a $2\pi/5$ rotation. We also determine the multiplicative factors in the corresponding tau-function relations, including their normalization constants. Together, these results give an explicit realization of single-valuedness after one full turn around infinity for both the Painlevé transcendent and its tau function, with the tau-function prefactors cancelling by Abel's five-term identity for the Rogers dilogarithm.

</details>


### [90] [The Effective Field Theory Origin of the Fuzzy Sphere Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38702)
*Mykola Dedushenko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a 'mother theory' framework on S²×ℝ to systematically derive fuzzy sphere Hamiltonians for (2+1)D CFTs; constructs Ising and easy-plane ferromagnet examples, yielding relativistic and z=2 non-relativistic critical points—with exact one-magnon solutions—and opens avenues for broader applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a systematic, local, and UV-complete derivation of fuzzy sphere Hamiltonians—previously written ad hoc on the lowest Landau level—by embedding them as low-energy descendants of local Lorentz-invariant 'mother theories' on S²×ℝ.

Method: Constructs mother theories (e.g., two charged Dirac fermions coupled to real/complex scalar mediators on S²×ℝ); integrates out massive degrees of freedom (higher Landau levels, fermions, or mediator) to obtain either fuzzy-sphere Hamiltonians (LL-projected) or conventional EFTs; analyzes symmetry breaking, critical scaling (z=2), and exact solvability via magnon dispersion.

Result: Derives fuzzy Ising and easy-plane ferromagnet Hamiltonians; identifies a z=2 non-relativistic critical point on the fuzzy sphere—the first such example—with analytically accessible critical coupling and exact one-magnon eigenstates; recovers relativistic pseudo-Goldstone modes in broken phase; generalizes to tricritical and Lee-Yang deformations.

Conclusion: The mother-theory framework unifies and generalizes fuzzy sphere constructions, enabling controlled access to diverse critical phenomena—including relativistic, non-relativistic (z=2), and topological—while preserving locality and offering new analytical and numerical pathways for quantum simulation and CFT studies.

Abstract: Fuzzy sphere Hamiltonians for (2+1)D conformal field theories are usually written directly on the lowest Landau level. We introduce a framework organizing them as descendants of local ``mother theories'' on $S^2\times\mathbb R$. We start with the example of Ising mother theory built from two charged Dirac fermions coupled to a real mediator $\phi$. Integrating out $\phi$ and the higher Landau levels produces the fuzzy Hamiltonian, while integrating out the fermions gives a conventional local $\phi$ effective field theory flowing to the Ising point. Mild modifications of the same theory reach the Gaussian point (tricritical route) and the Lee-Yang continuation. Exploring the space of mother theories, we next promote $\phi$ to a complex field $\Phi$ and construct a new example, whose descendant is an easy-plane ferromagnet on the fuzzy sphere. Its spontaneous symmetry breaking phase gives a pseudo-Goldstone relativistic scalar, similar to the model introduced by Y.-C. He, while its critical point is non-relativistic, providing the first example of $z=2$ scaling on the fuzzy sphere. Remarkably, it admits exact one-magnon solutions, and the $z=2$ critical point can be found analytically. We then outline a rich landscape of further applications of our approach.

</details>


### [91] [Generalized Symmetries of QED$_4$ on Non-Spin Manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38907)
*Michele Del Zotto,Vivek Saxena*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes generalized symmetries and anomalies in massless QED on non-spin four-manifolds, revealing a $w_2$-twisted magnetic 1-form symmetry, an order-two mixed magnetic-gravitational anomaly tied to oriented bordism, and framing-dependent chiral responses governed by the spin^c Dirac index — all illustrated explicitly on ℂℙ².


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how generalized symmetries and quantum anomalies in massless QED depend on spacetime's tangential structure—particularly when spin structures are absent and spin^c geometry is required.

Method: Uses differential cohomology, spin^c geometry, bordism classification (especially Ω₄^{SO}), anomaly inflow, index theory of the spin^c Dirac operator, and explicit computation on the Wu manifold and ℂℙ²; incorporates topological field theory (TFT) arguments including Witten–Reshetikhin–Turaev (WRT) theories for anomaly cancellation.

Result: Identifies a $w_2$-twisted 1-form magnetic symmetry; classifies a mixed magnetic-gravitational anomaly via reduced oriented bordism Ω̃₄^{SO} ≅ ℤ₂, detected on the Wu manifold; shows the anomaly is removable in fixed spin^c sectors but intrinsic to the sum over spin^c structures; reveals framing dependence in chiral defects and spin^c Hall TFT; constructs neutral noninvertible WRT layers that cancel framing anomalies for arbitrary rational angles.

Conclusion: The global structure of QED on non-spin manifolds is governed by spin^c geometry and twisted symmetries, with anomalies and defect responses fundamentally tied to gravitational topology—not just gauge data—implying that phase structure (gapped/Coulomb/Higgs) and anomaly cancellation mechanisms must account for both electromagnetic and gravitational bordism invariants.

Abstract: Generalized symmetries are sensitive to the tangential structure of spacetime. We study this interplay for massless QED on oriented non-spin four-manifolds, where the photon becomes a spin$^c$ connection and the path integral sums over spin$^c$ structures. The shifted flux quantization extends the magnetic 1-form symmetry into a $w_2$-twisted 1-form symmetry: its $2\pi$ rotation acts as a gravitational sign, fixed by the normal bundle of the surface the operator wraps. The obstruction to treating it as an ordinary magnetic 1-form symmetry is an order-two mixed magnetic-gravitational anomaly, which is classified by reduced oriented bordism and detected on the Wu manifold. In a fixed spin$^c$ sector, this anomaly is removable by a counterterm, so it is a property of the sum over spin$^c$ structures. We discuss its consequences for gapped, Coulomb, and Higgs phases. The chiral anomaly is governed by the index of the spin$^c$ Dirac operator, so rational chiral defects carry gravitational as well as electromagnetic responses. A spin$^c$ Hall topological field theory cancels the electromagnetic part but leaves a framing anomaly. At the level of anomaly inflow, this is canceled for every rational angle by stacking a neutral, generally noninvertible Witten-Reshetikhin-Turaev layer. The same framing subtlety affects the chiral defects of QED on curved spin manifolds. The complex projective plane illustrates each of these features explicitly.

</details>


### [92] [A density calculus for quantum field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40174)
*Tanay Kibe,Pratik Roy*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces Haagerup noncommutative $L^p$ spaces to quantum field theorists, extending density matrix calculus—including fractional powers and Schatten norms—to type III von Neumann algebras lacking a trace; it constructs these spaces via crossed products, clarifies the role of Haagerup densities for normal states, distinguishes the crossed-product trace from the canonical Haagerup functional, and applies the framework to compute sandwiched Rényi divergence for chiral current coherent states—verifying monotonicity and the Rényi quantum null energy condition for all $\alpha > 1$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between standard quantum information tools (e.g., density matrices, $L^p$ norms) and algebraic QFT settings where local observable algebras are type III and lack a faithful normal trace—thus requiring a rigorous, intrinsic generalization of $L^p$ spaces.

Method: Constructs Haagerup $L^p$ spaces first concretely for matrix algebras using the crossed product with $\mathbb{R}$, explicit dual action, and canonical trace; then generalizes to arbitrary von Neumann algebras. Emphasizes representation of normal states by Haagerup densities and distinguishes the crossed-product trace from the Haagerup functional used for expectations. Applies the formalism to compute sandwiched Rényi divergence in chiral CFT.

Result: Provides a pedagogical, operator-algebraic foundation for Haagerup $L^p$ spaces tailored to QFT; explicitly computes sandwiched Rényi divergence for coherent excitations of chiral currents; verifies its monotonicity under restriction and satisfies the Rényi quantum null energy condition for all real $\alpha > 1$.

Conclusion: Haagerup $L^p$ spaces provide the correct analytic framework for generalizing quantum information measures—especially Rényi divergences—to type III settings in QFT; the formalism is both mathematically rigorous and physically operational, enabling verification of fundamental inequalities like the Rényi QNEC.

Abstract: These notes provide an introduction to Haagerup noncommutative $L^p$ spaces for quantum field theorists. The aim is to explain how the familiar calculus of density matrices, fractional powers, and Schatten norms extends to local observable algebras of type~III, where an intrinsic trace is unavailable. We develop the construction first for a matrix algebra, making the crossed product, its trace, and the dual action explicit, before passing to general von Neumann algebras. Particular attention is paid to the representation of normal states by Haagerup densities and to the distinction between the crossed-product trace and the canonical Haagerup functional used to compute expectation values. As a concrete application, we calculate the sandwiched Rényi divergence for a coherent excitation of a chiral current, verifying monotonicity under restriction and the Rényi quantum null energy condition at every real order $\alpha>1$. The presentation assumes familiarity with quantum mechanics and elementary quantum field theory, while introducing the operator-algebraic concepts needed for the construction and calculation.

</details>


### [93] [Gravity between two masses beyond Newton's law](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40462)
*Zhengwen Liu,Xing-Bo Yuan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the static gravitational two-body interaction in general relativity up to 12th order in Newton's constant G, and derives an all-orders closed-form expression for the first backreaction correction beyond the test-mass (Schwarzschild) limit, using a stationary interaction-energy formulation with exact one-body background, stationarity constraints, and integrated response identities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing analytical challenge of extracting the static two-body interaction from Einstein's non-linear field equations—specifically, to go beyond the test-body approximation and capture mutual backreaction effects essential for high-precision gravitational-wave modeling and fundamental understanding of gravitational non-linearity.

Method: Formulates gravity as a stationary interaction energy problem; employs an exact one-body (Schwarzschild) background to resum self-interaction of one mass; exploits stationarity to reduce required field components from the second body; uses integrated response identities to expose and cancel coupled-mode contributions; combines complementary perturbative expansions to fully resolve arbitrary mass-ratio dependence at finite G-order.

Result: Static two-body interaction energy computed explicitly from G^9 to G^12 for arbitrary mass ratios; closed-form all-orders expression derived for the leading backreaction correction beyond the test-body limit (i.e., first correction linear in the finite mass of the 'test' object); systematic cancellation mechanisms identified across field modes via response identities.

Conclusion: The static gravitational two-body problem exhibits hidden analytical structure when recast around stationary interaction energy; the combination of exact background, symmetry reduction, and response identities enables unprecedented control over non-linearities; results provide foundational high-order terms for EOB models and waveform templates, and reveal intrinsic simplicity beneath GR's apparent complexity.

Abstract: Newton's law of universal gravitation describes the attraction between two masses through an inverse-distance potential. In Einstein's theory gravity gravitates: each mass reshapes the spacetime geometry through which the other acts. More than a century later, extracting the two-body interaction from this coupled geometry remains a fundamental analytical challenge. Even without motion or radiation, the static sector of the interaction harbours an infinite hierarchy of field couplings: the purest laboratory for gravitational non-linearity. Here we determine the static interaction from ninth to twelfth order in Newton's constant for arbitrary mass ratios, and derive a closed expression for the first backreaction beyond the Schwarzschild test-body limit to all orders in the gravitational coupling. We achieve both advances by formulating the problem around the stationary interaction energy. An exact one-body background resums one source's self-interaction, stationarity reduces the fields required from the other, and integrated response identities reveal cancellations across the coupled modes. Complementary expansions then resolve the complete mass dependence at finite order. Together, these results bridge the gap between an infinitesimal probe and comparable masses, exposing hidden analytical simplicity within Einstein's theory and providing essential building blocks for modelling orbital dynamics and gravitational-wave generation.

</details>


### [94] [Localization in Seven-Dimensional Supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40514)
*Florian Gaar,Michèle Levi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends relative equivariant localization to 7D maximal Cartan-gauged supergravity to compute supersymmetric observables—specifically the AdS₇ black-hole action—without solving full field equations, leveraging spinor-bilinear geometry, Majorana-adapted Killing-spinor equations, and massive 3-form self-duality to fix anomaly data and derive charge constraints via entropy extremization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the difficulty of explicitly solving the highly constrained, spinor-bilinear and massive higher-form dynamics of 7D supergravity for holographic access to 6D superconformal theories, while enabling systematic computation of supersymmetric observables (e.g., black-hole actions) without full solutions.

Method: Extends relative equivariant localization to seven dimensions in Euclidean maximal Cartan-gauged supergravity; reformulates action and Killing-spinor equations in terms of field strengths; adapts bilinear formalism to Majorana spinors (vs. symplectic-Majorana); uses massive 3-form self-duality to fix quartic anomaly class and determine higher-form fixed-point data from Abelian moment maps; localizes at a single fixed point of an auxiliary even-dimensional filling; proves gauge-covariant Killing-spinor invariance under supersymmetric Killing flow and R-symmetry; derives linear chemical-potential constraint and extremizes constrained entropy functional.

Result: Recovers the general supersymmetric AdS₇ black-hole action solely from fixed-point data; derives the linear chemical-potential constraint and nonlinear charge relation (equivalent to Lorentzian horizon extremality) via entropy extremization; shows real positive stationary entropy implies consistency with known spherical black-hole family; establishes applicability of the reorganized bilinear framework to both Euclidean and Lorentzian theories.

Conclusion: Relative equivariant localization is successfully generalized to 7D maximal supergravity, enabling exact computation of supersymmetric observables—including black-hole thermodynamics—from algebraic and geometric fixed-point data, bypassing full PDE solution; the method unifies treatment of Cartan charges, respects reality conditions, and bridges Euclidean localization with Lorentzian physical constraints like extremality.

Abstract: Seven-dimensional supergravity provides holographic access to strongly coupled six-dimensional superconformal theories through its highest-dimensional supersymmetric AdS vacua. Its unique spinor-bilinear geometry and massive higher-form dynamics make systematic analysis and solution-finding distinctively challenging. Relative equivariant localization can obtain supersymmetric observables in odd dimensions from fixed-point data on an auxiliary even-dimensional filling, without an explicit solution. We extend this approach to seven dimensions in maximal Cartan-gauged supergravity with all gauge fields active. We formulate the Euclidean action and Killing-spinor equations in terms of field strengths, and construct a bilinear system adapted to the change in spinor reality conditions from symplectic-Majorana to Majorana. On the non-null gauged active spinor branch, independent, oppositely R-charged spinors are paired by Majorana conjugation on the same complex background. One algebraic gauging correction and two algebraic bilinear projections suffice to extend the equal-charge sector to independent Cartan charges, and the underlying efficient reorganization also applies in the Lorentzian theory. Massive 3-form self-duality fixes the quartic anomaly class and determines the higher-form fixed-point datum from Abelian moment maps. Localization at the single fixed point of the filling recovers the general supersymmetric AdS$_7$ black-hole action, without extending the solution. We prove gauge-covariant Killing-spinor invariance under the combined supersymmetric Killing flow and R-symmetry action, deriving the linear chemical-potential constraint. Extremizing the constrained entropy functional and requiring real positive stationary entropy yields the nonlinear charge relation within the Euclidean analysis, equivalent to Lorentzian horizon extremality for the known spherical black-hole family.

</details>


### [95] [Creation of the Universe in Lorentzian Classical Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37748)
*Avipsa Chakrabarti,Avik Chakraborty,Martín Molina,Ayan Mukhopadhyay,Salomón Romero*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes 'cosmic creation gates'—Lorentzian gravitational junctions connecting multiple de Sitter regions at a timelike perfect-fluid interface—as a geometric, non-singular mechanism for universe creation within classical/semiclassical gravity, bypassing past-incompleteness theorems; it establishes geodesic completeness, volume asymptotics, perturbation spectra, and fine-tuning requirements for finite-volume beginnings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the apparent beginning of the Universe (suggested by singularity theorems and past-incompleteness of inflationary spacetimes) with Lorentzian general relativity without invoking quantum gravity or smooth manifold assumptions—by exploring non-smooth spacetime constructions via gravitational junction conditions.

Method: Constructs exact, rotationally invariant solutions with timelike perfect-fluid junctions (P = wρ) joining n ≥ 2 locally de Sitter bulk regions in D ≥ 3 dimensions; analyzes geodesic completeness, global-time foliation, volume evolution, nonlinear backreaction deformations, and linearized perturbations (including mode counting and stability); imposes Israel junction conditions and solves Einstein equations with distributional stress-energy.

Result: 1) Geodesically complete cosmic creation gates exist for w > −(D−3)/(D−2); 2) Past/future spatial volumes approach finite positive limits on global-time foliation; 3) Deformed gates allow finite initial but unbounded future volume; 4) Junction fluid always expands, cooling (w > 0) or heating (allowed w < 0); 5) Perturbation analysis reveals dimension- and n-dependent mode structure: 3D admits one hybrid sound + (n−1) transverse modes; D ≥ 4 retains only hybrid sound mode; 6) Finite-volume beginning requires fine-tuned homogeneous + inhomogeneous data; causal response generically implies unbounded future volume; 7) Introduces broader bounded-past-volume oscillation criterion.

Conclusion: The Universe’s creation can be modeled classically as a gravitational junction event—not a singularity—enabling a well-defined, geodesically complete, Lorentzian beginning. However, generic initial conditions lead to infinite future volume, and finite-volume beginnings are finely tuned; this reframes cosmological initial conditions as dynamically constrained by junction physics and causal propagation, with implications for inflation, quantum gravity emergence, and the arrow of time.

Abstract: The singularity theorems and the past incompleteness of inflationary spacetimes suggest that the beginning of the Universe lies beyond the domain of semiclassical gravity. We show that the creation of the Universe can nevertheless be understood within Lorentzian gravity by allowing gravitational junctions, so that the full spacetime need not be a smooth manifold. We construct rotationally invariant cosmic creation gates joining any finite number $n\geq2$ of locally de Sitter regions in $D\geq3$ spacetime dimensions at a timelike perfect-fluid junction with $P=w\rho$. For $w>-(D-3)/(D-2)$, they are geodesically complete in both temporal directions. The total spatial volume approaches finite, positive limits in the infinite past and future on the global-time foliation adapted to the junction fluid. We also construct exact deformed cosmic creation gates through nonlinear homogeneous backreaction that retain the initial volume while the future volume grows without bound. In these solutions, the junction fluid always expands, cooling for $w>0$ and heating for allowed $w<0$. Keeping the bulk regions locally de Sitter, we determine the linearized perturbations: in three dimensions, the generic inhomogeneous sector contains one hybrid sound mode and $n-1$ relative transverse modes; in four and higher dimensions, only the homogeneous hybrid sound mode survives. A finite-volume beginning requires fine-tuned homogeneous and inhomogeneous initial data, while causal response generically leads to unbounded future spatial volume. We also discuss a broader creation criterion allowing bounded past-volume oscillations. The gravitational equations determine conditioned admissible histories. We discuss implications for cosmology.

</details>


### [96] [Black Hole Entropy from Entangled Quantum Reference Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37750)
*Ludovic Varrin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes edge modes of a Schwarzschild black hole in the Donnelly–Freidel extended phase space, identifies them as dynamical SL(2,R) reference frames, relates two canonical gauge choices (Kuchař vs. Carlip–Teitelboim), quantizes the causal frames using SL⁺(2,R), and derives the Bekenstein–Hawking entropy plus a universal log correction as entanglement entropy—UV-cutoff independent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in black hole edge mode quantization and reconcile different canonical descriptions of Schwarzschild exterior regions by identifying their shared dynamical frame structure, and to derive black hole entropy as genuine entanglement entropy without UV cutoff dependence.

Method: Uses extended phase space formalism (Donnelly–Freidel) to identify edge modes as SL(2,R) cotangent bundle degrees of freedom; performs symplectic reduction at the bifurcation surface; interprets two known canonical pairs (Kuchař's mass/time-difference vs. Carlip–Teitelboim's area/rapidity) as gauge-equivalent descriptions; imposes causality to restrict to the semigroup SL⁺(2,R); applies harmonic analysis on SL⁺(2,R) using its Plancherel measure for quantization and entanglement entropy computation.

Result: The entanglement entropy between the two Schwarzschild exteriors equals the Bekenstein–Hawking area law S = A/4ℓₚ² plus a universal −3/2 log(A/ℓₚ²) correction, both independent of UV cutoff; the relative rapidity is identified with the time shift in crossed product algebra constructions.

Conclusion: Black hole entropy arises intrinsically from entanglement between exterior regions via causal dynamical reference frames, encoded in SL⁺(2,R) representation theory—providing a background-independent, gauge-invariant, and UV-robust derivation of semiclassical black hole thermodynamics.

Abstract: In the extended phase space of Donnelly and Freidel, the edge modes of a Schwarzschild black hole are dynamical reference frames whose phase space is the cotangent bundle of $\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{R})$. Gluing the two exterior regions at the bifurcation point leaves a single canonical pair. Within the extended phase space, this pair can be described either by Kuchař's mass and difference of asymptotic times or by the area and the relative rapidity of the two frames, which are two gauge choices of the same system. The latter is the pair of Carlip and Teitelboim, and the relative rapidity is the time shift of the crossed product construction. We quantize the frames in this gauge and construct the gravitational entangling product of the two exteriors. Requiring the frames to be causal restricts them to the semigroup $\mathrm{SL}^+(2,\mathbb{R})$, whose Plancherel measure yields the Bekenstein--Hawking entropy as the entanglement entropy between the two exteriors, together with a logarithmic correction, both independent of the ultraviolet cutoff.

</details>


### [97] [Circuit complexity of coupled four-mode squeezed states in two-field inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38254)
*Tao Li,Hai-Bing Fu,Lei-Hua Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes Nielsen circuit complexity for coupled curvature and entropy perturbations in two-field inflation, using a four-mode squeezed state derived from the full quadratic Hamiltonian; complexity grows post-horizon crossing, and interfield coupling has only a small effect on the equal-weight Gaussian complexity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify quantum computational complexity of cosmological perturbations in multi-field inflation, extending single-field complexity studies to coupled two-field systems and assessing the impact of field interactions on circuit complexity.

Method: Derives the full quadratic Hamiltonian from the action for two-field inflation; solves three coupled differential equations for pair amplitudes of a four-mode squeezed state; computes two principal squeezing strengths; evaluates Nielsen complexity for an equal-weight Gaussian circuit as C₂,k = √[2(r₁ₖ² + r₂ₖ²)].

Result: Circuit complexity increases after horizon crossing in both coupled and uncoupled cases; interfield coupling modifies pair amplitudes and the squeezed-state structure but induces only a small change in the equal-weight Gaussian complexity for the parameter space studied.

Conclusion: The equal-weight Gaussian circuit complexity is largely insensitive to interfield coupling in the considered two-field inflation models, suggesting degeneracy among distinct four-mode squeezed-state structures; this serves as a benchmark for future studies of complexity in more general multi-field or non-Gaussian inflationary scenarios.

Abstract: We study the circuit complexity of coupled curvature and entropy perturbations in two-field inflation. We start from the quadratic action and derive the full Hamiltonian. The Hamiltonian includes all diagonal and off-diagonal quadratic terms. We then obtain three coupled equations for the pair amplitudes of the four-mode squeezed state. From these amplitudes, we calculate the two principal squeezing strengths. We use them to determine the Nielsen circuit complexity. For an equal-weight Gaussian circuit, the complexity takes the form $\mathcal{C}_{2,k}=\sqrt{2(r_{1k}^{2}+r_{2k}^{2})}$. It depends on the squeezing strengths but not explicitly on the squeezing angles. Our numerical results show that the complexity grows after horizon crossing in both coupled and uncoupled models. Interfield coupling changes the pair amplitudes and the four-mode squeezed state. However, its effect on the circuit complexity is small for the parameters considered. This result shows that different four-mode pair-amplitude matrices can yield similar values of the chosen equal-weight circuit complexity. The present calculation provides a reference for studying Gaussian circuit complexity in more general two-field inflationary models.

</details>


### [98] [The Non-Relativistic Limit of Five-Dimensional Boson Stars and Scale Invariance Violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38606)
*Eduard Kim,Oleg Kondrashov,Emin Nugaev,Yakov Shnir*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the non-relativistic limit of 5D spherically symmetric boson stars in GR, revealing exact scale invariance yielding a critical configuration with fixed U(1) charge and zero binding energy; it identifies how relativistic and gravitational corrections break this symmetry and affect stability and the energy-charge relation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the role of scale symmetry in boson star structure and how its breaking by relativistic and gravitational effects determines physical properties like stability and mass-charge scaling in higher-dimensional GR.

Method: Analytical and comparative analysis of the (4+1)-dimensional Einstein-Klein-Gordon system in the non-relativistic limit; identification of scale-invariant Newtonian limit; comparison with the Wick-Cutkosky model to isolate relativistic and gravitational corrections; examination of energy-charge relation and kinematic stability.

Result: A critical boson star configuration emerges in the scale-invariant Newtonian limit with fixed global U(1) charge and vanishing binding energy; relativistic and gravitational corrections explicitly break scale invariance, lifting solution degeneracy and governing deviations from criticality, including stability thresholds and curvature-induced modifications to the energy-charge curve.

Conclusion: Scale invariance is an emergent symmetry in the non-relativistic limit of 5D boson stars, whose controlled breaking by GR effects fully determines the physical spectrum and stability landscape—bridging effective Newtonian descriptions and full relativistic gravity.

Abstract: We investigate the non-relativistic limit of spherically symmetric boson stars in (4+1)-dimensional general relativity (GR). In this limit, the resulting Newtonian theory exhibits an exact scale invariance, leading to a critical boson star configuration with a fixed global U(1) charge and vanishing binding energy. We identify the origin of these properties in the restored scale symmetry and analyze how relativistic and gravitational corrections break this symmetry. By comparing the full (4+1)-dimensional Einstein-Klein-Gordon system with the Wick-Cutkosky model, we quantify the effects of these corrections on the energy-charge relation and the kinematic stability of the configurations. Our results demonstrate how weak violation of scale invariance lifts the degeneracy of the non-relativistic solutions and determines the behavior of boson stars away from the critical limit.

</details>


### [99] [Unifying energy-entropy bounds in semiclassical gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38713)
*Jackson R. Fliss,Andrew Rolph*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces gravitational energy-entropy conditions (GEECs) — a unified class of semiclassical bounds that imply both the quantum focussing conjecture (QFC) and smeared null energy condition (SNEC), and reduce to the classical null energy condition (NEC). GEECs are constructed systematically and constrained via EFT-motivated test cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and strengthen existing semiclassical energy–entropy bounds (QFC and SNEC), neither of which implies the other nor reduces to the classical NEC, thereby addressing a gap in foundational constraints for semiclassical gravity.

Method: Define a new class of bounds—gravitational energy-entropy conditions (GEECs)—that satisfy criteria ensuring implication of both QFC and SNEC and classical reduction to NEC; construct an explicit family by applying these criteria to a broad class of stress-tensor bounds and constrain free parameters using EFT-inspired test cases.

Result: A concrete, parameterized family of GEECs is derived; it successfully implies QFC and SNEC, reduces to NEC in the classical limit, and passes key EFT consistency checks.

Conclusion: GEECs provide a more fundamental, unified framework for energy–entropy constraints in semiclassical gravity, subsuming prior leading bounds and offering a robust candidate for universal semiclassical limitations.

Abstract: We revisit bounds on energy and entropy in semiclassical gravity, aiming to strengthen and unify established bounds, in particular the quantum focussing conjecture (QFC) and the smeared null energy condition (SNEC). Both the QFC and SNEC have important applications and are the strongest in their respective hierarchies; however, neither implies the other, nor reduces to the null energy condition (NEC) in the classical limit. To that end, we define a new class of bounds dubbed gravitational energy-entropy conditions (GEECs) that imply both the QFC and SNEC, and reduce to the NEC classically. We construct an explicit family of GEECs by imposing the defining criteria on a broad set of stress tensor bounds, and constrain its remaining parameter space through test cases, in an effective field theory-inspired approach. Our GEECs are powerful candidate bounds on energy and entropy in semiclassical gravity.

</details>


### [100] [Holography and localization in fractional field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38806)
*Gianluca Calcagni,Iván Salvador-García*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes two local dual formulations of fractional field theory to address challenges posed by nonlocal operators: (1) a holographic Caffarelli–Silvestre-type extension to a higher-dimensional bulk with two scalars, and (2) spectral localization—representing the nonlocal theory as a continuum of massive local fields—enabling consistent fakeon quantization and classicization, with implications for fractional quantum gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome technical difficulties in quantizing gravitational interactions within fractional field theory caused by intrinsic nonlocality, particularly nonlocal kinetic terms and interactions.

Method: Two complementary localization strategies: (i) Generalized Caffarelli–Silvestre extension theorem applied to self-adjoint fractional field theories with multi-scale kinetics and nonlocal interactions, yielding a holographic bulk-boundary duality; (ii) Spectral localization via a continuum-mass representation, compatible with the fakeon prescription and classicization for unitary quantum gravity.

Result: A well-defined local dual description of fractional scalar field theory is achieved; spectral localization enables consistent quantization with fakeons, facilitating comparison to quadratic quantum gravity and supporting viability of fractional quantum gravity as a renormalizable, unitary framework.

Conclusion: Fractional field theory can be consistently localized—either holographically or spectrally—without sacrificing physical consistency; spectral localization in particular provides a robust, ghost-free quantization scheme compatible with the fakeon mechanism, positioning fractional quantum gravity as a compelling alternative to higher-derivative approaches.

Abstract: Fractional field theory is a promising framework wherein to quantize the gravitational interaction but the presence of nonlocal operators can make its handling difficult. With the purpose of easing this problem, we recast the theory as a local dual in two ways. First, we generalize the Caffarelli--Silvestre extension theorem to self-adjoint fractional field theories with multi-scale kinetic terms and nonlocal interactions, taking the example of a scalar field. The latter is reinterpreted as a nonlocal boundary degree of freedom holographically dual to a local theory of two scalars living in a higher-dimensional bulk. However, inclusion of a local non-linear potential is not straightforward. We therefore propose an alternative picture where the nonlocal theory is not extended to a higher-dimensional space but is localized at the price of introducing a continuum of masses. This \emph{spectral localization} allows for a natural implementation of the fakeon prescription and the related classicization procedure, already employed in local theories with higher-derivative and complex ghosts. Applications to fractional quantum gravity and its comparison with quadratic quantum gravity are discussed.

</details>


### [101] [Gravitational Reconstruction in $AdS_{4}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38887)
*David A. Lowe,Yiru Wang,Juanyi Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends linearized gravitational perturbation reconstruction in AdS₄ from boundary stress tensor data, using Lorentzian methods, light-cone cuts for spacelike-momentum-free data, and generalized functions for general momentum; it ensures FG gauge compatibility, normalizability, convergence, and connects to flat-space limits relevant to BMS and celestial holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically reconstruct bulk linearized gravitational perturbations in AdS₄ from boundary CFT-like data (conserved, traceless stress tensor) in a Lorentzian, real-space framework—extending prior work—and to establish rigorous, gauge-compatible, convergent reconstruction applicable to flat-space limits and asymptotic symmetry studies.

Method: Uses real Lorentzian analysis on conformally flat AdS₄ boundary; employs light-cone cut integrals for stress data with no spacelike Fourier support; applies boundary improvements to restore Fefferman–Graham gauge while preserving normalizability and linearized Weyl tensor; constructs explicit position-space generalized functions and boundary kernels for general (including zero-frequency) spacelike momentum; derives sufficient convergence conditions.

Result: Complete reconstruction of normalizable linearized metric perturbations in AdS₄ from physical boundary stress data; explicit boundary kernels satisfying FG gauge and normalizability; convergence guarantees under derived sufficient conditions; reduction to light-cone cut integrals in restricted momentum sector; formulation enabling controlled flat-space limits.

Conclusion: The reconstruction is gauge-consistent, convergent, and physically robust across momentum sectors; it provides a foundational real-space holographic map for linear gravity in AdS₄, bridging to Bondi–Metzner–Sachs symmetries and celestial holography via flat-space limits.

Abstract: We reconstruct linearized gravitational perturbations in $AdS_{4}$ from a conserved, traceless stress tensor on a fixed conformally flat boundary metric, extending our earlier real Lorentzian construction. For admissible data with no spacelike Fourier support in a flat conformal frame, the physical response reduces to an integral over boundary light-cone cuts. Boundary improvements restore Fefferman--Graham (FG) gauge while preserving normalizability and the linearized Weyl tensor. We then reconstruct perturbations with general spacelike boundary momentum, including nonzero frequency and its zero-frequency limit, using explicit position-space generalized functions on real stress data. Boundary kernels are constructed for normalizable perturbations and sufficient conditions are derived for the reconstruction to converge. The results motivate controlled flat-space limits relevant to Bondi--Metzner--Sachs symmetries and celestial holography.

</details>


### [102] [Are Black Hole and Cosmological Horizon Thermodynamics Necessarily Inequivalent?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39048)
*Zachary J. Hoelscher*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the equivalence between cosmological and black hole horizon thermodynamics in a toy universe with a non-rotating black hole and positive cosmological constant, focusing on the Nariai limit and parity-invariant QFT; it finds indistinguishability of local physics near both horizons semi-classically, suggesting a shared microscopic origin for their entropies in this regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test a recent conjecture that cosmological and black hole horizon thermodynamics are generically inequivalent in UV-complete quantum gravity, motivated by structural disparities—e.g., absence of string-theoretic features (like supersymmetry or AdS/CFT duality) in cosmological horizons.

Method: Analyzes a toy model: Schwarzschild–de Sitter spacetime in the Nariai limit (maximal black hole mass), assuming strict parity invariance of the quantum field theory; employs semi-classical gravity, neglecting quantum instability of the exact Nariai equilibrium, and compares local horizon physics via geometric and thermodynamic observables.

Result: In the semi-classical Nariai limit with parity-invariant QFT, an observer cannot locally distinguish physics near the black hole horizon from that near the cosmological horizon—implying identical leading-order thermodynamic behavior (e.g., temperature, entropy density) in their neighborhoods.

Conclusion: While not proving generic equivalence across all quantum gravity theories or backgrounds, the result challenges the strong inequivalence conjecture and implies that any consistent UV-complete theory describing black holes in de Sitter-like universes must unify the microscopic origins of black hole and cosmological horizon entropies—at least near the Nariai limit.

Abstract: It has recently been argued that cosmological horizon thermodynamics and black hole thermodynamics are generically inequivalent in any consistent, UV complete theory of quantum gravity, where it was suggested that this could be falsified by the identification of a counterexample. This conjecture was motivated in part by noting that cosmological horizons do not incorporate many of the convenient features found in the black holes described by string theory. In this work, we consider as a toy model a universe with a single non-rotating black hole and a positive cosmological constant. We work in the Nariai limit where the mass of the black hole is taken to its maximum possible value, and where we also assert that the quantum field theory living on this background is strictly parity invariant. In the semi-classical regime where we neglect instability of the exact Nariai equilibrium under quantum fluctuations, we find that the observer cannot readily distinguish the physics in the neighborhood of the black hole horizon from the physics in the neighborhood of the cosmological horizon. This does not prove that the two are generically equivalent in any universe, though it does suggest that they cannot be entirely unrelated in general. We propose that any consistent theory of quantum gravity that aims to describe black holes in a universe with a positive cosmological constant should provide a related microscopic origin for the leading semiclassical cosmological entropy and black hole entropy in the neighborhood of the particular limit considered here.

</details>


### [103] [Absorption of bound supernova axion-like particles by the neutron star weakens the heating limits on their photon coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39402)
*Probit J Kalita,Subha Mitra,Bharat Kumar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits constraints on axion-like particles (ALPs) coupled to protons using neutron star reheating, showing that ALP absorption via inverse nucleon bremsstrahlung in the neutron star strongly suppresses the reheating signal—except for ALPs on wide, nearly circular orbits—leading to revised, weaker but still significant exclusion limits on ALP mass (7.7–260 MeV) and photon coupling, sensitive to nuclear physics inputs (EoS, Δ(1232) isobars, bremsstrahlung rates).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct the earlier analysis by Bose et al. [arXiv:2610.30255] which neglected ALP absorption inside the neutron star, thereby overestimating the reheating contribution from bound ALPs and deriving overly stringent limits on ALP-photon coupling.

Method: The authors self-consistently compute protoneutron-star evolution, ALP production during core collapse, general-relativistic bound orbits, and ALP absorption via inverse nucleon bremsstrahlung; they vary twelve protoneutron-star cooling models, three normalized nucleon-bremsstrahlung rates, and equations of state with/without Δ(1232) isobars; they quantify survival probability, heating fraction, and exclusion contours using orbital penetration criteria and absorption timescales.

Result: Absorption eliminates nearly all bound ALPs whose orbits intersect the neutron star within minutes—reducing their heating contribution to only 9×10⁻⁹–6×10⁻⁵ of the total; surviving ALPs yield a revised exclusion window at mₐ ≈ 7.7–260 MeV, with lower gₐᵧ bound elevated by 280–700× compared to absorption-ignoring estimates; inclusion of pion conversion and Δ(1232) resonance widens the window; sensitivity is comparable across bremsstrahlung normalization and final-star radius.

Conclusion: ALP absorption is decisive for neutron-star reheating bounds: prior limits ignoring it are invalid; robust exclusions persist but are significantly weakened and nuanced, depending critically on nuclear EoS, resonance physics, and absorption microphysics—highlighting the need for consistent treatment of production, transport, and absorption in ALP astrophysical probes.

Abstract: Axion-like particles (ALPs) that couple to protons are produced in a core-collapse supernova, and the slow ones remain bound to the new neutron star. Bose, Ganguly, Kumar, and Raj [arXiv:2610.30255] proposed that the photons from the decay of this bound cloud reheat old neutron stars, and they derived strong limits on the ALP-photon coupling. They neglected the absorption of the ALPs by the star, but a bound ALP on an orbit that enters the star and reaches 50 km crosses it about $6\times 10^{18}$ times over the 333 Myr age of PSR J2144$-$3933. We show that inverse nucleon bremsstrahlung in the cold star absorbs every ALP whose orbit enters it within minutes, long before it decays, so only ALPs born outside the final star on nearly circular orbits survive; they carry $9\times 10^{-9} - 6\times 10^{-5}$ of the heating. We compute the protoneutron-star evolution, the production, the general-relativistic orbits and the absorption ourselves, for twelve protoneutron-star evolutions, three nucleon-bremsstrahlung rates normalized to the same supernova energy loss of light ALPs, and equations of state with and without $\Delta(1232)$ isobars. PSR J2144$-$3933 still excludes a window at $m_a\simeq 7.7-196$\ MeV, with a lower edge in the photon coupling $280-700$ times higher than without absorption. Pion conversion and the $\Delta(1232)$ resonance, which raise the production of surviving ALPs more than their absorption, widen this window to $m_a\simeq$ $7.7-260$\ MeV. The window depends at similar levels on the bremsstrahlung rate and on the radius of the final star. It requires a direct photon coupling that cancels the one induced by ALP-pion mixing, and existing supernova gamma-ray limits may already cover it.

</details>


### [104] [The Effective Kerr-Compton Tree Amplitude from Black Hole Perturbation Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40101)
*N. Emil J. Bjerrum-Bohr,Gang Chen,Konstantinos Papadimos,Nabha Shah,Chenliang Su*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: A new method is developed to compute the tree-level Compton amplitude for gravitational-wave scattering off a Kerr black hole in post-Minkowskian effective field theory, using black hole perturbation theory wave scattering functions to fix contact terms; explicit results up to sixth-order-in-spin are obtained, revealing novel transcendental structures and a leading dissipative contact term at sixth order, validated against known one-loop tail results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct the gravitational Compton amplitude for scattering off a spinning black hole in a systematic, physically consistent way within post-Minkowskian EFT—bypassing ambiguities from on-shell constraints alone by incorporating input from black hole perturbation theory.

Method: Combines post-Minkowskian effective field theory with wave scattering functions from black hole perturbation theory to iteratively fix higher-spin contact terms in the tree-level Compton amplitude; employs physical consistency (e.g., unitarity, causality) and matching to known perturbative solutions.

Result: Explicit tree-level Compton amplitude up to sixth order in black hole spin; discovery of previously unknown sectors at fifth and sixth orders; identification of complex transcendental structures and a leading dissipative contact term at sixth order in spin; successful reproduction of the one-loop tail effect from black hole perturbation theory.

Conclusion: The synergy between EFT and black hole perturbation theory enables unambiguous, high-precision amplitude construction beyond previous on-shell methods; the sixth-order result reveals novel dissipative physics and sets the stage for higher-loop and radiative-reaction calculations.

Abstract: We develop a new method to construct the tree-level Compton amplitude for gravitational-wave scattering off a Kerr black hole in post-Minkowskian effective field theory. Building on previous computations that required physical consistency of the amplitude, the method uses wave scattering functions from black hole perturbation theory to uniquely determine additional contact terms. We illustrate the iterative procedure by deriving explicit expressions up to sixth order in spin, including previously unknown sectors at the fifth and sixth orders in spin. The amplitude features complex transcendental structures and a leading dissipative contact term at sixth order in spin. We verify our result against known results in the literature and reproduce the one-loop tail result from black hole perturbation theory.

</details>


### [105] [Exact holographic superfluid at the Bogomolny point: transport, large charge, and Rényi entropies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40371)
*Siqing Yu*

Main category: hep-th
