TL;DR: This paper solves the Abelian Higgs model in AdS₅ analytically under special parameter conditions (BF-bound mass and Bogomolny coupling), yielding closed-form superfluid solutions across three AdS slicings—planar, global, and hyperbolic—each dual to distinct CFT states; it proves linear stability of nodeless condensates and derives exact physical quantities (e.g., two-fluid behavior, scaling dimensions, Rényi entropy phases).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain exact, analytic holographic superfluid solutions beyond numerical approaches, and to unify the interpretation of the Abelian Higgs model across different AdS slicings—thereby linking bulk physics to diverse CFT phenomena (finite-temperature superfluidity, ground-state energetics on S³, charged Rényi entropies).

Method: Exploits exact solvability when scalar mass saturates the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound and quartic coupling equals the Bogomolny value; constructs nodeless, static, uncharged probe-limit solutions on maximally symmetric static AdS₅ slicings (planar black brane, global AdS₅, hyperbolic slicing); performs linear stability analysis using standard quantization with nonnegative quartic coupling and zero superflow.

Result: Derives closed-form condensed-phase solutions for all three slicings: (i) planar black brane yields exact Gorter-Casimir two-fluid law, sound speed/attenuation; (ii) global AdS₅ gives exact scaling dimension Δ(Q) for charge-Q state on ℝ×S³; (iii) hyperbolic slicing yields homogeneous hairy black holes for charged Rényi entropy of a ball, with nonvanishing condensate at entangling surface and phase onset at Rényi index n=1. Proves linear stability of all such nodeless probe condensates.

Conclusion: The special-parameter Abelian Higgs model provides an exactly solvable, stable holographic superfluid framework whose solutions encode universal CFT data across geometries; the emergence of IR-fixed-point excitations beyond superfluid EFT highlights the richness of the probe sector’s dynamics.

Abstract: The Abelian Higgs model in AdS$_5$, in the probe limit, is a standard holographic model of a strongly coupled superfluid, usually solved numerically. When the scalar mass saturates the Breitenlohner-Freedman bound and the quartic coupling takes the Bogomolny value, its condensed phase can be found in closed form on every static, uncharged AdS$_5$ background with maximally symmetric spatial sections. We prove that it is linearly stable, as is any nodeless probe-limit condensate in the standard quantization with a nonnegative quartic coupling and no superflow. On each slicing it answers a different question about the dual conformal field theory. On the planar black brane it is the superfluid at all temperatures below $T_c$: the superfluid fraction follows the Gorter-Casimir two-fluid law exactly, and the speed and the attenuation of sound are found in closed form. In global AdS$_5$ it describes a state of charge $Q$ on $\mathbb{R}\times S^3$, arguably the lowest-energy one, and gives its scaling dimension in closed form at all $Q$, classically in the bulk. Its excitations include towers that the superfluid effective theory does not describe; they come from an infrared fixed point of the probe sector. The hyperbolic slicing gives in closed form a homogeneous hairy phase of the black holes that compute the charged Rényi entropies of a ball. Its condensate does not vanish at the entangling surface. When sourced by operators inserted there, the phase sets in at Rényi index exactly one at zero chemical potential, as found for a neutral scalar.

</details>


### [106] [Holographic duals of JT gravity at finite cutoff: the classical case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40391)
*Andreas Belaey,Thomas G. Mertens,Lorenzo Russo,Alex Tarana*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper formulates JT gravity with finite-cutoff boundaries and end-of-the-world branes using the first-order BF formalism, dynamically reduces it to a 1D boundary system, geometrically identifies boundary phase space degrees of freedom, derives a closed-form holographic Schwarzian description in phase space encoding the embedding of the cutoff curve in AdS₂, and sets up quantization (detailed in a companion paper).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand bulk locality and spacetime structure in JT gravity by incorporating finite-cutoff boundaries and end-of-the-world branes, and to geometrize the associated phase space degrees of freedom.

Method: First-order BF formulation of JT gravity; dynamical dimensional reduction to a one-dimensional boundary theory; symplectic analysis to identify geometric phase space variables; derivation of the holographic Schwarzian action directly in phase space for finite-cutoff boundaries.

Result: A geometric characterization of boundary phase space degrees of freedom; a closed-form phase-space Schwarzian description encoding the embedding of the finite-cutoff boundary curve in AdS₂; a consistent framework unifying finite-cutoff holography with brane dynamics in JT gravity.

Conclusion: The BF formulation provides a powerful geometric and symplectic framework for finite-cutoff JT gravity with branes, clarifying the origin of holographic boundary modes and enabling a precise phase-space formulation of bulk locality and AdS₂ embedding data.

Abstract: We formulate JT gravity with finite-cutoff boundaries and end-of-the-world branes in the first-order BF formulation. We dynamically reduce the BF model into a one-dimensional system living at the boundaries, generalizing earlier constructions. We obtain in particular a geometrical understanding of the phase space degrees of freedom associated to each of these boundaries. We leverage these results to probe bulk locality and the underlying structure of spacetime. Within our framework, we are able to derive a closed-form expression for the holographic Schwarzian description of JT gravity at finite-cutoff in phase space. The resulting degrees of freedom describe the embedding of the finite-cutoff curve in AdS$_2$. A quantization of these systems is presented in a companion paper.

</details>


### [107] [On Geometric Observables in Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40615)
*Manthos Karydas,Murat Kologlu,Allic Sivaramakrishnan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs coordinate-invariant, gravitationally dressed distance and time observables in perturbative quantum gravity, computes their leading graviton corrections in correlators, and shows their asymptotic limits yield memory and Shapiro time delay operators—bridging local geometric measurements with celestial holography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically define and compute quantum corrections to classical geometric observables (e.g., distance, time) in perturbative quantum gravity, addressing the lack of exploration of graviton-induced corrections to such quantities and their connection to observable asymptotic effects.

Method: Constructs finite-distance and finite-time observables using gravitational dressing to ensure diffeomorphism invariance; computes leading-order graviton contributions to their correlation functions in perturbative quantum gravity; takes asymptotic (null-infinity or celestial) limits to recover memory and Shapiro time delay operators.

Result: Explicit construction of dressed geometric observables; derivation of their graviton-corrected two- and higher-point correlators; demonstration that asymptotic limits reproduce known infrared observables—namely, gravitational memory and Shapiro time delay—as operator-valued quantities on the celestial sphere.

Conclusion: Finite geometric observables, when gravitationally dressed and quantized, provide a consistent framework linking local spacetime measurements to asymptotic infrared structures—offering a concrete operational basis for celestial holography and quantum gravity phenomenology.

Abstract: Measures of geometry like distance and time are staples of classical gravity. While geometric quantities arise broadly in quantum gravity, their graviton corrections are less explored. In this paper, we study geometric observables in perturbative quantum gravity. We form distance and time observables that model measurements, including in the presence of spacetime fluctuations. Gravitational dressing ensures these constructions are coordinate invariant. Then we show how these finite-distance observables become memory and Shapiro time delay operators upon taking asymptotic limits. We compute leading graviton effects in correlators of geometric observables throughout. Altogether, our results identify a class of geometric observables that link experiments and operations on the celestial sphere.

</details>


### [108] [Conformal Spectrum of (Super) Chiral Ising and XY Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38614)
*Oleg Antipin,Jahmall Bersini,Jacob Hafjall,Giulia Muco,Francesco Sannino*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the scaling dimension spectrum of composite operators in critical Gross-Neveu-Yukawa and NJL-Yukawa models at next-to-leading order semiclassically, resolves Lorentz structure, and obtains a closed-form spectrum at SUSY-enhanced parameter values—supporting emergent superconformal symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test and provide evidence for emergent superconformal symmetry in strongly coupled quantum field theories—specifically in chiral Ising and chiral XY universality classes—by computing operator spectra beyond leading order.

Method: Next-to-leading order semiclassical computation of scaling dimensions of composite operators; analysis of Lorentz representation structure; evaluation at parameter values where infrared supersymmetry is expected.

Result: Full operator spectrum resolved with Lorentz structure; closed-form expression for scaling dimensions at SUSY points; operators organized into superconformal multiplets.

Conclusion: The semiclassical spectrum organizes naturally into superconformal multiplets at SUSY parameters, strengthening the case for emergent superconformal dynamics in these Yukawa-type critical models.

Abstract: We determine the spectrum of scaling dimensions of composite operators in the critical Gross-Neveu-Yukawa and Nambu-Jona-Lasinio-Yukawa models through a next-to-leading order semiclassical computation. Their infrared fixed points belong to the chiral Ising and chiral XY universality classes, respectively. At next-to-leading order, we resolve the Lorentz structure of the operator spectrum. For the parameter values at which supersymmetry is expected to emerge in the infrared, we obtain a closed-form expression encoding the full spectrum within the semiclassical methodology. The resulting organization of operators into superconformal multiplets provides further evidence for emergent superconformal dynamics.

</details>


### [109] [Holomorphic fermions in $\text{AdS}_{2}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37762)
*Calvin Y.-R. Chen,Lukas W. Lindwasser*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies conserved, symmetric, traceless spinor-tensors for Majorana fermions on 2D AdS space with tuned masses, links them to holomorphic/anti-holomorphic half-integer spin currents, fully parametrises the classical phase space using arbitrary (anti-)holomorphic functions, reveals a hidden conformal symmetry via conformal Killing spinor-tensors, and identifies chiral algebras that are strict subalgebras of those in flat-space counterparts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover hidden symmetries and algebraic structures of massive Majorana fermions on two-dimensional Anti-de Sitter space, particularly exploring connections to conformal field theory and chiral algebras.

Method: Analysis of conserved spinor-tensors on AdS₂; use of complex coordinates and Laurent mode expansions; classical phase space parametrisation via arbitrary (anti-)holomorphic functions; covariant reformulation using conformal Killing spinor-tensors; comparison of chiral algebras with flat-space theory.

Result: Existence of symmetric, traceless conserved spinor-tensors corresponding to half-integer spin currents; full classical phase space parameterisation by arbitrary (anti-)holomorphic functions; covariant formulation in terms of conformal Killing spinor-tensors; identification of strict chiral subalgebras relative to flat space.

Conclusion: Massive Majorana fermions on AdS₂ exhibit rich hidden conformal structure — encoded in conserved spinor-tensors and chiral algebras — suggesting deeper integrability and symmetry beyond standard flat-space expectations.

Abstract: On two-dimensional Anti-de Sitter space, Majorana fermions with a discrete set of finely tuned, non-zero mass parameters possess conserved spinor--tensors that are symmetric and traceless. In complex coordinates, these correspond to pairs of holomorphic and anti-holomorphic half-integer spin currents and admit Laurent series mode expansions reminiscent of standard two-dimensional conformal field theories. We exploit this fact in order to fully parameterise the classical phase space associated with the equation of motion in terms of arbitrary (anti-)holomorphic functions. We further show that this parameterisation can be written covariantly in terms of a conformal Killing spinor--tensor, hinting at a hidden conformal symmetry. We also show they possess chiral algebras which are strict subalgebras of the one present in the corresponding theory on flat space.

</details>


### [110] [Quiver Lab -- A Computing Tool for Quiver Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37766)
*Zhenghao Zhong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Quiver Lab is a browser-based tool for visualizing quivers and analyzing moduli spaces of 3D N=4 supersymmetric theories, computing Hilbert series, plethystic logarithms, refinements, and Hasse diagrams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an accessible, interactive platform for studying the geometry and dynamics of 3D N=4 supersymmetric gauge theories via quiver representation and moduli space analysis.

Method: Implements computational algebraic geometry and representation-theoretic algorithms in a web-based interface, supporting both guided (Light) and advanced (Expert) user workflows for computing Higgs/Coulomb branch data and Hasse diagrams.

Result: Enables efficient computation and visualization of Hilbert series, plethystic logarithms, supported refinements, and Decay/Fission Hasse diagrams for 3D N=4 quiver gauge theories.

Conclusion: Quiver Lab bridges theoretical physics and computational tools, facilitating exploration and verification of moduli space structures in 3D N=4 theories.

Abstract: Quiver Lab is a browser-based workspace for drawing quivers and exploring the moduli spaces of three-dimensional $\mathcal{N} = 4$ theories. It computes Higgs and Coulomb branch Hilbert series, plethystic logarithms, supported refinements, and Decay and Fission Hasse diagrams. This note explains the Light and Expert workflows and the main computational methods

</details>


### [111] [Go with the flow(s): radial de Sitter holography and $T^2 (+ Λ_2)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37776)
*Matthew J. Blacker*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs Wheeler-DeWitt states localized on Schwarzschild-de Sitter spacetime, develops a slice-independent worldtube holographic framework in the static patch, and establishes a dictionary linking it to cut-off holography frameworks ($T^2$ and $T^2 + \Lambda_2$), thereby providing a bulk realization of $T^2 + \Lambda_2$ for dS$_4$ black holes; it identifies the worldtube’s average Hamiltonian as the seed energy and shows that recovering the full $T^2 (+ \Lambda_2)$ spectrum requires sacrificing slice-independence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge bulk gravitational physics in de Sitter (dS) spacetimes—particularly Schwarzschild-dS—with candidate boundary duals, and to clarify mutual constraints between putative dS holography and bulk structure via concrete midisuperspace quantum states.

Method: Constructs midisuperspace Wheeler-DeWitt states localized on Schwarzschild-de Sitter geometry; builds a slice-independent worldtube holographic framework in the static patch; derives dictionary mappings to $T^2$ and $T^2 + \\Lambda_2$ cut-off holography; identifies and analyzes the average Hamiltonian as seed energy; examines implications of enforcing spectral matching via slice-dependence.

Result: A bulk-realized $T^2 + \\Lambda_2$ framework for dS$_4$ black holes; a worldtube holographic dictionary unifying static-patch bulk description with cut-off duals; identification of average Hamiltonian as seed energy; demonstration that exact spectral recovery necessitates slice-dependent formulation, revealing a fundamental trade-off between locality and spectral fidelity.

Conclusion: The work establishes a concrete, state-based connection between bulk dS black hole geometries and $T^2 (+ \\Lambda_2)$ holography, showing that while a geometrically natural (slice-independent) worldtube yields the correct seed energy, full spectral agreement with $T^2 (+ \\Lambda_2)$ demands breaking slice-independence—highlighting tension between diffeomorphism invariance and boundary energy quantization in dS holography.

Abstract: What can we learn about a putative dual to de Sitter spacetime from the bulk? Conversely, what can a candidate dual theory teach us about the bulk? In this work, we construct midisuperspace Wheeler-DeWitt states that are localised on the Schwarzschild-de Sitter spacetime. Using these states, we construct a slice-independent worldtube holographic framework in the static patch. We then construct a dictionary between this worldtube proposal and both the $T^2$ and $T^2 + \Lambda_2$ frameworks for cut-off holography. In doing so, we provide a bulk realisation of $T^2+ \Lambda_2$ for dS$_4$ black holes. We identify the average Hamiltonian of our worldtube proposal as the seed energy of the $T^2 (+ \Lambda_2)$ theories. To recover the $T^2 (+ \Lambda_2)$ energy spectrum as the average Hamiltonian of a worldtube theory, one must make the bulk framework slice-dependent, and we discuss the consequences of this trade-off.

</details>


### [112] [Differential Equations for Dressed "in-in" Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37856)
*Ivo Sachs*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends differential equation and Integration-by-Parts (IBP) techniques to cosmological in-in correlators of conformally coupled scalars, treating them as dressed flat-space amplitudes; it demonstrates algorithmic formulation and functional insights for bubble-triangle and exchange diagrams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop systematic, algorithmic tools—borrowed from flat-space scattering amplitudes—for analyzing cosmological in-in correlators, especially to characterize the analytic structure (e.g., transcendental functions) arising from loop-level correlators in de Sitter space.

Method: Extends the differential equations method and Integration-by-Parts identities—standard in QFT loop calculations—to conformally coupled scalar in-in correlators in cosmology; leverages their representation as 'dressed' flat-space amplitudes; applies the formalism concretely to the bubble-triangle and exchange diagrams.

Result: Derives tractable differential equations for these cosmological correlators; shows they enable efficient determination of the class of functions (e.g., polylogarithms, elliptic integrals) the integrals evaluate to; confirms algorithmic constructibility of such equations.

Conclusion: Differential equations + IBP provide a powerful, generalizable, and algorithmic framework for studying analytic properties of cosmological correlators, bridging flat-space amplitude methods with inflationary observables.

Abstract: We extend the formalism of differential equations and Integration-by-Parts identities, to cosmological in-in correlators of conformally coupled scalar fields, that can be formulated as dressed flat space amplitudes. These differential equations turn out to be quite powerful for extracting information about the class of functions which the corresponding amplitudes integrate to. Furthermore, they can be formulated algorithmically. Here we illustrate this for the cosmological bubble- triangle and exchange diagram.

</details>


### [113] [Near-horizon BF bounds for the $t^+$ KK tower on extremal AdS$_5$ black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37908)
*Xiao-Long Liu,Zheng Miao,Yuezhang Tang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the charged Breitenlohner–Freedman (BF) bound for horizon harmonics in the type IIB $t^+$ Kaluza–Klein tower on extremal charged AdS$_5$ black holes with equal angular momenta, using 5D gauged supergravity and linearized exceptional field theory; it identifies regimes—BPS, $\Omega_H > 1$, and $\Phi_H > 1$—where modes either satisfy or violate the bound, reveals discretization-dependent stability patterns, and shows that higher-level (e.g., level-four) modes can violate the bound even when lower-spin U(2) singlets do not—though full radial stability remains unresolved.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine stability conditions—specifically, whether scalar modes in the type IIB $t^+$ Kaluza–Klein tower satisfy the charged BF bound—on extremal charged AdS$_5$ black holes with equal rotations, as a probe of near-horizon quantum gravity consistency and potential instabilities beyond standard truncations.

Method: Analyzes the scalar wave equation derived from maximal five-dimensional gauged supergravity (level zero) and linearized exceptional field theory (higher levels); classifies behavior across parameter space defined by horizon angular velocity $\\Omega_H$ and electric potential $\\Phi_H$; examines mode-by-mode BF bound satisfaction/violation as function of charge, spin, KK level, and black hole parameters; leverages symmetry (U(2) singlets) and asymptotic scaling (large radius, fixed rotation).

Result: The charged BF bound is universally satisfied on the BPS curve ($\\Omega_H = \\Phi_H = 1$); violated for all black holes with $\\Omega_H > 1$, though no finite truncation detects violation everywhere; below the BPS curve ($\\Phi_H > 1$), BF-passing modes occupy only a thin strip governed by charge/spin quantization; a level-four mode with spin $\\propto$ horizon radius violates the bound even where all U(2) singlets satisfy it.

Conclusion: Near-horizon stability of the $t^+$ KK tower is highly sensitive to global black hole parameters and KK level—discrete quantum numbers and higher-level excitations fundamentally affect the BF bound, implying that low-energy effective truncations are insufficient to assess full stability; radial stability remains an open problem.

Abstract: We study the charged Breitenlohner--Freedman (BF) bound for all horizon harmonics of the type IIB $t^+$ Kaluza--Klein tower on extremal charged AdS$_5$ black holes with equal rotations. The scalar equation follows from maximal five-dimensional gauged supergravity at level zero and the linearized exceptional-field-theory equations at higher levels. For aligned charge and rotation, a quadratic form in charge and spin changes signature on the curve of supersymmetric (BPS) black holes, where every mode satisfies the bound. Above the curve, where $\Omega_H>1$, every black hole has a violating mode, although no finite truncation in level or spin detects violation at every such black hole. Below the curve, where $\Phi_H>1$, the BF-passing set is confined to a thin strip and controlled by charge and spin discreteness. At large radius and fixed rotation, a level-four mode with spin proportional to the radius violates the bound even where all U(2) singlets satisfy it. These results concern the near-horizon BF bound of one tower in two-derivative supergravity; full radial stability remains open.

</details>


### [114] [Quotient Quiver Subtraction -- Product Groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38052)
*Sam Bennett,Amihay Hanany,Guhesh Kumaran*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'quotient quiver subtraction'—a combinatorial method, realized via brane systems, to gauge emergent U(n)×U(n′) and SU(n)×SU(n′) Coulomb branch global symmetries in 3d N=4 theories, using symplectisation of unitary nodes; validated through examples and linked to Class S and maximal implosion theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically implement gauging of emergent Coulomb branch global symmetries—specifically U(n)×U(n′) and SU(n)×SU(n′)—in 3d N=4 quiver gauge theories, translating physical gauging into a tractable combinatorial operation on quivers.

Method: Uses Type IIB brane constructions to derive quotient quiver subtraction: a two-step gauging procedure wherein unitary gauge nodes are symplectised (i.e., replaced by symplectic nodes) to realize the desired global symmetry gauging; formalized as a combinatorial operation on quiver diagrams.

Result: A well-defined, brane-realized algorithm for gauging U(n)×U(n′) and SU(n)×SU(n′) symmetries on the Coulomb branch; explicit verification across multiple quiver examples; established connections to 3d Class S theories and maximal implosion spaces.

Conclusion: Quotient quiver subtraction provides a precise, geometrically grounded, and computationally effective framework for gauging emergent Coulomb branch symmetries in 3d N=4 theories, unifying and extending recent techniques like symplectisation and quiver subtraction.

Abstract: Quotient quiver subtraction translates the gauging of Coulomb branch global symmetries into a combinatoric operation on 3d $\mathcal{N}=4$ gauge theories. This work uses brane systems to introduce a systematic method to gauge $\textrm{U}(n)\times\textrm{U}(n')$ and $\textrm{SU}(n)\times\textrm{SU}(n')$ emergent Coulomb branch global symmetries. Similar to other recently-introduced techniques, the gauging operation is a two-step process; in this case, it involves the symplectisation of unitary gauge factors into symplectic groups. A host of explicit examples are used to verify the procedure, and connections are made to Class $\mathcal{S}$ theories and three-dimensional theories realising maximal implosion spaces.

</details>


### [115] [Covering Surfaces, Conical-Defect Continuation, and Multi-Entropy at Arbitrary Genus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38208)
*Chen-Te Ma*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives normalized geometric factors for permutation-twist correlators at arbitrary genus, links the real part of the conical-defect exponent to the covering-space anomaly via hypergeometric uniformization, identifies its imaginary part as an Euler-characteristic Weyl phase, extends normalization to more branch points, computes higher replica-index corrections to geometric multi-entropy, and specifies an extra condition beyond large central charge needed for a holographic limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously define and normalize permutation-twist correlators on higher-genus Riemann surfaces for entanglement entropy calculations in 2D CFTs, especially in contexts where holography and replica symmetry are relevant.

Method: Uses hypergeometric uniformization for three uniformly ramified branch points; analytically continues conical-defect exponents; computes covering-space anomalies and Weyl phases; generalizes normalization to arbitrary numbers of branch values; derives higher-order replica-index corrections using geometric and conformal techniques; examines reducible topological examples to isolate limitations of large-c approximations.

Result: Normalized geometric factors for permutation-twist correlators at arbitrary genus; equivalence between real part of continued conical-defect exponent and covering-space anomaly factor; identification of imaginary part as Euler-characteristic Weyl phase; explicit higher replica-index corrections to geometric multi-entropy; identification of an additional constraint—beyond large central charge—for a consistent holographic limit.

Conclusion: Large central charge is necessary but insufficient for fixing full entanglement structure in holographic CFTs; precise geometric normalization, replica corrections, and topological consistency conditions are essential for reliable bulk reconstruction and entropy calculations.

Abstract: We derive normalized geometric factors for permutation-twist correlators at arbitrary genus. For three uniformly ramified branch values, hypergeometric uniformization shows that the real part of the continued conical-defect exponent equals the covering-space anomaly factor. Its imaginary part is an Euler-characteristic Weyl phase. We extend the normalization to additional branch values, derive higher replica-index corrections to the geometric multi-entropy, and state the extra condition required for a holographic limit. A reducible conformal/topological example demonstrates why large central charge alone does not fix the complete entanglement result.

</details>


### [116] [Entropically favored scalar hair on AdS black branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38226)
*Alex Buchel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs neutral AdS₅ black branes with stable, source-free scalar hair favored entropically over the symmetric phase, using a quadratic scalar–Gauss–Bonnet coupling and a simple quadratic scalar potential; the model was discovered via neural-network–guided search.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a minimal, controllable holographic system where spontaneous symmetry breaking and scalar hair formation occur thermodynamically (entropically) preferred over the symmetric black brane phase, without altering UV operator dimension.

Method: Constructs fully backreacted neutral AdS₅ black brane solutions in Einstein–scalar–Gauss–Bonnet gravity with a quadratic scalar potential; employs neural-network–based parameter space exploration to discover the viable model.

Result: Finds explicit hairy black brane solutions with source-free scalar hair that are entropically dominant over the symmetric phase; confirms stability of the hairy branch under the quadratic scalar–Gauss–Bonnet coupling while preserving Δ = 3 UV scaling dimension.

Conclusion: A simple, fully backreacted holographic model of spontaneous symmetry breaking is achieved with minimal ingredients—quadratic scalar potential and quadratic scalar–Gauss–Bonnet coupling—demonstrating that neural-network–assisted theory discovery can efficiently locate physically meaningful effective field theories.

Abstract: We construct neutral AdS$_5$ black branes with source-free scalar hair that is entropically favored over the symmetric phase. A quadratic scalar--Gauss--Bonnet coupling destabilizes the thermal state while leaving the ultraviolet operator dimension fixed at $\Delta=3$. The fully backreacted solutions require only a quadratic scalar potential. We describe how a neural-network search led to this simple model.

</details>


### [117] [Tachyon and massless sectors of the bosonic string effective action](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38364)
*Mohammad R. Garousi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies a minimal, T-duality-invariant basis for the bosonic string effective action up to six derivatives, separating couplings into massless and tachyon sectors; it fixes all massless-sector couplings at six-derivative order and determines two tachyon-sector couplings (one fixed by four-tachyon S-matrix), ensuring consistency with sphere-level S-matrix expansions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies between T-duality constraints and S-matrix predictions in the bosonic string effective action, particularly regarding field redefinitions needed for full Buscher-rule invariance (except for metric, dilaton, and tachyon).

Method: Imposes T-duality invariance under circle reduction on a minimal coupling basis where tachyon appears only via covariant derivatives or its potential; enforces consistency with sphere-level string S-matrix elements (2 tachyons + 2 massless, and 4 tachyons) to fix coupling constants and determine required field redefinitions.

Result: Full determination of six-derivative couplings in the massless sector (all constants fixed); identification of two T-dual tachyon-sector couplings (one arbitrary, one fixed by four-tachyon S-matrix); verification that leading-order action plus massless-sector couplings reproduce the two-tachyon–two-massless S-matrix at six-momentum order.

Conclusion: The effective action admits a clean separation into T-duality-covariant massless and tachyon sectors; S-matrix consistency fully constrains the massless six-derivative sector and partially fixes the tachyon sector, validating the minimal basis and required field redefinition.

Abstract: Recently, it has been observed that in certain schemes of the bosonic string effective action, the non-derivative tachyon appears only in the tachyon potential, while all other couplings involve covariant derivatives of the tachyon and massless fields. Within these schemes, a minimal basis can be found whose coupling constants are constrained by T-duality upon circle reduction. Using this constraint, one obtains sets of couplings at each order of \(\alpha'\) that are invariant under Buscher rules supplemented by higher-derivative corrections for all base-space fields except the tachyon. In this paper, we observe that consistency of these T-dual sets with S-matrix elements requires an appropriate field redefinition, such that each set becomes invariant under Buscher rules supplemented by higher-derivative corrections for all base-space fields except the metric, dilaton, and tachyon. This separates the couplings into a massless sector and a tachyon sector. We determine the six-derivative couplings in the massless sector, with all coupling constants fixed, and find two T-dual couplings in the tachyon sector with arbitrary coupling constants. We show that the leading-order action and the massless-sector couplings exactly reproduce the expansion of the sphere-level S-matrix element of two tachyons and two massless vertex operators at six-momentum order, and that the coupling constant of the four-tachyon coupling in the tachyon sector is fixed by the expansion of the four-tachyon S-matrix element at six-momentum order.

</details>


### [118] [The Topological String on the Quintic to Genus 83 and the Rank-Two D4-D2-D0 Indices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38473)
*Michael Hecht*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes high-genus (2–83) quintic Gopakumar-Vafa invariants up to degree 44 using holomorphic anomaly equations, resolving holomorphic ambiguities and polar D4-D2-D0 coefficients via wall-crossing and modularity constraints—yielding complete GV, PT, and DT data through degree 32.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute high-genus Gopakumar-Vafa invariants for the quintic Calabi–Yau threefold, where direct enumeration fails and modular/wall-crossing structures must be leveraged to fix ambiguities in the holomorphic anomaly framework.

Method: Integration of holomorphic anomaly equations; imposition of rank-one modularity on Gieseker invariants and rank-two charge conjugation symmetry; solution of coupled boundary and surplus wall-crossing equations; computer-assisted extension of wall crossing to close genus recursion; use of low-genus input and mock modularity for extrapolation.

Result: Quintic GV invariants fully determined for genera 2–83 and degrees ≤32; partial results up to degree 44 and genus 216; 22 of 36 polar rank-two D4-D2-D0 coefficients fixed unconditionally; conditional candidates provided for genera 84–85 and remaining polar coefficients using mock modularity.

Conclusion: The holomorphic anomaly equations, combined with refined wall-crossing and modularity constraints, enable robust, high-genus GV computations on the quintic—demonstrating the predictive power of BPS structure and providing benchmark data for DT/PT correspondence and topological string theory.

Abstract: We compute quintic Gopakumar-Vafa invariants for genera 2-83 and degrees up to 44 by integrating the holomorphic anomaly equations. Under rank-one modularity for the required Gieseker invariants and rank-two charge conjugation, a coupled system of boundary and surplus wall-crossing equations fixes the holomorphic ambiguities and 22 of the 36 polar rank-two D4-D2-D0 coefficients, without assuming any rank-two index. A computer-assisted extension of wall crossing supplies additional equations and the boundary datum needed to close the recursion through genus 83. With known low-genus input, the GV data are complete through degree 32 and yield PT and DT tables. Selected higher-genus wall-crossing invariants reach genus 216; 33 entries require additional hypotheses. Retrospectively selected extrapolations give conditional candidates at genera 84 and 85 and, with rank-two mock modularity, for the remaining polar coefficients.

</details>


### [119] [Bootstrapping Wilson lines in Critical Bosonic QED$_3$ Part 1: Large-$N$ endpoint correlators and CFT data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38663)
*Jun Maeda,Toshiki Onagi,Soichiro Shimamori*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes conformal Wilson line observables in critical bosonic QED₃ using diagrammatic four-point functions and endpoint crossing, yielding exact 1/N results for defect CFT data—including endpoint dimensions, g-function, and OPE coefficients—and finds unexpected bulk-defect spectral agreement in all non-singlet flavor channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute defect CFT data (e.g., endpoint dimension, g-function, OPE coefficients) for conformal Wilson lines in critical bosonic QED₃, and to test universality between bulk and defect spectra in strongly coupled gauge theories.

Method: Combines perturbative diagrammatic computation of four-point correlators with endpoint crossing symmetry—formulated covariantly under full SU(N) flavor symmetry—and expands results to first subleading order in 1/N while keeping positivity manifest.

Result: Closed-form 1/N expressions for four-endpoint and mixed (Wilson-line + Hubbard–Stratonovich) correlators; exact determination of endpoint scaling dimension Δ_q, defect g-function, and quantum numbers of lowest non-singlet Wilson-line primaries; bulk-defect spectral agreement in all non-singlet SU(N) channels at O(1/N), including OPE-weighted anomalous dimension averages at degenerate levels.

Conclusion: Endpoint crossing + diagrammatics provides a powerful, positivity-respecting framework for defect CFT in vector models; the observed bulk-defect spectral match suggests a hidden integrability or symmetry relating bulk higher-spin currents to Wilson-line excitations in critical QED₃.

Abstract: We study the conformal Wilson line with general charge $q$ in critical bosonic QED$_3$ with $N$ complex scalar flavors by combining diagrammatic four-point calculations with endpoint crossing. We formulate the endpoint crossing equations with the full $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ flavor structure, keeping positivity manifest. For arbitrary positive integer charge $q$ held fixed at large $N$, we obtain closed-form expressions for four-endpoint correlators and mixed correlators involving two bulk Hubbard--Stratonovich fields through first subleading order in $1/N$. These results determine the endpoint dimension, the defect $g$-function, and the dimensions and endpoint OPE coefficients of the lowest non-singlet Wilson-line primaries. Moreover, combining the diagrammatic correlator with endpoint crossing, somewhat surprisingly, we find agreement between the bulk and Wilson-line spectra in every non-singlet flavor channel through order $1/N$. At degenerate levels, this agreement concerns the OPE-weighted averages of anomalous dimensions probed by the endpoint correlator. For unit charge, we independently reproduce these averages from the known bulk higher-spin spectrum.

</details>


### [120] [Topological interfaces between universes and generalized families of 2d QFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38680)
*Pinak Banerjee,Yuhan Gai,Alonso Perez-Lona,Daniel Robbins,Subham Roy,Eric Sharpe,with an appendix by Xingyang Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies 2D quantum field theories with topological point operators, showing they decompose into 'universes'; it introduces multi-fusion categories as finite symmetries governing interfaces between universes, classifies interface types (I–III) based on categorical structure, derives symmetries for orbifolds with trivially acting abelian subgroups, constructs Type III pairs via 2-group extensions and RG flows, and proposes a correspondence with generalized QFT families.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize the symmetry structure and inter-universe relations in 2D QFTs that decompose due to topological point operators—extending beyond invertible symmetries using multi-fusion categories.

Method: Uses categorical algebra (multi-fusion categories and action functors) to formalize finite symmetries; classifies topological interfaces between universes by invertibility and indecomposability; computes inherited symmetries for orbifolds; constructs Type III examples via 2-group extensions and RG flows; draws analogies with equivariant and categorical QFT families.

Result: A complete classification of universe pairs into three types (I: invertible interfaces within indecomposable summands; II: non-invertible interfaces within summands; III: no interfaces between distinct summands); full symmetry characterization for abelian-trivially-acting orbifolds (yielding only Types I/II); explicit constructions of Type III pairs; and a proposed correspondence with Brennan–Intriligator's generalized QFT families.

Conclusion: Multi-fusion categories provide the correct algebraic framework for finite symmetries of decomposing 2D QFTs; the type-I/II/III classification reflects deep structural features of the symmetry category; and this framework unifies disparate phenomena—including orbifold interfaces, RG flows, and generalized QFT families—under a common categorical symmetry language.

Abstract: Two-dimensional quantum field theories with topological point operators decompose into universes. Just as fusion categories have played an important role in understanding non-invertible symmetries, multi-fusion categories play an analogous role for the symmetries of such decomposing theories. We describe a finite symmetry as a multi-fusion category together with its action functor, which keeps track of trivially acting symmetries and of the topological interfaces between universes. Relative to a chosen symmetry, we classify pairs of universes by the topological interfaces it contains, which reflect the structure of the multi-fusion category: universes in different indecomposable summands are joined by no interface (Type~III), while within a summand they are related by invertible (Type~I) or only non-invertible (Type~II) interfaces. For orbifolds with a trivially acting abelian normal subgroup, we derive the full inherited symmetry, which characterizes the operators between universes found case by case by Robbins, Sharpe and Vandermeulen and yields pairs of Types~I and~II but never of Type~III. We construct Type~III pairs instead from extensions of $2$-group type and from renormalization group flows out of a common ultraviolet theory. Finally, we propose a correspondence with the generalized families of quantum field theories of Brennan and Intriligator, in which equivariant families correspond to Type~I pairs and categorical ones to Type~II pairs whose gauging preserves the symmetry, family anomalies appear as universes carrying different symmetry-protected topological phases, and Type~III pairs can realize families over a discrete parameter. An appendix by Xingyang Yu works out the higher-charge Schwinger model as a Lagrangian example.

</details>


### [121] [Collective Bootstrap for Matrix Quantum Mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38716)
*Xianlong Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a collective bootstrap framework for large-N matrix quantum mechanics using loop space of single-trace coordinate moments; it reformulates the energy minimization as a semidefinite program via virial theorem and Schur complement, yielding rigorous lower bounds on planar ground state energies, validated on 1–3 matrix models and extendable to BFSS/BMN bosonic sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations in conventional moment-based or master-field approaches for large-N matrix quantum mechanics—particularly difficulties in handling momentum-containing moments, enforcing planar factorization, and scaling to multi-matrix models—the authors aim to develop a systematic, constraint-respecting, and computationally tractable bootstrap method grounded purely in coordinate loops.

Method: The method constructs a collective theory in loop space of single-trace coordinate moments; uses loop joining/splitting to encode kinetic constraints; applies the virial theorem to eliminate momentum variables; employs Schur complement and relaxation of quadratic loop products to convert the nonlinear constrained energy minimization into a semidefinite program (SDP); and implements a sequential SDP heuristic enforcing planar factorization to estimate ground state energy and loop expectations.

Result: The framework produces certified lower bounds on the planar ground state energy at each truncation level; achieves high-accuracy agreement with known analytic results (e.g., Gaussian and fuzzy-sphere solutions) and master-field optimization for one-, two-, and three-matrix models; and enables higher-word-length truncations than prior coordinate-moment methods.

Conclusion: The collective bootstrap framework provides a rigorous, scalable, and coordinate-only approach to non-perturbative large-N matrix quantum mechanics; it is generalizable to more complex models—including the bosonic sectors of BFSS and BMN—and establishes semidefinite programming as a powerful tool for planar spectral bounds in quantum mechanical matrix models.

Abstract: We develop a collective bootstrap framework for large $N$ matrix quantum mechanics formulated directly in the loop space of single trace coordinate moments. Loop joining and splitting operations organize the kinetic constraints systematically and avoid treating momentum containing moments as independent variables. Employing the virial theorem, a Schur complement, and a relaxation of quadratic products of loops, we recast the nonlinear constrained optimization of the collective energy into a semidefinite program, which yields a lower bound on the planar ground state energy at every truncation. A sequential semidefinite optimization heuristic then searches for configurations satisfying planar factorization and estimates the ground state energy and loop expectation values. Applications to one-, two-, and three-matrix models exhibit close agreement with analytic solutions and master field optimization. By working exclusively with coordinate moments, the framework permits truncations at higher word length and offers a promising approach to models with more matrices, including the bosonic sectors of the BFSS and BMN models.

</details>


### [122] [Elliptic master integrals and the one-loop cosmological wavefunction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38725)
*Amara McCune,Matthew D. Schwartz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop cosmological wavefunction coefficients using a bootstrap approach, showing that the triangle coefficient is polylogarithmic (dilogarithmic) despite elliptic master integrals, due to cancellation of elliptic periods; in contrast, the box coefficient is non-polylogarithmic and involves elliptic integrals and K3 surface periods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether loop-level cosmological wavefunction coefficients inherit the transcendental complexity (e.g., ellipticity) of their associated Feynman integrals, particularly for conformally coupled scalars in de Sitter space.

Method: Bootstrap approach leveraging analyticity, locality, and flat-space limit constraints; residue analysis of loop integrands; explicit computation of threshold discontinuities and period structures.

Result: The one-loop triangle wavefunction coefficient is rational in external momenta and expressible in dilogarithms—elliptic periods cancel exactly; the one-loop box coefficient is non-polylogarithmic, with elliptic threshold discontinuities and K3 surface periods emerging for tetrahedral kinematics.

Conclusion: Transcendental weight of cosmological wavefunction coefficients is not dictated solely by integral topology but by physical constraints (e.g., flat-space amplitude residues), leading to simplifications (triangle) or enhanced geometry (box/K3).

Abstract: Loop corrections to the cosmological wavefunction involve similar integrals to those required for scattering amplitudes. For the one-loop triangle of a conformally coupled scalar, the master integrals are elliptic, and so the triangle wavefunction coefficient was expected to be elliptic as well. Using a bootstrap approach, we compute this triangle coefficient exactly and show that it is not: the elliptic periods cancel, and the flat-space wavefunction coefficient is a combination of dilogarithms. The cancellation occurs because the residue of the loop integrand on the elliptic sector is a flat-space amplitude, rational in the loop momentum. The one-loop box, by contrast, is not polylogarithmic, even with a single external leg at each site. Its threshold discontinuity involves elliptic integrals, which we also give in closed form. For regular-tetrahedron momenta the periods of a K3 surface enter its loop integrand.

</details>


### [123] [From pq-webs to magnetic quivers and back I: dictionary and deformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38769)
*Sebastiano Garavaglia,Sara Pasquetti,Palash Singh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an exact parameter dictionary linking 5D N=1 SCFTs (engineered by (p,q)-brane webs) to their 3D N=4 magnetic quivers, specifically for N+SU(N)_0^{k−1}+N box quivers; it tests the dictionary via deformations (masses, weak coupling, VEVs) using the superconformal index and squashed-sphere partition function, enabling quantitative reconstruction of 5D physics from 3D observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively relate five-dimensional N=1 superconformal field theories—constructed via (p,q) five-brane webs—to their associated three-dimensional N=4 magnetic quivers, and to enable precise deformation analysis of the 5D SCFT using 3D tools.

Method: Proposes and tests an exact dictionary mapping 5D flavor masses and instanton couplings to Fayet-Iliopoulos (FI) parameters of the magnetic quiver; employs the 5D superconformal index under various deformations and matches them to FI deformations and monopole VEVs in the 3D squashed-sphere partition function, using a novel method tracking Higgsing, decoupled sectors, and IR limits.

Result: The dictionary is validated across mass deformations, weak-coupling limits, and vacuum expectation values; the squashed-sphere partition function analysis confirms consistency of FI deformations and monopole VEVs; enables exact reconstruction of 5D physics from 3D observables and navigation of the full deformation network bidirectionally.

Conclusion: Magnetic quivers are promoted from qualitative classification tools to quantitative instruments for studying deformations of 5D N=1 SCFTs; the established parameter dictionary provides a rigorous bridge between 5D dynamics and computable 3D observables.

Abstract: This is the first of two companion papers in which we study five-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ theories engineered by $(p,q)$ five-brane webs and their associated three-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=4$ magnetic quivers. We focus on the $N+\mathrm{SU}(N)_0^{k-1}+N$ box quiver theories. We propose an exact parameter dictionary relating the five-dimensional flavour masses and instanton couplings to the Fayet-Iliopoulos parameters of the corresponding strong-coupling magnetic quiver. We test this dictionary through a broad class of deformations, including mass deformations, weak-coupling limits, and vacuum expectation values. On the five-dimensional side, we implement these flows directly at the level of the superconformal index. The dictionary fixes the corresponding FI deformations and monopole VEVs. We analyse them in the magnetic-quiver squashed-sphere partition function using a new method that tracks Higgsing, decoupled sectors, and infrared theories. Our results promote magnetic quivers to tools for the quantitative study of how the five-dimensional superconformal index responds to deformations. Once the parameter dictionary is known, exact three-dimensional observables can be used to reconstruct the corresponding five-dimensional physics and to navigate the full deformation network in both directions.

</details>


### [124] [From pq-webs to magnetic quivers and back II: distributional indices and 5d theories breaking bad](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38770)
*Sebastiano Garavaglia,Sara Pasquetti,Palash Singh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores nilpotent moment-map VEV deformations in 5D N=1 SCFTs engineered by (p,q) five-brane webs, linking them to magnetic quivers and revealing distributional behavior of the superconformal index under excessive Higgsing—paralleling 'bad/broken' 3D theories—and interprets these via Hanany-Witten moves and tessellations of generalized toric diagrams.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nilpotent Higgs branch deformations in 5D N=1 SCFTs (engineered by (p,q) five-brane webs) map to 3D magnetic quiver deformations, especially when the dictionary between 5D theories and their magnetic quivers at infinite coupling is exact, and to clarify the physical meaning of 'excessive Higgsing' and its impact on the superconformal index.

Method: Uses the exact parameter dictionary between 5D N=1 theories (e.g., N+SU(N)_0^{k−1}+N) and their 3D magnetic quivers established in the companion paper; analyzes nilpotent moment-map vacuum expectation value (VEV) deformations; computes and interprets the 5D superconformal index under such deformations; maps results to Hanany-Witten transitions and tessellations of generalized toric diagrams in the five-brane web picture.

Result: Excessive Higgsing leads to a distributional 5D superconformal index, analogous to bad/broken behavior in 3D N=4 theories; Hanany-Witten moves encode the Higgsing cascade; inequivalent 5D frames correspond to distinct tessellations of the generalized toric diagram.

Conclusion: The structure of the Higgs branch and its deformations in 5D N=1 SCFTs is tightly constrained by their 3D magnetic quiver duals and brane-web realization; distributional indices signal pathological UV completions or non-unitary IR phases; the geometric correspondence between frames and toric tessellations provides a unifying framework for duality and deformation analysis.

Abstract: This is the second of two companion papers in which we study five-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ theories engineered by $(p,q)$ five-brane webs and their associated three-dimensional magnetic quivers. Leveraging the exact parameter dictionary between the $N+\mathrm{SU}(N)_0^{k-1}+N$ theories and their magnetic quivers at infinite coupling, proposed in the first paper, we investigate nilpotent moment-map VEV deformations and their three-dimensional counterparts. We show that, when the Higgsing is excessive, the five-dimensional superconformal index becomes distributional, mirroring a characteristic feature of bad and broken theories in three dimensions. We also develop a five-brane web interpretation of these results, identifying the Hanany-Witten moves associated with the Higgsing cascade and relating inequivalent frames to distinct tessellations of the generalised toric diagram.

</details>


### [125] [Deformed Amplituhedra and Interpolating Anomalous Dimensions on the Coulomb Branch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39052)
*Francesco Calisto,Subramanya Hegde,Martín Lagares,Elia Mazzucchelli*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the deformed Amplituhedron to general massive four-point kinematics in the Coulomb branch of N=4 SYM, uses copositivity of the inverse Gram matrix to fix the two-loop integrand structure, computes analytic results via 4D differential equations, and shows that the walking anomalous dimension is fully determined by the cusp and octagon anomalous dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and compute planar four-point amplitudes on the Coulomb branch of N=4 SYM for generic massive kinematics, bridging known limits (cusp, octagon) and probing the all-loop structure of the walking anomalous dimension.

Method: Extends the deformed Amplituhedron using a six-parameter embedding-space Gram matrix; imposes copositivity constraints on its inverse to constrain real geometry and fix the two-loop canonical form's numerator; specializes to a three-parameter massive configuration; evaluates one- and two-loop integrals via four-dimensional differential equations yielding multiple polylogarithms.

Result: Analytic one- and two-loop scattering amplitudes for a multiscale massive kinematic family; confirmation of exponentiation in IR limits; reproduction of known one-loop interpolation; determination of two-loop coefficients in the all-loop walking anomalous dimension ansatz; evidence that the walking anomalous dimension is fully fixed by cusp and octagon data.

Conclusion: The walking anomalous dimension in this Coulomb-branch setup is not independent but entirely determined by the cusp and octagon anomalous dimensions across all loop orders, revealing a deep structural constraint in N=4 SYM's infrared dynamics.

Abstract: We study planar four-point scattering amplitudes on the Coulomb branch of $\mathcal N=4$ super Yang--Mills theory, focusing on multi-parameter families of massive kinematics. We extend the deformed Amplituhedron construction of \cite{Arkani-Hamed:2023epq,Flieger:2025ekn} to general external kinematics characterized by a six-parameter embedding-space Gram matrix. We show that copositivity constraints on the inverse Gram matrix control the real geometry and restrict the numerator of the two-loop canonical form to the span of the two planar double-box structures expected for the scattering amplitude. We then specialize to a three-parameter massive configuration and evaluate the relevant one- and two-loop integrals using differential equations directly in four dimensions, obtaining analytic expressions in terms of multiple polylogarithms. We investigate multiscale infrared limits of the proposed two-loop amplitude and find exponentiation through two loops. The kinematic family contains the limits governed by the cusp and octagon anomalous dimensions and interpolates between them through the walking anomalous dimension introduced in~\cite{Alday:2026cpk}. Our result reproduces the one-loop interpolation and fixes the two-loop coefficients in their proposed all-loop ansatz. Interestingly, our results suggest that the walking anomalous dimension may be entirely determined through all loop orders by the cusp and octagon anomalous dimensions.

</details>


### [126] [Regularized Master-Field Approximation for Mass-Deformed Supersymmetric Yang-Mills Matrix Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39101)
*Yue Lei,Reishi Maeta*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies the Regularized Master-Field Approximation (RMA) to mass-deformed supersymmetric Yang–Mills matrix models with 3, 4, and 10 matrices, confirming fuzzy-sphere classical solutions and revealing their quantum destabilization by Dirac zero modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the quantitative reliability of the RMA—a scalable large-N method—for nontrivial supersymmetric matrix models admitting fuzzy-sphere vacua, and to probe quantum stability of such geometries against fermionic zero modes.

Method: Application of the Regularized Master-Field Approximation (RMA) to three mass-deformed supersymmetric Yang–Mills-type matrix models (with 3, 4, and 10 bosonic matrices); numerical simulation of fuzzy-sphere configurations; spectral analysis of the Dirac operator to identify zero modes and assess stability.

Result: RMA successfully reproduces fuzzy-sphere solutions and their quantum breakdown across all three models; Dirac zero modes are identified as a mechanism for destabilizing fuzzy-sphere configurations in specific parameter regimes.

Conclusion: RMA is quantitatively accurate for studying emergent geometry and quantum stability in supersymmetric large-N matrix models; fermionic zero modes play a critical role in the quantum viability of fuzzy-sphere vacua.

Abstract: The regularized master-field approximation (RMA) is an efficient computational method for large-$N$ reduced matrix models, including those with multiple matrices or complex integration weights. In this work, we apply the RMA to mass-deformed supersymmetric Yang--Mills-type matrix models with three, four, and ten matrices. All three models admit fuzzy-sphere configurations as classical solutions. We show that the RMA quantitatively reproduces these fuzzy-sphere solutions and their breakdown, and that zero modes of the Dirac operator can destabilize fuzzy-sphere configurations in certain cases.

</details>


### [127] [virasoro_blocks: Software for the 2d bootstrap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39106)
*David Poland*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: virasoro_blocks is software for high-precision, pole-reduced computation of Virasoro and related conformal blocks—optimized for numerical bootstrap—enabling sharp bounds on scalar gaps and average exchanged dimensions in 2D CFTs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Zamolodchikov's recursion yields Virasoro blocks with many Kac poles, hindering their use in semidefinite programming for the numerical bootstrap; there is a need for stable, high-accuracy, pole-minimized representations valid at arbitrary central charge—including singular cases like minimal models and c=1.

Method: Uses optimal interpolation to reduce the number of Kac poles while preserving accuracy; implements arbitrary-precision arithmetic; supports Virasoro, global, N=1 superconformal, torus character, and torus one-point blocks.

Result: Enables numerically stable bootstrap bounds: (i) scalar gap bounds approaching the Liouville theory window [(c−1)/16, (c−1)/12]; (ii) bounds on average exchanged dimension in identical-scalar four-point functions—revealing structured boundaries where known 2D CFTs lie or accumulate.

Conclusion: virasoro_blocks bridges analytic structure and numerical bootstrap requirements, significantly improving feasibility and precision of Virasoro-based bootstrap studies across generic and special central charges.

Abstract: We introduce virasoro_blocks, software that computes Virasoro conformal blocks at any central charge in a form suitable for the numerical bootstrap. At each finite order in the elliptic nome, Zamolodchikov's recursion gives the block as a positive prefactor times a rational function of the exchanged weight. However, its many Kac poles make it difficult to use in semidefinite programming approaches to the bootstrap. In this software we use optimal interpolation to reduce the number of poles while keeping high accuracy. The software works in arbitrary precision and handles singular central charges, including the minimal models and $c=1$. It also computes global blocks, $N=1$ superconformal blocks, torus characters, and torus one-point blocks. We illustrate it with two applications. Assuming only scalar Virasoro primaries, we compute bounds on the scalar gap that approach the window of Liouville solutions between $(c-1)/16$ and $(c-1)/12$. We also bound the average exchanged dimension in four-point functions of identical scalars at several central charges. These bounds have a rich structure, and many known 2d CFTs lie on or near their boundary.

</details>


### [128] [Ortho-symplectic interface line operators in $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39207)
*Yasuyuki Hatsuda,Tadashi Okazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates line operators at half-BPS interfaces in 4d N=4 SYM with orthogonal/symplectic gauge groups, classifies them for NS5- and D5-type interfaces, and proposes S-duality between Wilson and vortex lines, supported by matching line defect half-indices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure and duality properties of line operators localized at half-BPS interfaces in 4d N=4 SYM with orthogonal and symplectic gauge groups — extending previous work on unitary groups and probing S-duality at boundaries/junctions.

Method: Classification of line operators by electric/magnetic charges across NS5- and D5-type interfaces; proposal of S-duality maps between Wilson and vortex lines; computation and comparison of line defect half-indices as protected supersymmetric partition functions on S³/Zₙ × R to test duality.

Result: Line operators are systematically labeled by charge pairs (NS5) or single-group charges (D5); concrete S-duality proposals between Wilson and vortex lines at interfaces; exact matching of half-indices for dual line-operator/interface configurations provides strong evidence for the proposed dualities.

Conclusion: S-duality acts nontrivially on line operators at half-BPS interfaces in orthogonal/symplectic N=4 SYM, exchanging Wilson and vortex lines; the half-index matching confirms consistency of the duality at the level of protected interface-localized observables.

Abstract: We study the line operators at the half-BPS interfaces in 4d $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills (SYM) theories of orthogonal and symplectic gauge groups. For the NS5-type interfaces, line operators are labeled by pairs of electric or magnetic charges under the gauge groups on either side of the interface, whereas for the D5-type interfaces they are labeled by electric or magnetic charges under the single gauge group preserved by the interface. We propose dualities between the Wilson lines and vortex lines at the interfaces under the action of S-duality. As highly non-trivial evidence of the dualities on the junctions of the line operators and the interfaces, we find matching of the line defect half-indices for the dual pairs.

</details>


### [129] [Schwinger--Keldysh worldline effective field theory for quantum black holes: dissipation, noise and decoherence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39243)
*Hajime Kobayashi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a Schwinger-Keldysh worldline effective field theory for quantum black holes, unifying dissipation, fluctuations, and decoherence via real-time influence functionals; it derives Planck-suppressed stochastic forces in post-Newtonian binaries and reproduces horizon-induced decoherence scaling, while preserving momentum balance and enabling extensions to non-equilibrium and microscopic quantum structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a unified real-time framework for quantum gravitational open-system dynamics—specifically addressing dissipation, fluctuations, and decoherence in black hole systems—beyond equilibrium assumptions and without ad hoc noise prescriptions.

Method: Schwinger-Keldysh worldline effective field theory with a real-time influence functional; causal response and symmetric noise kernels constrained by positivity and KMS symmetry; Hubbard–Stratonovich linearization of noise; absorptive matching to horizon physics; application to post-Newtonian Schwarzschild binary and spatial superposition decoherence.

Result: Derives complete relative-acceleration noise for a leading post-Newtonian binary—including direct and metric-mediated contributions—that is Planck-suppressed and conserves center-of-mass momentum; recovers Danielson–Satishchandran–Wald horizon-induced decoherence scaling; establishes noise and response as independent matching data away from thermal equilibrium.

Conclusion: The Schwinger–Keldysh worldline EFT provides a consistent, first-principles foundation for quantum black hole open-system dynamics, bridging semiclassical gravity, quantum measurement, and decoherence, with natural extensions to non-equilibrium, microscopic structure, and gravitational interferometry.

Abstract: We formulate a Schwinger--Keldysh worldline effective field theory for quantum black holes that treats dissipation, fluctuations, and decoherence within one real-time influence functional. The reduced dynamics is encoded in causal response and symmetric noise kernels. Their spectra obey positivity constraints, while thermal Kubo--Martin--Schwinger symmetry relates them through the fluctuation--dissipation relation. A Hubbard--Stratonovich representation turns the noise kernel into a Gaussian source driving the resolved metric and worldline. For a leading post-Newtonian Schwarzschild binary, direct and metric-mediated stochastic forces combine into the complete relative-acceleration noise at this order, preserve center-of-mass momentum balance, and remain Planck suppressed. The same noise kernel gives the quadrupolar decoherence functional for a supported spatial superposition and reproduces the Danielson--Satishchandran--Wald horizon-induced scaling after absorptive matching. Away from thermal equilibrium, response and noise remain independent matching data. We discuss extensions to microscopic quantum structure in the worldline kernels, matching to spatially extended coarse-grained descriptions, and gravitational splitting.

</details>


### [130] [Quantum field theory with scale-dependent weighted measures: nonlinear momentum representations, vacuum fluctuations, and causal structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39285)
*S. González-Salud,R. Cartas-Fuentevilla*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs free quantum fields using momentum-space weighted measures with an independent energy scale, leading to a family of theories characterized by a dimensionless spectral function—including the Euclidean Snyder algebra as a special case—and analyzes their UV finiteness, causal structure, and observable consequences for the Klein-Gordon field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize free quantum field theory by introducing an independent energy scale and momentum-space weighting that modifies UV behavior while recovering standard QFT at low momenta; motivated by quantum gravity phenomenology, modified dispersion relations, and noncommutative geometry (e.g., Snyder algebra).

Method: Constructs free quantum fields via weighted momentum-space measures; introduces observer-covariant spectral weights using timelike four-velocity; derives equal-time commutation relations, conserved weighted observables, vacuum fluctuations, and two-point functions for the complex Klein-Gordon field in arbitrary spatial dimension; analyzes UV finiteness conditions and causal structure via convolution of the Pauli-Jordan distribution.

Result: Obtains explicit expressions for equal-time algebra, conserved weighted charges, vacuum expectation values, and two-point functions; establishes precise UV convergence conditions (governed by spectral function decay); reveals a nonlocal causal structure encoded in a spatial convolution kernel; provides numerical profiles of kernels and amplitudes, plus an illustrative interacting extension.

Conclusion: Momentum-space weighting with an independent energy scale yields a consistent, UV-regularizable deformation of free QFT, unifying features of modified dispersion, nonlinear momentum coordinates, and noncommutative geometry—while preserving covariance and enabling concrete phenomenological analysis.

Abstract: We reconsider the construction of free quantum fields through momentum-space weighted measures, introducing an independent energy scale that restores the conventional theory at low momenta. The resulting family is characterized by a dimensionless spectral function and includes, as a distinguished case, the spatial weight associated with the Euclidean Snyder algebra. We discuss its relation to modified dispersion relations and nonlinear momentum coordinates, and introduce an observer-covariant spectral weight defined using a timelike four-velocity. For the complex Klein-Gordon field we obtain the equal-time algebra, conserved weighted observables, vacuum fluctuations, and two-point functions in arbitrary spatial dimension. The ultraviolet conditions for their finiteness are established, and the nonlocal causal structure is analyzed in terms of a spatial convolution of the ordinary Pauli-Jordan distribution. An illustrative interacting extension and numerical profiles of the kernels and amplitudes are also presented.

</details>


### [131] [Does SO(2,2) symmetry lead to an additional U(1) symmetry in 5-dimensional Einstein-scalar theories?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39400)
*Saeedeh Sadeghian*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper challenges a conjecture about the uniqueness of SO(2,2)-symmetric solutions in 5D Einstein-scalar theory by constructing an explicit counterexample using Lie symmetry analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the conjecture in Sadeghian et al. (2014) that SO(2,2) symmetry plus a scalar field in five dimensions implies an additional U(1) symmetry (and hence uniqueness), which lacks proof.

Method: Lie symmetry analysis of the 5D Einstein equations coupled to a scalar field, under SO(2,2) symmetry assumptions, to systematically derive and classify invariant solutions.

Result: An explicit non-axisymmetric solution depending on both angular coordinates θ and ψ is constructed — violating the presumed U(1) symmetry and disproving the conjecture.

Conclusion: The SO(2,2) symmetry alone is insufficient to guarantee an extra U(1) symmetry or uniqueness in 5D Einstein–scalar gravity; the conjecture is false.

Abstract: Solving Einstein equations including the matter fields in dimensions higher than four is computationally challenging. However, the existence of symmetries facilitates these calculations. Among these symmetries, we can mention the SO(2,2) symmetry that arises in the near-horizon geometries of extremal black holes with vanishing horizon area in four dimensions \cite{EVH/CFT} and higher dimensions \cite{Sadeghian:2015}. Solutions of Einstein theories including scalar and Maxwell fields with SO(2,2) symmetry have been classified in four, five, and higher dimensions \cite{Sadeghian:2014}. For the Einstein-Maxwell-Lambda theory, a theorem states that SO(2,2) symmetry leads to an additional U(1) symmetry and therefore unique axisymmetric solutions. Nevertheless, in the presence of a scalar field, this symmetry group is not large enough in five dimensions to guarantee solution uniqueness, and the additional U(1) symmetry remains a conjecture in \cite{Sadeghian:2014}. Here, we study the Einstein equations of theories with a five-dimensional scalar field using the Lie symmetry method for solving partial differential equations. We challenge the conjecture in \cite{Sadeghian:2014} and find a solution that depends on both $\theta$ and $\psi$ coordinates as a counterexample to that conjecture.

</details>


### [132] [Carrollian CFTs and the Light Front](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39450)
*Arjun Bagchi,Kinjal Banerjee,Sourish Banerjee,Meghaa AK*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bridge between 4D lightfront physics in asymptotically flat spacetimes and 3D Carrollian CFTs via the isomorphism between 4D Poincaré and 3D conformal Carroll algebras, constructs new Carroll CFT representations relevant to massive bulk scattering, and computes two-point functions to map between the two frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To connect lightfront physics—naturally arising in asymptotically flat spacetimes—with Carrollian CFTs by exploiting the algebraic isomorphism between 4D Poincaré and 3D conformal Carroll algebras, enabling holographic and scattering interpretations.

Method: Leverages Lie algebra isomorphism between 4D Poincaré and 3D conformal Carroll algebras; constructs novel unitary representations of Carroll CFTs inspired by lightfront quantization; computes Carroll-invariant two-point correlation functions; establishes an explicit dictionary mapping 3D Carroll CFT data to 4D lightfront observables in AFS.

Result: New representations of Carroll CFTs tied to massive particle scattering in 4D AFS; explicit form of Carroll-invariant two-point functions; a concrete operator-state correspondence and kinematic map linking 3D Carroll CFT correlators to 4D lightfront S-matrix elements.

Conclusion: The isomorphism provides a rigorous algebraic foundation for treating lightfront theories in AFS as dual to Carrollian CFTs, enriching both flat-space holography and the representation theory of ultra-relativistic symmetry algebras.

Abstract: Carrollian symmetries play an important role in building holographic duals to asymptotically flat spacetimes (AFS). This construction hinges on the isomorphism between the (asymptotic) isometries of AFS and the conformal Carroll algebra. Carroll symmetries also naturally arise on the lightfront, as co-dimension one subalgebras in the Poincare algebra written in lightcone coordinates. In this paper, building on the isomorphism between four dimensional (4d) Poincare and 3d conformal Carroll algebras, we make a connection between physics on the lightfront and Carrollian CFTs. Using insights from the lightfront literature, we construct new representations of Carroll CFTs that connect to massive bulk scattering. We compute two-point functions and build a map between 3d Carroll CFTs and 4d lightfront theories living in AFS.

</details>


### [133] [Large-$N$ limit of chiral quiver theories: free energy, supersymmetric indices and operator counting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39569)
*Edoardo Colombo,Hyojoong Kim,Nakwoo Kim,Hoseob Shin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper presents the first analytic large-N solution for the S^3 free energy of a chiral N=2 Chern-Simons-matter theory with M-theory dual, resolving the N^{3/2} scaling puzzle via cancellation of long-range forces in the matrix model; it matches holographic predictions for all trial R-charges and extends to superconformal/topologically twisted indices, supporting the operator counting conjecture beyond non-chiral cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing tension between the expected N^{3/2} scaling of the free energy in M-theory duals and previous matrix model analyses that suggested problematic long-range force contributions in chiral quivers.

Method: Analytic solution of the large-N saddle-point equations for the S^3 free energy of a chiral N=2 Chern-Simons-matter theory (specifically the quiver dual to AdS_4 × Q^{1,1,1}/Z_k), supplemented by finite-N numerical verification across multiple R-charge assignments.
