Result: Demonstration that long-range forces cancel at large N, yielding exact N^{3/2} scaling; precise match with holographic free energy for all trial R-charges; extension to S^2×S^1 superconformal and topologically twisted indices in both large-N and Cardy limits; agreement with operator counting conjecture.

Conclusion: The analytic solution confirms consistency of chiral quivers with M-theory holography, validates the operator counting method beyond non-chiral cases, and establishes universality of the saddle-point structure across related supersymmetric observables.

Abstract: We find the first analytic solution to the large-$N$ saddle point equations for the $S^3$ free energy of a chiral $\mathcal N=2$ Chern-Simons-matter theory with an M-theory dual. Focusing on the chiral quiver dual to AdS$_4\times Q^{1,1,1}/\mathbb Z_{\rm k}$, we show how the long-range forces of the matrix model cancel at large $N$, resolving a long-standing tension with the expected $N^{3/2}$ scaling. The resulting free energy precisely matches the holographic prediction for all assignments of trial R-charges. Our results extend to the superconformal and topologically twisted indices on $S^2\times S^1$, whose large-$N$ and Cardy limit is determined by the same saddle. The solution agrees with the predictions of the operator counting method, giving strong evidence for this conjecture beyond non-chiral quivers. We support the analytic solution with a finite-$N$ numerical analysis for multiple assignments of R-charges, extending recent numerical results obtained for this quiver.

</details>


### [134] [Superstring Field Theory with Double Background Independence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39594)
*Chris Hull*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper presents a symmetric superstring field theory with two decoupled sectors—physical and shadow—both expanded around the same background CFT; it then generalizes the theory to allow independent backgrounds for each sector, preserving invariance under separate marginal deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a superstring field theory that treats physical and shadow sectors on equal footing and extends background independence to distinct conformal field theory backgrounds for each sector.

Method: Constructs a symmetric action for a recently developed superstring field theory with two decoupled sectors; generalizes it by allowing independent background CFTs and verifying invariance under separate marginal deformations.

Result: A symmetric action formulation is achieved for the two-sector theory; a generalized version is derived where each sector can be expanded around different background CFTs while maintaining independence under their respective marginal deformations.

Conclusion: The generalized theory provides a more flexible and background-independent framework for superstring field theory, enabling independent treatment of physical and shadow sectors with distinct conformal backgrounds.

Abstract: A recently constructed superstring field theory has two decoupled sectors, the physical superstring and a shadow sector with a second copy of the superstring. These are both expanded around the same background conformal field theory and the theory is independent of marginal deformations of this common background. A form of the action is presented that is symmetric between the two sectors. A generalisation of this theory is given in which the two sectors can be expanded around two different backgrounds, and the theory is independent under separate marginal deformations of the two background conformal field theories.

</details>


### [135] [Majorana Neutrino Physics and Matter-Antimatter Asymmetry in Type IIA String Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39804)
*Yang Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a Type IIA string theory model on a T^6/(Z2×Z2) orientifold with intersecting D6-branes, where Euclidean D2-instantons with Sp(2) Chan-Paton symmetry generate both the seesaw mechanism and Weinberg operator to explain Majorana neutrino masses and baryogenesis via leptogenesis; it provides an existence proof (not full top-down derivation), selects normal-ordered seesaw dominance by matching observed Δm², respects swampland constraints, yields η_B ≈ 8.6×10⁻¹¹ (within factor ~3 of observed value), and predicts ⟨m_ββ⟩ = 2.594 meV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize Majorana neutrino masses and explain the observed baryon asymmetry (η_B) within a concrete, UV-complete string compactification—specifically addressing whether intersecting brane models with instanton effects can simultaneously satisfy neutrino data, leptogenesis, and swampland constraints.

Method: Constructs a Type IIA orientifold compactification on T^6/(Z2×Z2) with intersecting D6-branes; identifies Euclidean D2-brane instantons carrying Sp(2) Chan-Paton symmetry that generate both the dimension-5 Weinberg operator and the right-handed neutrino mass term (enabling type-I seesaw); fixes flavoured couplings by phenomenological matching (not full CFT computation); applies swampland distance and de Sitter conjectures to constrain instanton action and reheating temperature; performs parameter scans and benchmark analysis.

Result: Demonstrates existence of a consistent string embedding where D2-instantons generate viable neutrino masses and leptogenesis; finds normal mass ordering is selected; obtains baryon asymmetry η_B ≈ 8.6×10⁻¹¹ (O(1) agreement with Planck value 6.1×10⁻¹⁰); predicts effective Majorana mass ⟨m_ββ⟩ = 2.594 meV; shows mass hierarchy is accommodated but not uniquely predicted.

Conclusion: A concrete, swampland-compliant Type IIA string construction realizes both neutrino mass generation (via instanton-induced seesaw + Weinberg operator) and successful thermal leptogenesis; however, flavour structure remains input rather than derived, making it an existence proof—not a fully predictive top-down model.

Abstract: We study Majorana neutrino masses and the matter-antimatter asymmetry in Type IIA string theory on a $T^6/(\mathbb{Z}_2 \times \mathbb{Z}_2)$ orientifold with intersecting D6-branes. Euclidean D2-instantons with $\text{Sp}(2)$ Chan-Paton symmetry generate both the seesaw mechanism and the Weinberg operator. The flavour vectors are not computed from a compactification but fixed by matching, so our results are an existence proof. Matching the measured mass-squared differences selects the seesaw-dominated case with normal ordering. Swampland criteria constrain the instanton action and the reheating temperature. A benchmark example reproduces the neutrino parameters, gives $\eta_B \approx 8.6 \times 10^{-11}$, within an order of magnitude of observation, and predicts $\langle m_{\beta \beta} \rangle =2.594 \text{meV}$. A parameter scan shows the mass hierarchy is accommodated rather than predicted.

</details>


### [136] [On functional classes in higher-spin theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39851)
*Yu. A. Tatarenko*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous functional-space framework for the higher-spin algebra A and its twisted adjoint module A^{tw} in d-dimensional Vasiliev theory, enabling a well-defined free-level factorization over ideals; it links admissible function classes to locality—showing spin-local expressions are finite while certain non-local ones diverge due to A^{tw} properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in ideal factorization within Vasiliev's higher-spin gravity by providing a precise functional realization of the higher-spin algebra A and its twisted adjoint module A^{tw}, thereby addressing foundational issues tied to star-product closure, module structure, and locality.

Method: Imposes algebraic and analytic conditions—closure of A under the Moyal star product, module compatibility of A^{tw} over A via the star product, and auxiliary regularity constraints—to characterize A and A^{tw} as specific functional spaces; compares with Soloviev’s prior work on A and analyzes divergence behavior of non-local vs. spin-local expressions.

Result: Derives simple algebraic conditions defining admissible functional classes for A and A^{tw}; confirms that spin-local interactions are well-defined and finite, whereas certain non-local expressions diverge intrinsically due to structural properties of A^{tw}.

Conclusion: A consistent free-level factorization procedure is achieved through rigorously defined functional spaces for A and A^{tw}, directly linking algebraic consistency to physical locality—establishing that spin-locality is necessary and sufficient for finiteness in this formulation of Vasiliev theory.

Abstract: The factorization over the ideal in the $d$-dimensional Vasiliev theory requires an accurate description of the higher-spin algebra $\mathcal{A}$ and its twisted adjoint module $\mathcal{A}^{tw}$ as certain functional spaces. It is shown that the conditions of $\mathcal{A}$ to be closed under action of the Moyal star product, and of $\mathcal{A}^{tw}$ to be a star-product $\mathcal{A}$-module along with some auxiliary conditions lead to properly defined free-level factorization procedure. The conditions on admissible functions have simple algebraic form; in the case of $\mathcal{A}$ the analogous functional space has been considered by Soloviev \cite{Soloviev:2013iba}. The relation of the obtained functional classes to the locality problem in the Vasiliev theory is discussed. In particular it is observed that certain non-local expressions diverge due to properties of $\mathcal{A}^{tw}$ while the spin-local expressions are well-defined.

</details>


### [137] [Initial conditions leave IR imprints in quantum cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39965)
*Manishankar Ailiga,Akash Mondal,Gaurav Narain*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop wavefunction of the Universe for $\mathbb{R} \times S^3$ Lorentzian geometries, analyzing how an infinite family of covariant boundary actions—parameterized by $c$ and $\omega$—affect saddle-point structure, perturbative stability, UV regularization, and IR sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in defining the gravitational path integral for cosmological wavefunctions by systematically incorporating covariant boundary conditions for metric fluctuations on $S^3$, and to understand their impact on saddle selection, perturbative consistency, and UV/IR behavior of the wavefunction.

Method: Lorentzian gravitational path integral over $\mathbb{R} \times S^3$ with metric fluctuations on $S^3$; imposition of covariant boundary actions parameterized by $(c,\omega)$; identification of lapse and scale-factor saddles; one-loop determinant computation with UV divergence isolation; use of $c$ as a regulator and pole-shifter for the fluctuation determinant; counterterm addition for UV finiteness.

Result: Dominant initial momentum restricts $\omega$ to half-integers; four $c$-dependent dominant conjugate momentum branches emerge (two dS, one HH, one tunnelling); only one dS and the HH saddle admit well-behaved perturbations; $c$ regulates UV divergences and shifts poles off the real $q_f$-axis for dS; UV-finite no-boundary and dS wavefunctions obtained via counterterms; IR-divergences and IR-sensitivity to initial boundary choices are revealed.

Conclusion: Covariant boundary parameters $(c,\omega)$ fundamentally shape the semiclassical landscape of the wavefunction: they constrain viable saddles, regulate UV physics, control analytic structure of fluctuations, and induce IR sensitivity—highlighting that boundary data is not auxiliary but physically consequential in quantum cosmology.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational path integral of $\mathbb{R} \times S^3$ geometries in Lorentzian signature along with the metric fluctuations which live on the $S^3$ hypersurfaces. Each metric configuration is characterised by lapse, scale factor and fluctuations, and is weighted by the exponential of the Einstein-Hilbert action. A particular boundary requirement for the scale-factor is achievable via an infinite family of covariant boundary actions, each restricting the boundary behaviour of the fluctuation field which cannot be arbitrarily chosen. The paper explores the effect of this infinite family of covariant boundary actions on the wavefunction of Universe to one-loop. These boundary actions are characterized by parameters $c$ and $\omega$. Dominant contribution of initial momentum in the path-integral restricts $\omega$ to be half-integer, leading to an infinite family of $c$-dependent conjugate momenta. In special case for fixed $\omega$, four such $c$-dependent dominant branches of conjugate momenta emerge: two lead to deSitter, one leads to Hartle-Hawking and one to the tunnelling Universe. For all these $c$-dependent initial conjugate momenta, the lapse-saddles are relevant, however only, one of deSitter saddle and Hartle Hawking saddle have well-behaved perturbations. The one-loop wavefunction at these well-behaved saddles is computed systematically by isolating the UV-divergences, where the covariant boundary parameter $c$ acts as a UV-regulator and also helps in shifting the poles of one-loop determinant, away from the real $q_f$ axis for dS saddles, where $q_f$ is the final size of the Universe. UV-finite wavefunction for no-boundary and dS is obtained by adding suitable counterterms. The infrared behaviour of these wavefunctions show not only IR-divergences but also sensitivity toward the initial boundary choices.

</details>


### [138] [$\mathcal{N}=4$ Super Yang-Mills Flux Tube in AdS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40156)
*Victor Gorbenko,Orr Sela,Brian R. Zielazienski*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies a supersymmetric Wilson line on the boundary of N=4 SYM in AdS₄ as a holographic model for confining flux tubes, bridging weak/strong coupling regimes via defect spectrum and superconformal index analysis, and proposes an operator map linking worldsheet axion to a two-particle state, supporting smooth spectral interpolation from gauge theory to string worldsheet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a non-integrable yet supersymmetric holographic model—via a boundary-defect setup in N=4 SYM—that captures key features of confining flux tubes while retaining enough symmetry to enable controlled analysis across coupling regimes.

Method: Analyzes the defect CFT defined by a supersymmetric Wilson line on a half-space boundary in planar N=4 SYM; computes the defect spectrum at weak and strong coupling; evaluates the superconformal index; identifies operator-state correspondence between bulk worldsheet excitations (e.g., axion) and boundary defect operators; leverages holography to relate gauge-theory data to long-string worldsheet dynamics.

Result: Derives a precise map between worldsheet excitations (notably the axion) and multi-particle defect operators; shows spectral reorganization from exponential growth (gauge theory) to Cardy-like density (string worldsheet); confirms compatibility of defect CFT data with a non-integrable, confining-string-like regime preserving N=2 superconformal symmetry on the boundary.

Conclusion: The boundary Wilson line defect provides a tractable, non-integrable, supersymmetric framework realizing holographic confinement: it smoothly interpolates between perturbative gauge theory and semiclassical string spectra, offering a concrete setting to study spectral reorganization in confining theories.

Abstract: We study a supersymmetric Wilson line on the boundary of $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills theory in AdS$_4$, or equivalently on a half-space, as a model for exploring the connection between holographic strings and confining flux tubes. The boundary breaks four-dimensional conformal symmetry and is expected to remove integrability, bringing this system closer to a confining string while preserving enough supersymmetry to constrain its dynamics. Working in the planar limit, we analyze the defect spectrum at weak and strong coupling and compute the superconformal index, thereby relating the perturbative gauge-theory description to the worldsheet excitations of a long holographic string. These complementary perspectives allow us to propose a map between operators in the two regimes, under which the worldsheet axion, in particular, becomes a two-particle state at strong coupling. The resulting picture supports a smooth interpolation from an exponentially growing gauge-theory spectrum to the Cardy-like spectrum of a string worldsheet, providing a non-integrable supersymmetric model for investigating the spectral reorganization expected in confining gauge theories.

</details>


### [139] [Quantum curves of 6d SCFTs on a -3 curve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40203)
*Minsung Kim,Sung-Soo Kim,Xin Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies half-BPS defects and quantum Seiberg-Witten curves in 6d N=(1,0) SCFTs on a -3 curve, computing defect partition functions on ℝ⁴×T² via generalized blowup equations and proposing elliptic difference equations as quantum curves for the NS limit of those partition functions; applications include minimal su(3), g₂, so(N) theories on Higgsing trees, and circle-compactified su(3) with ℤ₂ twist.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand half-BPS defects and their role in encoding quantum-corrected geometry—specifically quantum Seiberg-Witten curves—in 6d N=(1,0) SCFTs, which lack conventional Lagrangian descriptions but admit geometric engineering via F-theory or M-theory on singular Calabi–Yau threefolds.

Method: Uses generalized blowup equations to compute codimension-2 and codimension-4 defect partition functions on ℝ⁴×T²; takes the Nekrasov–Shatashvili (NS) limit to extract quantum-corrected periods; identifies the resulting functional relations as elliptic difference equations constituting quantum Seiberg–Witten curves; applies to specific gauge theories linked via Higgsing trees and twisted compactifications.

Result: Derives explicit quantum Seiberg–Witten curves as elliptic difference equations for the minimal su(3), g₂, and so(N) SCFTs on a -3 curve, and extends the construction to the ℤ₂-twisted circle reduction of su(3); establishes defect partition functions as natural wavefunctions satisfying these quantum curves.

Conclusion: Quantum Seiberg–Witten curves in these 6d SCFTs are intrinsically encoded in the analytic structure of defect partition functions, realized concretely as elliptic difference equations; this framework unifies defect physics, integrable systems, and quantum geometry across a web of Higgs-related theories.

Abstract: We study half-BPS defects and quantum Seiberg-Witten curves in 6d $ \mathcal{N}=(1,0) $ SCFTs on a $ -3 $ curve. We compute the partition functions in the presence of codimension-4 and codimension-2 defects on $ \mathbb{R}^4 \times T^2 $ using generalized blowup equations. We then propose quantum Seiberg-Witten curves as elliptic difference equations satisfied by the Nekrasov-Shatashvili limits of the defect partition functions. We apply this construction to the minimal $ \mathfrak{su}(3) $ gauge theory, and to the $ \mathfrak{g}_2 $ and $ \mathfrak{so}(N) $ gauge theories on a Higgsing tree whose endpoint is the minimal $ \mathfrak{su}(3) $ theory. We also investigate defects and the quantum curve of the $ \mathfrak{su}(3) $ gauge theory compactified on a circle with a $ \mathbb{Z}_2 $ twist.

</details>


### [140] [Competition and coexistence of baryonic condensates in AdS hardwall model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40244)
*Akash Singh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates coexistence vs. exclusivity of diquark and dibaryon condensates in cold dense QCD using a backreacted 5D AdS hardwall model with two charged scalars dual to baryon-number-breaking operators; it finds that both condensates coexist only within a narrow, $q_2$-dependent window of operator dimension $\Delta_2$, persisting across confined/deconfined phases, and identifies a low-$T$, high-$\mu_B$ region where the doubly-condensed confined phase is thermodynamically favored.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether multiple baryon-number-breaking order parameters—specifically diquark and dibaryon condensates—can coexist or are mutually exclusive in cold, dense QCD, beyond mean-field approximations.

Method: Uses a fully backreacted five-dimensional anti-de Sitter (AdS) hardwall model with a $U(1)_B$ gauge field coupled to two charged scalar fields, each dual to a baryon-number-breaking operator with charge $q_i$ and conformal dimension $\\Delta_i$; fixes the diquark-like operator ($q_1=2, \\Delta_1=3$) and scans $\\Delta_2$ and $q_2$; analyzes phase structure in the baryon chemical potential–temperature ($\\mu_B$–$T$) plane.

Result: Both condensates form simultaneously only within a narrow $\\Delta_2$ window, whose width increases with $q_2$ and vanishes when the two operators coincide; outside this window, only one condensate forms; coexistence persists in both confined and deconfined bulk geometries; in the $\\mu_B$–$T$ phase diagram, the doubly-condensed confined phase dominates at low $T$, separated by a narrow band of deconfined single-condensate phase.

Conclusion: Diquark and dibaryon condensation are not generically exclusive but require fine-tuned operator dimensions for coexistence; their competition is robust under gravitational backreaction and confinement/deconfinement transitions, suggesting that multi-order-parameter phases in dense QCD may be possible but highly constrained.

Abstract: Cold dense QCD admits several order parameters for baryon-number breaking, among them diquark and dibaryon condensates. We ask whether they exclude one another or coexist, using a five-dimensional AdS hardwall model with full backreaction in which two charged scalars, dual to operators of charges $q_i$ and dimensions $\Delta_i$, couple to a common $U(1)_B$ gauge field. With the diquark-like operator fixed at $q_1=2$, $\Delta_1=3$, both operators condense only within a narrow window of $\Delta_2$, which widens with $q_2$ and closes when the operators coincide. Outside the window, only a single condensate forms. The structure persists in both confined and deconfined phases. In the $\mu_B$-$T$ plane, confined matter with both condensates is preferred at low temperature, below a narrow band of deconfined matter with one condensate.

</details>


### [141] [$\mathcal{N}=2$ conformal higher-spin actions in harmonic superspace](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40274)
*Evgeny Ivanov,Nikita Zaigraev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs linearized 4D, N=2 Weyl-type off-shell actions in harmonic superspace for superconformal higher-spin gauge multiplets, building on prior work on N=2 supergravity; it establishes superconformal and linearized gauge invariance, grounded in Grassmann harmonic and half-analyticity principles, and identifies consistency of spin-s gauge invariance only on N=2 Weyl-flat super-backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the geometric harmonic superspace framework—previously applied to N=2 conformal supergravity—to linearized superconformal higher-spin gauge theories, and to explore pathways toward their nonlinear completion.

Method: Uses harmonic superspace formalism with Grassmann analyticity and half-analyticity principles to construct off-shell Weyl-type actions; derives prepotential structure, gauge transformations, and generalized N=2 super-Weyl tensors; verifies superconformal and linearized gauge invariance; analyzes consistency of higher-spin gauge symmetry on generic N=2 supergravity backgrounds.

Result: Explicit linearized off-shell actions for N=2 superconformal higher-spin multiplets; full characterization of prepotentials and their transformations via Grassmann harmonic analyticity; invariant action and super-Weyl tensor construction via Grassmann half-analyticity; proof that linearized spin-s gauge invariance holds only on N=2 Weyl-flat super-backgrounds without modifying gauge laws or zero-curvature equations.

Conclusion: The harmonic superspace formulation based on Grassmann analyticity provides a robust, geometric foundation for linearized N=2 superconformal higher-spin theory and hints at viable routes to its nonlinear generalization, while constraining admissible backgrounds to Weyl-flat ones.

Abstract: We construct linearized $4D, \mathcal{N}=2$ Weyl-type off-shell actions in harmonic superspace for the superconformal higher-spin gauge multiplets introduced in arXiv:2404.19016. This work is a followup of our arXiv:2511.16325, where an analogous construction was carried out for conformal $\mathcal{N}=2$ supergravity. We check the superconformal invariance of the new actions and their linearized gauge invariance. As the characteristic features of our formulation, the structure of the underlying prepotentials and their gauge and superconformal transformations are entirely determined by the fundamental \textit{principle of Grassmann harmonic analyticity}, while the construction of the invariant actions and of the generalized $\mathcal{N}=2$ super-Weyl tensors relies upon the \textit{principle of Grassmann half-analyticity}. This provides not only a new geometric framework for the linearized $\mathcal{N}=2$ superconformal gauge higher-spin theories, but also suggests possible ways of promoting them to the full nonlinear level. We consider a generalization to an arbitrary $\mathcal{N}=2$ supergravity background and show that the linearized spin-$\mathbf{s}$ gauge invariance, without radical modifications of the gauge transformation law and/or harmonic zero-curvature equations, is consistent only with $\mathcal{N}=2$ Weyl-flat super-backgrounds.

</details>


### [142] [Bounds on the Diagonal Degree of the Biadjoint Scalar Amplitude Matrix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40355)
*Freddy Cachazo,Rajbir Longia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper disproves the existence of a critical n_c beyond which the biadjoint scalar theory matrix M_n contains a full-rank diagonal submatrix; instead, the largest non-singular diagonal submatrix (diagonal degree Δ_n) is exponentially smaller than the CHY rank (n−3)!, satisfying exp(−1.5n) ≤ Δ_n/(n−3)! ≤ exp(−0.01n+O(1)).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether there exists a critical n_c such that for all n > n_c, the biadjoint scalar theory matrix M_n contains a diagonal submatrix of maximal CHY rank (n−3)!, motivated by minimizing the number of terms in the KLT relation between gravity and Yang–Mills amplitudes.

Method: Combinatorial and asymptotic analysis of the structure of the biadjoint scalar amplitude matrix M_n; derivation of rigorous upper and lower exponential bounds on the diagonal degree Δ_n—the size of the largest invertible diagonal submatrix—using probabilistic and extremal matrix theory techniques.

Result: The diagonal degree Δ_n satisfies exp(−1.5n) ≤ Δ_n/(n−3)! ≤ exp[−0.01n + O(1)], proving Δ_n/(n−3)! = exp(−Θ(n)); thus, no such critical n_c exists—the ratio decays exponentially with n.

Conclusion: The conjectured existence of asymptotically full-rank diagonal submatrices in M_n is false; the diagonal degree is exponentially suppressed relative to the CHY rank, fundamentally limiting simplifications of the KLT formula based on diagonal selections.

Abstract: Biadjoint scalar theory partial amplitudes, $m_n(\alpha,\beta)$, define an $(n-1)!/2\times (n-1)!/2$ matrix, $M_n$, with rows and columns labeled by orderings. The CHY construction of $M_n$ implies that its rank is $(n-3)!$. Invertible submatrices of maximal rank enter in the KLT construction relating graviton amplitudes to gluon amplitudes. Motivated by finding the KLT formula with the smallest number of terms, one of the authors posed the question of whether there is a critical $n_c$ such that for all $n>n_c$ there exists a diagonal submatrix of $M_n$ of rank $(n-3)!$. In this work we obtain bounds on the size of the largest non-singular diagonal submatrix of $M_n$, which we call its diagonal degree $\Delta_n$, and we answer the question in the negative. We prove that the diagonal degree is exponentially suppressed relative to the CHY rank: $\Delta_n/(n-3)! = \exp(-\Theta (n))$ as $n\to \infty$. More explicitly, we establish the bounds $\exp(-1.5n)\leq\Delta_n/(n-3)!\leq \exp \left[ -0.01n+O(1)\right]$.

</details>


### [143] [Breaking of integrability in elliptic deformations of AdS3 strings with mixed flux](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40386)
*Ian Le Meur,Fiona K. Seibold*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs elliptic deformations of the AdS₃×S³×T⁴ superstring with mixed NSNS/RR fluxes in type IIB supergravity, finds that generic elliptic deformations break worldsheet integrability due to inelastic 2→2 scattering, and shows that the Wess-Zumino term—while integrability-preserving in rational/trigonometric cases—destroys integrability for elliptic deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore whether elliptic deformations of the AdS₃×S³×T⁴ superstring with mixed fluxes preserve classical integrability—a key property underlying exact solvability and AdS/CFT integrability—and to clarify the role of the Wess-Zumino term across different deformation classes.

Method: Constructs elliptic sigma-model deformations preserving 8 supersymmetries; imposes constraints from type IIB supergravity (including B-field coupling between deformed AdS₃ and S³); analyzes integrability via two complementary methods: (i) existence of a flat Lax connection on the worldsheet, and (ii) computation of the tree-level worldsheet S-matrix and its elasticity and Yang-Baxter consistency.

Result: Generic elliptic deformations yield inelastic 2→2 scattering amplitudes, violating integrability; integrable cases (identified by flat Lax connection) coincide precisely with vanishing inelastic amplitudes and satisfaction of the classical Yang-Baxter equation by the S-matrix; crucially, the Wess-Zumino term—compatible with integrability in rational and trigonometric limits—breaks integrability in the elliptic case.

Conclusion: Elliptic deformations of AdS₃×S³×T⁴ with mixed fluxes are generically non-integrable at the quantum (worldsheet S-matrix) level, and the Wess-Zumino term is incompatible with elliptic integrability—highlighting a qualitative distinction between elliptic and lower-dimensional (rational/trigonometric) deformations in integrable string theory.

Abstract: We construct elliptic deformations of the $AdS_3 \times S^3 \times T^4$ superstring supported by mixed NSNS and RR fluxes in type IIB supergravity and investigate their integrability. The NSNS sector includes a Wess-Zumino term, and imposing that the theory preserves 8 supersymmetries also requires the presence of a B-field coupling the deformed $AdS_3$ and $S^3$ factors. We explore integrability from two different perspectives: through the construction of a flat Lax connection and the computation of the tree-level worldsheet S-matrix. For generic elliptic parameters and NSNS flux, we find inelastic $2 \rightarrow 2$ amplitudes, violating the elasticity required by integrability. In the integrable cases as identified by the Lax analysis, these inelastic amplitudes vanish and the tree-level S-matrix satisfies the classical Yang-Baxter equation. In particular, the addition of a Wess-Zumino term, which preserves integrability in the rational and trigonometric models, breaks integrability for elliptic deformations.

</details>


### [144] [A Simplified Born Rule for Cosmological Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40412)
*Fan Zhu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves universal cancellation of double-boundary poles in equal-time in-in cosmological correlators in flat spacetime, leading to a simplified Born rule expressed via embedded wavefunction coefficients; for Tr(ϕ³) theory with conformal coupling, it introduces the Born polytope—a geometric object whose canonical function encodes the normalized correlator and admits a stringy integral representation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in the analytic structure of cosmological correlators arising from boundary singularities, and to uncover a deeper geometric and combinatorial structure underlying the Born rule in de Sitter/inflationary quantum gravity.

Method: Analytic continuation and diagrammatic analysis of in-in correlators in flat spacetime; derivation of pole cancellation identities independent of loop momentum integration; introduction of embedded wavefunction coefficients; construction of the Born polytope from physical pole combinatorics; derivation of canonical form and stringy integral representation.

Result: Universal cancellation of double-boundary poles before loop integration; formulation of a simplified Born rule using embedded wavefunction coefficients; forest-cut expansion for Tr(ϕ³); definition and characterization of the Born polytope whose canonical function reproduces normalized correlators; emergence of a stringy formula.

Conclusion: The analytic structure of cosmological correlators is tightly constrained by fundamental consistency (pole cancellation), revealing an intrinsic geometric description—the Born polytope—that unifies combinatorics, locality, and quantum probability in primordial observables.

Abstract: Cosmological correlators are natural observables of the primordial universe and are related to wavefunction coefficients by the Born rule. For equal-time in-in correlators in flat spacetime, we prove that all double-boundary poles cancel for arbitrary graphs, including in theories with local derivative interactions. These cancellations occur before integration over spatial loop momenta and yield a $\textit{simplified Born rule}$ in terms of what we call embedded wavefunction coefficients. The resulting expansion reveals which physical poles can occur together while keeping the interaction dependence explicit. For conformally coupled $\operatorname{Tr}(\phi^3)$ theory in flat spacetime, the simplified Born rule takes the form of a uniformly weighted forest-cut expansion. From its pole combinatorics, we construct what we call the $\textit{Born polytope}$, whose canonical function reproduces the normalized correlator for arbitrary graphs after matching its facet supports to the physical energies. The same geometry also yields a stringy formula for correlators.

</details>


### [145] [Generalised $Ω$-backgrounds in twisted $N=4$ super Yang-Mills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40465)
*Elisa Iris Marieni,Itamar Yaakov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces rigid supergravity backgrounds for 4d N=4 super Yang-Mills that generalize topological twists and the Omega-deformation, and derives twisted supersymmetry variations for the Weyl and matter multiplets across all three inequivalent topological twists.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the framework of topological twisting and Omega-deformation from N=2 to N=4 super Yang-Mills by constructing compatible rigid supergravity backgrounds.

Method: Derivation of twisted supersymmetry variations for the N=4 Weyl multiplet and matter multiplets coupled to rigid bosonic supergravity backgrounds, classified by the three inequivalent topological twists.

Result: Explicit twisted SUSY variations for N=4 multiplets on rigid supergravity backgrounds realizing generalized topological twists; identification of new and exotic background geometries.

Conclusion: The construction provides a unified geometric setting for deformed and topologically twisted N=4 SYM, enabling new exact computations and connections to integrable systems and geometric Langlands.

Abstract: We introduce rigid supergravity backgrounds for 4d $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills which deform its topological twists and generalise the $\Omega$-deformation of $\mathcal{N}=2$ gauge theories. We derive the twisted supersymmetry variations of the $\mathcal{N}=4$ Weyl multiplet, and the $\mathcal{N}=4$ matter multiplet coupled to a rigid bosonic background, for each of the three inequivalent topological twists. Other, more exotic backgrounds are also discussed.

</details>


### [146] [Thermodynamics of Swirling Black Holes and Kerr-Taub-NUT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40529)
*Ernesto Frodden,Diego Hidalgo,Francisco Manzano*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes the first law of black hole thermodynamics for Kerr swirling (KS), accelerated Kerr swirling (AKS), and Kerr-Taub-NUT (KTN) black holes, using the surface charge method adapted for spacetimes with conical singularities; it finds non-standard entropy-area relations for KS and KTN due to string-like defects, but recovers the standard 1/4-area entropy for regularized AKS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously derive the first law of black hole thermodynamics for recently discovered black hole solutions—KS, AKS, and KTN—that possess non-trivial asymptotics and/or string-like defects (conical singularities), where conventional mass/angular momentum definitions and entropy formulas break down.

Method: Adapted Noether–Wald surface charge formalism to compute quasi-local, symmetry-derived mass and angular momentum in spacetimes with axial conical singularities; avoided asymptotic flatness assumptions; derived first law by varying the Hamiltonian constraint; validated AKS mass via Christodoulou–Ruffini formula and cross-checked with surface charges.

Result: Finite, well-defined mass and angular momentum for KS and KTN despite conical singularities; first law holds without ad hoc string tension terms; entropy deviates from A/4 for KS and KTN due to topological defects; for AKS—free of such defects—the entropy satisfies S = A/4 and mass agrees between Christodoulou–Ruffini and surface charge methods.

Conclusion: The standard Bekenstein–Hawking entropy formula S = A/4 is not universally valid for black holes with string-like defects; its applicability depends on spacetime topology and regularity. A geometrically consistent thermodynamic framework must incorporate defect structure, and the surface charge method provides a robust, symmetry-based route to define conserved charges in non-asymptotically-flat or topologically nontrivial black hole spacetimes.

Abstract: We establish the first law of black hole thermodynamics for the recently discovered Kerr swirling (KS) and accelerated Kerr swirling (AKS) black hole solutions. We also revisit the first law for the Kerr-Taub-NUT (KTN) spacetime. Guided by Noether's original insight that global symmetries give rise to conservation laws, we employ and adapt the surface charge method for computing the associated charges (mass and angular momentum) in the presence of spacetime string-like defects (conical singularities) along the axes of both KS and KTN black holes. We obtain finite integrated charges in a quasi-local manner, without relying on the non-flat asymptotic regions characteristic of these spacetimes. We then establish a first law without introducing independent stringy terms unrelated to symmetries. For both KS and KTN, the quantities that play the role of the black hole entropy differ from the simple geometrical rule of one-fourth of the horizon area. This mismatch arises because we keep first-law charges associated with conservation laws. It indicates that standard formulas may not be naively used for black holes with string-like defects; they have a different topology and suggest the need for a new geometrical framework. In stark contrast, for the AKS spacetime, which can be regularized outside the singularity and lacks string-like defects, we find that the charges are consistent with a first law having the usual one-fourth of the area entropy. We first determine the mass using the Christodoulou-Ruffini formula and then check its agreement with the result obtained from the surface charge method.

</details>


<div id='hep-ex'></div>

# hep-ex [[Back]](#toc)

### [147] [A Search in LHCb Open Data for $f$-Baryon Resonances Predicted at HADRON 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37674)
*Soctt Chapman*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper tests predictions of two new baryon-like states (with a hypothesized f quark) in the decay B̄ → Λb Λc K⁻, using LHCb data and a 2D Dalitz analysis; it finds nominal significances of 4.02σ and 6.17σ for fixed- and floating-mass resonance models over baseline, but cautions that these are preliminary due to boundary effects, trials factor, and unaccounted systematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test a prior HADRON 2025 prediction of two narrow Λc K⁻ resonances at 3800 and 3950 MeV—interpreted as baryon-like states containing a hypothesized f quark—in the decay B̄ → Λb Λc K⁻.

Method: Unbinned, background-aware two-dimensional Dalitz likelihood fit to public LHCb data; compares three hypotheses: (i) baseline (nonresonant phase space + N(1900) in Λb K⁻), (ii) fixed-mass resonances at 3800/3950 MeV in Λc K⁻ with fitted widths, (iii) one fixed resonance at 3800 MeV, one floating-mass Λc K⁻ resonance, plus two exotic Λc Λb contributions.

Result: Model (ii) yields nominal Wilks-equivalent significance of 4.02σ over baseline; model (iii) yields 6.17σ; however, significances are diagnostic only—resonance fractions constrained ≥0, some widths hit parameter boundaries, floating mass introduces trials effect, and efficiency/systematic uncertainties are not yet included.

Conclusion: The data show intriguing support for the predicted resonances, particularly under the more flexible third model, but the observed significances cannot be interpreted as discovery-level evidence without addressing parameter boundary issues, look-elsewhere effects, and full uncertainty quantification.

Abstract: A prediction presented at HADRON 2025 proposed two baryon-like states containing an additional hypothesized $f$ quark that could appear in the decay $\Bbar\to\Lb\Lc K^-$ in the form of narrow $\Lc K^-$ resonances at 3800 and 3950~MeV. Public LHCb data are used here to compare three hypotheses with an unbinned, background-aware two-dimensional Dalitz likelihood. The baseline contains nonresonant three-body phase space and an $N(1900)$ contribution in $\Lb K^-$. A second model tests the two a-priori prediction masses directly, fixing $\Lc K^-$ resonances at 3800 and 3950~MeV while fitting their widths. A third model keeps the 3800-MeV mass fixed, allows the second $\Lc K^-$ mass and width to float, and adds two exotic $\Lc\Lb$ contributions. The second model gives a nominal Wilks-equivalent improvement of $4.02\sigma$ over the baseline, while the third gives a nominal value of $6.17\sigma$. The quoted significances are model-comparison diagnostics only: the resonance fractions are constrained to be non-negative, some fitted widths reach parameter boundaries, the floating mass introduces a trials effect, and efficiency and systematic uncertainties are not yet incorporated.

</details>


### [148] [First search for sidereal and annual modulation of $Z$-pole asymmetries with the recovered SLD data set](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40282)
*Wonyong Chung*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents the first time-resolved analysis of electroweak asymmetries at the Z pole using recovered SLD polarized e+e−→Z collision data, finding no evidence for Lorentz violation via sidereal, diurnal, or annual modulations in the left-right asymmetry A_LR; it sets stringent 95% C.L. bounds on anisotropy tensor components ρ₀, ρ₁, ρ₂ in the Sun-centered frame and maps them to novel constraints on SME coefficients k_W and k_φφ, while also identifying a new birefringence-sensitive channel in thrust-axis azimuthal modulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test Lorentz symmetry violation (LSV) in the electroweak sector—particularly in the Z-boson resonance region—using time-dependent (sidereal/diurnal/annual) modulations of the left-right asymmetry A_LR, which would arise from background fields coupling to Standard-Model particles in the Standard-Model Extension (SME) framework.

Method: Unbinned maximum-likelihood fits to sidereal-phase-binned A_LR reconstructed from 385,583 hadronic Z decays (1996–1997 SLD data) and independently verified on 229,833 events (1998 run); used published SLD selection criteria, per-event signed Compton polarization, and as-built SLC beam azimuth to define the Sun-centered frame; performed tensor decomposition of A_LR anisotropy; mapped SME sensitivity via leading-order matching to k_W (electroweak gauge sector) and k_φφ (Higgs sector); introduced and analyzed a new helicity-odd thrust-axis azimuthal modulation as a birefringence probe.

Result: No statistically significant sidereal, solar-diurnal, or annual modulation observed in A_LR: sidereal first-harmonic amplitude bounded at 0.0086 (95% C.L., stat.) in initial dataset and confirmed null (0.0018, p=0.90) in 1998 subset; anisotropy tensor components bounded as ρ₁ ≤ 0.060, ρ₂ ≤ 0.18, ρ₀ ≤ 0.092 (95% C.L.); derived O(10⁻²) sensitivities to previously unconstrained two-index components of SME coefficients k_W and k_φφ; null result for new birefringence channel (thrust-axis azimuthal modulation); instrumental drifts constrained at 10⁻⁴–10⁻³ level via hadronization/leptonic asymmetry sidereal analyses.

Conclusion: The absence of time-dependent asymmetry modulations strongly constrains Lorentz-violating backgrounds in the electroweak and Higgs sectors; this work establishes the Z pole as a high-sensitivity probe for SME coefficients k_W and k_φφ, introduces a novel birefringence observable, and demonstrates the robustness of SLD data for precision Lorentz tests beyond original analyses.

Abstract: We report what is, to our knowledge, the first time-resolved analysis of electroweak asymmetries at the $Z$ pole, using the public release of the recovered SLD collision data: $6.6\times10^5$ polarized $e^+e^-\to Z$ events from the 1996-1998 SLC runs, with 385583 selected hadronic $Z$ decays spanning 782 sidereal cycles. The left-right asymmetry $A_{LR}$, reconstructed with the published SLD selection and per-event signed Compton polarization, shows no sidereal, solar-diurnal, or annual modulation; a simultaneous unbinned likelihood fit bounds the sidereal first-harmonic amplitude at 0.0086 (95% C.L., statistical). The analysis, frozen on the initial partial release, applied unchanged to the 229833 events of the later-released 1998 run confirms the null (amplitude 0.0018, p=0.90). Using the as-built SLC beam azimuth from the release's documentation corpus, we bound the asymmetry's anisotropy tensor in the Sun-centered frame: $\rho_1\le0.060$, $\rho_2\le0.18$, and, for vector backgrounds, $\rho_0\le0.092$ (95% C.L.). A leading-order matching maps these onto minimal Standard-Model-Extension coefficients as $10^{-2}$-level sensitivities to the two-time-index block of the electroweak gauge-sector coefficient $k_W$ (components with no existing laboratory constraint) and to the Higgs-sector coefficient $k_{\phi\phi}$; the insertions split the $Z$ pole by polarization, so for the tensor families assessed the resonance saturates the sidereal amplitude near $10^{-2}$ and the Born-model bounds exclude finite coefficient bands rather than set an upper limit. We also derive a previously unpublished $Z$-pole birefringence channel in the helicity-odd azimuthal modulation of the thrust axis, with response enhanced by about $2M_Z/\Gamma_Z$; a Born-level pilot is null. First sidereal analyses of hadronization and leptonic asymmetries bound instrumental drifts at the $10^{-4}$-$10^{-3}$ fractional level.

</details>


### [149] [FCC-μe: High Energy, High Luminosity Muon-Electron Collider & Neutrino Factory at CERN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40451)
*K. Piotrzkowski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes adding μ⁺e⁻ collisions at 1–2 TeV center-of-mass energy and >10³⁴ cm⁻²s⁻¹ luminosity to the FCC-ee (or CEPC) facility alongside standard e⁺e⁻ collisions, enabling precision Higgs self-coupling measurements and serving as a high-intensity neutrino factory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable unique electroweak and Higgs physics—especially direct measurement of the Higgs boson self-interaction—and to provide an intense, well-characterized neutrino beam for neutrino oscillation and scattering studies.

Method: Design and conceptual integration of a muon collider stage (FCC-μe) into the FCC-ee infrastructure, including ultra-cold anti-muon production, muon cooling and acceleration via a dedicated booster, injection into a main muon storage ring, and co-location with e⁺e⁻ operation; includes machine-detector interface considerations and neutrino factory performance estimates.

Result: Feasibility assessment shows that FCC-μe can achieve ≥10³⁴ cm⁻²s⁻¹ luminosity at 1–2 TeV, support precision Higgs self-coupling measurements, and deliver high-flux, low-background neutrino beams suitable for next-generation neutrino experiments.

Conclusion: The FCC-μe configuration is a scientifically compelling and technically plausible extension of FCC-ee/CEPC, offering dual-purpose capabilities for Higgs physics and neutrino science, and establishing anti-muon beams as essential infrastructure for future high-energy storage rings.

Abstract: The concept is presented of making at the FCC-$ee$ (or CEPC) facility $\mu^+e^-$ collisions at the centre-of-mass energy between 1 and 2 TeV and the average luminosity above $10^{34}$cm$^{-2}$s$^{-1}$, concurrently to the $e^+e^-$ collisions. First, a concise scientific motivation is given, including the exciting prospect of measurements of the Higgs boson self-interaction, and is followed by a presentation of the FCC-$\mu e$ layout -- from the source of ultra-cold anti-muons, through the muon booster, to the main muon ring. Basic considerations are also given regarding the machine-detector interface and the performance of the FCC-$\mu e$ and associated neutrino factory. The paper concludes with an outlook underlying relevance of such anti-muon beams for any future high energy storage ring as well as for neutrino science.

</details>


### [150] [The Position Resolution of a Charged Particle Hodoscope Consisting of Triangular Scintillator Counters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37955)
*Edmond Craig Dukes*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the position resolution uncertainty in triangular-counter hodoscopes with wavelength-shifting fibers, deriving approximate expressions for interpolated x and y position uncertainties as functions of particle impact position.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify and improve the position resolution of low-cost, uncooled hodoscopes based on triangular scintillation counters with embedded wavelength-shifting fibers—particularly leveraging light-yield interpolation between adjacent counters.

Method: Analytical derivation of approximate expressions for the uncertainty in interpolated x and y positions, building on an explanation of the interpolation technique used to enhance spatial resolution beyond the physical counter pitch.

Result: Closed-form approximate expressions for the position uncertainty in both x and y directions as explicit functions of the charged particle's impact position within the detector geometry.

Conclusion: The derived uncertainty expressions enable predictive assessment and optimization of position resolution in triangular-counter hodoscope designs, confirming that interpolation yields sub-pitch resolution—typically ~10× better than counter separation—while revealing its dependence on impact location.

Abstract: Hodoscopes consisting of triangular counters with embedded wavelength shifting fibers are becoming more popular as they are relatively inexpensive, are easily fabricated, need no cooling systems, employ low-cost photodetectors, require only modest voltages, and perhaps most importantly, have good position resolution. Through interpolation of the light yields between adjacent counters, position resolutions that are typically an order of magnitude better than the counter separation can be obtained. Here, after a brief explanation of how the interpolation is done to improve the position resolution, we derive an approximate expressions for the uncertainty in the interpolated $x$ and $y$ positions as a function of the impact position of the charged particle.

</details>


### [151] [Amplitude analysis of the charmed baryon decay Lambda_c+ to p K_S^0 pi0](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38406)
*M. Ablikim,M. N. Achasov,P. Adlarson,X. C. Ai,C. S. Akondi,R. Aliberti,A. Amoroso,L. P. An,Q. An,M. S. Anderson,Y. Bai,O. Bakina,H. R. Bao,X. L. Bao,M. Barbagiovanni,V. Batozskaya,K. Begzsuren,N. Berger,M. Berlowski,M. B. Bertani,D. Bettoni,F. Bianchi,E. Bianco,A. Bortone,I. Boyko,R. A. Briere,A. Brueggemann,D. Cabiati,H. Cai,M. H. Cai,X. Cai,X. K. Cai,G. F. Cao,N. Cao,X. Cao,S. A. Cetin,X. Y. Chai,J. F. Chang,T. T. Chang,G. R. Che,Y. Z. Che,C. H. Chen,Chao Chen,E. N. Chen,G. Chen,H. S. Chen,H. Y. Chen,J. Chen,J. D. Chen,M. L. Chen,S. J. Chen,S. M. Chen,T. Chen,W. Chen,X. R. Chen,X. T. Chen,X. Y. Chen,Y. B. Chen,Y. Q. Chen,Z. K. Chen,J. Cheng,L. N. Cheng,S. K. Choi,X. Chu,G. Cibinetto,F. Cossio,J. Cottee-Meldrum,H. L. Dai,J. P. Dai,X. C. Dai,A. Dbeyssi,R. E. de Boer,D. Dedovich,Z. Y. Deng,A. Denig,I. Denisenko,M. Destefanis,F. De Mori,E. Di Fiore,X. X. Ding,Y. Ding,Y. Q. Ding,J. Dong,L. Y. Dong,M. Y. Dong,Z. J. Dong,K. B. Du,M. C. Du,S. X. Du,Shaoxu Du,X. L. Du,Y. Q. Du,C. D. Duan,Z. H. Duan,P. Egorov,G. F. Fan,K. X. Fan,Y. H. Fan,Y. L. Fan,Z. F. Fan*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First amplitude analysis of Λc+ → p KS0 π0 decay using 4.5 fb⁻¹ e⁺e⁻ annihilation data at √s = 4.600–4.699 GeV; observes and measures resonant contributions from p̄K*⁰(892), Δ(1600)⁺, and N(1710)⁺ with high significance, reporting their masses, widths, and branching fractions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To perform the first amplitude analysis of the singly charmed baryon decay Λc+ → p KS0 π0, enabling identification and characterization of intermediate resonant states contributing to this three-body final state.

Method: Amplitude analysis (partial wave analysis) applied to high-statistics Λc+ → p KS0 π0 events reconstructed from e⁺e⁻ annihilation data collected by BESIII at center-of-mass energies 4.600–4.699 GeV (integrated luminosity 4.5 fb⁻¹); includes resonance modeling, likelihood fit, and statistical significance evaluation.

Result: Three resonant contributions identified: Λc+ → p K̄*⁰(892) (12.8σ), Λc+ → Δ(1600)⁺ KS0 (14.5σ), and Λc+ → N(1710)⁺ KS0 (6.2σ); mass and width measurements for Δ(1600)⁺ and N(1710)⁺ reported; absolute branching fractions determined for all three modes with statistical and systematic uncertainties.

Conclusion: This analysis establishes the dominant resonant substructures in Λc+ → p KS0 π0, provides first direct measurements of Δ(1600)⁺ and N(1710)⁺ properties in this production mechanism, and enables improved understanding of Λc+ decay dynamics and excited nucleon spectroscopy.

Abstract: Using $e^+e^-$ annihilation data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 4.5 fb$^{-1}$ collected with the BESIII detector at center-of-mass energies between 4.600 and 4.699 GeV, the first amplitude analysis of the charmed baryon decay $\Lambda_c^+ \to p K_S^0 \pi^0$ is performed. The processes $\Lambda_c^+ \to p \bar{K}^{*}(892)^0$, $\Lambda_c^+ \to \Delta(1600)^+ K_S^0$, and $\Lambda_c^+ \to N(1710)^+ K_S^0$ are identified with statistical significances of $12.8\sigma$, $14.5\sigma$, and $6.2\sigma$, respectively. The mass and width of $\Delta(1600)^+$ are measured to be $(1520.4 \pm 8.8 \pm 37.7)~\text{MeV}$ and $(199.4 \pm 26.4 \pm 39.9)~\text{MeV}$, respectively. The mass and width of $N(1710)^+$ are measured to be $(1719.7 \pm 9.4 \pm 9.1)~\text{MeV}$ and $(72.0 \pm 18.0 \pm 15.0)~\text{MeV}$, respectively. The branching fractions of these processes are determined to be $\mathcal{B}[\Lambda_c^+ \rightarrow \Delta(1600)^+ (\rightarrow p \pi^0) K_S^0] = (8.10 \pm 0.80 \pm 5.57) \times 10^{-3}$, $\mathcal{B}[\Lambda_c^+ \rightarrow N(1710)^+ (\rightarrow p \pi^0) K_S^0] = (1.58 \pm 0.48 \pm 0.77) \times 10^{-3}$, and $\mathcal{B}[\Lambda_c^+ \rightarrow p \bar{K}^{*}(892)^0 (\rightarrow K_S^0 \pi^0)] = (2.63 \pm 0.47 \pm 0.29) \times 10^{-3}$, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second systematic.

</details>


### [152] [The TAMBO Project and the TAMBITO Prototype](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38777)
*Pablo Fernández-Menéndez*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: TAMBO is a proposed mountain-based observatory in Peru designed to detect ultra-high-energy (1–1000 PeV) tau neutrinos using ~5,000 particle detectors on a canyon face, aiming to overcome low astrophysical flux and atmospheric background limitations faced by IceCube/KM3NeT; current R&D centers on the 100-detector TAMBITO prototype.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the limited sensitivity of current neutrino telescopes (e.g., IceCube, KM3NeT) to astrophysical PeV–EeV neutrinos due to small fluxes and dominant atmospheric neutrino backgrounds—especially for tau neutrinos—by exploiting the 'tau air-shower' signature in a mountain-based geometry where backgrounds are suppressed.

Method: TAMBO employs an array of up to 5,000 surface particle detectors deployed on a steep canyon wall in the Colca Valley, Peru, to detect extensive air showers induced by tau leptons produced when tau neutrinos interact in the rock face opposite; the design leverages Earth-skimming geometry and energy-dependent tau regeneration to enhance signal acceptance in the 1–1000 PeV range.

Result: The paper reports the current status of TAMBO’s R&D, including the deployment and performance of the 100-unit TAMBITO prototype, projected construction timeline, and estimated physics reach—including improved sensitivity to diffuse astrophysical tau neutrino fluxes and potential point-source identification above ~100 PeV.

Conclusion: TAMBO represents a novel, complementary approach to neutrino astronomy at the highest energies, filling the observational gap between optical-Cherenkov (IceCube/KM3NeT) and radio-detection (ARA/ARIANNA) techniques; its successful realization would significantly advance multi-messenger studies of cosmic-ray origins and neutrino properties.

Abstract: The detection of astrophysical PeV-scale neutrinos by IceCube and KM3NeT observatories has opened the window to probe new phenomena in the universe. However, a deeper study of the sources remains limited by the small astrophysical flux and the sizable atmospheric neutrino background. The Tau Air-Shower Mountain-Based Observatory (TAMBO) aims to overcome these limitations by detecting tau neutrinos from 1\,PeV to 1\,000\,PeV, an energy range in which backgrounds are largely suppressed and which lies between the reach of optical-Cherenkov observatories and that of radio-based experiments. TAMBO will employ up to 5,000 particle detectors installed on the face of a steep hill in the Colca Valley (Perú) to detect tau neutrinos interacting with the face of the canyon in front via the decay products of the associated tau leptons. In this contribution, we will describe the current status of the project and its R\&D, focusing on the 100-unit array TAMBITO prototype, the projected timeline, and the physics potential that this unique detector brings to the field.

</details>


### [153] [Measurements of transverse kinematic imbalance in muon neutrino charged-current interactions on argon with a single proton and no pions in the final state with SBND](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39042)
*R. Acciarri,L. Aliaga-Soplin,D. Andrade Aldana,C. Andreopoulos,A. Antonakis,S. Balasubramanian,A. Barnard,V. Basque,J. R. Bateman,A. Beever,B. Behera,M. Betancourt,A. Bhat,M. Bishai,A. Blake,B. Bogart,D. Brailsford,A. Brandt,S. Brickner,M. B. Brunetti,G. A. N. Buzzard,L. Camilleri,D. Caratelli,D. Carber,B. Carlson,M. F. Carneiro,R. Castillo Fernandez,F. Cavanna,A. Chappell,H. Chen,S. Chung,R. Coackley,J. I. Crespo-Anadón,C. Cuesta,Y. Dabburi,O. Dalager,M. Dall'Olio,S. K. Das,M. Del Tutto,Z. Djurcic,V. do Lago Pimentel,S. Dominguez-Vidales,M. Donnachie,M. Dubnowski,K. Duffy,S. Dytman,A. Ereditato,E. Espinoza,J. J. Evans,A. C. Ezeribe,C. Fan,A. Filkins,B. Fleming,W. Foreman,D. Franco,G. Fricano,I. Furic,A. P. Furmanski,S. Gao,D. Garcia-Gamez,S. Gardiner,I. Gil-Botella,S. Gollapinni,P. Green,W. C. Griffith,L. Hagaman,P. Hamilton,B. Harris,A. Hergenhan,M. Hernandez-Morquecho,C. James,R. S. Jones,M. Jung,T. Junk,D. Kalra,G. Karagiorgi,F. Kumar,L. Kashur,K. J. Kelly,W. Ketchum,M. J. King,J. Klein,L. Kotsiopoulou,I. Kreslo,T. Kroupová,V. A. Kudryavtsev,H. Lay,R. LaZur,J.-Y. Li,K. Lin,B. R. Littlejohn,L. Liu,D. Lorca,W. C. Louis,X. Luo,A. A. Machado,P. Machado,C. Mariani,F. Marinho,J. Marshall*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First neutrino-argon cross-section measurement from SBND using 8.8×10¹⁹ POT; reports flux-averaged single-differential CC cross sections on argon for 1p0π final states as functions of transverse kinematic imbalance variables; 12,804 signal events (91% purity); data strongly constrain event generators—none reproduce all three variables, especially transverse missing momentum; establishes new benchmark for neutrino-argon modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide high-statistics, high-purity neutrino-argon interaction data to test and improve event generators critical for precision oscillation and cross-section measurements in current and future accelerator-based neutrino experiments.

Method: Analysis of SBND Run 1 data (8.8×10¹⁹ protons-on-target); selection of muon neutrino charged-current interactions on argon with exactly one proton and zero pions in the final state; extraction of flux-averaged single-differential cross sections as functions of transverse kinematic imbalance variables (e.g., transverse missing momentum, transverse lepton momentum, transverse hadronic recoil); use of data-driven background estimation and detailed detector simulation.

Result: 12,804 signal candidates with 91% purity; first high-statistics differential cross-section measurements on argon for 1p0π CC interactions; strong discrimination among neutrino event generators (GENIE, GiBUU, NuWro); largest discrepancies observed in transverse missing momentum distribution; no model simultaneously describes all three measured variables.

Conclusion: These results establish a new experimental benchmark for neutrino-argon interactions, revealing deficiencies in current theoretical models—particularly in modeling final-state interactions and nuclear effects—and underscore the need for improved generator tuning and nuclear physics inputs for next-generation neutrino experiments.

Abstract: This letter presents the first neutrino-argon cross-section measurement from the Short-Baseline Near Detector (SBND) at the Fermi National Accelerator Laboratory. We report the nominal flux-averaged single-differential cross sections of muon neutrino charged-current interactions on argon with a single proton and no pions in the final state as functions of the transverse kinematic imbalance variables. Using a subset of Run~1 data corresponding to $8.8\times10^{19}$ protons-on-target, we select 12,804 signal candidates with a purity of 91%. The extracted cross sections discriminate strongly among event-generator predictions, owing to the high statistics and the design of the analysis. Among the compared models, none successfully describes all three variables, with the largest discrepancies in the transverse missing momentum variable. These results set new benchmarks for neutrino-argon interaction modeling, which is crucial for high-precision measurements at current and future accelerator neutrino experiments.

</details>


### [154] [Dark sector searches at BESIII](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39618)
*Zhijun Li,Zhengyun You*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent BESIII searches for dark sector phenomena—including sub-GeV dark matter, dark photons, invisible decays, dark baryons, and light vector bosons—using charmonia, charm mesons, hyperons, and light mesons; special emphasis is placed on the η→π⁰+invisible channel to constrain scalar-mediated dark matter and test thermal relic models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe sub-GeV dark sector interactions—especially those coupling to charm and light quarks—using high-statistics samples from BESIII, addressing gaps in sensitivity left by high-energy colliders and direct detection experiments.

Method: Analysis of high-luminosity data from BESIII: inclusive and exclusive reconstruction of rare or forbidden decays (e.g., η→π⁰+inv, J/ψ→φ+inv, D⁰→ωγ′, D⁰→γγ′, K⁰_S→inv, Ξ⁻→π⁻χ, χ_{cJ}→J/ψV with V→e⁺e⁻); background suppression via kinematic constraints, missing mass/momentum techniques, and signal region optimization.

Result: New limits on branching fractions for invisible and semi-invisible decays; strongest constraints to date on scalar-mediated dark matter from η→π⁰+invisible, excluding thermal relic cross sections for m_χ ≲ 200 MeV; improved bounds on dark photon kinetic mixing ε and light vector boson couplings.

Conclusion: BESIII provides world-leading sensitivity to light dark sector physics coupled to u, d, s, and c quarks; results significantly constrain theoretical models including scalar DM, dark photons, and dark baryons, and demonstrate the unique role of charm-factory experiments in exploring the MeV–GeV scale.

Abstract: Large samples of charmonia, charm mesons, hyperons and light mesons collected by BESIII offer sensitive tests of dark sector interactions with charm and light quarks. This contribution reviews recent searches for sub-GeV dark matter in $\eta\to\pi^0+\mathrm{invisible}$ and $J/\psi\to\phi+\mathrm{invisible}$, massless dark photons in $D^0\to\omega\gamma'$ and $D^0\to\gamma\gamma'$, invisible $K^0_S$ decays, dark baryons in $\Xi^-\to\pi^-\chi$, and light vector bosons in $\chi_{cJ}\to J/\psi V$, $V\to e^+e^-$. Particular attention is given to the $\eta$ search, which constrains scalar-mediated dark matter and tests thermal relic benchmarks.

</details>


### [155] [Dark-field Search for Quantum Vacuum Signals in the Low-Energy Regime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39867)
*Aimé Matheron,Michal Šmíd,Felix Karbstein,Sebastian Göde,Willi Hippler,Robert Lötzsch,Michal Andrzejewski,Sergey Antipov,Carsten Baehtz,Victorien Bouffetier,Erik Brambrink,Jakub Bulička,Samuele Di Dio Cafiso,Rafael Celestre,Jaromír Chalupský,Thomas E. Cowan,Jörg Grenzer,Travis Griffin,Věra Hájková,Jens Hauser,Peter Hilz,Hauke Höppner,Alžběta Horynová,Lingen Huang,Oliver Humphries,Šimon Jelínek,Libor Juha,Pooyan Khademi,Alejandro Laso-García,Masruri Masruri,Nikita Medvedev,Motoaki Nakatsutsumi,Gerhard G. Paulus,Jennifer Popp,Thomas R. Preston,Isabella Probst,Sripati V. Rahul,Thomas Roth,Felipe C. Salgado,Roland Sauerbrey,Alexander Sävert,Hans-Peter Schlenvoigt,Jan Patrick Schwinkendorf,Frank Seiboth,Georgiy Shoulga,Minxue Tang,Abha Thazhathe Peedika,Monika Toncian,Toma Toncian,Vojtěch Vozda,Edgar Weckert,Colin Wessel,Jan Wild,Ulf Zastrau,Matt Zepf*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a dark-field experiment at the European XFEL to search for light-by-light scattering using colliding XFEL and high-power laser beams; no signal was observed, yielding the most stringent upper limit on the low-energy cross section to date—10 orders of magnitude tighter than prior limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally probe low-energy light-by-light scattering—a pure quantum electrodynamics (QED) process forbidden in classical electrodynamics—and improve upon weak existing experimental constraints.

Method: A pump-probe dark-field setup at EuXFEL's HED instrument, colliding 116 eV XFEL photons with near-infrared laser photons from the 300-TW ReLaX system; polarization filtering, per-shot spatiotemporal overlap diagnostics, polarizing beam splitters, and residual background characterization were employed; start-to-end simulations supported analysis.

Result: No excess over background observed in 2751 pump-probe collisions; derived 95% CL upper limit on the light-by-light scattering cross section is 7×10⁴ × σ_QED.

Conclusion: The experiment sets the world’s most stringent bound on low-energy light-by-light scattering, improving previous limits by ten orders of magnitude and demonstrating feasibility of QED tests in the XUV/soft X-ray regime using high-intensity laser–XFEL collisions.

Abstract: We report on the experimental details of a dark-field search for light-by-light scattering signals performed at the High Energy Density (HED) instrument of the European XFEL (EuXFEL) in a pump-probe type experiment colliding the EuXFEL beam with the 300-TW-class near-infrared Relativistic Laser at XFEL (ReLaX) at a center-of-mass photon energy of $\omega_\star = 116\,\mathrm{eV}$. Especially in combination with polarization filtering, the dark-field concept allows the signal-to-background ratio to be significantly enhanced and both polarization channels to be recorded. This work elaborates on the experimental setup and the diagnostics used, from the per-shot spatiotemporal-overlap diagnostics, via the polarizing beam splitter, to the characterization of the residual background. Moreover, it provides details on the start-to-end simulations of the setup used for analysis. Accumulating 2751 pump-probe collisions, no excess above background is observed, and an upper limit on the light-by-light scattering cross section of $7\times10^{4}$ times the QED prediction at a 95% confidence level is derived. This is the most stringent bound on low-energy light-by-light scattering achieved to date, representing an improvement by ten orders of magnitude upon previous experimental limits.

</details>

### [156] [Nonlinear response and retarded three-point correlators of charge transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38571)
*Jin Hu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes nonlinear charge transport responses in relativistic kinetic theory using the Boltzmann equation under relaxation time approximation, obtaining analytic second-order retarded three-point correlators for massless charged particles, verifying Ward identities and analyzing their analytic structure and connections to Schwinger–Keldysh effective theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study nonlinear (beyond linear-response) charge transport in relativistic systems, especially to access higher-order hydrodynamic and quantum fluctuation effects not captured by linear response alone.

Method: Perturbative expansion of the Boltzmann equation in the external $U(1)$ gauge field source within the relaxation time approximation; recursive solution for the distribution function; analytic computation of fully retarded three-point current correlators at second order in the source.

Result: Analytic expressions for second-order fully retarded three-point correlators at arbitrary frequencies and momenta; explicit verification of Ward identities from charge conservation; characterization of analytic structure (e.g., poles, branch cuts), consistency with hydrodynamic and equilibrium limits, and mapping to Schwinger–Keldysh effective theory of diffusive fluctuations.

Conclusion: Nonlinear charge transport in relativistic kinetic theory is systematically accessible via perturbative Boltzmann solutions; second-order correlators encode universal diffusive dynamics and satisfy fundamental constraints (Ward identities), bridging kinetic theory, hydrodynamics, and quantum effective theories.

Abstract: We study nonlinear response of charge transport in relativistic kinetic theory within the relaxation time approximation. For a classical gas of massless charged particles coupled to an external $U(1)$ gauge field, we organize the Boltzmann equation perturbatively in the source and derive a recursive solution for the distribution function to arbitrary response order. At second order we analytically obtain fully retarded three-point correlators at generic frequencies and momenta, verify the Ward identities implied by charge conservation. We also discuss the general properties of these correlators, including their analytic structure, consistency limits, and relation to Schwinger--Keldysh effective theories of diffusive fluctuations.

</details>


### [157] [Predicting $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ states from a reanalysis of lattice QCD data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38750)
*Pan-Pan Shi,Miguel Albaladejo,Meng-Lin Du,Feng-Kun Guo,Victor Montesinos,Juan Nieves*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: We reanalyze the lattice QCD data for the isoscalar $\bar b\bar c ud$ systems with $J^P=0^+$ and $1^+$ at $m_{\pi}\simeq 220$ MeV, incorporating both short- and long-range interactions constrained by heavy-quark spin and chiral symmetries. Using the interaction fitted to the lattice results, we predict a spectrum of isoscalar $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ molecular states with quantum numbers $J^P=0^+$, $1^+$, and $2^+$. The predicted $J^P=0^+$ and $1^+$ $B\bar D$ and $B^*\bar D$ bound states are consistent with the lattice results. Furthermore, the two resonances reported by the lattice collaboration in the $J^P=0^+$ and $1^+$ $\bar b\bar c ud$ channels can be naturally interpreted as $B^*\bar D{}^*$ and $B\bar D{}^*$ hadronic molecules, respectively. We also predict two previously unreported $B^*\bar D{}^*$ states with $J^P=1^+$ and $2^+$: a bound state and a shallow state whose pole may correspond to either a virtual state or a bound state, respectively. Our results provide a unified interpretation of the lattice spectrum and highlight the interplay between short- and long-range interactions in the dynamics of the $B^{(*)}\bar D{}^{(*)}$ systems.

</details>


### [158] [Nucleon Wave Functions from QCD Vacuum Topology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39215)
*Wei-Yang Liu,Ismail Zahed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the instanton liquid model to derive a microscopic description of the nucleon from instanton-induced quark–quark correlations, yielding a nucleon mass of 939.53 MeV with 98% scalar-diquark and 2% axial-vector components; the small axial-vector admixture reproduces the experimental axial charge g_A = 1.265, and the resulting light-front wave function yields parton distributions consistent with lattice QCD and global fits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a microscopic, topology-driven description of the nucleon grounded in QCD vacuum structure—specifically instantons—as drivers of chiral symmetry breaking, the U_A(1) anomaly, and emergent diquark correlations.

Method: Combines the instanton liquid model (ILM) with covariant Bethe–Salpeter–Faddeev equations to derive diquark interaction kernels from single instantons and instanton–anti-instanton molecules; analytically continues the resulting nucleon amplitude to the light front to compute leading three-quark wave functions and parton distribution functions (PDFs).

Result: Predicts nucleon mass m_N = 939.53 MeV; nucleon Fock composition: 98% scalar-diquark + 2% axial-vector-diquark; axial charge g_A = 1.265; derived unpolarized, helicity, and transversity PDFs and their QCD-evolved moments agree with lattice QCD and global-fit constraints.

Conclusion: QCD vacuum topology—encoded by instantons—not only generates chiral dynamics and nucleon mass but also organizes the leading valence partonic structure; this provides a first-principles foundation for nucleon structure that bridges nonperturbative vacuum effects, hadron spectroscopy, and partonic observables.

Abstract: Instantons, as topologically nontrivial gauge field configurations, provide a microscopic mechanism for chiral symmetry breaking and the $U_A(1)$ anomaly in QCD. Within the instanton liquid model (ILM), instantons generate multi-fermion interactions governed by their density and size. Using this framework, we develop a microscopic description of the nucleon based on instanton-induced quark--quark correlations. We derive the diquark interaction kernels arising from single instantons and instanton--anti-instanton molecules, revealing a strong attraction in the scalar channel and weaker correlations in the spin-one channels. Embedding these interactions in a covariant, permutation-symmetric Bethe--Salpeter--Faddeev equation yields a nucleon mass $m_N=939.53~{\rm MeV}$, with a predominantly scalar-diquark component ($98\%$) and a small axial-vector admixture ($2\%$) around the mass pole. Despite its small weight, the small admixture generates substantial spin--isospin interference, yielding an axial charge $g_A=1.265$, close to experiment. The resulting covariant amplitude is analytically continued to the light front to obtain the leading three-quark wave function. The corresponding unpolarized, helicity, and transversity parton distributions, along with their QCD-evolved moments, provide a direct connection to lattice QCD calculations and global-fit analyses. Our results show that the topology of the QCD vacuum organizes not only the nucleon mass and spin structure, but also its leading valence partonic content, providing a microscopic foundation for a more complete description including confinement and higher Fock components.

</details>


### [159] [Inverse Problem Approach for Non-Perturbative QCD: Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40111)
*Yong Zheng,Ao-Sheng Xiong,Fu-Sheng Yu,Ting Wei*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies the inverse problem approach—a novel theoretical method for non-perturbative QCD—to extract pion and a₁(1260) decay constants and the continuum spectral function, achieving agreement with experimental data and demonstrating rigorous, systematic, and convergent quantification of non-perturbative physics without relying on quark-hadron duality assumptions or Borel transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional approaches to non-perturbative QCD—such as ad hoc duality assumptions, uncontrolled systematic uncertainties, and reliance on Borel transformations—by developing a mathematically rigorous, input-driven inverse problem framework that simultaneously resolves ground states, excited states, and continuum.

Method: Solves a dispersion integral equation as an inverse problem, using perturbative QCD and operator product expansion (OPE) as inputs; employs regularization to ensure stable, convergent solutions; quantifies both statistical and systematic uncertainties rigorously.

Result: Extracted pion decay constant f_π = 130.8 ± 8.1 MeV agrees with experiment; reconstructed spectral function reproduces ALEPH τ-decay data up to s ≃ 3 GeV²; quark-hadron duality emerges naturally at high energies without low-energy assumptions.

Conclusion: The inverse problem approach provides a robust, unified, and systematically improvable framework for non-perturbative QCD observables, validated here for decay constants and spectral functions, and broadly applicable to other non-perturbative quantities.
