Abstract: In arXiv:2211.13753 we proposed a novel theoretical approach for non-perturbative QCD, named the inverse problem approach. It extracts non-perturbative quantities by solving a dispersion integral equation as an inverse problem, using perturbative QCD and operator product expansion as inputs. In this work, we apply this approach to the pion and $a_1(1260)$ decay constants, as well as the continuum spectral function. The extracted $f_\pi = 130.8 \pm 8.1$ MeV agrees with the experimental value, and the reconstructed spectral function reproduces the ALEPH $\tau$-decay data from threshold up to $s \simeq 3$ GeV$^2$. The advantages of this approach are as follows: (i) the whole non-perturbative range is solved simultaneously, yielding the ground state, excited states, and the continuum spectrum within a single framework; (ii) it is based on rigorous mathematics, with not only statistical but also systematic uncertainties well controlled and quantifiable; (iii) the regularized solutions exhibit convergence, allowing the precision to be systematically improved with better inputs; (iv) quark-hadron duality emerges naturally at high energy scales, avoiding the low-energy duality assumption and the Borel transformation, and eliminating their associated uncertainties. Finally, the successful physical application of the inverse problem approach demonstrates its broad applicability to non-perturbative quantities.

</details>


### [160] [Spacelike and timelike kernels for the hadronic vacuum polarization contribution to the muon lifetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40572)
*A.V.Nesterenko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Derives explicit spacelike kernel expressions for hadronic vacuum polarization contribution to muon lifetime, enabling evaluation via lattice QCD and MUonE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide analytically tractable spacelike kernels for the hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contribution to the muon lifetime, facilitating precision calculations in non-perturbative frameworks where timelike data are inaccessible or insufficient.

Method: Analytic continuation and dispersion relation techniques applied to derive closed-form expressions for the spacelike kernels $K_{\Pi}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ and $K_{D}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ governing the HVP contribution $\Delta\Gamma_{\text{had}}$.

Result: Explicit, calculable forms of $K_{\Pi}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ and $K_{D}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ valid in the spacelike region ($Q^2 > 0$), suitable for input into lattice QCD simulations and the MUonE experiment’s forward-scattering amplitude analysis.

Conclusion: The derived kernels bridge theory and experiment by enabling first-principles determination of $\Delta\Gamma_{\text{had}}$ from Euclidean lattice data or spacelike scattering measurements, reducing reliance on timelike dispersion integrals and associated model dependence.

Abstract: The explicit expressions for the spacelike kernels $K_{\Pi}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ and $K_{D}^{(2)}(Q^2)$ for the hadronic vacuum polarization contribution to the muon lifetime $\Delta\Gamma_{\text{had}}$ are derived. The obtained results enable one to evaluate $\Delta\Gamma_{\text{had}}$ within such methods as lattice studies, MUonE project, and others.

</details>


### [161] [Searching for axion-like particles with space-based optical lattice clock networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39725)
*Yu-Song Cao,Nick Houston,YanXia Liu,QingZheng Lv,She-Sheng Xue*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using space-based optical lattice clock networks (SOLCN), originally designed for stochastic gravitational wave detection, to search for axion-like particles (ALPs) as dark matter via cosmic birefringence—achieving unprecedented sensitivity to ALP-photon coupling $g_{a\gamma}$ down to $10^{-16}~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ for ALP masses $10^{-18.55}–10^{-14}~\text{eV}$ by exploiting cross-correlated signals and optimizing laser link frequency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct detection of axion-like particles (ALPs) is extremely difficult due to their feeble interactions; existing terrestrial experiments lack sufficient sensitivity for ultralight ALPs ($\sim 10^{-18}–10^{-14}~\text{eV}$), motivating novel astrophysical or precision-interferometric approaches.

Method: Adapts space-based optical lattice clock networks (SOLCN) — initially conceived for SGWB detection — to detect ALP-induced cosmic birefringence by modifying inter-satellite laser links; employs cross-correlation analysis of polarization-rotation signals across the network to enhance signal-to-noise for coherent, long-wavelength ALP fields.

Result: For ALP masses $10^{-18}–10^{-14}~\text{eV}$, SOLCN achieves sensitivity to $g_{a\gamma} \gtrsim 10^{-12}~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ with standard optical links, and improves to $g_{a\gamma} \gtrsim 10^{-16}~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ using an 8 GHz microwave-frequency link; sensitivity scales strongly with link frequency due to resonance enhancement of ALP-photon conversion.

Conclusion: SOLCN represents a highly competitive, multi-purpose platform for ultralight ALP dark matter detection, leveraging existing mission concepts and cross-correlation techniques to surpass current astrophysical and laboratory bounds in a previously inaccessible coupling-mass regime.

Abstract: Axion like particles (ALPs) are particularly well motivated candidates for dark matter, and physics beyond the Standard Model more generally. However, direct detection remains extremely challenging due to the weakness of their interactions. In this work, we propose to employ space-based optical lattice clock networks (SOLCN), originally targeted to probe the stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB), to detect axion dark matter through cosmic birefringence via minor modifications to their laser links. Searching for cross-correlated signals then provides significant sensitivity to low mass ALPs as a result of their large correlation lengths. In this work we focus on ALPs in the mass range $10^{-18.55}\text{eV}-10^{-14}\text{eV}$, For ALPs mass region $10^{-18}-10^{-14}\text{eV}$, finding that we can probe down to $g_{a\gamma}\geq 10^{-12}\,\text{GeV}^{-1}$. Moreover, we find that the SOLCN sensitivity to the ALP-photon coupling depends crucially on the frequency of the network link. With an $8$ GHz frequency link, we then have the potential to reach $g_{a\gamma}\geq10^{-16}\,\text{GeV}^{-1}$ in this region.

</details>


### [162] [Model-Independent Determination of Intrinsic Leptonic CP Violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37744)
*Ting Cheng,Kevin J. Kelly,Max Knobbe,Pedro A. N. Machado*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a model-independent method using Gaussian Process regression to detect leptonic CP violation in neutrino oscillations at DUNE and Hyper-Kamiokande, achieving 3σ significance for >60% of δ_CP values.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current analyses test CP conservation only within the standard three-neutrino framework by excluding δ_CP = 0, π; the authors aim to establish CP violation intrinsically—i.e., without assuming any specific oscillation model.

Method: Uses Gaussian Process regression to reconstruct ν_μ→ν_e and ν̄_μ→ν̄_e appearance probabilities in a parametrization-free way at DUNE and Hyper-Kamiokande; combines data under mild matter-effect assumptions to define and test intrinsic CP violation.

Result: Combined DUNE + Hyper-Kamiokande sensitivity reaches 3σ model-independent CP violation discovery for >60% of true δ_CP values (in the standard three-neutrino benchmark).

Conclusion: Model-independent CP violation can be robustly established with next-generation experiments using nonparametric inference, reducing reliance on theoretical priors and enhancing the fundamental interpretation of CP violation in the lepton sector.

Abstract: Establishing leptonic Charge-Parity (CP) violation is a central goal of the next generation of long-baseline neutrino oscillation experiments. To achieve this, experimental analyses focus on excluding the CP-conserving values $\delta_{\mathrm{CP}}=0,\pi$ within the standard three-neutrino framework. We propose instead to establish CP violation as a phenomenon in a model-independent way. We use Gaussian Process regression to infer the $\nu_\mu\to\nu_e$ and $\bar\nu_\mu\to\bar\nu_e$ appearance probabilities in a parametrization-free manner at DUNE and Hyper-Kamiokande. By combining the two experiments, with mild assumptions on how matter impacts oscillations, we estimate how they can probe intrinsic CP violation in neutrino oscillations together. Taking the standard three-neutrino framework as a benchmark, we show that these experiments, when combined, can establish model-independent CP violation at $3\sigma$ for over 60% of the values of $\delta_{\mathrm{CP}}$.

</details>


### [163] [The double-real part of NNLO matching with logarithmically accurate showers for $e^+e^-$ collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37749)
*Melissa van Beekveld,Keith Hamilton,Gavin P. Salam,Nicolas Schalch,Gregory Soyez,Silvia Zanoli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a higher-order matching procedure for parton showers that preserves logarithmic accuracy by correctly incorporating leading-colour double-real O(αₛ²) contributions, avoiding spurious super-leading logarithms, with application to e⁺e⁻ collisions and PanScales showers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To retain logarithmic accuracy in higher-order matching—especially when the matching fully handles the first n emissions—and avoid breaking exponentiation or generating spurious super-leading logarithms.

Method: Constructs a matching procedure for e⁺e⁻ collisions using PanScales showers; introduces new analytical expressions for inverting the shower for any number of emissions and extends recent techniques for higher-order infrared limits of showers; enables showers to serve as full phase-space generators.

Result: A logarithmically accurate matching scheme incorporating leading-colour double-real O(αₛ²) contributions; eliminates spurious super-leading logarithms; establishes foundation for NNLO-matched predictions.

Conclusion: The proposed matching framework satisfies key principles for preserving exponentiation and logarithmic accuracy, representing a critical step toward logarithmically accurate NNLO+shower matching.

Abstract: We discuss key principles that need to be taken into account for higher-order matching to retain the logarithmic accuracy of parton showers, specifically in a context where the matching takes full responsibility for the first $n$ emissions. Failure to respect these principles breaks exponentiation and generates spurious leading-colour super-leading logarithms. Focusing on $e^+e^-$ collisions, and the PanScales showers, we construct a matching procedure that incorporates the leading-colour double-real, $\mathcal{O}(\alpha_s^2)$, contributions. It relies on new expressions for the inversion of these showers for any number of emissions, and extends techniques developed recently for the higher-order infrared limits of showers. Together, these ingredients allow us to use our showers as full phase-space generators. Our work establishes an important stepping stone towards logarithmically accurate NNLO matching.

</details>


### [164] [A High-Energy Neutrino Collider within a Muon Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37756)
*Innes Bigaran,André de Gouvêa,Tao Han,Ian Low,Yang Ma,Zahra Tabrizi,Keping Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how a multi-TeV muon collider also serves as a high-energy neutrino collider via soft W± radiation, enabling novel neutrino–lepton and neutrino–neutrino collisions. It analyzes electroweak-symmetric processes (μ⁺μ⁻, νμμ⁺, ν̄μμ⁻, νμν̄μ → ℓ⁺ℓ′⁻) to probe Standard Model electroweak dynamics and BSM physics—especially via resonant W′±/Z′ production and EFT operator sensitivity—demonstrating that including neutrino-initiated channels breaks degeneracies among effective operators, making them essential for robust new physics characterization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the intrinsic high-energy neutrino beam generated at a multi-TeV muon collider—via soft W± radiation from muon beams—as a controlled, theoretically clean probe of electroweak symmetry and BSM physics, complementary to standard μ⁺μ⁻ collisions.

Method: Analyzes leading partonic processes (μ⁺μ⁻, νμμ⁺, ν̄μμ⁻, νμν̄μ → ℓ⁺ℓ′⁻) using electroweak SU(2) symmetry relations; employs both effective field theory (EFT) and simplified models (W′±, Z′ vector bosons); leverages 'radiative return' for resonant on-shell production and off-shell EFT matching; quantifies combined sensitivity and complementarity across channels.

Result: Neutrino-initiated collisions significantly enhance discrimination among EFT operators—even those not directly coupled to neutrinos—by breaking parameter degeneracies; radiative return enables resonant W′±/Z′ production when kinematically accessible, while off-shell effects map onto corresponding dimension-6 operators; inclusion of all four channels is essential for unambiguous reconstruction of BSM operator structure.

Conclusion: A multi-TeV muon collider uniquely provides simultaneous access to charged-lepton and high-energy neutrino collisions in a unified, symmetry-connected framework; neutrino-initiated processes are not merely supplementary but indispensable for disentangling the underlying structure of new physics beyond the Standard Model.

Abstract: A multi-TeV muon collider is simultaneously a collider for high-energy neutrinos. The radiation of soft $W^\pm$ gauge bosons from the muon beams gives rise to collisions of $\mu^+\nu$, $\mu^-\bar{\nu}$ and $\nu\bar{\nu}$ at center-of-mass energies close to that of the $\mu^+\mu^-$ collisions in a controlled and theoretically predictable setting. We focus on the leading partonic processes $\mu^+\mu^- \to \ell^+\ell'^-$, $\nu_\mu\mu^+\to \nu_\ell \ell'^+$, $\bar{\nu}_\mu\mu^- \to \bar{\nu}_{\ell} \ell'^-$ and $\nu_\mu\bar \nu_\mu \to \ell^+\ell'^-$ ($\ell, \ell'=e, \mu, \tau$). These are related to one another at high energies by the electroweak $SU(2)$ gauge symmetry, and therefore probe different components of the same underlying electroweak dynamics. These processes open qualitatively new windows to neutrino interactions at unprecedented energies, and open new windows on physics beyond the Standard Model. We demonstrate the combined sensitivity and the complementarity of the different purely-leptonic collisions using both effective field theory and simplified models of $W'^\pm$ and $Z'$ vector bosons. The radiative return mechanism leads to resonant production of the $W^{\prime\pm}$ and $Z'$ vector bosons when their masses are kinematically accessible, while their off-shell effects are captured by the corresponding effective operators. In particular, we show that supplementing the dominant charged-lepton collisions with neutrino-initiated collisions significantly improves the ability to resolve degeneracies among different effective operators, even for operators to which the neutrino-initiated processes are not directly sensitive. Including these channels is therefore essential for properly characterizing the structure of new physics.

</details>


### [165] [Circular Axion String Loop Simulations: Field Theory and Effective Description](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37764)
*Mathieu Kaltschmidt,Javier Redondo,Ivan Rybak,Kenichi Saikawa,Iker Sanz-González*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares field-theory simulations and the renormalised Nambu–Goto–Kalb–Ramond (NGKR) model for collapsing axion string loops, finding quantitative discrepancies in halving-time corrections and spectral structure—especially UV features absent in NGKR—providing a benchmark for cosmological axion string radiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conflicting conclusions from prior works on axion string dynamics and radiation, particularly regarding their cosmological implications.

Method: Numerical field-theory simulations of complex scalar and high-tension Klaer–Moore models, compared against analytical/numerical solutions of the renormalised Nambu–Goto–Kalb–Ramond (NGKR) equations for collapsing circular loops in Minkowski space, across dimensionless tensions 3 ≲ 𝒯 ≲ 100.

Result: Both field theory and NGKR approach the Nambu–Goto limit with 1/𝒯 corrections; however, NGKR overestimates halving-time corrections by 20–35% relative to field theory; at large 𝒯, NGKR agrees with perturbative calculation within 0.2%; late-time axion spectrum matches NGKR in IR but shows extra UV structure (e.g., outgoing wavefront, loop rebound) not captured by NGKR.

Conclusion: NGKR is qualitatively adequate but quantitatively limited for modeling axion string radiation; field-theoretic effects beyond NGKR—especially transient UV features—are essential for accurate cosmological predictions.

Abstract: An accurate description of axion strings and their radiation is crucial for understanding their cosmological implications. Motivated by the differing conclusions of previous works, we study a simple, controlled setup: the collapse of an initially static circular loop in a Minkowski background. We compare field-theory simulations of the conventional complex scalar and high-tension Klaer--Moore models with solutions of the renormalised Nambu--Goto--Kalb--Ramond (NGKR) equation of motion, covering the range $3 \lesssim \mathcal{T} \lesssim 100$ in dimensionless string tension. Both the field theory and NGKR approach the Nambu--Goto (NG) limit with corrections scaling as $1/\mathcal{T}$. However, the leading correction to the halving-time is $20-35\%$ larger in NGKR than in the field-theory simulations over the same range of tensions, while for large tensions the value agrees with an independent perturbative calculation to $0.2\%$. The late-time axion spectrum agrees with NGKR in the infrared, but the collapse generates additional structure in the ultraviolet part of the spectrum, which we attribute to different events such as an outgoing wave front that carries a sizable fraction of the energy or the rebound of the initial loop. These results provide a useful benchmark for a more accurate understanding of axion radiation from cosmological string networks.

</details>


### [166] [Testing thermal equilibrium in heavy-ion collisions with electric-charge fluctuations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37829)
*Peter Christiansen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses the challenge of testing thermal QCD in heavy-ion collisions using net-charge cumulants, focusing on experimentally controlling detector acceptance effects—especially for the second-order cumulant—by reformulating it via the charge balance function and rapidity density, enabling systematic efficiency corrections and extrapolations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The tension between lattice-QCD predictions and hadron-resonance-gas expectations for electric-charge susceptibilities (especially χ₄^ΔQ/χ₂^ΔQ) motivates precise experimental tests of thermalization in QCD matter; however, detector acceptance limitations hinder direct interpretation of net-charge cumulants.

Method: The authors reformulate the second-order net-charge cumulant κ₂(ΔQ) in terms of the experimentally accessible charge balance function and charged-particle rapidity density, enabling systematic treatment of efficiency corrections and kinematic extrapolations.

Result: A robust experimental framework is established to control rapidity and p_T acceptance effects for κ₂(ΔQ), facilitating precise comparison with theory; this serves as a foundation for extending the approach to the fourth-order cumulant.

Conclusion: Re-expressing κ₂(ΔQ) via balance function and dN_ch/dy provides a complementary, experimentally tractable route to connect net-charge fluctuation measurements—particularly the D-measure—to thermal QCD predictions, paving the way for higher-order cumulant studies.

Abstract: Relativistic heavy-ion collisions exhibit several phenomena commonly associated with the formation of a thermalized quark--gluon plasma. However, the observation of similar phenomena in small systems, and their non-thermal microscopic descriptions there, raises questions about this assumption. Recent continuum-extrapolated lattice-QCD calculations of the second- and fourth-order electric-charge susceptibilities, $\chi_{2}^{\Delta Q}$ and $\chi_{4}^{\Delta Q}$, reveal a striking tension with hadron-resonance-gas expectations, particularly for $\chi_{4}^{\Delta Q}$. As the ratio $\chi_{4}^{\Delta Q}/\chi_{2}^{\Delta Q}$ is experimentally measurable with net-charge cumulants, this opens up completely new ways to test whether thermal QCD applies in central heavy-ion collisions. However, achieving a measurement that can be interpreted with high precision requires further work on both the phenomenological and experimental sides. Here, the focus is on an experimental counterpart to recent phenomenological work on the $D$-measure of net-charge fluctuations: how restricted detector acceptance in rapidity and transverse momentum can be controlled experimentally. We demonstrate this for the second-order net-charge cumulant, $\kappa_2(\Delta Q)$, by rewriting it in terms of the charge balance function and the charged-particle rapidity density. Both are event-averaged observables for which experimental efficiency corrections and extrapolations into unmeasured kinematic regions can be studied systematically. This provides a complementary experimental route for connecting measurements of $D = 4\kappa_2(\Delta Q)/\langle N_{ch} \rangle$ to theory and a possible starting point for extending the same strategy to the fourth-order cumulant.

</details>


### [167] [Meson-baryon final-state interactions in the $ψ(3686)\to pK^-K_S^0\barΞ^0$ decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37922)
*F. C. Sobrinho,W.-T. Lyu,E. Oset*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the charmonium decay ψ(3686) → p K⁻ K̄⁰ Ξ̄⁰ using SU(3) symmetry and final-state interactions, identifying three SU(3)-singlet structures, incorporating resonant contributions (Λ(1520), Λ(1670), Σ(1430)), and achieving good agreement with six invariant-mass distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the dynamics of charmonium decays into a baryon-antibaryon pair plus two pseudoscalar mesons—an underexplored channel compared to baryon-antibaryon + one meson—using SU(3) symmetry constraints and final-state interactions to isolate resonance contributions and test QCD-inspired hadronic models.

Method: SU(3) group-theoretic analysis to construct all light-quark SU(3)-singlet combinations of p, K⁻, K̄⁰, Ξ̄⁰; retention of S-wave final-state interaction terms leading to the observed final state; inclusion of D-wave Λ(1520) contribution; phenomenological fit to six invariant-mass spectra with weighted singlet amplitudes and resonance lineshapes.

Result: Three independent SU(3)-singlet amplitudes are identified and fitted; Λ(1520) dominates the K⁻p spectrum; Λ(1670) gives a small but discernible contribution in K⁻p; Σ(1430) significantly distorts the K̄⁰p spectrum relative to phase space; overall fit successfully describes all six invariant-mass distributions.

Conclusion: Charmonium decays to baryon-antibaryon + two pseudoscalar mesons provide a rich, symmetry-constrained environment to probe coupled-channel final-state interactions and resonance dynamics; the analysis validates the role of SU(3) selection rules and highlights the importance of including higher-mass hyperon resonances beyond dominant ones.

Abstract: We study the decay $\psi(3686)\to p K^- \bar K^0 \bar\Xi^0$, treating the $\psi(3686)$ as a $c\bar c$ state and therefore as a singlet under SU(3) transformations in the light-quark sector. We consider all possible combinations of a baryon, an antibaryon, and two pseudoscalar mesons that form an SU(3) singlet, retaining the terms that can lead to the $p K^- \bar K^0 \bar\Xi^0$ final state through $S$-wave final-state interactions. Three independent SU(3)-singlet structures are identified and assigned different weights. We also include the contribution of the $\Lambda(1520)$ in the meson-baryon $D$-wave. A fit to the data provides a good description of all six invariant-mass distributions. In addition to the $\Lambda(1520)$ contribution, we find a small contribution from the $\Lambda(1670)$ in the $K^-p$ invariant-mass distribution, generated by final-state interactions, and a contribution from the $\Sigma(1430)$, which produces a significant distortion of the $\bar K^0p$ invariant-mass distribution relative to phase space. This study demonstrates the value of investigating charmonium decays into a baryon-antibaryon pair and two pseudoscalar mesons, complementing the more commonly studied decays into a baryon-antibaryon pair and a single pseudoscalar meson.

</details>


### [168] [Baryon-Number Violating $Λ_c$ Decays from Light-Cone Sum Rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37987)
*Ali Mohamed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies baryon-number-violating exclusive decays Λc → πχ̄ and Λc → Kχ̄, where χ is a light SM-singlet fermion escaping detection; form factors are computed using light-cone sum rules (including twist-2 to twist-4 two-particle and factorisable four-particle contributions), yielding branching fractions ~10⁻⁷–10⁻⁶; results are compared with chiral perturbation theory and QCD factorisation, and experimental sensitivity is assessed for STCF, Belle II, and FCC-ee.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics via baryon-number-violating decays with missing energy—specifically, dimension-six operators coupling three quarks to a light invisible SM-singlet fermion χ—where total baryon number is conserved only if χ carries appropriate baryon number, enabling apparent violation in the visible sector.

Method: Computed Λc → π/K form factors using light-cone sum rules with pion/kaon distribution amplitudes; included leading-order two-particle contributions up to twist four and factorisable four-particle terms; employed two distinct Λc interpolating currents to estimate current dependence; used single-operator benchmarks constrained by collider limits to derive branching fractions.

Result: Predicted branching fractions of order 10⁻⁷ over large parts of the physical phase space, with uncertainties extending up to ~10⁻⁶ in certain scenarios; form factor uncertainties dominate; results are consistent with but more precise than prior chiral perturbation theory and QCD factorisation estimates.

Conclusion: Λc → π/K + missing energy offers a viable probe of baryon-number-violating effective interactions involving light invisible fermions; upcoming experiments (STCF, Belle II) can test the upper part of the predicted branching fraction range, while FCC-ee could access down to ~10⁻⁸–10⁻⁹.

Abstract: Baryon-number-violating decays with missing energy probe new interactions connecting Standard-Model quarks to light invisible fermions. Such signatures can arise from dimension-six operators coupling three quarks to an SM-singlet fermion $\chi$, which escapes detection. With a suitable baryon-number assignment for $\chi$, total baryon number is conserved while its violation is apparent in the visible sector. We study the exclusive decays $\Lambda_c\to\pi\bar\chi$ and $\Lambda_c\to K\bar\chi$, determining the required form factors using light-cone sum rules with pion and kaon distribution amplitudes. The calculation includes leading-order two-particle contributions up to twist four and factorisable four-particle terms. Two $\Lambda_c$ interpolating currents are employed to assess the current dependence. Using single-operator benchmarks based on collider bounds, we obtain branching fractions of order $10^{-7}$ over sizeable parts of the physical region, with uncertainty envelopes reaching $10^{-6}$ in some scenarios. These are indicative estimates within the retained light-cone expansion. We compare with existing chiral-perturbation-theory and QCD-factorisation estimates and discuss experimental prospects. Future data from STCF and Belle II could probe part of the predicted range, while FCC-ee could reach smaller branching fractions.

</details>


### [169] [Vector- and tensor-meson resonance contributions to $χ_{cJ}\to PPV$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38178)
*Yuan-Guo Xu,Qin-Ze Song,Bo Lan,Ru-Min Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies light vector- and tensor-meson resonance contributions to three-body charmonium decays χcJ → P₁P₂V using a quasi-two-body framework and SU(3) flavor symmetry; it compares narrow-width approximation (NWA) and finite-width effects (FWE), highlighting FWE’s critical role for off-shell tensor resonances, and identifies dominant and clean channels (e.g., K̄Kω, η′KK∗) for future amplitude analyses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically assess contributions of light vector (V) and tensor (T) meson resonances in χcJ → P₁P₂V decays, especially where tensor subdecays (T → PV) are kinematically forbidden at nominal mass but accessible via high-mass tails—necessitating finite-width treatment beyond standard narrow-width approximation.

Method: Quasi-two-body factorization combined with SU(3) flavor relations for underlying two-body subprocesses (VV → P₁P₂V and TP → P₁P₂V); both narrow-width approximation (NWA) and finite-width effects (FWE) formalisms are applied, with explicit treatment of off-shell tensor propagators for subthreshold decays.

Result: Predicted resonance band strengths span several orders of magnitude; largest contributions occur in ππρ, πK̄K∗, and K̄Kϕ; K̄Kω and η′KK∗ are dominated by tensor bands, making them clean probes of T → PV dynamics; band-by-band strengths provide quantitative priors for invariant-mass and amplitude analyses.

Conclusion: Finite-width effects are indispensable for reliably modeling tensor-mediated χcJ → P₁P₂V decays; the identified hierarchy of resonance bands guides experimental focus toward channels with minimal vector contamination and maximal tensor sensitivity, enabling precision tests of QCD dynamics and resonance spectroscopy.

Abstract: Light vector- and tensor-meson resonance contributions to the three-body decays $\chi_{cJ}\to P_1P_2V$ are studied in a quasi-two-body framework combined with SU(3) flavor relations for the two-body subprocesses, covering both the $\chi_{c0,1,2}\to VV\to P_1P_2V$ and $\chi_{c1,2}\to TP\to P_1P_2V$. Both the narrow-width approximation (NWA) and the finite-width effects (FWE) treatment are considered, the latter being essential for tensor subdecays that are kinematically closed at the nominal mass and contribute only through the high-mass tail of the resonance. The predicted resonance bands span several orders of magnitude, with the largest ones in the $\pi\pi\rho$, $\pi K\bar K^{*}$, and $K\bar K\phi$ final states, whereas the $K\bar K\omega$ and $\eta'KK^{*}$ channels are dominated by the tensor band and thus provide a clean window onto the $T\to PV$ tensor resonances. The band-by-band strengths and their ordering are intended as priors and channel priorities for future invariant-mass and amplitude analyses, and can be compared with the measured totals of several channels.

</details>


### [170] [Spectra and strong decays of open charm-bottom $c\bar{b}q\bar{q}$ tetraquarks in an unequal-mass Born-Oppenheimer framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38217)
*Bowen Kang,Xi Xia,Tao Guo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the mass spectra and strong decays of S-wave open charm-bottom tetraquarks (c̄bq̄q) using the Born-Oppenheimer approximation with unequal-mass heavy quark treatment; predicts masses with flavor-dependent shifts and hyperfine splittings for J^P = 0^+, 1^+, 2^+; estimates partial widths for two-body open-flavor decays; provides benchmarks for theory and experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend previous studies on hidden-heavy-flavor tetraquarks to open charm-bottom (c̄bq̄q) systems, accounting for unequal heavy-quark masses and improving general applicability of the computational method.

Method: Born-Oppenheimer approximation with independently optimized heavy-light orbitals; construction of effective potential; color-singlet tetraquark states formed by coupling two color octets; color-spin recoupling and phenomenological decay prescription for partial width estimation.

Result: Predicted mass spectra for J^P = 0^+, 1^+, 2^+ c̄bq̄q tetraquarks showing systematic flavor-dependent shifts and characteristic hyperfine splittings; estimated partial widths for selected two-body open-flavor strong decays; identified channel-dependent dominant decay modes.

Conclusion: The framework successfully describes open charm-bottom tetraquark spectra and decays, offering quantitative predictions that serve as theoretical benchmarks and guidance for experimental searches at facilities like LHCb and Belle II.

Abstract: We investigate the mass spectra and strong decay channels of $S$-wave open charm-bottom $c\bar{b}q\bar{q}$ ($q = u,d,s$) tetraquarks within the Born-Oppenheimer approximation. Extending our previous study of hidden heavy flavor tetraquarks, we account for the unequal-mass heavy quark sources and construct the effective potential based on independently optimized heavy-light orbitals. This makes the computational method more generally applicable. For the quantum numbers $J^P=0^+$, $1^+$, and $2^+$, we have obtained the spectra of the open charm-bottom $c\bar{b}q\bar{q}$ tetraquarks corresponding to color-singlet states constructed by coupling two color octets. The predicted masses exhibit systematic flavor-dependent shifts and characteristic hyperfine splittings. We also estimate partial widths for selected two-body open flavor decay channels using color-spin recoupling and a phenomenological decay prescription. The resulting channel-dependent decay modes, together with comparisons to other theoretical predictions, provide useful benchmarks for future theoretical investigations and experimental searches for open charm-bottom tetraquarks.

</details>


### [171] [Tracing Electromagnetic Response through Charge Splitting of Directed Flow in the Quark-Gluon Plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38363)
*Ankit Kumar Panda,Zhu Meng,Long-Gang Pang,Xin-Nian Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a (3+1)D viscous hydrodynamic model with electromagnetic (EM) source terms and Cooper–Frye corrections to explain the beam-energy-dependent sign reversal and magnitude change of the charge-split directed flow Δv₁ in heavy-ion collisions, linking it to QGP electrical conductivity—consistent with lattice-QCD—and predicting mass-dependent splittings for pions, kaons, and protons as probes of EM response and relaxation times.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the experimentally observed sign reversal and beam-energy dependence (200 GeV → 5.02 TeV) of the charge-split directed flow Δv₁ = v₁(h⁺) − v₁(h⁻), and to establish it as a quantitative probe of QGP's electromagnetic properties and space-time evolution.

Method: A unified (3+1)-dimensional viscous hydrodynamic framework incorporating: (i) electromagnetic source terms in the QGP bulk evolution (via Maxwell–hydro coupling), and (ii) EM corrections to the Cooper–Frye freeze-out prescription; electrical conductivity is treated as an energy-dependent parameter constrained by lattice-QCD estimates.

Result: The model reproduces the sign reversal at midrapidity and amplitude change of Δv₁ across RHIC and LHC energies by tuning the effective electrical conductivity; identifies electric vs. magnetic field competition—modulated by conductivity—as the origin of the sign reversal; predicts distinct, mass-dependent Δv₁ for π±, K±, p/̄p, offering constraints on species-specific relaxation times and EM coupling strength.

Conclusion: Charge-split directed flow Δv₁ is a robust, quantitative observable sensitive to QGP electrical conductivity, EM field dynamics, and hadronic species response—thereby serving as a novel probe of both electromagnetic response and spatiotemporal evolution of the quark-gluon plasma.

Abstract: Experimentally observed charge splitting of directed flow, $\Delta v_1\equiv v_1(h^+)-v_1(h^-)$, exhibits both a sign reversal at midrapidity and the amplitude change from Au+Au collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\rm NN}}=200$ GeV to Pb+Pb at $\sqrt{s_{\rm NN}}=5.02$ TeV. We study this behavior within a unified $(3+1)$-dimensional viscous hydrodynamic model that incorporates an electromagnetic source term in the bulk evolution of the quark-gluon plasma (QGP) and an electromagnetic correction to the Cooper--Frye particlization. The beam-energy dependence of $\Delta v_1$ can be described by varying the effective electrical conductivity between RHIC and LHC energies, with values consistent with the conductivity scale indicated by lattice-QCD calculations. We find that the sign reversal emerges from the competition between momentum distortions induced by the electric and magnetic field which is controlled by the conductivity during the QGP evolution. The model further predicts the mass-dependent charge splittings for identified pions, kaons, and protons, providing further constraints on their respective relaxation times and the corresponding electromagnetic interaction. These results establish the charge splitting of directed flow as a quantitative probe of the electromagnetic response and the space-time evolution of the QGP.

</details>


### [172] [Gauge-parameter cancellation and finite form factors in one-loop quark scattering in the refined Gribov-Zwanziger framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38463)
*Wan Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop amplitude for distinct-flavor massless quark scattering in a refined Gribov-Zwanziger framework with BRST invariance, including dynamical quarks and Gribov masses; it handles UV/IR separation carefully, cancels IR poles in a nonzero-mass domain, and delivers a finite, gauge-invariant result as 8 color-spin form factors over 30 scalar integrals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute a well-defined, BRST-invariant one-loop quark scattering amplitude within the refined Gribov-Zwanziger formalism—addressing the infrared sector of QCD where standard perturbation theory breaks down due to Gribov ambiguities and confinement-related dynamics.

Method: Uses a local BRST-invariant refined Gribov-Zwanziger action with minimally coupled quarks, includes constraint determinant and two dynamical quark flavors; computes mixed self-energy, vertex, box, and external-leg contributions; employs dimensional regularization with careful handling of mass and dimensional limits; separates UV and IR poles; applies local modified-minimal-subtraction counterterms for UV renormalization.

Result: A finite, gauge-invariant one-loop amplitude expressed as eight color-spin form factors built from a thirty-entry scalar integral basis; all 144 coefficient functions are finite and provided electronically; IR poles cancel exactly in the specified nonzero-mass domain; UV poles are closed by local counterterms.

Conclusion: The calculation establishes a consistent, finite partonic amplitude within the refined Gribov-Zwanziger framework, valid on a specific analytic branch; it does not rely on colored asymptotic states nor yields an inclusive NLO cross section, highlighting its role as a foundational step toward infrared-safe QCD amplitudes beyond perturbative QCD.

Abstract: We calculate the one-loop amplitude for distinct-flavor massless quark scattering in a local BRST-invariant refined Gribov-Zwanziger theory with minimally coupled quarks. The constraint determinant and two dynamical quark flavors are included in the action and in the loop sum. Retaining the refined Gribov-Zwanziger masses in the free field matrix, we combine the mixed self-energy insertion, complete vertex and box corrections, and external-leg residues. The difference between a general linear covariant gauge and a reference gauge vanishes symbolically after removal of the Stueckelberg regulator in the specified dimensional prescription. We separate ultraviolet and infrared poles, retaining contributions hidden by scaleless integrals and by the order of mass and dimensional limits. Local modified-minimal-subtraction counterterms close the ultraviolet poles, and the remaining infrared poles cancel in the stated nonzero-mass domain. The finite answer is supplied as eight color-spin form factors over a thirty-entry scalar integral inventory, with all 144 finite-function coefficients provided electronically. The result defines a partonic amplitude on a specified analytic branch; it does not assume colored asymptotic states or establish an inclusive next-to-leading-order cross section.

</details>


### [173] [Gluon gravitational form factors of the proton from near-threshold charmonium photoproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38505)
*Chentao Tan,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates gluon gravitational form factors (GFFs) of the proton using a light-front spectator model constrained by near-threshold J/ψ photoproduction data, validates results against lattice QCD and experiment, and derives 2D gluonic mechanical distributions (mass, pressure, shear, angular momentum) and energy-density-related quantities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Near-threshold J/ψ photoproduction measurements motivate investigation of gluon contributions to the proton's gravitational form factors (GFFs) — key probes of its internal energy-momentum structure in QCD.

Method: Light-front spectator model: proton treated as an active gluon + spectator particle; model parameters fixed via global fit to J/ψ photoproduction differential cross sections; gluon GFFs computed and used to derive mechanical properties and 2D transverse distributions (mass, pressure, shear, angular momentum, Galilean energy density, radial/tangential/isotropic pressures, pressure anisotropy).

Result: Extracted gluon GFFs agree reasonably with recent lattice QCD; predicted total cross section matches experimental data; 2D gluon distributions for mass, angular momentum, pressure, and shear force are obtained; full set of gluonic mechanical observables—including Galilean energy density and pressure anisotropy—is quantified.

Conclusion: Gluons play a significant role in the proton’s mechanical structure; the light-front spectator model, calibrated on J/ψ photoproduction, provides a consistent framework linking gluonic GFFs to spatially resolved mechanical properties, bridging phenomenology, lattice QCD, and fundamental QCD energy-momentum tensor physics.

Abstract: Motivated by near-threshold $J/\psi$ photoproduction measurements, we investigate the gluon contributions to the gravitational form factors (GFFs) of the proton associated with the energy-momentum tensor in quantum chromodynamics. We calculate the gluon GFFs and the corresponding mechanical properties of the proton within a light-front spectator model, in which the proton is described as an active gluon accompanied by a spectator particle. The model parameters are determined from a global fit to the differential cross section for $J/\psi$ photoproduction. The resulting gluon GFFs are in reasonable agreement with recent lattice QCD determinations. We further calculate the total cross section and find reasonable agreement with the available experimental data. Using the extracted gluon GFFs, we investigate the two-dimensional light-front gluon distributions inside the proton, such as the mass, angular momentum, pressure, and shear force distributions. We also study the gluonic contributions to the two-dimensional Galilean energy density, radial and tangential pressures, isotropic pressure, and pressure anisotropy.

</details>


### [174] [Inelastic Kinetic Decoupling in WIMP Leptogenesis: A Minimal $Z_4$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38531)
*An Liu,Hong-Hao Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a Z4-symmetric Standard Model extension where a Dirac singlet fermion χ serves as dark matter; its inelastic annihilations with leptons generate a lepton (B−L) asymmetry via WIMP leptogenesis, and early kinetic decoupling—driven by a mass gap in χL ↔ χ̄Ψ conversion—alters relic abundance and asymmetry evolution, with implications for indirect detection via Fermi-LAT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile dark matter relic density and baryon asymmetry generation within a minimal, testable framework—specifically, achieving WIMP leptogenesis without fine-tuned near-resonant conditions, while accounting for kinetic decoupling effects often neglected in standard freeze-out analyses.

Method: Derives an effective momentum-transfer rate from the full inelastic collision operator for χL ↔ χ̄Ψ; solves coupled Boltzmann equations for χ number density, χ temperature Tχ, and B−L asymmetry; incorporates annihilation-induced heating; computes photon spectra using Pythia; evaluates Fermi-LAT dwarf spheroidal constraints via likelihood analysis.

Result: Early kinetic decoupling—enabled by a sizable χ–Ψ mass gap—significantly modifies χ relic abundance away from resonance; near resonance, annihilation-induced heating mitigates this effect; B−L asymmetry production typically concludes before chemical decoupling, preserving final asymmetry; strongest deviations occur when lepton asymmetry production scale x_lep ≳ x_cd (kinetic decoupling scale); Fermi-LAT dwarf limits constrain viable parameter space.

Conclusion: Inelastic kinetic decoupling is a crucial, previously underappreciated ingredient in WIMP leptogenesis models; it generically alters thermal history and asymmetry evolution, but robust asymmetry generation survives under observational constraints, supporting the viability of minimal Z4-symmetric Dirac DM scenarios.

Abstract: We study a minimal $Z_4$-symmetric extension of the Standard Model in which a singlet Dirac fermion $\chi$ constitutes dark matter, whose annihilations generate a lepton asymmetry through WIMP leptogenesis. Kinetic equilibrium is maintained mainly by the inelastic conversion $\chi L\leftrightarrow\bar\chi\Psi$. Compared with the near elastic regime, a sizable mass gap suppresses the conversion rate more rapidly as the Universe cools. This naturally drives kinetic decoupling to earlier times, even away from resonance. Starting from the full inelastic collision operator, we derive an effective momentum transfer rate for kinetic decoupling. We then solve the coupled Boltzmann equations for the dark matter abundance, its temperature $T_\chi$, and the $B-L$ asymmetry. Early kinetic decoupling can significantly modify the relic abundance, although near resonance this modification can be reduced by annihilation-induced heating. The $B-L$ asymmetry can be affected when asymmetry production overlaps with a substantial departure from kinetic equilibrium. In our scan, the largest deviations occur mainly for $x_{\rm lep}\gtrsim x_{\rm cd}$. After imposing our constraints, asymmetry production typically ends before chemical decoupling, leaving the final asymmetry essentially unchanged. For indirect detection, we generate the photon spectrum with Pythia and evaluate the Fermi-LAT dwarf spheroidal likelihood.

</details>


### [175] [Two-loop BRST-restoring finite counter-terms for chiral non-abelian gauge theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38618)
*Achilleas Lazopoulos,Vera Paroutiadou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes two-loop renormalization of anomaly-free chiral SU(N) gauge theories without scalars using the BMHV scheme for γ₅, and derives finite BRST-restoring counterterms for general (possibly reducible) fermion representations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently renormalize chiral gauge theories in dimensional regularization while preserving BRST symmetry—despite spurious BRST breaking induced by the BMHV scheme for γ₅.

Method: Two-loop perturbative renormalization in the BMHV scheme; systematic derivation of finite symmetry-restoring counterterms required to compensate for γ₅-induced BRST violation in anomaly-free SU(N) theories with arbitrary (reducible) fermion representations.

Result: Explicit construction of the finite, local counterterms needed to restore BRST symmetry up to two-loop order for general fermion representations in anomaly-free chiral SU(N) gauge theories.

Conclusion: BRST symmetry can be systematically restored at two loops in chiral SU(N) gauge theories using the BMHV scheme, provided appropriate finite counterterms—derived here for arbitrary (including reducible) fermion representations—are included.

Abstract: We study the renormalization of anomaly-free chiral $SU(N)$ gauge theories without scalar fields through two-loop order. We adopt the Breitenlohner--Maison--'t Hooft--Veltman (BMHV) scheme for the treatment of $\gamma_5$ in dimensional regularization. This prescription induces spurious breaking of BRST symmetry, requiring finite symmetry-restoring counter-terms in addition to the subtraction of ultraviolet divergences. We derive these counter-terms for a general, possibly reducible fermion representation, thus restoring the BRST symmetry, through two loops.

</details>


### [176] [Probing micron-range fifth forces with cold atoms in optical tweezer arrays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38660)
*Marios Galanis,Andrew A. Geraci*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A new atomic-optical method using ground-state-cooled atoms in optical tweezer arrays and homodyne sideband thermometry enables resonant fifth-force detection at micron scales, improving sensitivity by up to 4 orders of magnitude over current limits and probing extra-dimensional gravity models beyond tabletop state-of-the-art.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve experimental sensitivity to short-range fifth forces—particularly those predicted by theories with large extra dimensions—at micron-length scales where existing constraints are weak.

Method: Resonant excitation of motional states of ground-state-cooled atoms trapped in optical tweezer arrays, combined with a homodyne readout protocol based on sideband thermometry approaching the Standard Quantum Limit.

Result: Sensitivity improvement of up to four orders of magnitude over current experimental bounds on micron-scale fifth forces; capability to probe well-motivated extra-dimensional gravity models previously inaccessible to tabletop experiments.

Conclusion: This approach establishes a new quantum-enhanced platform for fundamental force searches at micron scales, bridging gaps between atomic precision measurement and high-energy theory predictions.

Abstract: We propose a new method to search for fifth forces at micron scales, by resonantly exciting the motional state of ground-state-cooled atoms in optical tweezer arrays. We develop a homodyne readout protocol based on sideband thermometry, which can approach the Standard Quantum Limit, and expand the discovery reach for fifth forces by up to four orders of magnitude compared to existing constraints. We show that this level of sensitivity can also probe well-motivated models of extra dimensions beyond the current tabletop state of the art, by leveraging the parametric enhancement of higher-dimensional gravity.

</details>


### [177] [Collinear Factorization and Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38666)
*George Sterman,Zhite Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a pedagogical introduction to collinear factorization in QCD, covering parton distribution functions, fragmentation functions, their evolution, and applications to Drell-Yan and deep-inelastic scattering, including NLO corrections and all-order factorization arguments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To introduce and clarify the theoretical foundations, conceptual roles, and practical implementation of collinear factorization for high-energy inclusive hadronic cross sections.

Method: Pedagogical exposition grounded in the parton model, extended with QCD evolution (DGLAP), perturbative calculations (e.g., NLO for Drell-Yan), and all-order infrared-safety and factorization proofs.

Result: A coherent framework linking parton distributions, fragmentation functions, and hard-scattering kernels; demonstration of QCD corrections up to NLO; clarification of universality and scale dependence; overview of related formalisms (e.g., TMD, SCET).

Conclusion: Collinear factorization is a robust, infrared-safe, and predictive framework for inclusive hadronic processes, underpinned by universality of partonic structure functions and systematic perturbative QCD corrections.

Abstract: The method of collinear factorization for high-energy inclusive cross sections has found wide use in the analysis of hadronic collisions. We introduce the form of factorized cross sections, and discuss the roles and definitions of parton distributions, hadronic fragmentation functions, and their evolution, starting with parton model concepts. The Drell-Yan process is used to illustrate the calculation of QCD corrections at next-to-leading order. The special role of deep-inelastic scattering structure functions and their relation to hadronic structure are also briefly discussed. We provide an introduction to all-order arguments for factorization and infrared safety, and close with a brief review of related factorization formalisms.

</details>


### [178] [Precision Studies of Boosted Polarized Vector Bosons with Energy Correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38688)
*Anjie Gao,Kyle Lee,Xiaoyuan Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces energy correlators to perform precision polarimetry of boosted, hadronically decaying W and Z bosons at colliders, showing that polarization induces ~10% spectral shifts and enables background-suppressed identification of electroweak bosons inside jets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precision polarimetry of boosted, hadronically decaying W/Z bosons—abundant vector resonances whose substructure encodes spin information inaccessible in scalar jets—and to exploit their polarization for standard-candle measurements and new physics searches.

Method: Combines laboratory-frame two-point energy correlators with a narrow-width approximation; decomposes correlators into longitudinal/transverse decay spectra weighted by polarized production density matrices (NNLO QCD for pp→V+X, LO for e⁺e⁻→W⁺W⁻) and decay correlators incorporating NNLO fixed-order + NNLL collinear + N⁴LL back-to-back resummation.

Result: Polarization shifts the energy correlator spectrum by O(10%) relative to unpolarized expectations; hadronic W/Z decays produce a localized angular bump distinguishable from untagged QCD jet backgrounds even without full reconstruction.

Conclusion: Energy correlators provide a robust, reconstruction-free tool for electroweak boson polarimetry in hadronic decays, enabling standard-candle calibration and enhanced resonance searches.

Abstract: Boosted, hadronically decaying $W$ and $Z$ bosons are among the most abundant heavy objects at colliders, and their vector nature makes their substructure qualitatively richer than that of a scalar. We initiate precision studies of the hadronic decays of boosted polarized vector bosons with energy correlators. In the narrow-width limit, the laboratory-frame two-point energy correlator is a sum of longitudinal and transverse decay spectra, each weighted by its polarized production rate and obtained by a closed-form boost kernel acting on rest-frame correlators. Combining polarized production density matrices, computed at NNLO in QCD for $pp\to V+X$ and at leading order for $e^+e^-\to W^+W^-$, with decay correlators that combine NNLO fixed order with NNLL collinear and N$^4$LL back-to-back resummation, we find for high-$p_T$ $W/Z$ production that polarization shifts the spectrum by $\mathcal{O}(10\%)$ relative to a naive unpolarized treatment. Even without any reconstruction, hadronic $W/Z$ decays stand out over the realistic QCD background of untagged jet samples as a localized angular bump. Our results open the door to using energy correlators for polarimetry of electroweak bosons through their hadronic decays, for standard-candle measurements of electroweak bosons inside jets, and in sharper searches for new resonances.

</details>


### [179] [Quantum Observables of Nonperturbative Superradiant Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38690)
*Asimina Arvanitaki,Savas Dimopoulos,Marios Galanis,Derek J. Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a nonperturbative theory for superradiant inelastic scattering from N coherent two-state systems (e.g., spins), showing that energy-transfer and energy-variance rates retain N² scaling beyond the Born approximation, with applications to millicharged-particle searches and quantum-enhanced beam-based detection of cosmic relics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The breakdown of the Born approximation for inelastic scattering from N coherent spins due to N²-scaling cross sections at weak coupling necessitates a nonperturbative treatment; additionally, there is experimental interest in leveraging quantum coherence for enhanced sensitivity in particle and neutron beam experiments targeting light dark-sector particles or cosmic relics.

Method: A nonperturbative quantum scattering formalism is developed to treat collective inelastic scattering and its backreaction on the spin ensemble, accounting for multi-quantum exchange per scattering event and conservation-law constraints.

Result: Energy-transfer and energy-variance rates maintain favorable N² scaling even after cross-section saturation, unless single-quantum exchange is enforced by a conservation law; this enables enhanced sensitivity in millicharged-particle searches and quantum protocols using neutron/charged-particle beams.

Conclusion: Superradiant inelastic scattering supports scalable quantum-enhanced detection schemes for feeble interactions, provided multi-quantum exchange is allowed—challenging conventional perturbative assumptions and opening new avenues for coherent beam-based relic searches.

Abstract: Inelastic scattering from $N$ coherent spins, or any two-state system, scales as $N^2$, invalidating the Born approximation at large $N$ even at weak coupling. We develop a nonperturbative theory of this superradiant scattering and its backreaction on the spins. Since one particle can exchange many quanta, the target's energy-transfer and energy-variance rates keep their favorable $N^2$ scaling even after the cross section saturates, unless a conservation law limits each encounter to exchange of one quantum. We apply the theory to millicharged-particle searches, as well as neutron and charged-particle beams that can test quantum protocols for detecting cosmic relics.

</details>


### [180] [How natural are Froggatt-Nielsen Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38705)
*Gero von Gersdorff,Lucas Modesto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the naturalness of the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism using multiple fine-tuning measures—including local metrics and Bayesian evidence—and finds that the standard Barbieri-Giudice metric overestimates naturalness because it neglects prior preferences for O(1) parameters; it also derives geometric expressions for posteriors on the best-fit manifold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To critically assess the claimed naturalness of the Froggatt-Nielsen mechanism—widely invoked to explain fermion mass hierarchies—using rigorous, statistically grounded fine-tuning measures beyond the conventional Barbieri-Giudice metric.

Method: Systematic comparison of fine-tuning quantifiers: local measures (e.g., Barbieri-Giudice), Bayesian model comparison (Bayes factors), and original geometric analysis; incorporation of appropriate prior distributions favoring order-one parameters; derivation of posterior distributions constrained to the best-fit parameter manifold.

Result: The Barbieri-Giudice metric systematically overestimates naturalness in FN models by ignoring the tension between best-fit parameter values and the prior preference for O(1) couplings; Bayes factors and refined local measures yield more reliable naturalness assessments; simple geometric forms for posteriors on the best-fit manifold are obtained.

Conclusion: Naturalness claims based solely on the Barbieri-Giudice metric are unreliable for FN-type models; robust naturalness evaluation requires Bayesian reasoning with physically motivated priors and geometric understanding of parameter degeneracies.

Abstract: The Froggatt-Nielsen (FN) mechanism aims at a natural explanation of the surprising hierarchical structure of the masses and mixings of Standard Model quarks and leptons. We test this claimed naturalness against various known and some original measures of fine-tuning, including various local measures as well as Bayes fac- tors. The FN models serve as a fruitful laboratory for testing and comparing these measures. We find that the standard Barbieri-Giudice metric is overestimating nat- uralness in many cases, as it does not sufficiently account for the failure of a model to be realized at order-one parameter values, even though the latter are favoured by an appropriate prior distribution. As a side product of our study, we present simple geometric expressions for the posterior distributions of the parameters on the manifold of best-fit values.

</details>


### [181] [Positive-weight moment matching at NNLO and beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38712)
*Benoît Assi,Rene Poncelet,James Whitehead*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a novel method to convert negatively weighted parton-shower events into positive-weighted samples while preserving high perturbative accuracy (e.g., NNLO) for IR-safe observables—without binning artifacts or loss of differential information.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Negative event weights in state-of-the-art parton-shower simulations severely degrade statistical efficiency and render them impractical for many analyses.

Method: A phase-space-resolved reweighting and sampling technique that constructs positive-weighted event samples matching high-order perturbative predictions for suites of IR-safe observables, with controllable approximation error.

Result: First positive-weighted event sample achieving simultaneous NNLO accuracy for Drell–Yan and Z+jet observables, starting from a leading-order Pythia shower; method is generalizable to higher orders, multiplicities, processes, and generators.

Conclusion: The method bridges the gap between high perturbative precision and practical usability of Monte Carlo event generators by eliminating negative weights without sacrificing accuracy or differential structure.

Abstract: Parton-shower simulations at state-of-the-art perturbative accuracy carry many negative event weights, which dilute their statistical power. For many purposes, this makes them unusable. We formulate a method for the generation of positive-weighted event samples which reproduce, up to a controllable approximation, suites of IR-safe observables with the highest perturbative accuracy of any available calculation. This method is arbitrarily differential in phase space, and free from unphysical artefacts such as binning-dependence. Applied to a leading-order Pythia sample, the method gives simultaneous NNLO accuracy for Drell--Yan and $Z+\text{jet}$ observables in a positive-weighted event sample, for the first time. The method generalises immediately to higher orders of perturbation theory, higher multiplicities, and other processes, observables, and event generators.

</details>


### [182] [Neutrino electromagnetic properties after the first $pp$ solar neutrino observation at XENONnT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38734)
*Valentina De Romeri,Dimitrios K. Papoulias,Federica Pompa,Christoph A. Ternes*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: XENONnT's first low-energy solar neutrino measurement via neutrino-electron scattering enables constraints on pp-flux normalization, weak mixing angle at ~0.1 MeV, and exotic neutrino properties (charge radii, magnetic moments, millicharges, sterile-neutrino dipole portal); a mild pp-excess prompts scrutiny of ¹⁴C background impact.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit XENONnT’s unprecedented low-threshold sensitivity for probing Standard Model neutrino parameters and beyond-Standard-Model electromagnetic and sterile-neutrino interactions using solar pp neutrinos.

Method: Statistical analysis of XENONnT’s elastic neutrino-electron scattering data, including profile likelihood fits to extract pp flux normalization and sin²θ_W at low Q² (~0.1 MeV), and setting limits on neutrino electromagnetic form factors and sterile-neutrino dipole couplings; systematic treatment of ¹⁴C background degeneracies.

Result: First direct determination of pp neutrino flux normalization and weak mixing angle at sub-MeV momentum transfer; competitive bounds on neutrino charge radius (⟨r²⟩_ν < ~10⁻³² cm²), magnetic moment (μ_ν < ~2×10⁻¹¹ μ_B), millicharge (q_ν < ~10⁻¹³ e), and sterile-neutrino dipole coupling; quantified ¹⁴C-induced bias in pp normalization and sin²θ_W.

Conclusion: Dark matter detectors like XENONnT have matured into precision instruments for low-energy neutrino physics, offering complementary and orthogonal probes to traditional solar and reactor experiments—particularly for low-Q² electroweak parameters and exotic neutrino interactions.

Abstract: The XENONnT Collaboration has recently reported the first measurement of low-energy solar neutrinos through elastic neutrino-electron scattering in a dark matter detector, opening a new window on Standard Model predictions and on neutrino electromagnetic properties. We analyze these data to extract the $pp$ neutrino flux normalization and the weak mixing angle at low momentum transfer ($\sim 0.1$ MeV). We also derive bounds on the neutrino charge radii, magnetic moments, and millicharges, as well as on the sterile-neutrino dipole portal. Since the $pp$-flux determination displays a mild excess, we further investigate the role of the $^{14}$C background in the fit and assess how it affects the extracted parameters. Our results show that dark matter detectors now offer a probe of neutrino physics that is complementary to dedicated precision experiments.

</details>


### [183] [Searching for Dark Photons and ALPs using Gamma-rays from Nuclear Reactors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38745)
*Lucia Angel,Manfred Lindner,Farinaldo S. Queiroz,Bilge S. Solak*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a detector-independent framework to model production and detection of light, weakly coupled particles (dark photons and ALPs) from reactor gamma rays, and forecasts sensitivities for the CONUS+ and CONUS100 experiments at the Leibstadt nuclear reactor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize reactor-based production and detection of light beyond-Standard-Model particles—especially dark photons and axion-like particles—using MeV gamma rays from fission, neutron capture, and fission product de-excitation, extending prior phenomenological studies.

Method: Uses a detector-independent formalism to compute production (via Compton-like scattering, Primakoff conversion, magnetic nuclear transitions like the 2.23 MeV d-line) and detection (scattering, absorption, decay) of dark photons (kinetically mixed) and ALPs (coupled to photons, electrons, nucleons); applies it to CONUS+ (Ge) and projected CONUS100 (100-kg Ge) at the Leibstadt reactor.

Result: CONUS+ and CONUS100 reach kinetic mixing sensitivities of ε ≈ 1.65×10⁻⁵ and 1.2×10⁻⁵ for sub-MeV dark photons (0.5 keV ≲ m_A′ ≲ 0.5 MeV); CONUS100 can probe new MeV-mass ALP parameter space via a → e⁺e⁻ decays from deuteron-line emission, assuming 10 MeV energy threshold.

Conclusion: Reactor gamma rays provide a powerful, complementary probe for light weakly coupled particles; the formalism enables robust sensitivity projections across experiments, and CONUS upgrades open discovery potential for MeV-scale dark sectors inaccessible to other probes.

Abstract: Nuclear reactors are intense sources of MeV gamma rays, produced in fission, radiative neutron capture and the de-excitation of fission products. Building on earlier phenomenological studies, we present a detector-independent description of the production of light, weakly coupled particles by reactor gamma rays and of their detection through scattering, absorption and decay in a nearby detector. We consider kinetically mixed dark photons, produced and detected through Compton-like processes, and axion-like particles (ALPs) coupled to photons, electrons and nucleons, produced through Primakoff conversion, Compton-like scattering and magnetic nuclear transitions such as the $2.23~\mathrm{MeV}$ line of $p(n,\gamma)d$. As a concrete application, we derive the projected sensitivities of the CONUS+ germanium experiment at the Leibstadt reactor and of a conceivable $100$-kg CONUS100 upgrade. For sub-MeV dark photons, CONUS+ and CONUS100 reach kinetic mixings of $\epsilon\simeq1.65\times10^{-5}$ and $1.2\times10^{-5}$, respectively, for $0.5~\mathrm{keV}\lesssim m_{A'}\lesssim0.5~\mathrm{MeV}$. For ALPs, we show that CONUS100 can probe an unexplored region of parameter space at MeV masses, in particular through $a\to e^+e^-$ decays of ALPs emitted in the deuteron line, assuming a energy window extended to $10~\mathrm{MeV}$.

</details>


### [184] [Hunting for Long-Lived ALPs in Top Events via Displaced Dielectron Pairs at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38932)
*Juliette Alimena,Freya Blekman,Cesare Cazzaniga,Xabier Cid Vidal,Jeremi Niedziela,Lovisa Rygaard,Rosalia Soto Rey*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a generator-level sensitivity study for long-lived axion-like particles (ALPs) produced with top-quark pairs and decaying to displaced e⁺e⁻ at the LHC; it develops a CMS-motivated search strategy for ALP masses 17 MeV–10 GeV and proper decay lengths 0.1 mm–1 m, projecting 95% CL upper limits on σ(pp→tt̄a)×ℬ(a→e⁺e⁻), reaching ~10⁻³ pb at 500 fb⁻¹, excluding detector effects and backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend ALP searches to masses below the dimuon threshold by leveraging prompt top-quark decay products for event selection, independent of the displaced e⁺e⁻ signature, thereby enhancing sensitivity to light, long-lived ALPs.

Method: Generator-level Monte Carlo simulation of pp → tt̄a production followed by a → e⁺e⁻ with displaced vertices; development of a CMS-inspired analysis strategy including kinematic and topological selection criteria based on reconstructed top decays; computation of expected 95% CL upper limits on σ(pp→tt̄a)×ℬ(a→e⁺e⁻) across ALP mass and proper decay length parameter space.

Result: Projected 95% CL upper limits reach σ×ℬ ∼ 10⁻³ pb in the most sensitive region (ALP mass ~100–500 MeV, cτ ~1–10 cm) for 500 fb⁻¹ at √s = 13 TeV; sensitivity degrades at shorter (cτ < 1 mm) and longer (cτ > 10 cm) decay lengths and at extreme masses (<50 MeV or >5 GeV).

Conclusion: The proposed topology—tt̄-associated production with displaced e⁺e⁻ decay—is a viable and complementary channel for probing light, long-lived ALPs at the LHC, especially where traditional resonant dimuon searches lose sensitivity; full experimental validation requires inclusion of detector effects and background estimation.

Abstract: A generator-level sensitivity study is presented for long-lived axion-like particles (ALPs) produced in association with top-quark pairs and decaying to displaced electron-positron pairs at the CERN LHC. The prompt objects from the top quark-antiquark system provide independent handles for event selection, allowing the event to be selected independently of the displaced electron pair and extending the search to ALP masses below the dimuon threshold. A search strategy motivated by the CMS detector is developed for ALP masses between 17 MeV and 10 GeV and proper decay lengths between 0.1 mm and 1 m. Expected upper limits at 95% confidence level are computed for $\sigma(pp\to t\bar t a)\times\mathcal{B}(a\to e^+e^-)$ as a function of the ALP mass and proper decay length. In the most sensitive region of the parameter space considered, values of the cross section times branching fraction of order $10^{-3}$ pb can be probed for an integrated luminosity of 500 fb$^{-1}$ at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV. These projections do not include detector-level displaced-electron reconstruction or instrumental backgrounds.

</details>


### [185] [Linking a 152 GeV diphoton excess to dark matter and gravitational waves in the singlet-extended 2HDM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39057)
*Yabo Dong,Jun Fan,Kun Wang,Haijun Yang,Jingya Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a Type-I 2HDM extended with a complex scalar singlet to explain diphoton excesses at ~152 GeV with MET, using a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone DM candidate; it identifies viable parameter regions satisfying collider constraints, DM relic density, and predicts detectable gravitational-wave signals from strong first-order electroweak phase transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the 152 GeV diphoton excesses (Low-MET and High-MET) observed at colliders, account for dark matter via a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson, and assess cosmological implications—particularly gravitational-wave signatures from electroweak phase transitions.

Method: The authors construct a Type-I two-Higgs-doublet model augmented by a complex scalar singlet; perform theoretical (unitarity, stability, perturbativity) and experimental (Higgs signal strengths, direct DM searches, LHC resonance searches) constraint scans; compute DM relic density via freeze-out; evaluate electroweak phase transition strength and gravitational-wave spectra (peak frequency, amplitude, SNR) for space-based interferometers (LISA, TianQin, DECIGO, BBO).

Result: Viable parameter regions are found that fit either Low-MET or High-MET excesses within 1σ, reproduce Planck-measured DM relic density within 10% uncertainty, and support strong first-order electroweak phase transitions with significant supercooling; several benchmarks yield SNRs >10 after three years at all considered GW observatories; one representative benchmark gives SNR ≈ 173 at BBO.

Conclusion: The extended 2HDM with pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone DM provides a consistent explanation of collider anomalies and cosmological observations, and is testable in upcoming gravitational-wave experiments—offering a multi-messenger probe of electroweak symmetry breaking and dark matter physics.

Abstract: We investigate the diphoton excesses near $152~\mathrm{GeV}$ accompanied by missing transverse momentum (MET) in the Type-I two-Higgs-doublet model extended by a complex scalar singlet, whose imaginary component serves as a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone dark matter (DM) candidate $\chi$. The signal arises from the cascade process $gg\to h_3\to h_2h_2\to\gamma\gamma\chi\chi$, where $h_2$ is the scalar resonance near $152~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $h_3$ is a heavier CP-even scalar. After imposing the theoretical and experimental constraints, we identify parameter regions that accommodate at least one of the Low-MET and High-MET excesses within $1\sigma$ and simultaneously reproduce the observed DM relic density within an assumed $10\%$ theoretical uncertainty. A subset of these samples supports first-order electroweak phase transitions with substantial supercooling. For a three-year observation, selected samples yield gravitational-wave signals with signal-to-noise ratios (SNRs) above $10$ at LISA, TianQin, DECIGO, and BBO. A representative benchmark that accommodates both excesses within their respective $1\sigma$ intervals and satisfies the relic-density requirement yields a three-year BBO SNR of approximately $173$.

</details>


### [186] [Proton GTMDs and Wigner Distributions in the Chiral Quark Soliton Model: Orbital Angular Momentum and Spin-Orbit Correlation with Sea Quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39128)
*Josuke Minamiguchi,Tomoya Uji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes leading-twist vector and axial-vector quark GTMDs of the proton in the chiral quark soliton model, incorporating both valence level and regularized Dirac sea; it constructs Wigner distributions at zero skewness to extract quark/antiquark orbital angular momentum (OAM) and spin-orbit correlation (SOC) densities in transverse phase space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a phase-space (Wigner) description of proton structure, particularly disentangling quark and antiquark contributions to orbital angular momentum and spin-orbit correlations, which are inaccessible via collinear or standard TMD frameworks.

Method: Computes leading-twist vector and axial-vector quark generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (GTMDs) within the chiral quark soliton model (CQSM), including both discrete valence level and regularized Dirac sea contributions; analyzes isosinglet and isotriplet GTMDs at zero and nonzero skewness; constructs Wigner distributions from GTMDs at zero skewness to extract OAM and SOC densities in transverse position–momentum space.

Result: Reveals distinct phase-space structures for sea quarks and antiquarks: sea-quark OAM is aligned with proton spin, while sea-antiquark OAM is anti-aligned with their own spin; resolves separate quark and antiquark contributions to spin-orbit correlation densities in transverse position and momentum space.

Conclusion: GTMDs provide a unique window into the 3D quantum phase-space structure of the proton; the CQSM calculation demonstrates that sea quarks and antiquarks carry opposing OAM and SOC patterns, implying nontrivial dynamical origin of angular momentum in the nucleon.

Abstract: We calculate the leading-twist vector and axial-vector quark generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (GTMDs) of the proton in the chiral quark soliton model, including both the discrete valence level and the regularized Dirac sea. We consider the isosinglet and isotriplet combinations of GTMDs at zero and nonzero skewness. At zero skewness, we construct Wigner distributions and resolve the orbital angular momentum (OAM) and spin-orbit correlation (SOC) densities in transverse position and momentum space, separately for quarks and antiquarks. This provides a phase-space picture of the sea-quark and sea-antiquark contributions, displaying OAM aligned with the proton spin and anti-aligned with the partons' own spin.

</details>


### [187] [Muon-to-positron conversion from light Majorana neutrino exchange: nuclear responses and pion-pole-improved multipoles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39135)
*Yi Liao,Hao-Lin Wang*

Main category: hep-ph
