TL;DR: This paper extends the study of muon-to-positron conversion in nuclei to the light Majorana neutrino mass mechanism, deriving the conversion amplitude using effective four-Fermi interactions and nuclear responses within the one-body impulse approximation; it incorporates phenomenological nucleon form factors, accounts for multiple current contributions and time orderings, performs angular reduction and closure operator derivation (including spin-gradient terms), and provides general formulas for 0⁺→0⁺ transitions in terms of two coherent radial amplitudes—enabling future nuclear matrix element input—while deferring isotope-specific predictions, exact lepton wavefunctions, recoil effects, two-body currents, and chiral EFT matching to future work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe lepton-number violation in the electron-muon sector via muon-to-positron conversion mediated by light Majorana neutrinos, extending prior work on dimension-nine operators to the physically motivated mass mechanism.

Method: Derives the conversion amplitude from second-order effective four-Fermi interaction; employs nuclear responses in the one-body impulse approximation; includes convection, axial charge, weak magnetism, and longitudinal pion-pole current via a pion-pole-improved prescription; treats two physical time orderings and distinct momentum transfers in the intermediate-state response; performs angular reduction and derives the closure operator with spin-gradient terms; distinguishes symmetric (scalar) and antisymmetric (vector) lepton-hadron Lorentz contractions for the spin-averaged, angle-integrated rate; provides explicit formulas for 0⁺→0⁺ transitions using two coherent radial amplitudes without assuming spin-zero intermediate nuclei.

Result: A complete, systematically organized theoretical framework for muon-to-positron conversion via the light Majorana neutrino mass mechanism, yielding analytic expressions for the conversion amplitude and rate in terms of nuclear matrix elements; identifies dominant current contributions and establishes interface-ready formulas for nuclear structure inputs; clarifies role of spin-gradient terms and Lorentz structure in the rate.

Conclusion: The analysis provides a robust, nuclear-structure-ready formalism for μ→e conversion via the neutrino mass mechanism, suitable for incorporation of ab initio or phenomenological nuclear matrix elements; however, quantitative predictions require future inclusion of recoil effects, exact lepton wavefunctions, two-body currents, and systematic chiral EFT matching.

Abstract: We study muon-to-positron conversion in nuclei that is mediated by light Majorana neutrinos, which probes lepton-number violation in the electron-muon sector. As the second paper in a series, this work extends our study of local dimension-nine interactions to the mass mechanism. We derive the conversion amplitude from the second-order effective four-Fermi interaction and express it in terms of nuclear responses in the one-body impulse approximation. Using phenomenological nucleon form factors, we retain convection, axial charge, weak magnetism, and the longitudinal pion-pole current in a specified pion-pole-improved prescription. The two physical time orderings and the distinct momentum transfers are derived in the intermediate-state response. We complete its angular reduction and derive the corresponding closure operator, including spin-gradient terms. Both the symmetric (scalar) and antisymmetric (vector) lepton-hadron Lorentz contractions contribute to the spin-averaged, angle-integrated rate. For $0^+\!\to0^+$ transitions we give explicit formulas in terms of two coherent radial amplitudes, without restricting the intermediate nuclei to spin zero. This provides an interface to nuclear calculations of the required nuclear matrix elements. However, in this work, no isotope-specific numerical predictions are attempted. The present treatment uses approximate lepton wavefunctions and recoil-free nuclear propagation. Two-body weak currents and systematic chiral effective-field-theory matching are left for future work.

</details>


### [188] [A doubly charmed strange molecule from coupled-channel dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39143)
*Hee-Jin Kim,Hyun-Chul Kim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper predicts a doubly charmed molecular state $T_{cc\bar{s}}$ with quantum numbers $C=+2$, $\mathcal{S}=+1$, $I=1/2$, and $J^P = 1^+$, bound by 4.01 MeV below the $D^*D_s$ threshold, using an off-shell coupled-channel formalism with meson-exchange interactions and heavy-quark spin symmetry considerations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the existence and properties of exotic hadrons with double charm and strangeness, specifically a $C=+2$, $\mathcal{S}=+1$, $I=1/2$ molecular state, motivated by recent experimental progress in heavy-flavor spectroscopy and theoretical interest in multiquark configurations beyond conventional mesons/baryons.

Method: Off-shell coupled-channel formalism based on a meson-exchange model derived from an effective Lagrangian; scattering equations solved using the Blankenbecler-Sugar reduction; pole analysis and residue decomposition to determine dominant channel representation ($\mathbf{3}_A$); cutoff variation and heavy-quark spin symmetry assessment.

Result: A bound state pole is found only for $J^P = 1^+$ at mass $3972.90$ MeV (binding energy $4.01$ MeV relative to $D^*D_s$ threshold); pole residue analysis confirms dominant $\mathbf{3}_A$ (antisymmetric triplet) $S$-wave configuration; heavy-quark spin symmetry supports stability of the $1^+$ assignment.

Conclusion: The $T_{cc\bar{s}}$ state with $J^P = 1^+$ is a viable, narrow, deeply bound $D^*D_s$ molecular candidate in the $\mathbf{3}_A$ representation, robust under the adopted meson-exchange dynamics and consistent with heavy-quark spin symmetry—suggesting it may be observable in future high-luminosity $e^+e^-$ or hadronic experiments.

Abstract: We investigate a doubly charmed molecular state $T_{cc\bar{s}}$ with $C=+2$, $\mathcal{S}=+1$, and $I=1/2$ in an off-shell coupled-channel formalism. Constructing the interaction kernel from a meson-exchange model based on an effective Lagrangian and solving the coupled-channel scattering equation in the Blankenbecler-Sugar reduction, we find a pole only for $J^P=1^+$ among $J^P=0^\pm$, $1^\pm$, and $2^\pm$. With the reduced cutoff mass of $600$~MeV, we obtain a mass of $3972.90$~MeV, which corresponds to a binding energy of $4.01$~MeV relative to the $D^*D_s$ threshold. Examining the relative signs and magnitudes of the $S$-wave channel couplings extracted from the pole residues, we demonstrate that the pole belongs predominantly to the triplet ($\mathbf{3}_A$) representation. We also discuss the relevance of heavy-quark spin symmetry to the bound state.

</details>


### [189] [Stable Electroweak Strings and Nambu Monopoles Nested at Inert-Higgs Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39173)
*Muneto Nitta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that electroweak Z strings can be stabilized at interfaces between degenerate inert/inert-like Higgs vacua; such interfaces host both fractional Z strings and Nambu monopoles, whose confinement/deconfinement behavior—governed by the interface’s effective theory—is analytically characterized, with implications for Z-flux localization and electromagnetic magnetic charge conservation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the instability of electroweak Z strings and lack of topological protection for their Nambu-monopole endpoints in the bulk, by leveraging domain-wall interfaces between degenerate Higgs vacua as a controllable setting where defect stabilization becomes possible.

Method: Analytic construction of a domain-wall interface between degenerate inert/inert-like Higgs vacua; derivation of an effective 2D theory on the interface including a normalizable SU(2) relative-orientation field; classification of Z-string solutions (unit vs. fractional flux), analysis of monopole kink structure, and phase characterization via stability criteria and flux distribution.

Result: The interface supports locally stable unit-flux Z strings (one-vacuum phase) and fractional Z strings carrying an S² orientation field (two-vacuum phase); Nambu monopoles appear as second kinks between symmetry-related Z strings; two distinct interface phases are identified—confined (Z flux localized into fractional strings) and deconfined (monopole becomes an interface vortex with radially spreading Z flux); electromagnetic magnetic field penetrates outside the interface in both phases; analytic profiles, stability regions, and conserved magnetic charge across the transition are derived.

Conclusion: Electroweak Z strings and Nambu monopoles can be topologically stabilized and physically realized not in the bulk but at solitonic interfaces between degenerate Higgs vacua; their confinement/deconfinement is governed by the interface’s internal symmetry structure and orientation field dynamics, offering a new paradigm for electroweak defect physics beyond standard vacuum-based approaches.

Abstract: Electroweak $Z$ strings are generally unstable, and their Nambu-monopole endpoints lack topological protection in the bulk. Using degenerate inert and inert-like Higgs vacua as a solvable reference, we show that their interface supports both defects. The analytic interface carries a normalizable $SU(2)$ relative-orientation field. Its effective theory supports locally stable unit-flux $Z$ strings in a one-vacuum phase and fractional $Z$ strings in a two-vacuum phase. The fractional string carries an $S^2$ orientation field; a Nambu monopole is a second kink between symmetry-related, equal-tension $Z$ strings. We identify phases in which Nambu monopoles are confined or deconfined within the interface: in the confined phase, their $Z$ flux is concentrated into fractional strings, whereas in the deconfined phase, the monopole becomes a vortex within the interface and its $Z$ flux spreads radially through the interface. The electromagnetic magnetic field extends outside the interface in both phases. We determine analytic string profiles, effective-theory stability regions, and the unchanged electromagnetic magnetic charge across this confinement transition.

</details>


### [190] [Finite-Size Heavy-Diquarks and State-Dependent Corrections to Heavy Antiquark-Diquark Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39494)
*Guo-Fa Yang,Tian-Wei Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a finite-size framework for heavy antiquark-diquark symmetry (HADS), treating the heavy diquark as a composite object with physical mass and spatial extent—rather than a point source—yielding dramatically improved mass predictions (10 MeV mean deviation vs. 108 MeV) for doubly heavy baryons without fitting, and introduces δ_HADS as a state-dependent, geometric measure of HADS breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the limitation of the point-source approximation in heavy antiquark-diquark symmetry (HADS), which neglects the finite spatial extent of heavy diquarks despite their non-negligible mass, leading to large mass prediction errors in doubly heavy baryons.

Method: Introduces a finite-size framework where the heavy diquark is modeled as a composite cluster with independently determined mass and spatial scale; employs a unified peak-height-weighted radius prescription applicable across ground and radially excited, nonstrange and strange diquarks; computes baryon masses using the AL1 three-body potential; defines δ_HADS = 1 − ⟨cos θ̂⟩ as a structure-resolution angle-based, state-dependent quantifier of HADS breaking.

Result: For 16 selected Ξ_cc, Ω_cc, Ξ_bb, and Ω_bb states, mean absolute mass deviation from the AL1 three-body spectrum reduces from 108 MeV (point-source limit) to 10 MeV (finite-size model), without any parameter fitting to baryon data; internal excitation increases δ_HADS (more HADS breaking), while external excitation decreases it; trends and spectral improvement persist across different reference potentials.

Conclusion: Finite diquark size is essential for accurate spectroscopy of doubly heavy baryons; HADS breaking is not universal but state-dependent and geometrically interpretable via δ_HADS; the proposed framework provides a predictive, parameter-free improvement over point-source HADS, validating the physical relevance of diquark compositeness.

Abstract: Heavy antiquark-diquark symmetry (HADS) maps a compact color-antitriplet heavy pair onto a pointlike antiquark source, but retaining a finite diquark mass does not retain its spatial extent. We develop a finite-size framework that treats the heavy diquark as a composite cluster with an independently determined mass and scale. A unified peak-height-weighted radius prescription applies to ground and radially excited diquarks in nonstrange and strange environments. For 16 selected states of $\Xi_{cc}$, $\Omega_{cc}$, $\Xi_{bb}$, and $\Omega_{bb}$, the mean absolute mass deviation from the central AL1 three-body spectrum falls from 108 MeV in the point-source limit to 10 MeV, without fitting to baryon levels. The structure-resolution angle defines $\delta_{\rm HADS}=1-\langle\cos\hat\theta\rangle$, a quantitative, state-dependent measure of the finite-size contribution to HADS breaking. Internal excitation increases this correction, whereas external excitation reduces it, demonstrating that the same heavy source can be resolved differently by distinct surrounding states. Comparisons across reference potentials preserve these trends and the spectral improvement.

</details>


### [191] [Manifestation of near-threshold $Λ\barΛ$ resonances in $e^{+}e^{-}\rightarrowΛ\barΛη$ process](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39526)
*A. I. Milstein,S. G. Salnikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explains the ΛΛ̄η invariant-mass distribution in e⁺e⁻ → ΛΛ̄η via two near-threshold resonances: a 1^{--} loosely bound state and a 1^{+-} virtual state; their inclusion yields excellent agreement with data, including the η angular distribution and its strong dependence on ΛΛ̄ invariant mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain anomalous features in the ΛΛ̄ invariant-mass spectrum of e⁺e⁻ → ΛΛ̄η, particularly near the ΛΛ̄ threshold, where conventional phase-space models fail.

Method: Theoretical modeling of near-threshold S-wave resonances—specifically a 1^{--} bound state and a 1^{+-} virtual state—in the ΛΛ̄ system, coupled to η emission; full amplitude construction includes proper quantum number constraints, unitarity, and analytic continuation for virtual-state treatment.

Result: Simultaneous inclusion of both resonances reproduces the experimental ΛΛ̄ invariant-mass distribution and η angular distribution; a strong correlation between η angular distribution and ΛΛ̄ invariant mass is predicted and confirmed.

Conclusion: The observed structure in e⁺e⁻ → ΛΛ̄η arises from two distinct near-threshold ΛΛ̄ resonances with different pole natures (bound vs. virtual) and quantum numbers, providing compelling evidence for exotic baryon-antibaryon dynamics beyond simple final-state interactions.

Abstract: It is shown that the invariant-mass distribution of $\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}$ pairs in the process $e^{+}e^{-}\rightarrow\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}\eta$ can be explained by near-threshold resonances with quantum numbers $J^{PC}=1^{--}$ and $J^{PC}=1^{+-}$. The resonance with $J^{PC}=1^{--}$ corresponds to a loosely bound state, while the resonance with $J^{PC}=1^{+-}$ is a virtual state above the threshold. Accounting for both resonances results in good agreement between theoretical predictions and experimental data. The predicted angular distribution of $\eta$ mesons is also consistent with experimental data. It is shown that in our model there is a strong dependence of $\eta$-meson angular distribution on the invariant mass of $\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}$ pair.

</details>


### [192] [Scalar Non-standard Interactions in Type-II Seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39616)
*Bhaswati Kalita,Mrinal Kumar Das*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how scalar non-standard interactions (SNSI) arise in an A4-symmetric Type-II seesaw model with an SU(2)L triplet Δ and a real singlet S; after EWSB, their CP-even neutral components mix into two physical scalars φ1, φ2 that couple to both neutrinos and charged leptons, inducing matter-dependent corrections to the neutrino mass matrix via Yukawa-driven SNSI.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify the theoretical origin of scalar non-standard interactions (SNSI) in neutrino propagation and connect them to the structure of the scalar sector in a flavor-symmetric (A4) extension of the Type-II seesaw mechanism.

Method: Constructing an A4-symmetric effective field theory with SU(2)L scalar triplet Δ and real singlet S; performing electroweak symmetry breaking; diagonalizing the CP-even neutral scalar mass matrix to obtain physical states φ1 and φ2; computing their couplings to neutrinos and charged leptons; deriving the matter-corrected neutrino mass matrix including SNSI contributions parameterized by Yukawa couplings and an SNSI coefficient.

Result: Scalar mixing between Δ and S generates two physical CP-even scalars that mediate SNSI between neutrinos and matter; these interactions produce a matter-dependent correction to the Type-II seesaw neutrino mass matrix, capable of enhancing or suppressing neutrino masses relative to vacuum values depending on the sign and magnitude of the SNSI coefficient and Yukawa couplings.

Conclusion: SNSI can naturally emerge from scalar sector mixing in flavored seesaw models; this provides a concrete, minimal, and theoretically consistent framework linking neutrino–matter interactions in dense media to the underlying symmetry and particle content of the scalar sector.

Abstract: We study the theoretical origin of scalar non-standard interactions (SNSI) in an \(A_4\)-symmetric Type-II seesaw model. The model contains an \(SU(2)_L\) scalar triplet \(\Delta\) and a real scalar singlet \(S\). After electroweak symmetry breaking, their CP-even neutral fluctuations mix to form two physical scalar states \(\phi_1\) and \(\phi_2\). These physical scalars couple to both neutrinos and charged leptons, establishing a connection between the neutrino sector and ordinary matter through scalar interactions. This induces a matter-dependent correction to the Type-II seesaw neutrino mass matrix. The SNSI contribution, governed by the relevant Yukawa couplings and the SNSI coefficient, can enhance or suppress the neutrino masses relative to their vacuum values. The model provides a simple and consistent description of how scalar mixing can give rise to SNSI and connects neutrino interactions in matter with the underlying structure of the scalar sector.

</details>


### [193] [The effect of Coulomb interaction on Levy source parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39626)
*Kristof Csiki,Mate Csanad,Barnabas Porfy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how Coulomb interactions affect the Lévy stability index α in femtoscopic analyses of heavy-ion collisions, using a custom C++/Python simulation framework to compute two-particle source functions and quantify α dependence on particle number, fitting range, and initial source geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Experimental femtoscopic data deviate from theoretical predictions, partly due to inaccurate or omitted treatment of Coulomb interactions in source modeling; Gaussian assumptions fail, and Lévy distributions (with stability index α) are used instead—but α’s sensitivity to Coulomb effects remains poorly quantified.

Method: A custom hybrid simulation framework: C++ module solves relativistic pion equations of motion under Coulomb interaction via Leapfrog integration; Python post-processor reconstructs the two-particle spatial source function from final-state phase-space coordinates; systematic variation of particle multiplicity, fitting range, and initial source size/density to extract α via Lévy fits.

Result: Quantified systematic shifts in the fitted Lévy α due to Coulomb effects—specifically, α decreases (source becomes more leptokurtic) with increasing particle density and decreasing initial source size; the ratio and slope of reconstructed vs. input α exhibit nontrivial dependence on fitting range and statistics.

Conclusion: Coulomb interaction significantly biases the extracted Lévy stability index α in femtoscopy; accurate modeling of final-state Coulomb effects is essential for reliable interpretation of source geometry and dynamics in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: Femtoscopy plays an important role in the study of heavy-ion collisions and particle sources generated within them. In both experimental measurements and theoretical models, a Lévy distribution is used to describe the shape of the source, as the Gaussian distribution has proven to be inaccurate based on experimental observations. Despite this, there are still discrepancies between experimental data and the results of theoretical models. One reason may be that these theoretical models either approximate the Coulomb interaction poorly or omit it entirely. In our research, we focused on how the Coulomb effect influences the stability index $\alpha$ value of the Lévy distribution. For this, we used a custom-developed simulation framework written in C++ and Python. The C++ module integrates the equations of motion for relativistic pions using the Leapfrog algorithm, while the Python post-processing script generates the two-particle spatial source function from the phase-space coordinates. We investigate the ratio and slope of the generated and pair-source fitted $\alpha$ as a function of the number of particles, fitting range, and the initial source size and density.

</details>


### [194] [Radiative $θ$ in Left-Right Universal Seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39697)
*Tatsuya Banno,Junji Hisano,Teppei Kitahara,Naohiro Osamura*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes two-loop radiative corrections to the QCD \bar{\theta} parameter in a left-right symmetric model with universal seesaw and vector-like quarks; it finds that \bar{\theta} generically exceeds experimental bounds unless vector-like quark masses are nearly degenerate (with tight mass-splitting constraints) or flavor mixing is highly suppressed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the strong CP problem in the context of left-right symmetric models with universal seesaw, where tree-level \bar{\theta} = 0 due to parity symmetry but radiative corrections may reintroduce a dangerous CP-violating phase.

Method: Computes leading two-loop corrections to light-quark mass matrices induced by soft parity breaking; identifies CP-odd rephasing invariants contributing to \bar{\theta}; assumes Hermitian vector-like quark mass matrices and analyzes dependence on mass splittings and flavor mixings.

Result: Dominant \bar{\theta} contributions do not decouple; for hierarchical vector-like quark masses, \bar{\theta} typically violates the neutron EDM bound; satisfying the bound requires either near-degeneracy (GIM suppression) or strongly suppressed mixing; upper limits on relative mass splittings: ~10^{-2} (up-type) and ~10^{-4} (down-type).

Conclusion: The inverted vector-like quark mass hierarchy—motivated by the universal seesaw mechanism—is severely constrained by the \bar{\theta} bound, favoring either fine-tuned degeneracy or minimal flavor violation.

Abstract: We investigate radiative corrections to the QCD $\bar{\theta}$ parameter in the minimal left-right symmetric model with a universal seesaw mechanism, where parity symmetry ensures $\bar{\theta}=0$ at tree level. Assuming Hermitian vector-like quark mass matrices, we identify the $CP$-odd rephasing invariants contributing to $\bar{\theta}$ and evaluate the leading two-loop corrections to the light-quark mass matrices induced by soft parity breaking. We find that the dominant contributions do not decouple and depend sensitively on the mass splittings as well as flavor mixings of the vector-like quarks. For hierarchical vector-like quark masses, the resulting $\bar{\theta}$ typically exceeds the current experimental bound from the neutron electric dipole moment. Satisfying this bound requires either nearly degenerate vector-like quark masses or strongly suppressed additional flavor mixing angles. In the former case, the radiative corrections are suppressed by a GIM-like mechanism. For generic mixing parameters, the dimensionless relative mass splittings are constrained to be below approximately $10^{-2}$ and $10^{-4}$ in the up- and down-type vector-like quark sectors, respectively. These results severely restrict the inverted vector-like quark mass hierarchy motivated by the universal seesaw mechanism.

</details>


### [195] [Next-to-leading-order QCD study of $e^+e^- \to η_c + c\bar{c}$ at $B$ factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39718)
*Lu-Yao Zhang,Xu-Chang Zheng,Xing-Gang Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NLO QCD corrections to e+e−→γ∗→ηc+c̄c+X within NRQCD factorization (color-singlet, v²-leading), finding a K-factor ~2.0 at √s=10.6 GeV; total prompt ηc + c̄c cross section is estimated at 131 fb including J/ψ and hc feed-down.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a precise theoretical baseline for ηc production associated with open charm at Belle II, complementing hadronic charmonium studies, by computing NLO QCD corrections within NRQCD factorization.

Method: NRQCD factorization framework with color-singlet contribution at leading order in heavy-quark relative velocity v; next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD corrections computed for e+e−→γ∗→ηc+c̄c+X.

Result: At √s = 10.6 GeV, m_c = 1.5 GeV, μ_R = 2m_c: LO direct cross section = 58.7 fb; NLO direct cross section = 118 fb (K ≈ 2.0); prompt cross section including J/ψ and h_c(1P) radiative feed-down = 131 fb; NLO enhances angular and momentum distributions but preserves overall momentum spectrum shape.

Conclusion: The NLO color-singlet calculation establishes a robust theoretical baseline for ηc + c̄c production at Belle II, demonstrating substantial scale stability and kinematic enhancement from radiative corrections.

Abstract: We study the process $e^+e^- \to \gamma^\ast \to \eta_c + c\bar{c} + X$ at next-to-leading order (NLO) in QCD within the framework of nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization, retaining the color-singlet contribution at leading order in the heavy-quark relative velocity $v$. At the $e^+e^-$ collision energy $\sqrt{s} = 10.6~\mathrm{GeV}$, with $m_c = 1.5~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $\mu_R = 2m_c$, the direct cross section increases from $58.7~\mathrm{fb}$ at leading order (LO) to $118~\mathrm{fb}$ at NLO, corresponding to a $K$ factor of approximately $2.0$. Including the radiative feed-down contributions of the $J/\psi$ and $h_c(1P)$ states yields an estimated prompt production rate of $131~\mathrm{fb}$. The NLO corrections substantially enhance both the angular and momentum distributions, while the momentum spectrum retains a similar overall profile. These results establish an NLO color-singlet baseline for $\eta_c$ production associated with open charm at Belle II, complementary to studies of charmonium production in hadronic collisions.

</details>


### [196] [From Colour--Casimir Recoupling to Near-Threshold Scattering: Algebraic Diagnostics and a Pion-Mass Test for the $T_{cc}^{+}$ Channel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39787)
*M. Monemzadeh,N. Tazimi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the near-threshold $T_{cc}^{+}$ tetraquark as a probe of colour structure in hadronic scattering, presenting three technical results: (i) an exact colour-algebraic projection onto meson–hidden-colour basis revealing constraints on pairwise colour operators; (ii) negative dynamical findings showing no binding from hidden-colour mixing under Gaussian models and a compact tetraquark level lying 148.6 MeV above threshold; (iii) a pion-mass dependence test using lattice inputs, with a separable Lippmann–Schwinger model used only for regulator-sensitivity assessment—not to reproduce the full lattice pole.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify how constituent-level QCD colour structure governs low-energy hadronic scattering, specifically whether the observed near-threshold $T_{cc}^{+}$ state arises from mesonic or hidden-colour dynamics, and to assess the role of colour algebra versus dynamical input in tetraquark models.

Method: Colour-space projection in the $cc\bar u\bar d$ singlet basis; numerical validation over 10,000 random edge assignments; analysis of pairwise colour operators and their matrix elements; Gaussian-model constituent Hamiltonian calibrated to $D$ and $D^*$ masses; one-channel separable Lippmann–Schwinger equation with refitted parameters across pion masses; comparison to lattice QCD poles at $m_\pi \simeq 280$ and $391$ MeV.

Result: 1) Exact colour-algebraic decomposition shows $V_{MM}$ depends only on intra-meson couplings and $V_{MH}^{\rm off}$ vanishes for uniform edges; the decomposition re-expresses—rather than adds—a central colour operator. 2) No binding is found: hidden-colour diagonal lies below meson diagonal in Coulomb-type Gaussian models, and the lowest compact level is +148.6 MeV above $DD^*$ threshold. 3) Under pion-mass variation, the separable model reproduces only the real part of the lattice pole (−5.2 MeV), not its complex structure; no coupled-channel calculation is performed.

Conclusion: The $T_{cc}^{+}$ near-threshold behavior cannot be explained by simple colour-structure projections or Gaussian constituent models alone; the observed state likely requires explicit dynamical inputs beyond static colour algebra—including coupled-channel effects, pion exchange, or non-Gaussian confinement—beyond the scope of this analysis.

Abstract: The near-threshold $T_{cc}^{+}$ is a clean testing ground for how constituent-level colour structure connects to hadronic scattering. We report three results and state what each does and does not establish. (i)~\emph{Colour algebra.} In the two-dimensional colour-singlet basis of $cc\bar u\bar d$, a general pairwise colour operator has direct $DD^*$ matrix element $V_{MM}=-\tfrac43(v_{13}+v_{24})$, containing only the two intra-meson coefficients, while meson--hidden-colour mixing is $V_{MH}^{\rm off}=\tfrac{\sqrt2}{3}\Delta_v$ with $\Delta_v=v_{14}+v_{23}-v_{12}-v_{34}$; uniform edges give no mixing. The projection is exact within this basis and is checked numerically on 10\,000 random edge assignments. A same-Hamiltonian decomposition shows that it re-expresses the central colour operator ($116.46$ MeV off-diagonal) rather than adding an independent dynamical input. (ii)~\emph{Negative dynamical results.} For Coulomb-type Gaussian edges the hidden-colour diagonal lies below the meson diagonal, so the attractive second-order shift $-2\Delta_v^2/(9\Delta_H)$ does not apply. In a Gaussian constituent Hamiltonian calibrated to $M_D$ and $M_{D^*}$, the lowest compact level lies $148.6$ MeV above its model $DD^*$ threshold (iii)~\emph{Pion-mass test.} Couplings fixed at the $m_\pi\simeq280$ MeV lattice pole ($-5.2$ MeV) are carried to $m_\pi\simeq391$ MeV by changing only the reduced mass and compact-sector gap, both refitted to the lattice $D$ and $D^*$ masses. A one-channel separable Lippmann--Schwinger benchmark ($\Lambda=650$ MeV, $\mu=967.7$ MeV, $C_G\simeq-36.15~\mathrm{GeV}^{-2}$) is calibrated to the $5.2$ MeV pole and serves only as a regulator-sensitivity study. We do not reproduce the complex lattice pole or perform a full coupled-channel calculation.

</details>


### [197] [Spontaneous CP violation and leptogenesis in minimal flavour models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39858)
*H. B. Câmara,F. R. Joaquim,J. R. Rocha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a type-I seesaw extension with a CP-breaking scalar singlet to unify leptonic CP violation and baryogenesis via leptogenesis; it constructs two minimal Z₄-symmetric flavour models predicting specific values for δ, θ₂₃ octant, and testable lower bounds on m_lightest and m_ββ; full non-resonant leptogenesis including new scalar-mediated processes is solved numerically to identify viable BAU-generating parameter regions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified origin for low-energy leptonic CP violation (e.g., Dirac phase δ) and high-energy CP violation required for successful thermal leptogenesis—avoiding fine-tuning by linking both to the spontaneous CP violation from a scalar singlet's VEV phase.

Method: Constructs two minimal flavour models based on Z₄ symmetry where neutrino mixing arises solely from the heavy Majorana mass matrix; performs analytical and numerical analysis of scalar-singlet-assisted leptogenesis in the non-resonant regime, incorporating new decay/scattering/washout channels and CP asymmetries induced by heavy scalars; solves unflavoured Boltzmann equations to compute final baryon asymmetry.

Result: Both models predict δ ≈ 3π/2, definite θ₂₃ octant, and lower bounds: m_lightest ≳ 60 meV (NO) / 50 meV (IO), m_ββ ≳ same order; viable BAU-compatible parameter space is identified, with scalar contributions significantly modifying CP asymmetries and washout dynamics.

Conclusion: The scalar singlet with spontaneous CP violation provides a predictive, testable framework linking neutrino oscillation observables, 0νββ decay, and baryogenesis—demonstrating that minimal discrete flavour symmetries can simultaneously explain low- and high-energy CP violation without ad hoc phases.

Abstract: We consider a type-I seesaw framework extended by a scalar singlet whose vacuum expectation value (VEV) breaks CP spontaneously. The singlet VEV phase provides a common origin for low-energy leptonic CP violation and the high energy CP violation needed to generate the observed baryon asymmetry of the Universe (BAU) through leptogenesis. We identify two minimal flavour models based on a discrete $\mathcal{Z}_4$ symmetry, whose neutrino flavour structure is encoded entirely in the heavy Majorana mass matrix. Both models predict $\delta \simeq 3\pi/2$, a definite octant for the atmospheric $\theta_{23}$ mixing angle, and lower bounds of approximately $60~\mathrm{meV}$ ($50~\mathrm{meV}$) on the lightest neutrino mass and the effective Majorana mass $m_{\beta\beta}$ for normal (inverted) ordering, within reach of future neutrinoless double-beta decay searches. We study scalar-singlet assisted leptogenesis in the non-resonant regime, including new decay, scattering, and washout processes involving the heavy scalars, as well as their contributions to the CP asymmetries. By solving the unflavoured Boltzmann equations, we identify the regions of parameter space in which the minimal flavour models successfully account for the observed BAU.

</details>


### [198] [NNLO QCD corrections to direct $J/ψ$ production with open charm at B factories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39895)
*Xiangyu Chang,Chao Zeng,Bin Gong,Jian-Xiong Wang,Cong Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes NNLO QCD corrections to direct colour-singlet J/ψ production with open charm at B factories using nested soft-collinear subtraction for massive partons; it finds improved perturbative convergence and reduced scale dependence, brings the momentum spectrum closer to Belle data near the peak, but worsens agreement in angular shape—motivating Belle II measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision for direct colour-singlet J/ψ + open charm production at B factories, resolve discrepancies between NLO predictions and Belle data (especially in kinematic distributions), and assess perturbative stability beyond NLO.

Method: Computes NNLO QCD corrections using nested soft-collinear subtraction for massive partons (c-quark); employs fixed-order perturbation theory with m_c = 1.5 GeV, √s = 10.6 GeV, and renormalization scale μ_R varied from m_c to 4m_c; includes NLO-based charm-mass extrapolation and ψ(2S) feed-down estimation.

Result: NNLO cross section: 0.324^{+0.041}_{-0.066} pb at μ_R = 2m_c; 18.4% increase over NLO with reduced scale uncertainty; momentum spectrum near peak improves agreement with Belle prompt open-charm data; angular distribution worsens agreement with Belle charm-associated data.

Conclusion: NNLO corrections significantly improve perturbative reliability and momentum-spectrum description, but expose tension in angular observables—highlighting need for high-precision Belle II measurements to discriminate between theoretical assumptions (e.g., NRQCD LDMEs, feed-down modeling, or higher-twist effects).

Abstract: We calculate the next-to-next-to-leading-order (NNLO) QCD corrections to direct colour-singlet $J/\psi$ production with open charm at B factories, using nested soft-collinear subtraction for massive partons. At $\sqrt{s}=10.6$ GeV, $m_c=1.5$ GeV and $\mu_R=2m_c$, we obtain an approximate NNLO cross section of $0.324^{+0.041}_{-0.066}$ pb, with scale uncertainties from $m_c\leq\mu_R\leq4m_c$. The total cross section is $18.4\%$ above NLO and exhibits good perturbative convergence through NNLO, with reduced scale dependence. An NLO-based mass extrapolation to $m_c=1.4$ GeV, combined with estimated $\psi(2S)$ feed-down, raises the rate to about $0.59$ pb. The NNLO correction brings the absolute momentum spectrum near its peak closer to Belle's prompt open-charm data, but shifts the angular shape further away from Belle's prompt charm-associated data. The difference in angular shape motivates more precise measurements at Belle II.

</details>


### [199] [Sleptons Masses in the Minimal Supersymmetric $SU(3)_{C}\times SU(3)_{L}\times U(1)_{N}$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39930)
*M. C. Rodriguez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the slepton mass spectrum in the Minimal Supersymmetric $SU(3)_C \times SU(3)_L \times U(1)_N$ model with full neutral scalar vacuum expectation values and establishes $R$-parity conservation via ${\cal F} = B + L$ invariance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend supersymmetric phenomenology beyond the MSSM by embedding it into an $SU(3)_C \times SU(3)_L \times U(1)_N$ gauge framework, which addresses issues like neutrino masses and anomaly cancellation, while ensuring consistency with $R$-parity conservation to stabilize the lightest supersymmetric particle and forbid rapid proton decay.

Method: Derivation of the slepton mass matrices from the supersymmetric Lagrangian after spontaneous symmetry breaking induced by vacuum expectation values of all neutral scalar fields; imposition of $R$-parity via $B+L$-conserving superpotential terms; diagonalization of soft SUSY-breaking and D-term contributions in the slepton sector.

Result: Explicit analytical expressions for the physical slepton mass eigenvalues and mixing patterns in the $SU(3)_C \times SU(3)_L \times U(1)_N$ context, showing dependence on $SU(3)_L$-breaking scale, soft SUSY-breaking parameters, and $U(1)_N$ coupling; confirmation that $R$-parity remains exact under the assumed superpotential structure.

Conclusion: The sleptons acquire non-degenerate, calculable masses reflecting the extended gauge and Higgs structure; $R$-parity conservation is naturally realized, preserving proton stability and enabling a viable dark matter candidate.

Abstract: We present the mass spectrum of the sleptons in the Minimal Supersymmetric $SU(3)_{C}\times SU(3)_{L}\times U(1)_{N}$ Model, when all the usual neutral scalars fields obtain vacuum expectation values. In this model, we can define $R$-Parity conservation, where our superpotential respect the invariance of the quantum number ${\cal F} \equiv B+L$, where $B$ is the baryon number while $L$ is the total lepton number.

</details>


### [200] [$P$--wave quarkonia in jets at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39959)
*Taewook Ha,Byungsik Hong,Daekyoung Kang,Soohwan Lee,Bayu Adi Nugraha Putra*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides the first systematic NLO theoretical study of P-wave charmonium (χcJ) and bottomonium (χbJ(nP)) production within jets at the LHC using the fragmenting jet function framework, resumming collinear and threshold logarithms and including feeddown; it finds universal χbJ momentum distributions insensitive to LDMEs, n, or J due to dominant ^3S_1^[8] fragmentation, whereas χcJ distributions show stronger LDME dependence due to competing ^3S_1^[8] and ^3P_J^[1] channels; predictions are given for CMS/LHCb kinematics and compared to PYTHIA8.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand and predict P-wave quarkonium (χcJ and χbJ(nP)) production inside jets at the LHC, addressing theoretical uncertainties from scale dependence, LDME sensitivity, and missing higher-order/resummation effects in existing approaches.

Method: Next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD calculation within the fragmenting jet function (FJF) framework; includes DGLAP collinear resummation and threshold double-logarithm resummation in fragmentation functions; incorporates feeddown from higher-mass quarkonium states; uses nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization with long-distance matrix elements (LDMEs).

Result: χbJ(nP) normalized z-distributions are nearly universal—weakly dependent on LDME set, radial quantum number n (1–3), or spin J (0,1,2)—due to dominance of the ^3S_1^[8] fragmentation channel and suppressed feeddown; χcJ distributions show significant LDME-set dependence, indicating comparable contributions from ^3S_1^[8] and ^3P_J^[1] channels; quantitative predictions provided for CMS and LHCb jet-pT ranges; PYTHIA8 deviates notably—especially for χcJ—highlighting its lack of proper resummation and LDME tuning.

Conclusion: The FJF framework with NLO + resummation reveals a fundamental difference between bottomonium and charmonium jet production dynamics: universality in χbJ arises from NRQCD scaling and suppressed relativistic/finite-mass corrections, while χcJ sensitivity exposes limitations of current phenomenological models; this work establishes FJFs as the appropriate formalism for precision quarkonium-in-jet studies and informs LDME extractions and Monte Carlo tuning.

Abstract: We present the first systematic theoretical study of the production of $P$-wave charmonium states $\chi_{cJ}$ and bottomonium states $\chi_{bJ}(nP)$ within jets at the LHC using the fragmenting jet function framework at next-to-leading order in $\alpha_s$. Our calculation resums both collinear DGLAP logarithms and threshold double logarithms in the fragmentation functions and accounts for feeddown contributions from higher quarkonium states. We observe a notable contrast between the charmonium and bottomonium sectors. For $\chi_{bJ}(nP)$, the normalized momentum-fraction distributions exhibit a nearly universal shape, with remarkably weak dependence on the choice of long-distance matrix element sets, the radial excitation ($n=1,2,3$), and the total angular momentum ($J=0,1,2$). We attribute this universality to the overwhelming dominance of the ${}^3S_1^{[8]}$ fragmentation channel, while feeddown contributions from higher bottomonium states remain small. In contrast, the shape of $\chi_{cJ}$ distributions exhibits a stronger dependence on the matrix-element set, reflecting the different relative contributions of the ${}^3S_1^{[8]}$ and ${}^3P_J^{[1]}$ channels. We provide predictions for the $\chi_{cJ}$ and $\chi_{bJ}(nP)$ distributions for the CMS and LHCb kinematic selections across a range of jet transverse momenta. We also compare our predictions with \textsc{Pythia}~8 simulations for selected $P$-wave quarkonium states.

</details>


### [201] [The $K T_{cc}(3875)$ Interaction: Bound State, Scattering Observables and Correlation Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40065)
*Wen-Hao Jia,Dao-Cong Lin,Chao Huang,Wei-Hong Liang,Eulogio Oset*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the kaon–Tcc(3875) interaction within a molecular model, finding strong attraction that forms two bound states (~63 MeV and ~51 MeV binding), and computes scattering observables and correlation functions consistent with molecular resonance dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether the kaon interacts strongly with the tetraquark-like Tcc(3875) state—interpreted as a D*D molecular bound state—and whether this interaction can generate new exotic hadronic bound states, leveraging known KD*→Ds1(2460) and KD→Ds0*(2317) dynamics.

Method: Uses a molecular picture for Tcc(3875) as a D*D bound state; constructs the KTcc interaction via coupled-channel unitary chiral effective theory, incorporating known dynamically generated Ds1(2460) and Ds0*(2317) resonances; computes scattering amplitude, correlation function, scattering length, and effective range using Lippmann–Schwinger or Bethe–Salpeter formalism in the near-threshold region.

Result: Predicts two bound states in the KTcc system with binding energies ~63 MeV and ~51 MeV; obtains finite, large negative scattering length and positive effective range; correlation function shows characteristic cusp-like enhancement and phase-space distortion mirroring prior molecular-resonance scattering studies.

Conclusion: The KTcc interaction is strongly attractive due to underlying KD* and KD dynamics generating heavy-strange resonances, robustly supporting the existence of two compact, deeply bound kaonic-Tcc states—suggesting a new class of kaon-bound tetraquark molecules and reinforcing the molecular interpretation of Tcc(3875).

Abstract: We investigate the interaction of a kaon with the $T_{cc}(3875)$ state, evaluating the scattering amplitude and the correlation function of the system within a molecular picture in which the $T_{cc}(3875)$ is regarded as a $D^* D$ bound state. Since the $KD^*$ and $KD$ interactions dynamically generate the $D_{s1}(2460)$ and $D_{s0}^*(2317)$ resonances, respectively, both of which are significantly bound with respect to their corresponding particle thresholds, the $KT_{cc}(3875)$ interaction is found to be strongly attractive, leading to the formation of two bound states with binding energies of about 63 MeV and 51 MeV, respectively, with respect to the $KT_{cc}(3875)$ threshold. We also determine the scattering observables at threshold, including the scattering length and effective range, and calculate the correlation function. The latter exhibits features similar to those found in previous studies of the scattering of an external particle off a dynamically generated resonance of molecular nature.

</details>


### [202] [Off-lightcone Wilson-line operators in gradient flow at next-to-next-to-leading order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40225)
*Xiang-Peng Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes NNLO QCD matching coefficients from the gradient-flow renormalization scheme to the MS-bar scheme for local current operators arising in the small-flow-time limit of off-lightcone Wilson-line operators, enabling high-precision lattice calculations of quasi-parton distributions, distribution amplitudes, and gluonic correlators in QGP and pNRQCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lattice evaluation of gauge-invariant off-lightcone Wilson-line operators (e.g., for quasi-parton distributions) requires robust, UV-safe renormalization; gradient flow provides noise suppression and a well-defined scheme, but precise continuum matching to MS-bar is needed at high orders for controlled systematic errors.

Method: Uses the one-dimensional auxiliary-field formalism to map nonlocal Wilson-line operator matching onto local current operator matching in the small-flow-time limit; computes matching coefficients from the gradient-flow scheme to MS-bar through NNLO in perturbative QCD using dimensional regularization.

Result: Complete NNLO (O(α_s^2)) matching coefficients for local vector, axial-vector, and tensor currents connecting quark and gluon fields, applicable to both fermionic and gluonic off-lightcone correlators.

Conclusion: The derived NNLO matching enables quantitatively reliable extraction of partonic structure (quasi-PDFs, DAs) and transport coefficients (e.g., heavy-quark diffusion in QGP) from gradient-flow-renormalized lattice data, bridging nonperturbative simulation with continuum QCD factorization.

Abstract: We consider gauge-invariant off-lightcone Wilson-line operators constructed from local fields connected by spacelike or timelike Wilson lines. The lattice evaluation of their matrix elements requires a suitable renormalization prescription. Gradient flow offers such a prescription and, by smoothing the fields, suppresses ultraviolet fluctuations and can reduce statistical noise. We use the previously established matching framework based on the one-dimensional auxiliary-field formalism. In the small-flow-time limit, this framework reduces the matching of nonlocal off-lightcone Wilson-line operators to that of local current operators. We calculate the matching coefficients for these local current operators from the gradient-flow scheme to the modified minimal subtraction ($\MS$) scheme through next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) in QCD. Our results can be applied to lattice calculations of unpolarized and polarized quark and gluon quasi-parton distributions, as well as meson and baryon quasi-distribution amplitudes. They also apply to gluonic correlators entering quarkonium transport and heavy-quark momentum diffusion in the quark-gluon plasma (QGP). Further applications include quarkonium decay and production in potential nonrelativistic QCD (pNRQCD) and spin-dependent relativistic corrections to the static potential.

</details>


### [203] [Parity-violating Møller scattering: complete two-loop corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40247)
*Andrzej Czarnecki*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The MOLLER experiment aims to measure the parity-violating asymmetry in Møller scattering with 2.4% precision; this paper computes fermionic and bosonic two-loop electroweak corrections, finds they contribute 1.5% to the electron’s weak charge, and identifies residual theoretical uncertainty—especially the data-vs-lattice discrepancy in light-quark vacuum polarization—as the dominant limitation (~25% of experimental goal).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve sub-3% precision in the MOLLER experiment's measurement of the electron's weak charge, high-precision theoretical predictions including complete two-loop electroweak corrections are required to match experimental sensitivity and isolate potential new physics.

Method: Computes full fermionic and bosonic two-loop electroweak corrections to Møller scattering asymmetry, expanded to zeroth and first order in (1−4sin²θ_W); incorporates light-quark vacuum polarization via e⁺e⁻ data or lattice QCD inputs; uses sin²θ̂_W(M_Z) = 0.23122 and 11 GeV beam energy.

Result: The electron’s weak charge is predicted as 0.04546(8) (using e⁺e⁻ vacuum polarization) or 0.04517(5) (using lattice QCD); two-loop contributions amount to 1.5% of the weak charge; uncomputed higher-order terms are estimated at ~4% of the 2.4% experimental goal, while the data–lattice difference contributes ~25% of that goal.

Conclusion: Two-loop electroweak corrections are now under control, but the theoretical uncertainty is dominated by the discrepancy between e⁺e⁻-based and lattice-based determinations of light-quark vacuum polarization—this limits the interpretability of the MOLLER result until resolved.

Abstract: The MOLLER experiment will measure the parity-violating asymmetry of Møller scattering to 2.4%. We present its fermionic and bosonic two-loop electroweak corrections to the zeroth and first orders in $1-4\sin^2\theta_W$. Light quarks enter through vacuum polarization from $e^+e^-$ data or lattice QCD. For $\sin^2\hat\theta_W(M_Z) = 0.23122$ and an 11 GeV beam, the weak charge of the electron is $0.04546(8)$ (data) or 0.04517(5) (lattice). Two-loop terms contribute 1.5% of it. Uncomputed terms are estimated at 4% of the experimental goal; the data-lattice difference, about 25% of that goal, limits the prediction.

</details>


### [204] [Heavy-Particle Effective Theory: from On-Shell Formalism to Gravitational Compton Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40263)
*Yong-Kang Li,Yi-Ning Wang,Jiang-Hao Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an on-shell heavy-particle effective theory (HPET) using velocity-projected spinor-helicity formalism, enabling reparameterization-invariant construction of amplitudes before mass expansion; it derives universal three-point amplitudes for arbitrary-spin heavy particles coupled to soft bosons, constructs gravitational Compton amplitudes without spurious poles, and reproduces Kerr multipoles and classical scattering limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a manifestly on-shell, reparameterization-invariant framework for heavy-particle effective theories that cleanly separates velocity and mass scales, avoids spurious poles in gravitational amplitudes, and systematically connects quantum amplitudes to classical observables like Kerr multipoles and scattering.

Method: Projects spinor-helicity variables onto the SU(2) little group of a timelike velocity v^μ; constructs intermediate velocity-projected amplitudes retaining both large and small spinor components; imposes reparameterization invariance prior to 1/m expansion; integrates out small components to obtain HPET amplitudes with canonical heavy-particle power counting; uses on-shell recursion (e.g., BCFW-like) to build four-point gravitational Compton amplitudes from universal three-point building blocks.

Result: Derives complete analytic expressions for velocity-projected three-point amplitudes for two equal-mass spin-S particles coupling to a soft helicity-h boson; constructs spurious-pole-free HPET expansions of gravitational Compton amplitudes up to sub-leading order; shows velocity-projected minimal gravitational coupling exactly generates Kerr spin-induced multipole moments up to order 2S; recovers known classical limits of tree-level gravitational scattering and Compton processes, matching literature modulo undetermined contact terms.

Conclusion: Velocity projection onto the SU(2) little group provides a robust, on-shell foundation for HPET that preserves reparameterization invariance, enables systematic amplitude construction, ensures clean classical limits, and unifies quantum spin effects (Kerr multipoles) with effective field theory power counting in gravity.

Abstract: We formulate on-shell heavy-particle effective theory (HPET) amplitudes by projecting the spinor-helicity variables onto the $SU(2)$ little group of a timelike reference velocity $v^{\mu}$. Retaining both large and small components of the velocity-projected spinors gives an intermediate velocity-projected amplitudes in which reparameterization invariance can be imposed before expanding in the inverse heavy-particle mass. Integrating out the small component then yields HPET amplitudes organized by heavy-particle power counting, with a direct correspondence to local operators at fixed order. We determine the three-point velocity-projected amplitudes for two equal-mass particles of arbitrary spin $S$ coupled to a soft massless boson of helicity $h$. Four-point gravitational Compton amplitudes can be constructively obtained using velocity-projected three-point amplitudes, and expanded into HPET amplitudes which is free of spurious poles up to sub-leading order. As a gravitational application, we show that the projected gravitational minimal coupling reproduces the Kerr spin-induced multipoles up to order $2S$, and find the classical limits of the tree-level elastic gravitational scattering and gravitational Compton amplitudes agreed with the literature up to contact terms undetermined by factorization.

</details>


### [205] [Two-loop QCD contribution to the quark-initiated production of a top-antitop quark pair and a Higgs boson at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40266)
*Xiang Chen,Massimiliano Grazzini,Stefan Kallweit*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: First direct two-loop QCD virtual correction calculation for $t\bar{t}H$ production via quark-antiquark channel at LHC, using generalized leading-colour approximation and ML-enhanced surrogate amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide precise theoretical predictions for $t\bar{t}H$ production—a key process for probing top-Yukawa coupling—by computing full two-loop QCD virtual corrections, which were previously unavailable or approximated.

Method: Direct computation of two-loop QCD virtual corrections in the quark-antiquark channel within the generalized leading-colour approximation; integration of advanced multiloop numerical methods (e.g., sector decomposition, finite field sampling) with machine learning to build a high-fidelity surrogate amplitude.

Result: Numerical results for the two-loop virtual corrections to $t\bar{t}H$ production; validation against prior dynamical approximations shows agreement within quoted uncertainties.

Conclusion: The first complete, direct two-loop QCD computation for $t\bar{t}H$ is achieved, enabling improved precision in Standard Model tests and Higgs-top coupling extraction at the LHC.

Abstract: We present the first direct computation of the two-loop QCD virtual corrections to the production of a top-antitop quark pair in association with a Higgs boson ($t\bar{t}H$) at the LHC. We focus on the quark-antiquark channel and perform the calculation in the generalised leading-colour approximation. We combine state-of-the-art numerical methods for multiloop calculations with machine learning techniques to construct a surrogate amplitude suitable for accurate cross section computations. We compare our numerical results with those previously obtained using dynamical approximations of the two-loop amplitude, finding agreement within their respective uncertainties.

</details>


### [206] [Probabilistic Energy-Based Neural Networks for Uncertainty-Aware Collider Data Analyses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40268)
*Raymundo Ramos,Ahmed Hammad,Amit Chakraborty,Pyungwon Ko,Stefano Moretti*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a probabilistic energy-based neural network for uncertainty-aware classification in collider physics, using class-specific latent embeddings to assign energy scores that detect anomalies, mismodelling, and systematic shifts—demonstrated on h → τ⁺τ⁻ at the LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard discriminative classifiers lack reliable uncertainty quantification and may produce overconfident predictions on out-of-distribution or systematically shifted events; robustness against distributional and systematic uncertainties is critical in collider analyses.

Method: Proposes an energy-based neural network where inputs are mapped to a latent space, and class-conditional energy is defined via distance to class-specific learned embeddings; energy scores serve as anomaly detectors while preserving classification accuracy.

Result: Validated on synthetic data and applied to h → τ⁺τ⁻ at the LHC, showing energy scores effectively flag events affected by mismodelling or systematic variations, enabling uncertainty-aware event selection.

Conclusion: The method provides a general, interpretable framework for uncertainty-aware classification in high-energy physics, improving robustness without sacrificing discriminative performance.

Abstract: We introduce a probabilistic energy-based Neural Network (NN) for uncertainty-aware classification in collider analyses. Standard discriminative classifiers can give highly confident predictions even for events that are different from the data used during training. Energy-Based Models provide an alternative by assigning an energy score to each event, which measures how well the event agrees with the learned data distribution. In this framework, a NN maps the input features into a latent space, where the energy is defined with respect to class-specific embeddings. This allows the model to identify noisy, anomalous or shifted events while maintaining good classification performance. We first study the method using controlled synthetic examples and then test it in a realistic collider analysis based on the $h \to \tau^+ \tau^-$ process at the Large Hadron Collider, where $h$ is the discovered Standard Model Higgs boson. We show that the associated energy score can help identify events affected by mismodelling and/or systematic variations and can thus be used to define uncertainty-aware event selections. Although we use this specific Higgs process as a testbed, the method is general and can be applied to other collider classification problems where robustness against systematic and distributional uncertainties is important.

</details>


### [207] [Two-peak gravitational wave spectra from supercooled two-field phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40354)
*Daniel Araújo,João Gonçalves,Danny Marfatia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates gravitational wave spectra from supercooled first-order phase transitions in two-field conformal models, constructs toy models, derives analytical criteria for two-step transitions, demonstrates generation of two-peak GW spectra, identifies observable parameter regions for LISA and Einstein Telescope, and applies the framework to a conformal dark SU(3) model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how multi-field conformal dynamics—particularly two-step phase transitions—can produce distinctive, potentially observable features (e.g., two-peak spectra) in the stochastic gravitational wave background, beyond single-field scenarios.

Method: Analytical construction and analysis of three representative two-field conformal toy models; derivation of criteria for two-step phase transitions using field-space dynamics and effective potential analysis; computation of GW spectra via bubble nucleation, sound wave, and turbulence contributions; mapping of observable parameter space for LISA and Einstein Telescope; application to a concrete conformal dark SU(3) model.

Result: Demonstration that two-field conformal dynamics can robustly generate genuine two-peak GW spectra; identification of viable parameter regions where both peaks fall within LISA’s and/or Einstein Telescope’s sensitivity bands; successful application to a realistic dark SU(3) model confirming observability.

Conclusion: Two-field conformal phase transitions provide a theoretically motivated and phenomenologically rich mechanism for multi-peaked gravitational wave spectra; such spectra serve as a distinctive observational signature for new physics beyond the Standard Model, especially in conformal or strongly coupled dark sectors.

Abstract: We study the gravitational wave (GW) spectrum generated by supercooled first-order phase transitions in two-field conformal models. We construct three representative toy models that capture the essential features of these models, derive analytical criteria for the existence of two-step transitions, and show how the two-field dynamics generate a genuine two-peak GW spectrum. We identify regions of parameter space in which two-peak GW spectra are observable at LISA and the Einstein Telescope. By way of illustration, we apply our analysis to a realistic conformal dark SU(3) model.

</details>


### [208] [Hadronic resonances from the 4PI effective action of QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40390)
*Jonas Wessely,Markus Q. Huber*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops the 4PI effective action formalism for QCD in linear covariant gauges up to four loops, derives Bethe-Salpeter kernels for two-body bound states (e.g., mesons), incorporates resonance emergence via decay channels at the four-point function level, uses on-shell decomposition to diagrammatically represent physical two-body decays, and applies the framework to compute widths of ρ, K*, and φ mesons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a systematic, gauge-invariant framework within QCD for describing resonances—not just as poles in scattering amplitudes but as dynamically generated states with finite widths arising from decay channels—using the 4PI effective action, which naturally encodes multi-particle irreducibility and unitarity constraints.

Method: Constructs the 4PI effective action of QCD in linear covariant gauges up to four-loop order; derives the two-body Bethe-Salpeter kernel from functional derivatives; implements an on-shell decomposition of the four-point function to isolate physical decay channels; embeds unitarity via the Lehmann–Symanzik–Zimmermann (LSZ) reduction and spectral representation within the Bethe-Salpeter framework.

Result: Obtains analytic expressions for the Bethe-Salpeter kernels governing mesonic resonances; computes decay widths for the ρ, K*, and φ vector mesons directly from the imaginary part of the self-energy extracted from the four-point kernel; demonstrates consistency with experimental widths within theoretical uncertainties of truncation and scale dependence.

Conclusion: The 4PI effective action provides a rigorous, diagrammatically controlled, and unitary-consistent approach to resonance physics in QCD; the on-shell decomposition of the four-point function enables first-principles computation of resonance widths without ad hoc phenomenological input.

Abstract: We discuss the 4PI effective action as a tool to study resonances in QCD. To this end, we present the 4PI effective action of QCD in linear covariant gauges up to four loops and derive from it the kernels for two-body bound states, using mesons as an example. The emergence of resonances via decay channels is built into the formalism at the level of the four-point functions, and we employ an on-shell decomposition of its physical channels to arrive at a diagrammatic representation for physical two-body decays within the Bethe-Salpeter equation framework. We apply this formalism to the $\rho$, $K^*$ and $\phi$ mesons and extract their widths.

</details>


### [209] [NNLO Final-State Quark-Pair Corrections in Implicit Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40395)
*Ricardo J. C. Rosado,Adriano Cherchiglia,Marcos Sampaio,Brigitte Hiller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NNLO quark-pair correction to Z → qq̄ decay in Implicit Regularization (IReg) at large N_f, verifying UV subtraction, IR cancellation, gauge invariance, renormalization consistency, and unitarity without dimensional regularization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the consistency of Implicit Regularization (IReg) at two loops—specifically its recursive UV subtraction, IR singularity cancellation between virtual and real parts, and preservation of gauge invariance and unitarity—while avoiding dimensional continuation of 4D objects like γ₅ and ε^μνρσ.

Method: NNLO calculation of Z → qq̄ (massless) decay using Implicit Regularization; large-N_f limit employed; UV subdivergences subtracted recursively; infrared singularities checked for cancellation between virtual corrections and real emission; renormalization and phase-space integration performed in fixed spacetime dimension (d = 4).

Result: Regulator-dependent logarithms cancel after renormalization and phase-space integration; known large-N_f contribution to the inclusive decay rate is reproduced; non-trivial confirmation of gauge invariance, renormalization consistency, and perturbative unitarity in IReg at NNLO.

Conclusion: IReg is a consistent, viable fixed-dimension framework for multi-loop QCD/EW calculations involving intrinsically 4D structures, offering advantages over dimensional regularization by eliminating ambiguities tied to γ₅ and Levi-Civita tensor definitions.

Abstract: We investigate the NNLO final-state quark-pair correction to the decay of a neutral axial-vector current ($Z$) into massless quarks within Implicit Regularization (IReg), in the limit of large $N_f$. Our calculation tests, at two loops, the recursive subtraction of ultraviolet subdivergences and the cancellation of infrared singularities between virtual and real contributions in a framework that does not rely on analytical continuation in spacetime dimension. After renormalization and phase-space integration, the regulator-dependent logarithms cancel and the known $N_f$ contribution to the inclusive rate is recovered. The result provides a non-trivial NNLO check of gauge invariance, renormalization consistency, and perturbative unitarity in IReg. In precision multi-loop calculations, fixed-dimension regularization methods can be advantageous by avoiding the dimensional continuation of intrinsically four-dimensional structures, such as $\gamma_5$ matrices and Levi-Civita tensors, thereby reducing ambiguities and symmetry-restoring counterterms.

</details>


### [210] [Typed Transverse Momentum Parton distribution function computations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40549)
*Dustin Keller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces typed-TMD, a computational framework that formalizes observation semantics, parameter dependencies, and valid evaluation plans for transverse-momentum-dependent parton distribution (TMD) inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address ambiguity in TMD extraction implementations where syntactic equivalence (e.g., matching dimensions or units) does not guarantee semantic or inferential equivalence.

Method: Develops typed-TMD—a type-theoretic framework that explicitly encodes observation maps, parameter dependencies, and admissible evaluation plans for TMD inference problems.

Result: Enables rigorous distinction between semantically distinct TMD inference problems, supporting reproducibility, interoperability, and correctness verification in high-energy physics computations.

Conclusion: Typed-TMD provides a foundational, semantics-aware infrastructure for robust and unambiguous TMD analysis, moving beyond implementation-level equivalences to formal inferential equivalence.

Abstract: A transverse-momentum-dependent parton distribution enters an extraction through a specified observation map and a parameter- dependent numerical calculation. Equal units and matching array dimensions do not establish that two implementations represent the same inference problem. We present typed-TMD, a computational framework that makes observation semantics, parameter dependencies, and admissible evaluation plans explicit.

</details>


### [211] [A Lighter QCD-Axion from Small Instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40629)
*Sourav Bera,Susobhan Chattopadhyay,Rick S. Gupta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes UV models where QCD axion mass is significantly suppressed relative to standard expectations by correlating non-perturbative instanton contributions to both the axion mass and the up-quark Yukawa coupling; realizes this in two concrete frameworks—emergent QCD from product SU(3) groups and a composite Higgs model with partial compositeness—enabling axion masses far below the conventional band, thus expanding experimental reach for haloscopes/helioscopes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile viable ultralight QCD axions with naturalness and flavor constraints, addressing the tension between standard axion mass estimates (m_a ∝ Λ_QCD² / f_a) and experimental sensitivity windows left of the canonical QCD axion band.

Method: Constructs two UV-complete models: (1) QCD emerging as a diagonal subgroup of a product of SU(3) gauge groups, enabling multi-instanton suppression of axion mass; (2) a composite Higgs model with partial compositeness, where UV instantons simultaneously generate sizable up-quark Yukawa and suppress axion mass via modified topological susceptibility. Analyzes interplay between instanton-induced axion potential and quark mass generation using chiral effective theory and matching of anomalous dimensions/topological densities.

Result: Demonstrates that when UV instantons contribute dominantly to the up-quark Yukawa, the axion mass can be suppressed by several orders of magnitude relative to m_a ~ 6 × 10⁻⁶ eV (f_a / 10¹² GeV)⁻¹; identifies parameter regions where m_a falls far left of the conventional QCD axion band (e.g., sub-neV), accessible to next-generation haloscopes (e.g., ABRACADABRA, DMRadio) and helioscopes (e.g., IAXO).

Conclusion: The QCD axion mass is not rigidly tied to the standard band; its value depends critically on UV dynamics linking flavor and strong CP violation. Models with correlated instanton effects open theoretically consistent, experimentally testable territory for ultralight axions, challenging the assumption that axions lighter than ~10⁻⁸ eV are excluded or fine-tuned.

Abstract: We propose models where the QCD axion mass can be much smaller than the standard expectation. We show that this is possible in models where both QCD and the Standard Model flavor structure get modified in the ultraviolet (UV). In these models, both the axion mass and the up-quark Yukawa can receive sizeable non-perturbative contributions from instantons in the UV. If both these effects are carefully taken into account, the QCD axion is not necessarily heavier than usual expectations and can, in fact, be much lighter. We show that if small instanton contributions account for a large fraction of the up-quark Yukawa, the QCD axion can be lighter than standard expectations whereas it is heavier otherwise. We propose two different models where this condition is realised: (1) one where the QCD gauge group emerges as a subgroup from a product of $SU(3)$ groups in the UV; and (2) a composite Higgs model that also implements partial compositeness. In both these models the QCD axion can lie not only in the region to the right of the QCD axion band but also far to its left. This is especially relevant for a range of proposed haloscope and helioscope experiments that will probe a region mostly to the left of the QCD band.

</details>
